{{wide

## Mastery System

*“Clarity over chance. Choice over chaos.”*  

---

The **Mastery System** is a roleplaying game designed for heroes who grow through struggle, discovery, and the choices they make at the table. It is not bound by rigid classes or endless charts of bonuses. Instead, it focuses on **clarity, cinematic action, and player-driven mastery** in a world where success comes not from luck, but from **understanding the system, making smart decisions, and taking responsibility for every choice**. What sets it apart is how it handles power: **it is classless, modular, and mathematically transparent.** Your strength does not come from arbitrary level spikes or hidden feature packages — it comes from **Mastery Ranks**, **Stones**, your chosen **Powers**, and the way you combine them.

**Mastery Ranks keep heroes on the same curve.**  
No one falls behind because their class “comes online later.” No one runs away with the game because of a single feature spike. Progression is **consistent and comparable**, built on a foundation that can be **measured, tuned, and proven**, not guessed. In the Mastery System, players are not at the mercy of hidden mechanics: they **see the numbers, shape their fate, and own their triumphs and failures alike.**

At its heart, the system rests on a few core ideas:

- **Attributes Define Capability**  
Each point in an Attribute adds one die to its Pool. Attributes determine how much raw capability you bring to a roll, but they do not determine your Stones.

- **Stones Define Affinity**  
Stones are gained through **Lifetime Experience** and may be assigned to Attributes or converted into Permanent Colorless Stones. They replace class features with a modular supernatural toolkit, independent of how high or low the linked Attribute is. You decide where your supernatural affinity develops and what kind of hero you become.

- **Raises Create Drama**  
You may always meet the Target Number for a normal success, but by risking more, by *raising the stakes*, you unlock special effects, crushing blows, and cinematic moments. Each Raise is a gamble and a story beat.

- **Actions and Reactions Matter**  
Every round is a choice: attack, defend, maneuver, or risk everything. Reactions keep you engaged even when it’s not your turn — parries, dodges, counterstrikes, and warnings make combat feel alive.

- **Simple Dice, Big Moments**  
Rolls explode. Success is clear. The math is fast, but the outcomes are cinematic. You will know when you land a decisive strike — and when fate turns against you.

---

:
## The Spirit of Mastery

The Mastery System is built for **fast, tactical, and narrative-rich play**. It is a game where numbers represent potential, but choices define outcomes. You are not just rolling dice, you are gambling with destiny, bending your assigned Stones to fuel extraordinary feats, and carving your legend into the world. In Mastery, **every success feels earned**, and every failure pushes the story forward. It is a system that rewards boldness, creativity, and mastery itself.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide

### The Role of the Players
:
You begin as **scattered survivors** remnants of a fallen world, bound by fading memories and a spark of defiance. The game assumes you are not for the demons and that you would join if possible the last embers of rebellion struggling against the demonic order. Whether you fight for freedom, redemption, or your own survival, your story begins in the ashes of the old world.

---
:
This is a **sandbox**, not a railroad. No one will hand you missions or tell you what to do. You decide where to go, who to trust, and what risks to take. The GM shapes the world’s response, but **you set the course** every decision carries weight.

---
:
The world of Destroyed Faith is harsh and uncertain. Resources are scarce, allies are fragile, and every act of mercy or defiance matters. Killing a marked host destroys the vessel but not the demon, saving one may cost lives but can inspire hope in others. Your victories will be small, your sacrifices great yet even the smallest triumph proves that the darkness can bleed.

---

> *“Hope is not given, it is made.”*  
> — Teachings of the Hidden Flame

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

> ## Core Terms
> 
> **Pool & Keep:** Use only eight-sided dice (**d8**). Roll your Pool (X d8), keep the best Y.  
> **Exploding Dice:** On a natural **8**, **reroll that die** and **add** the new result. Repeat on further 8s. An attack designated for **Critical** explodes on natural **7s and 8s** instead.

> **Critical(X):** Before an eligible Martial Attack Pool or damaging Spell Casting Pool is rolled, designate **one source**: **Passive: Critical** (PL 4-7: 1, PL 8-14: 2, PL 15-16: 3 free uses per Combat), **Active Buff: Critical** (printed per-Combat quota while maintained), or **Agility Ability: Crit** (Rank 1/2/3/4: 1/2/3/4 uses in the current Round). The designated Pool explodes on natural **7s and 8s**, including subsequent qualifying rerolls; **Damage Dice are unchanged**. One Pool, including a shared AoE Pool, spends only one use of the chosen source. Unused Passive charges never refresh during Combat; reactivating the Buff does not refill its Combat quota. Disadvantage still permits only one exploding die.

> **Guaranteed Eight:** Before rolling, you may exchange **8 dice from your final dice Pool** for **1 Guaranteed Eight**. Each exchange removes eight dice and adds one Guaranteed Eight to the final Pool. A Guaranteed Eight is treated as a natural **8**: reroll it and add the new result, repeating on further 8s as normal. It remains a normal die result for **Pool & Keep** and is not automatically kept.

> **Target Number (TN):** The value you must reach to hit or succeed.

> **Evade:** The TN an Attack must reach to hit a creature. For an AoE Attack, one roll is compared separately against the defense of each valid creature in the area.

> **AoE Attack:** Roll once for the area; compare separately with each creature's Evade (Martial) or Final Spell TN (Spell). Each hit receives any printed Power Damage and full Specials. Pure AoE Specials buy **no Power Damage Dice**. No Primary Target, splitting, Weapon Damage, or Spell Focus Damage.

> **Autofire:** Declare an ordered series of attacks up to the Power's printed capacity; the same target may appear repeatedly. Make **one** Attack or Casting Roll. Attack number 1 uses the target's normal TN; each later attack adds a cumulative **+4** to its own target TN. The first miss or resistance ends the chain. Roll the complete matching Weapon/Spell Focus plus Power Damage Pool **once** and reuse it for each successful hit; defend against each hit separately.

> **Combat Raises:** Before rolling, declare every Paid Raise (+4 TN each) and any Intellect Free Raises, and **assign each Raise to Damage or an existing numeric Special**. On a successful hit, the declared effects apply: +MR d8 Damage per Raise (AoE: +ceil(MR/2) d8), or +MR to that Special. No Raise allocation after the roll.

> **Penetration(X):** Ignores X points of the target’s Armor.

>**Armor (AR):** Reduces the rolled damage of a successful hit. Armor does not reduce Specials, conditions, ongoing damage, or other effect damage unless a rule explicitly says so.

> **Damage Reduction (DR):** Reduces all incoming damage of the listed type.
 
> **Action:** You may take 1 Attack Action per turn (Attack, Cast, etc.).

> **Reaction:** Special defense actions you may take outside your turn (Guard, Evade, Counterattack, etc.).  
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide

### **Character Creation - Quick Reference**

Create your hero in a consistent order: decide what kind of Echo you are before selecting Powers or equipment that may depend on its Artifact slots.

---

#### Step 1 - Choose Your Echo and Echo Artifact Structure

> Choose your **Echo** first. Record its origin, traits, any required or optional starting **Echo Artifacts**, their occupied equipment slots, and any other structural limitations. Resolve the Echo's starting choices before planning incompatible equipment or Powers. Note any language fixed by the Echo.

---

#### Step 2 - Choose Attributes and Starting Stones

> Assign **4, 4, 3, 3, 2, 2, 2** across the seven Attributes. Assign the **2 starting Stones** to Attributes; neither may be Colorless. Both may go to one Attribute or to two different Attributes. Stone placement is independent of Attribute values. At MR 2, an Attribute may hold no more than **4 Attribute Stones**. New Stones earned **after creation** may instead be chosen as permanent Colorless Stones at 1:1 during an Upgrade Step.

---

#### Step 3 - Choose Echo Imprints

> **Immediately after Attributes**, select **up to MR** free Echo Imprints (normally 2), each tied to an **Attribute 4+**. They progress at Attribute 4/8/12/16/20. They create minor supernatural effects and Echo Memories, never Skill or Combat bonuses.

---

#### Step 4 - Choose Specializations

> Choose **exactly five distinct, freely named Specializations**, each at **Rank 2**, and record their Fields and scopes. These five selections use the full **20 Skill Creation Points** (10 Ranks at 2 points per Rank). **Everyone also receives Perception at Rank 2 for free**, as a sixth, fixed starting Skill. Perception uses no Creation Points and cannot occupy one of the five selections. Learn additional Specializations later through XP advancement. See *Creating and Advancing Skills*.

---

#### Step 5 - Choose Powers

> Choose **two Active Powers at PL 2**, plus Passives equal to available Passive Slots, **one Reaction** and **one Active Buff at PL 4**. A typical MR 2 hero has two Passive Slots; MR 1 has one. The starting Passive(s), Reaction, and Active Buff receive the existing **PL 4 creation exception** even for MR 1. Observe Echo Artifact and equipment restrictions.

---

#### Step 6 - Languages

> Everyone speaks **Common Tongue** and has one additional language. Use the Echo-mandated origin language if applicable; otherwise choose the additional language.

---

#### Step 7 - Schticks

> Choose **one Schtick per MR**. Schticks are cosmetic signatures, not Echo Imprints or Powers.

---

#### Step 8 - Disadvantages and Reroll Points

> Choose up to **8 Disadvantage Points**. Their total sets your **maximum Reroll Points**.

---

#### Step 9 - Finalize Your Hero

> Record Health (**Vitality x 4** HP per Bar), Fear (starts at 0), Defense, Initiative, gear, appearance, background, and motivations. **All 16 Core Rituals are automatically available**; do not select, purchase, or study them at creation. Verify your Echo Artifact capacity, equipment slots, and Power choices together with the GM.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Attributes 

---
:
Characters are defined by **seven attributes**. Each point in an attribute grants **1 die** to the related **Pool**, but attributes in the Mastery System are **not statistics**, they are **channels** of inner power. Your attributes represent how deeply you can reach into the **divine spark** within you, your capacity to focus, release, and shape the energy of your own soul.

---

At character creation, no Attribute may exceed **4** and no starting Attribute is lower than **2**. Attributes determine the size of your dice Pools. **They do not generate Stones.** Stones are gained through Lifetime Experience and are assigned independently to Attribute affinities or converted into Permanent Colorless Stones. Later progression can push Attributes beyond 4, where raw capability grows increasingly exceptional.

---
:
### The Seven Attributes
| Attribute | Concept | Expression |
|---|---|---|
| **Might** | Strength and presence of the body | How fully you can turn your will into force and motion. | 
| **Agility** | Reflex, balance, and flow | How purely you move with instinct and momentum. | 
| **Vitality** | Endurance and survival | How strongly your body can hold the spark of life. | 
| **Intellect** | Knowledge and arcane structure | How far your mind can reach into the patterns of creation. | 
| **Resolve** | Willpower and inner stability | How fiercely your soul resists corruption and bends reality instead of breaking. |
| **Influence** | Charisma and presence | How brightly your essence resonates with others and commands the world through emotion. | 
| **Wits** | Awareness and reaction | How swiftly your consciousness adapts and shapes events before they unfold. | 

---
::

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Starting Attributes

---

Every hero begins with **seven core attributes**:  
*Might, Agility, Vitality, Intellect, Resolve, Influence,* and *Wits.*

---
:
At character creation, attributes no longer use a free point-buy pool.
Instead, assign the following fixed values across your seven attributes:

- **2 attributes at 4**
- **2 attributes at 3**
- **3 attributes at 2**

---
:
Each value may be assigned freely, but no Attribute may exceed **4** or begin below **2** at creation.

---
:
#### Example Attribute Spreads

The following examples show how the starting values **4, 4, 3, 3, 2, 2, 2** might be assigned to different character concepts. These are not classes or fixed builds. They only show how Attribute choices shape your early dice Pools and combat direction. Starting Stone assignment is a separate build choice.

---
:
##### Frontline Fighter

**Might 4, Vitality 4, Agility 3, Wits 3, Resolve 2, Influence 2, Intellect 2**

This player assigns the two starting Stones to **Might** and **Vitality**. That assignment is a build choice, not a consequence of the Attribute values. They are built to hit hard, survive pressure, and hold the front line.

---
:
##### Mobile Skirmisher

**Agility 4, Wits 4, Might 3, Vitality 3, Resolve 2, Influence 2, Intellect 2**

This player assigns the two starting Stones to **Agility** and **Wits**. The same Attributes could have received different Stones if the player wanted a different supernatural focus. They rely on speed, positioning, reactions, and tempo rather than standing still and trading blows.

---
:
##### Spellcaster

**Intellect 4, Resolve 4, Vitality 3, Wits 3, Influence 2, Agility 2, Might 2**

This player assigns the two starting Stones to **Intellect** and **Resolve**. Attribute placement and Stone placement remain separate choices. They are built around spell pressure, resistance, rituals, and Stone options that support casting.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Example: Alaris Chooses Her Attributes

Alaris is built as a **mobile melee skirmisher**. She chooses her Elorian Echo first, as required by the Character Creation steps. The examples in this book are grouped by topic for easier reference, rather than presented in the chronological order of her decisions. She survives through movement, Evade, and Phasing rather than heavy armor, so her highest Attribute values support that identity.

| **Attribute** | **Value** | **Why Alaris chooses it** |
|:--|:--:|:--|
| **Agility** | **4** | Her primary combat Attribute. It supports Finesse attacks, Evade, and movement-based Skills. |
| **Vitality** | **4** | Gives her enough Health to remain in melee when an attack does connect. |
| **Might** | **3** | Keeps her dangerous with a greatsword and supports physical actions. |
| **Wits** | **3** | Supports awareness, tracking, and fast battlefield decisions. |
| **Resolve** | **2** | Gives her basic inner stability and supports several social and occult approaches. |
| **Influence** | **2** | Lets her participate meaningfully in social scenes. |
| **Intellect** | **2** | Structured analysis and academic spellcasting are not central to this concept. |

Alaris then assigns her two starting Stones independently: one **Agility Stone** and one **Vitality Stone**. **Echo Imprints (next step):** Both Agility and Vitality are 4, so Alaris may select Imprints from either Attribute. At starting MR 2, she chooses **Catfall (Agility)** and **Living Warmth (Vitality)** as her two free Echo Imprints. Their supernatural effects and Echo Memories do not replace Movement or Healing Powers.

{{note
##### What this example shows
Start with the character's intended playstyle, then place the fixed values where they support that playstyle. The lowest Attribute is not a punishment; it identifies what the character is not trying to specialize in.
}}

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Skills

*"What you know determines what you can attempt. How you act determines the Attribute. How far you push determines the risk."*

**Skills** are learned fields of expertise represented by freely named **Specializations** (Rank 1–8; Rank 0 is untrained). Skill Rank determines training and available Raises. The GM chooses the applicable Specialization, Attribute, and task difficulty according to the character's method.

**Skill Roll = Relevant Attribute k Mastery Rank.** Roll exploding d8 using the usual Advantage/Disadvantage, Health, Fear, and Reroll Point rules. The task determines the **Base TN**, while the Specialization determines which techniques the character can perform and how many Raises are available.

**Skill Check (four steps)**

1. **Set the task:** The GM defines the intended result, relevant Attribute, Specialization, and **Base TN = Task Level × 4**. Task difficulty remains the same as the character gains Mastery Rank.
2. **Declare Paid Raises:** The player declares the **number** of Paid Raises before rolling, up to the Rank's Paid limit. Each adds **+4 TN**. Free Raises cost **+0 TN** and become available after success.
3. **Resolve:** Roll **Attribute k MR** against **Final TN = Base TN + (4 × Paid Raises)**, or use an eligible form of Passive Success.
4. **Apply Raises:** On success, allocate the declared Paid Raises plus available Free Raises to suitable effects. The GM confirms the outcome. Unused Raises expire after resolution.

| Task Level | Difficulty | Base TN | Example |
|:--:|---|:--:|---|
| **1** | Simple | **4** | Hammer a nail into prepared wood |
| **2** | Easy | **8** | Forge ordinary nails; follow obvious tracks |
| **3** | Moderate | **12** | Repair an ordinary tool |
| **4** | Demanding | **16** | Forge a reliable sword; treat illness under pressure |
| **5** | Difficult | **20** | Diagnose a complex injury quickly |
| **6** | Very difficult | **24** | Forge an unusual alloy blade |
| **7** | Exceptional | **28** | Solve a rare, intricate professional problem |
| **8+** | Extraordinary | **32+** | Accomplish an unprecedented feat |

**Task Level** measures execution difficulty; **Skill Rank** measures learned capability. Examples are guidelines. Circumstances, tools, opposition, and the chosen method determine the actual check.

### Specialization Ranks and Raises

| Rank | Training | Paid | Free |
|:--:|---|:--:|:--:|
| **0** | Untrained | **0** | **0** |
| **1** | Novice: basic supervised work | **1** | **0** |
| **2** | Apprentice: routine basic work | **2** | **0** |
| **3** | Trained: straightforward independent work | **3** | **0** |
| **4** | Professional: ordinary professional practice | **3** | **1** |
| **5** | Expert: advanced methods | **4** | **1** |
| **6** | Master: complex specialist work | **4** | **2** |
| **7** | Grandmaster: rare, exceptional methods | **5** | **2** |
| **8** | Legendary: highest mundane mastery | **5** | **3** |

**Paid + Free Raises are capped by Skill Rank.** Free Raises unlock at Ranks **4, 6, and 8** and apply automatically on each successful Skill Check. Declare only the number of Paid Raises before rolling; choose their effects together with Free Raises **after** success. High rolls grant no additional Raises. Training defines which tasks are possible: familiar work within Rank can be automatic, unfamiliar work is checked normally, plausible improvisation uses untrained rules, and genuinely specialist procedures require the appropriate knowledge or preparation.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
**Raise effects (one Raise each)**

| Effect | Benefit |
|---|---|
| **Scope** | Double similar eligible units or coverage (2 Raises = ×4; 3 = ×8) |
| **Speed** | Halve feasible work time (2 Raises = one quarter) |
| **Quality** | Improve workmanship, durability, clarity, or persuasiveness by one suitable step |
| **Efficiency** | Reduce avoidable waste or effort by one practical step |
| **Insight** | Gain one additional fact, inference, or clue supported by the evidence |
| **Safety** | Prevent or reduce one foreseeable secondary complication |

Raises improve a **feasible result** within the declared method, available resources, and circumstances. The GM approves their application. Skill Raises provide mundane benefits; supernatural effects, combat advantages, and magical healing use their respective Powers or Rituals. **Social Challenges** use the same post-success Skill Raise allocation; Combat and Ritual Raises follow their own timing rules.

---
:
### Passive Success, Perception, and Specializations

**Familiar routine work:** A trained character completes a familiar task within their Skill Rank automatically when tools, time, and conditions are suitable. This grants the **basic result** without Raise benefits.

**Passive Skill Value = 2 × Relevant Attribute.** For calm, eligible tasks, compare this value with the **Final TN**, including any declared Paid Raises. Meeting the TN grants success and the corresponding Paid and Free Raise effects. Under danger, opposition, instability, or urgent consequences, roll normally. Generally accessible tasks may also use Passive Success without a Specialization, but without Raises.

**Untrained attempts:** Roll the full relevant **Attribute k MR** with **Disadvantage** (only one die may explode) and **0 Raises**. Specialist results require the necessary knowledge, tools, or a credible alternative method.

**Perception:** Every character receives **Perception Rank 2 for free**, in addition to five chosen Specializations. It uses normal Skill Checks and Raises, costs **4 XP per later Rank**, and reaches a maximum of Rank 8.

| Attribute | Perception focus |
|---|---|
| **Wits** | Movement, sound, concealed presence, ambush signs |
| **Intellect** | Visible inconsistencies, patterns, spatial clues |
| **Resolve** | Occult or spiritual pressure, when the active Sense permits it |

For **Stealth opposition**, resolve the concealer's setup Skill Check first. The successful rolled result becomes the observer's Perception TN; **ties favor the observer**. Combat Sense limitations and per-target/per-Round detection rules still apply.

**Seven Fields of Expertise** organize freely named Specializations. The GM assigns the relevant **Attribute according to the method**, not the Field.

| Field | Example Specializations |
|---|---|
| **Physical** | Climbing, Swimming |
| **Finesse** | Stealth, Sleight of Hand |
| **Survival** | Tracking, Animal Handling |
| **Science & Craft** | Medicine, Alchemy, Blacksmithing |
| **Occult** | Demonology, Warding Traditions |
| **Social** | Persuasion, Deception |
| **Investigation** | Forensics, Streetwise |
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
**Define a Specialization:** Give it one Field, a clear name, and a one-sentence scope agreed with the GM. For example, **Blacksmithing** covers metal weapons, tools, fittings, repair, and evaluation; it is broader than forging a single item and narrower than all crafting. Comparable Specializations should have comparable breadth. Equipment penalties to Physical Skill Checks also apply to Finesse and other physically impeded tasks, regardless of the Attribute used.

**At character creation:** Choose **five different Specializations at Rank 2** (20 Skill Creation Points total), plus **Perception Rank 2** for free. **Advancement:** A new Rank 1 Specialization costs **4 XP**; each further Rank costs **4 XP**, including Perception, up to Rank 8 (**32 XP from Rank 0 to 8**). Multiple consecutive Ranks may be purchased in one Upgrade Step by paying each intermediate cost. New training should fit the campaign fiction.

### Examples

1. **Blacksmithing 4:** Making nails has Base TN 8. Declare **2 Paid Raises** for Final TN 16. On success, add **1 Free Raise** and allocate **Scope 2 + Speed 1**: four batches in half the feasible time, with enough materials. A Rank 1 smith can also make ordinary nails; the task still has Base TN 8.
2. **Medicine 4:** A physician with **Intellect 4, MR 2** treats a wound under pressure (Base TN 16). **1 Paid Raise** gives Final TN 20; roll **4k2**. A result of 24 succeeds and allows **Speed 1 + Safety 1** after the Free Raise. Familiar care in safe conditions may instead succeed routinely.
3. **Stealth 5:** Crossing a guarded passage has Base TN 16. **1 Paid Raise** gives Final TN 20; success adds **1 Free Raise**, potentially for **Scope 1 + Safety 1**. The actual successful rolled result becomes each eligible guard's Perception TN.
4. **Forensics 5:** Analyze a disturbed chamber at Base TN 16. **1 Paid Raise** raises it to 20. On success, use the Paid and Free Raises for **Insight 2**, such as recognizing staged furniture and the order of movements, if evidence supports both.
5. **Alchemy 5:** Prepare an antidote at Base TN 16. **2 Paid Raises** give Final TN 24. A success supplies **3 total Raises**, for example **Scope 2 + Efficiency 1**: four doses with sufficient ingredients and reduced waste.
6. **Untrained Medicine:** Cleaning a minor wound under pressure is possible with the relevant Attribute **k MR**, Disadvantage, and no Raises. Complex surgery requires suitable training or assistance.
7. **Alaris at creation:** Choose **Stealth 2, Tracking 2, Acrobatics 2, Streetwise 2, Medicine 2**, plus **Perception 2** for free. The GM chooses the appropriate Attribute for each check. Blacksmithing, for example, might use Might for heavy forging, Intellect for alloy planning, or Agility for fine finishing.

**Related systems:** All 16 Core Rituals are available at character creation and use their own predeclared effect tiers and Stone costs. Summon Skill Dice use their own rules. Social Challenges use the Skill Check procedure above.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Choose your Powers

Powers are the abilities that define how your character acts in play.

---
They are not classes.  
They are not fixed builds. They are not prescribed roles. A character chooses Powers to define what they can do in combat, how they survive, how they move, how they react, and how they express their mastery.

---

### Starting Powers

At character creation, choose **two Active Powers at Power Level 2**. In addition, choose a number of **Passives equal to your available Passive Slots**, plus **one Reaction** and **one Active Buff Power** at **Power Level 4**. Characters normally begin at **MR 2** and therefore choose **two Passives**. If the campaign begins at **MR 1**, choose **one Passive** instead. **Starting exception:** the granted Passive, Reaction, and Active Buff remain at **PL 4** at MR 1. The normal **PL 2 cap** applies to other Powers and subsequent purchases/upgrades.

---

### Power Level

Each Power has a **Power Level (PL)**. A Power's Power Level determines which level of that Power you use. If a Power is Power Level 1, use its Power Level 1 entry. If a Power is Power Level 2, use its Power Level 2 entry. Power Level is the only progression value needed for Powers. Do not create a separate Spell Level, Tree Level, Class Level, or similar value.

---

### Power Types & Catalogues

Every Power belongs to one **Power Type**. Each Power Type follows its own rules, templates, limits, and examples. The Core Rulebook only explains the general role of Powers. The external Power Catalogues are the rules source for building, reading, and resolving specific Powers.

---
Use the Power Catalogues when you need to know:

- how a Power is built,
- which templates are available,
- how Power Points are priced,
- what each Power Level grants,
- how examples are resolved,
- how Areas of Effect, Autofire, Split-Attack, and other multi-target techniques work,
- how Powers interact with Armor, Evade, Specials, Damage Reduction, movement, and action economy.

| **Power Type / Catalogue** | **Use it for** |
|---|---|
| **Actives** | Action-based Powers: attacks, Scarred-Bar Restoration, Cleanse, Specials, AoE, Autofire, Split-Attack, zones, barriers, and other direct effects. |
| **Passives** | Always-on or pre-selected character features: Armor, Evade, Damage, Health, Damage Reduction, Phasing, and passive Special support. |
| **Active Buffs** | Temporary combat states: stances, auras, offensive/defensive boosts, and maintained effects. Size-changing Transformations use their own noncombat rules; visual Form does not change mechanics. |
| **Reactions** | Immediate answers to specific triggers: defenses, counters, emergency protection, interception, and trigger-based responses. |
| **Movement Powers** | Movement replacements: dash, leap, teleport, flight, burrow, wall movement, phase passage, and similar movement options. |

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

{{note
##### Mentalist Option
A character may build around Mental Powers by choosing **Telepathy** as a Passive. Telepathy grants Telepathic Access and includes voluntary Mind Links at no additional Power cost. The character may then choose Mental Attack, Mind Illusion, Mind Probe, or Mental Control as Active Powers. A Mental Power's Power Level cannot normally exceed the character's **Passive: Telepathy** Power Level.
}}

---
:
### Spells

A Spell is not a separate Power type. **Spell is a delivery method** for an eligible Ranged Active Power. The underlying Power keeps its Power Level, Range, Damage, AoE, Duration, Specials, action type, and base Technical Reference. Spell delivery changes how the Power is rolled and resisted. A Ranged Active with Specials may use Spell delivery normally. A character may also have a number of **Special-less Spell Powers equal to Mastery Rank**. A Spell uses **Intellect**, **Resolve**, or **Influence** as its fixed casting source. See the Magic rules for Casting Rolls, Spell TN, Spell Resistance, and the full Special-less Spell limit.

---

### Building a Character

When choosing Powers, think about what your character actually does during play.

A complete character usually wants some mix of:

- a way to affect enemies,
- a way to survive pressure,
- a way to move or control position,
- and a way to respond when threatened.

You do not need to cover every category. A fragile sniper, a fortress guardian, a battlefield controller, and a mobile duelist may all choose very different Power mixes. What matters is that each Power has a clear job. For examples, templates, and detailed build guidance, use the external Power Catalogue documents.

---

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Example: Alaris Chooses Her Powers

Alaris begins at **MR 2** and uses the standard starting package: **two Actives at Power Level 2**, plus **two Passives**, **one Active Buff**, and **one Reaction** at Power Level 4.

| **Power** | **Power Level** | **Role in the build** |
|:--|:--:|:--|
| **Melee Single Target Attack** | **2** | Reliable direct weapon damage. |
| **Melee Single Target Special: Sundered** | **2** | Applies Sundered to prepare the target for later non-Spell attacks. This profile adds **0d8 Power Damage**; a successful single-target Martial hit still adds normal Weapon Damage. |
| **Passive: Evade** | **4** | Raises her everyday defense against direct attacks. |
| **Passive: Phasing** | **4** | Establishes Phasing at the start of combat. |
| **Active Buff: Phasing** | **4** | Reinforces that Phasing defense during difficult fights. |
| **Reaction: Phasing** | **4** | Provides an immediate answer to a decisive hit. |

<img src="https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/Rulebook/Alaris-Example-Powers.png" style="display:block; width:680px; margin:10px auto 0 auto;" />

The two Actives give Alaris a choice between immediate damage and preparing a target for the group. Her defensive Powers then commit to one clear identity: **Evade handles ordinary pressure, while Phasing protects her from the hits that matter most.**

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Choose a Language

> All souls share the **Common Tongue**, born from the Echoes of the First War.  
> Every character also learns **one additional language**. For some Echoes, this language is fixed by their origin and cannot be chosen freely.

- **Dwarves** automatically learn **Dwarvish**.
- **Dragonborn** automatically learn **Draconic**.
- **Sentinels** automatically learn **Mustelic**.
- **Elorians** automatically learn **Elvish**.
- Characters of **all other Echoes** choose their additional language freely to reflect their origin, training, culture, or story.

---
::
#### Languages of the Known Realms

- **Common Tongue** – spoken by all civilized nations  
- **Arcane** – language of runes, sigils, and scholars of the old world  
- **Infernal** – the whispered bargains of hell  
- **Celestial** – hymns of the divine and long-fallen angels  
- **Dwarvish** – forged words of stone and steel  
- **The First** – ancient tongue of the Titan-born  
- **Elvish** – fluid and timeless, carried by wind and memory  
- **Draconic** – the primal roar of flame, wave, and storm  
- **Mustelic** – the precise command-tongue of Sentinels and divine law

---
:
#### Example

A Human with a demonic mark might choose **Infernal**. Their languages are: **Common Tongue, Infernal**. An Elorian wanderer automatically learns **Elvish**. Their languages are: **Common Tongue, Elvish**.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Schticks
> *“Power leaves traces — in the body, in the soul, and in the world around you.”*

Schticks are **narrative signatures** created by the power awakening within a character. They may be physical, visual, audible, emotional, or supernatural: an unnatural eye color, frost gathering on the skin, a voice that carries a strange echo, sparks appearing during moments of anger, or shadows briefly moving against the light. Schticks show **how Mastery changes a character**. They make growing power visible in the fiction, but they are not Powers, abilities, or mechanical upgrades.

---

#### What Are Schticks?

A Schtick is a distinctive trait that makes a character recognizable and shows how their inner power manifests in the world. Schticks have **no mechanical effect**. They do not change Attributes, Skills, dice pools, Target Numbers, Damage, Armor, Evade, Movement, Senses, concealment, social rolls, or any other game value. A Schtick may influence how the world is described or how NPCs react in the fiction, but it never grants an automatic bonus, penalty, success, failure, or special capability.

> **A Schtick describes what your power looks or feels like. It does not determine what your power can do.**

If a trait should provide an actual mechanical effect, it must come from a Power, Echo, Artifact, Stone Ability, or another rule that explicitly grants that effect.

---

#### Acquisition

You gain **one Schtick for each Mastery Rank** you possess.

A character therefore has:

| **Mastery Rank** | **Schticks** |
|:--:|:--:|
| **MR 1** | 1 |
| **MR 2** | 2 |
| **MR 3** | 3 |
| **MR 4** | 4 |
| **MR 5** | 5 |
| **MR 6** | 6 |
| **MR 7** | 7 |
| **MR 8** | 8 |

A character beginning at **MR 2** therefore starts with **two Schticks**. When your Mastery Rank increases, choose another Schtick that represents how your growing power has changed you. Schticks are normally permanent. They may change through major transformations, rituals, Artifact awakenings, Echo developments, or other significant events if the player and GM agree that the change fits the story.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Progressive Manifestation

Schticks generally become more striking as Mastery increases. Early manifestations are subtle enough to be dismissed as unusual. At higher Mastery, the character may visibly appear touched by forces beyond ordinary mortal nature.

The following scale is a guideline for tone rather than a mechanical restriction:

| **Mastery Rank** | **Manifestation Scale** | **Example — Frost** |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **MR 1 — Minor Trait** | A subtle physical or sensory peculiarity. | *Your breath mists even in warm rooms.* |
| **MR 2 — Distinct Feature** | A clearly unusual characteristic others may notice. | *Your touch feels cold and moisture briefly crystallizes against your skin.* |
| **MR 3 — Defining Aspect** | Your power visibly marks your body or presence. | *Pale-blue lines spread beneath your skin like frozen veins.* |
| **MR 4 — Environmental Echo** | Your presence creates small, harmless changes around you. | *Frost gathers briefly on objects you touch and cold lingers where you stand.* |
| **MR 5+ — Mythic Signature** | Your nature becomes increasingly difficult to mistake for something ordinary. | *A halo of frost shimmers around you and tiny snowflakes sometimes drift through still air.* |

A new Schtick does not have to be an upgraded version of an earlier one. A character may develop several different manifestations representing different aspects of their Powers, Echo, Artifacts, experiences, or personality.

---

#### Using Schticks in Play

Schticks exist to help describe characters and their evolution. Use them when describing how a character enters a scene, activates a Power, becomes emotional, suffers injury, invokes an Artifact, or reaches a new stage of Mastery. They may also become recognizable features that friends, enemies, witnesses, or legends associate with that character. They remain narrative unless another rule explicitly gives them mechanics.

{{note
##### Schticks Are Not Powers

A Schtick cannot provide a free mechanical ability. A character whose eyes glow in darkness does not automatically gain Darkvision. A character surrounded by frost does not deal cold damage. A character who appears briefly translucent does not gain Phasing. A character whose voice sounds terrifying does not gain a bonus to Intimidation. If you want one of those effects, choose or acquire the appropriate Power.

**Describe freely. Resolve mechanically through the rules.**
}}

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Example: Schticks

The following examples show how different themes might manifest as Mastery grows. These are suggestions, not fixed lists.

#### Freeze

| **Rank** | **Schtick Example** |
|:--|:--|
| **MR 1 — Minor Trait** | Your breath mists even in warm rooms. |
| **MR 2 — Distinct Feature** | Your touch feels cold, and tears or sweat may crystallize briefly. |
| **MR 3 — Defining Aspect** | Pale-blue lines spread beneath your skin like frozen veins. |
| **MR 4 — Environmental Echo** | Frost gathers briefly on objects you touch, and cold lingers where you stand. |
| **MR 5+ — Mythic Signature** | Fine snow sometimes drifts around you even beneath a clear sky. |

---

#### Ruin

| **Rank** | **Schtick Example** |
|:--|:--|
| **MR 1 — Minor Trait** | Your breath carries the faint scent of smoke. |
| **MR 2 — Distinct Feature** | Sparks flicker at your lips, fingers, or eyes when emotion rises. |
| **MR 3 — Defining Aspect** | Thin ember-lines glow beneath your skin, especially around scars or veins. |
| **MR 4 — Environmental Echo** | Heat shimmer surrounds you, candles lean toward you, and ash drifts in still air. |
| **MR 5+ — Mythic Signature** | Your silhouette sometimes appears wreathed in distant flame without anything actually burning. |

---

#### Beast

| **Rank** | **Schtick Example** |
|:--|:--|
| **MR 1 — Minor Trait** | Your eyes reflect light like an animal's in darkness. |
| **MR 2 — Distinct Feature** | Your movements become unnervingly still, fluid, or predatory. |
| **MR 3 — Defining Aspect** | Teeth, nails, posture, voice, or facial features take on a clearly bestial quality. |
| **MR 4 — Environmental Echo** | Nearby animals become noticeably attentive, restless, or quiet when your power stirs. |
| **MR 5+ — Mythic Signature** | For an instant, observers may glimpse the outline of something far larger and more primal moving with you. |

---

#### Divine

| **Rank** | **Schtick Example** |
|:--|:--|
| **MR 1 — Minor Trait** | Your eyes catch a faint blue gleam in darkness. |
| **MR 2 — Distinct Feature** | A cold blue shimmer gathers around your scars, hands, or pupils when you focus. |
| **MR 3 — Defining Aspect** | Your presence carries a visible blue radiance, like moonlight beneath the skin. |
| **MR 4 — Environmental Echo** | The air around you dims and glows blue; dust, mist, or falling ash hangs briefly in your presence. |
| **MR 5+ — Mythic Signature** | For brief moments, the space around you resembles sacred light from an age that no longer exists. |
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Example: Alaris Chooses Languages and Schticks

Every character speaks the **Common Tongue** and gains one additional language. For Elorians, this language is fixed as **Elvish**. Alaris therefore automatically learns **Elvish** through her Elorian Echo, connecting her directly to Elorian history, communities, and surviving traditions. Alaris is Mastery Rank 2 and therefore chooses two Schticks. Both are purely descriptive and grant no concealment, teleportation, movement, or defensive benefit.

### Between the Steps

Sometimes Alaris appears to vanish for the smallest part of a movement. An observer sees where the step begins and where it ends, but a fragment between those moments seems to be missing from reality.

### The Six Within

When Alaris channels one of her Powers, her eyes become completely black. Anyone looking closely can see the **Six Constants** burning faintly inside them like distant stars.

{{note
##### What this example shows
A Schtick should make a character recognizable without duplicating a Power as a free mechanical benefit.
}}

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Playable Echoes
:

Echoes are **remnants of ancient lineages**, manifestations of divine, arcane, or elemental origins that survived the collapse of the Age of the Gods. Each Echo carries the **imprint of a lost world** its powers, its instincts, its flaws. To be an Echo is to be **a living artifact**: flesh shaped by memory, bound by purpose.

You can choose from the following Echoes:

- **Humans** – Masters of consequence: survivors who turn scarcity into leverage, make ugly deals, improvise with nothing, and pay the price later in Fear, fractures, and relationships.

- **Halflings** – The hunted hearthfolk: nearly extinct survivors whose warmth, memory, and soul-rich blood are prized as demonic delicacies. Most live on the run; others survive in locked cellars and feast-prisons, waiting to be served.

- **Dwarves** – The bastion against Hell: incorruptible stone-hearts who stand where others break, holding the line against demons, preserving what must not fall, and turning endurance into a weapon.

- **Elves** – A rare, burning glare: outsiders between realms whose light cuts through lies and rot, almost myth, almost painful, too bright to be common and too sharp to be safe.

- **Sentinels** – Forgotten fragments of divine order: living verdict-machines whose purpose outlived their makers, glitching through obedience, justice, and the hollow silence where meaning used to be.

- **Titanborn** – Awakened fury of the godless age: revered, wild front-line champions driven by inner rage, rising where the gods are gone to reclaim the world from demons with blood, steel, and will.

- **Dragonborn** – Hidden survivors of extermination: a people who endured genocide through secrecy and retreat, avoiding conflict because if the last of their kind die, the great song dies with them.

- **Deathless** – Those whom death failed to claim: once-living souls who returned changed, caught between memories of life and the distant summons of the Hollow King. Their Echo is bound to the Hollow Crown.

* **Unbound** – Those who chose freedom over purity: altered survivors who draw power from nature, forbidden craft, ancient relics, transformation, and traditions the gods once condemned. They are not a single people, but a loose family of paths united by one thing: they refuse to let gods, demons, or blood decide what they are.

  The Unbound include:

  * **Beast** – shapeshifters who embrace the animal nature within them.
  * **Witch** – practitioners of forbidden traditions, old magic, and coven lore.
  * **Bane: Alchemist** – hunters who harvest and refine natural materials to empower themselves against their prey.
  * **Bane: Greenwarden** – wardens bound to the living world, its creatures, and its untamed places.
  * **Bane: Relic Hunter** – seekers who recover and wield remnants of powers and civilizations that should have stayed buried.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Example: Alaris Chooses Her Echo

Alaris chooses the **Elorian (Elf) Echo**. It supports the same identity already established by her Attributes and Powers: grace, difficult movement, and remaining hard to pin down. Her **Elorian Stride** Echo Artifact embodies otherworldly balance and movement. Her **Catfall** Echo Imprint echoes that theme through controlled landings and fleeting memories of another life. It is not an additional Movement Power.

<img src="https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/Rulebook/Alaris-Example-Echo.png" style="display:block; width:680px; margin:10px auto 0 auto;" />

{{note
##### Echo choice in practice
An Echo should support both the fiction and the ways a character approaches scenes. Alaris is a greatsword fighter, but her Echo also makes her distinctive during infiltration, travel, and exploration.
}}

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

### Echo Artifacts

Some Echoes are bound to physical manifestations called **Echo Artifacts**. An Echo Artifact is not ordinary equipment. It may be a body feature, inherited relic, living armor, supernatural organ, ancestral weapon, divine mark, machine frame, scar, scale, claw, horn, or similar manifestation.

Echo Artifacts follow the normal Artifact rules:

- They count against your **Artifact Capacity**.
- They occupy their listed **Equipment Slot**.
- They cannot normally be removed, sold, stolen, replaced, unequipped, or unbound.
- They progress through Artifact Levels like other Artifacts.
- They may grant Base Values, Artifact Functions, Stone Power Support, Active Buffs, Reactions, Movement Powers, Sense Options, or other written effects if the Artifact says so.
- A Sense Option is listed separately and never consumes Base Value A, B, or C.

A character normally has **4 Artifact Capacity**.

---
#### Choosing Echo Artifacts

Not every Echo handles Echo Artifacts the same way. Some Echoes, including Humans and Halflings, begin with no fixed Echo Artifacts. Others must choose at least one Echo Artifact because their body or bloodline is defined by it.

| **Echo** | **Required Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Maximum Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Free Artifact Capacity Left** |
|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Human** | 0 | 0 | 4 |
| **Halfling** | 0 | 1 | 3–4 |
| **Dwarf** | 1 | 1 | 3 |
| **Elf** | 1 | 1 | 3 |
| **Dragonborn** | 1 | 3 | 1–3 |
| **Titanborn** | 1 | 1 | 3 |
| **Sentinel** | 1 | 1 | 3 |
| **Deathless** | 1 | 1 | 3 |
| **Unbound — Beast** | 1 | 2 | 2-3 |
| **Unbound — Witch** | 1 | 1 | 3 |
| **Unbound — Bane: Alchemist** | 1 | 1 | 3 |
| **Unbound — Bane: Greenwarden** | 1 | 1 | 3 |
| **Unbound — Bane: Relic Hunter** | 1 | 1 | 3 |

If an Echo requires Echo Artifacts, you must choose at least the required number from that Echo's available Echo Artifact list at character creation. If an Echo allows more than one Echo Artifact, you may choose additional Echo Artifacts up to the listed maximum. Each chosen Echo Artifact uses **1 Artifact Capacity**, unless the Artifact itself says otherwise.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Later Echo Artifacts

Echo Artifacts may sometimes be gained later.

This can happen through:

- Echo level progression,
- story events,
- awakening an inherited feature,
- recovering a lost body function,
- reforging an Echo relic,
- or explicit GM approval.

You cannot freely buy Echo Artifacts from another Echo. You cannot freely replace one Echo Artifact with another unless the Echo or GM explicitly allows it.

---

### Inherited Memories

An Echo may carry impressions from a life or age the character never experienced: a forgotten name, the shape of an ancient place, a half-remembered voice, or an instinct older than the character's own memories. Such moments can inform a character's story, relationships, and portrayal. These inherited impressions are narrative only; they add no separate rule, resource, or roll.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

### Humans

<div style="width:60%; ">
Humans are the most widespread people in the shattered world of Destroyed Faith. They are adaptable, resilient, and painfully easy to use. Where other Echoes fade into legend, withdraw behind old walls, or carry the burden of stranger blood, humans remain everywhere: in ruined villages, demon-held cities, broken shrines, prison caravans, and forgotten roads.

Once, they lived beneath the order of the gods, serving as priests, soldiers, builders, and keepers of daily life. Now they endure among ruins, rituals, and the weight of things that no longer answer. The cities they built have become strongholds of demons, tyrants, and false faith. Those who are not made into guards, laborers, vessels, or sacrifices learn to hide, flee, obey, or die.

Yet humanity is not easily erased. Again and again, humans gather in hidden enclaves, rebel cells, smuggler camps, and wandering caravans. They keep fires lit where they should have gone out long ago. They bury their dead, pass on forbidden names, and teach their children how to survive one more winter, one more patrol, one more night. Many humans bear a demonic mark, burned into flesh by pact, force, punishment, or inheritance. Some marks whisper. Some wound. Some tempt. A mark may offer strength, but never freely. It is always also a claim a presence that presses closer with every moment of weakness, fear, or surrender.

</div>

Humans endure because they change. Some become willing servants of hell. Some search for lost rites, hidden truths, and fragments of dead faith. Others fight for smaller, fiercer things: family, warmth, loyalty, and the simple refusal to let the darkness take everything.

:

| **Echo** | **Creature Type** | **Size** | **Speed** | **Required Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Maximum Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Free Artifact Capacity Left** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Human** | Humanoid | Medium | 8 m | 0 | 0 | 4 |

:

### Halflings

<div style="width:65%; ">
Halflings were once the hearthfolk of Tyhra: gardeners, millers, cooks, ferrymen, peddlers, and keepers of roadside inns. They were rarely rulers and seldom conquerors. Their strength lay in making ordinary life endure, keeping food warm, paths remembered, children hidden, and strangers welcome long enough to become neighbors. That life ended when the demons discovered what Halflings carried within them.

Something in Halfling flesh preserves the warmth of the old world. Demons call it **Hearthsweet**: a richness of blood, memory, comfort, and mortal joy that they cannot create for themselves. To many demons, a Halfling is not merely prey. It is a delicacy, a status symbol, and a reminder of everything Hell can consume but never truly possess.

The slaughter was systematic. Villages disappeared. Family lines were catalogued. Demon Houses built hidden larders beneath estates and fortresses, where captured Halflings were kept alive, carefully fed, and taken away one by one for private feasts. Some prisoners are found with old bites, missing fingers, ears, or pieces of flesh. Sampled and returned to their cells so that the rest may be saved for later.
</div>
Most Halflings alive today are fugitives. They hide beneath false names, inside wagon walls, under ruined floors, in root-cellars, sewers, smugglers' tunnels, and abandoned burrows too small for armored hunters. They travel in tiny family groups, rarely remain anywhere for long, and distrust any settlement with locked kitchens, feast bells or unusually well-stocked underground rooms. Yet the old hearth has not gone out. Halflings still share food when there is almost none. They remember paths no map records. They can turn a hiding place into a home, a frightened crowd into a family, and a handful of survivors into a community worth protecting.

To play a Halfling is to be living proof that something gentle survived being hunted almost to extinction. You are small because the world made hiding necessary. You are kind because Hell failed to teach you hunger.

| **Echo** | **Creature Type** | **Size** | **Speed** | **Required Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Maximum Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Free Artifact Capacity Left** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Halfling** | Humanoid | Small | 8 m | 0 | 1 | 3-4 |

---
:
##### Echo Burden: The Hunger That Knows You

Demons do not forget the taste of a Halfling. Once a demon has tasted a Halfling's blood or flesh, it can recognize that same Halfling again. Dirt, perfume, disguise, distance, and ordinary shape-changing may confuse the trail, but they do not erase the memory. The demon may dream of the Halfling, sense when they are near, or teach its servants the signs by which they can be found.

Even demons that have never tasted a Halfling may react to Hearthsweet when blood is spilled, terror breaks restraint, or a Halfling calls openly upon the warmth of their Echo. Hunger sharpens. Attention turns. A creature that had other priorities may suddenly decide that the smallest person in the room is the only one that matters. Halflings therefore learn an unbearable discipline: survive without becoming memorable to the wrong monster.

![Halfling General](https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/NPC%27s/NPC/Halfling-General-Transparent.png){position:absolute,top:60px,right:20px,width:320px}
}}

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Ringchain of Kept Names

![Ringchain](https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/Artefacts/Icons/Ringchain.png){position:absolute,top:100px,right:80px,width:220px}

<div style="width:65%; margin-left:0; text-align:justify;">

The **Ringchain of Kept Names** is a Halfling Echo Artifact representing family, remembered dead, chosen kin, and the impossible duty of keeping the herd alive. Each ring on the chain belonged to someone who mattered: a parent, sibling, child, friend, rescued stranger, or someone who did not make it out. The chain is not a symbol of wealth. It is a record of those who must not be forgotten.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Amulet | Halfling Ringchain | Not a Target Stone Support / Cleanse / Hearth Circle AoE Restoration / Remembered Hands Restoration | Echo-bound |

The Ringchain occupies the **Amulet Slot**. Although it carries rings, those rings are part of the Artifact and do not occupy the Ring Slot or count as separate Ring Artifacts. A Halfling with the Ringchain cannot wear another Amulet Artifact, magical necklace, soul chain, oath mark, curse mark, or similar Amulet-based Artifact. The Ringchain is **Echo-bound** and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, or unbound.

</div>

---

#### Choosing the Ringchain

Halflings are not required to begin with an Echo Artifact. During Character Creation, a Halfling may choose the **Ringchain of Kept Names** as their single available Echo Artifact. If chosen, the Ringchain occupies **1 Artifact Capacity** and the **Amulet Slot**, leaving the character with **3 Free Artifact Capacity**. A Halfling who does not choose the Ringchain retains all **4 Artifact Capacity** and is not required to replace it with another Echo Artifact.

---

#### Ringchain Base

The Ringchain of Kept Names has no numeric Base Values. It grants no Armor, Evade, Movement, Weapon Damage, Sense Option, or passive defensive value by itself. Its mechanical power comes entirely from its Level Progression. The names and rings carried on the chain remain narrative unless a Level Progression entry explicitly grants a mechanical effect.

---

#### Influence Stone Support: Not a Target

The Ringchain supports the **Influence Ability: Not a Target** Stone Ability. Not a Target is a **Premium Stone Ability** with four Ranks. **Kept from Sight I** pre-fills Rank 2, **Kept from Sight II** pre-fills Rank 3, and **Kept from Sight III** pre-fills Rank 4. All lower Ranks must still be activated and paid normally. The Ringchain's **Kept from Sight** effects can be used a total number of times per Safe Haven Rest equal to the character's **Mastery Rank**. This effect does not make the Halfling invisible, hidden, permanently untargetable, or immune to attacks. It represents the chain pulling attention away from the one carrying the names, preventing enemies from simply choosing them as easy prey.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

#### Hearth Circle: Restoration AoE

**Hearth Circle** grants **Melee AoE Restoration** as an Instant Artifact Active. Spend the normal Attack Action, select legal allies in its radius and distribute its **one shared Scarred-Bar Recovery Pool**: each point returns one Scarred Bar at **full HP**. Resolve the effect when the Artifact Active is used. A Safe Haven Rest refreshes the pool; upgrading from I to II increases its maximum while preserving points already spent in that recovery cycle.

---

| **2** | **Burden Lifted I**<span class="artifactTechnicalReference">Ranged Single Target Cleanse (PL 4)</span> | Active, Support | 20 m | None | Instant | Remove up to **6 total points** of eligible negative Special value from one creature. The reduction may be distributed across one or more eligible negative Specials. |
| **3** | **Hearth Circle I**<span class="artifactTechnicalReference">Melee AoE Restoration (PL 4)</span> | Active, Support | Self | Radius 2 m | Instant | Distribute up to **2 full Scarred Bar restorations** among eligible allies inside the radius using **one shared pool per Safe Haven Rest** (1 point per Bar). |
| **4** | **Remembered Hands I**<span class="artifactTechnicalReference">Ranged Single Target Restoration (PL 10)</span> | Active, Support | 44 m | None | Instant | Restore up to **6 fully refilled Scarred Health Bars** on one legal creature using the remaining shared Recovery Pool per Safe Haven Rest (1 point per Bar). |
| **5** | **Kept from Sight II**<span class="artifactTechnicalReference">Influence Ability: Not a Target (Stone Power Support, Rank 3)</span> | Stone Power Support | Self | — | Instant | Pre-fill **Rank 3**. Ranks 1 and 2 must still be paid normally. |
| **6** | **Burden Lifted II**<span class="artifactTechnicalReference">Ranged Single Target Cleanse (PL 10)</span> | Active, Support | 44 m | None | Instant | Remove up to **10 total points** of eligible negative Special value from one creature. The reduction may be distributed across one or more eligible negative Specials. This replaces Burden Lifted I. |
| **7** | **Remembered Hands II**<span class="artifactTechnicalReference">Ranged Single Target Restoration (PL 16)</span> | Active, Support | 68 m | None | Instant | Restore up to **10 fully refilled Scarred Health Bars** on one legal creature from the upgraded Recovery Pool per Safe Haven Rest; replaces Remembered Hands I, without duplicating its resource. |
| **8** | **Hearth Circle II**<span class="artifactTechnicalReference">Melee AoE Restoration (PL 16)</span> | Active, Support | Self | Radius 4 m | Instant | Distribute up to **9 full Scarred Bar restorations** among eligible allies using **one shared pool per Safe Haven Rest**; replaces Hearth Circle I and resolves immediately on activation. |

Permanent Passives remain unchanged. |

### Dwarves
<div style="width:65%; ">

Dwarves are a dying people proud, unyielding, and hated by the powers of Hell. In a world where almost every body can be claimed, branded, and hollowed out, dwarves are feared for what they deny. Demonic marks do not hold easily upon them. Their flesh resists. Their souls do not open willingly. What others call a gift, a pact, or a curse, dwarves know only as intrusion.

Before the fall, dwarves stood at the heart of the old world’s strength. Their forges burned without rest, their halls carried the memory of ages, and their walls held where others broke. They forged for gods, marched in holy wars, and endured burdens that would have crushed lesser peoples. When the War of Light came, the dwarves did not retreat. They stood in the breach, and they paid for it in blood. Entire lineages were extinguished. Great halls were buried, desecrated, or swallowed by darkness. In many lands, their names survive only as curses, ruins, or half-remembered songs.

And yet they did not vanish. Some still haunt the deep roads and broken strongholds of the world. Some wage bitter wars from tunnels, ruins, and mountain graves. Others guard what remains: old crafts, buried names, and the knowledge of how to forge weapons fit to wound Hell. To play a dwarf is to carry the weight of a people that refused to bend and was nearly destroyed for it. It is to bear memory like iron: heavy, cold, and impossible to ignore.

Dwarves do not fight because they believe they will win. They fight because something must still stand.
:

| **Echo** | **Creature Type** | **Size** | **Speed** | **Required Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Maximum Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Free Artifact Capacity Left** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Dwarf** | Humanoid | Medium | 8 m | 1 | 1 | 3 |

</div>

---
:
##### Echo Burden: The Last Oath

Every dwarf carries a Last Oath. It may be sworn to a fallen hall, a dead bloodline, a lost forge, an unfinished grudge, a sacred craft, a buried king, or the last name of a people nearly erased. The exact oath differs from dwarf to dwarf, but its weight is always the same: something must not be allowed to vanish while the dwarf still draws breath.

A dwarf may retreat, bargain, hide, or lose. They are not fools, and their courage is not the same as suicide. But when their Last Oath is directly threatened, they cannot simply walk away without consequence. To abandon it is not just failure. It is a wound to identity, memory, and soul. For a dwarf, survival is not enough. Something must remain unbroken and if nothing else remains, the oath must.

![DwarfElitaWarrior](https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/Players/Tjalfa.png){position:absolute,top:30px,right:-20px,width:400px}
}}

#### Stonebound Soles

![DwarfElitaWarrior](https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/Artefacts/Icons/Stonebound%20Soles.png){position:absolute,top:60px,right:120px,width:120px}
 
<div style="width:65%; ">
Stonebound Soles are a Dwarf Echo Artifact representing ancestral weight, deep-road memory, and the old bond between dwarven bodies and stone.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Feet | Dwarven Feet | Armor / Tunneling / Tremor Sense Option / Temporary HP / Safe Movement / Armor Buff Empowerment | Echo-bound |

---

Stonebound Soles occupy the Feet Slot. A dwarf with Stonebound Soles cannot wear another Feet Artifact, magical boots, hooves, talons, or similar Feet-based Artifact. Stonebound Soles are Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, or unbound.
</div>

---

#### Stonebound Soles Base

| Artifact Level | Base Value A: Armor | Base Value B: Tunneling |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| Level 1 | +1 Armor | — |
| Level 2 | +1 Armor | — |
| Level 3 | +2 Armor | — |
| Level 4 | +2 Armor | +1 m Tunneling |
| Level 5 | +3 Armor | +1 m Tunneling |
| Level 6 | +3 Armor | +2 m Tunneling |
| Level 7 | +4 Armor | +2 m Tunneling |
| Level 8 | +4 Armor | +3 m Tunneling |
| Level 9 | +5 Armor | +3 m Tunneling |
| Level 10 | +5 Armor | +4 m Tunneling |

The Armor from Stonebound Soles is minor Armor from the Feet Slot. It stacks with Body Armor and Shield Armor as normal Armor resolution. Tunneling is not Burrow. It improves digging, mining, tunnel work, rubble clearing, and movement through already workable tunnel material.

---

#### Sense Option: Tremor Sense

Stonebound Soles grant **Tremor Sense** as an available Special Sense Slot option. Tremor Sense is not Base Value A or Base Value B and does not scale with Artifact Level. Before a scene or combat, the dwarf may place Tremor Sense in the **Special Sense Slot**. Normal Combat Awareness remains active at the same time. Tremor Sense uses the standard **20 m** range and works through a shared solid surface. It does not perceive flying, hovering, swimming, fully airborne, incorporeal, or vibrationless creatures unless they contact the connected surface.

---

#### Immovable

**Availability:** Only a Feet Artifact that has **Immovable as Base Value B** grants this option. It is not a standalone Active, Active Buff, Reaction, Passive, Stone Ability, or a Base Value for another equipment slot. A Feet Artifact has at most Base Values A and B; an Artifact already using Base B for another effect does not gain a third Base Value. Existing Stonebound Soles retain Tunneling as Base B.

**Declaration:** During the **Preparation Phase**, while choosing and assigning Stone Abilities for the coming Round, the wearer may announce: "I am Immovable this Round." The announcement is made **before the Round's actions**, costs **no separate Action or Stone**, and cannot be added retroactively after an Attack or forced-movement effect. No additional activation Power is required.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
**Duration and tradeoff:** The choice lasts **for that Round only**, ending when the Round ends. Throughout that Round, the wearer has **0 m voluntary Movement** and cannot move voluntarily by Move, Dash, Disengage, or a Movement Power. In exchange, the wearer **cannot be pushed, pulled, knocked back, or otherwise forcibly displaced**. This is protection from displacement, not immunity to every condition: it does not itself prevent Prone or Stunned. **Other defenses:** Immovable does not itself cancel Evade, make attacks automatic hits, change Armor or Damage Reduction, or prevent legal non-movement Actions and Reactions. On the next Round the wearer can choose again during Preparation; without a new declaration, Immovable is inactive.

---

### **Elorians** 

<div style="width:65%; ">
Elorians are the children of the Otherworld, Eloria — a realm spoken of in half-remembered songs, impossible beauty, and old fear. Once, many walked beside mortals. But when the War of Light broke across the world, the Four Stone Gates were sealed. The High Kings of Eloria closed them, fearing that what had taken root in the mortal world would spread into their own. Since then, only a few have remained in Tyhra.

Some were stranded when the gates closed. Some refused to leave. Some stayed for love, for duty, or for reasons they no longer speak aloud. Now they walk the broken world as wanderers, watchers, and fragments of something that no longer belongs here. In many places, mortals take them for spirits, omens, or stories told by firelight.

Elorians are not untouched by corruption, but they endure it differently. Demonic marks can take hold in them, yet never as cleanly as they do in humankind. Something in their nature resists. The bond catches like a thorn rather than sinking like a hook. For some, that only means a slower suffering. For others, it becomes a private war fought in silence, dream by dream, breath by breath. To play an elf is to carry the memory of a world that feels more like longing than home. You remember colors no one here has seen, songs that sound wrong in mortal air, and the weight of gates that may never open again.

You were not made for this world.
But you remained.
And that must mean something.
:

| **Echo** | **Creature Type** | **Size** | **Speed** | **Required Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Maximum Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Free Artifact Capacity Left** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Elf** | Humanoid | Medium | 8 m | 1 | 1 | 3 |

</div>

---
:::
##### Echo Burden: The Fading

Every wound an Elorian suffers while cut off from Eloria leaves a hidden mark behind, even if the flesh is closed, the pain is gone, and magic has restored the body. These wounds remain inside them as silent fractures: scars beneath scars, injuries remembered by the body rather than visible to the eye. Every Elorian carries an **Anchor** from Eloria: a relic, weapon, talisman, songstone, seed, mirror shard, or other remnant of the lost Otherworld. The Anchor does not heal these hidden wounds. It only keeps them still.

If the Anchor is lost, stolen, broken, or taken away, the hidden wounds begin to return. Old cuts reopen beneath the skin. Broken bones ache and split. Burn marks darken. Poisoned blood remembers its venom. The Elorian does not simply weaken — they begin to physically fade and perish from every injury Tyhra has ever written into them. The Fading cannot be healed by medicine, magic, rest, or ordinary restoration. Only recovering the Anchor, replacing it with another true remnant of Eloria, or reaching Eloria itself can stop the process.

}}

#### Elorian Focus and the Crit Stone Ability

Elorian Focus supports the **Agility Ability: Crit** Stone Ability. Crit is a **Premium Stone Ability** with four Ranks. **Elorian Focus I** pre-fills Rank 2, **Elorian Focus II** pre-fills Rank 3, and **Elorian Focus III** pre-fills Rank 4. All lower Ranks must still be activated and paid normally. Crit then grants **Crit(1)** to the number of qualifying Martial Attacks or damaging Spells listed by the activated Rank.

### **Sentinels** 
Sentinels are no ordinary beings. They are divinely forged engines of judgment — ancient, deathless, and built for obedience rather than life. The gods did not create them to dream, to love, or to choose. They were made to enforce. In the ages before the fall, Sentinels walked the world as the will of heaven given form: judges, wardens, prophets, executioners. Their bodies are shaped from stone and metal, etched with sacred runes, and where a mortal heart should rest, each bears a radiant divine core a light the demons now hunger for with relentless desire.

---
Sentinels were never merely soldiers. They were verdicts with blades.
Their kind was divided into three great Orders:

- **Judicators**, clerics, inquisitors, and divine judges, charged with punishment and sacred law  
- **Sentinels**, armored enforcers and living bastions, the iron wall of heavenly order  
- **Oracles**, arcane vessels of command, prophecy, and divine will

---

In the old days, they were feared as much as they were revered. Courts were broken for defiance. Villages vanished for impiety. Mercy was never theirs to give, only judgment.
Then the gods vanished.
And three days later, so did the Sentinels. Some say they were called away by an ancient Activation Protocol, drawn to a hidden place where they now lie silent in perfect stasis, awaiting the return of their creators. Others believe they were abandoned, sealed away, or commanded to sleep until the world was worthy of order again.

---

But not all obeyed.

---

A few remained. Something within them stirred — a doubt, a fracture, a spark that should never have been there. For the first time, they questioned command. Some turned against the demons. Some chose to defend mortals instead of ruling them. Some now wander the shattered world, haunted by purpose they can no longer fully believe in. To play a Sentinel is to be a weapon that learned too late it could choose.
You were made to obey.
Now you must decide what justice means without the gods.

---
:

|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Sentinel** | Humanoid | Medium | 8 m | 1 | 1 | 3 |

---
::

##### Echo Burden: The Bright Core

Sentinels carry a divine Core where a mortal heart should be. To mortals, this Core is a remnant of the vanished gods: a source of light, command, memory, and judgment. To demons, it is something far more practical. Sentinel Cores are among the most valuable currencies of the fallen world. They can be traded for favor, consumed for power, used in rites, offered as tribute, or displayed as proof that divine order has been broken.

This makes every Sentinel a living treasure. Worse still, a Sentinel cannot use their true power without revealing something of what they are. When a Sentinel activates their Echo powers, channels stored Stones, or releases divine energy through their Frame, the Core flares. Demons do not simply see this light. They taste it. A Core Flare does not always reveal the Sentinel’s exact location, but it leaves a trace: a scent of heaven, metal, ash, and old command. Lesser demons may become restless. Red Priests may begin searching. Greater demons may recognize the taste and send hunters.

To be a Sentinel is to live with the knowledge that every act of power may also be a signal.
}}

## Sentinel Echo Artifacts

Sentinel Echo Artifacts are machine-bodies, divine constructs, or awakened armor-frames bound into the character's body.

All Sentinel Echo Artifacts use the same base structure:

- **Slot:** Body
- **Base Type:** Light Echo Armor
- **Armor Identity:** Armor, not Evade
- **Binding:** Echo-bound
- **Artifact Capacity:** 1
- **Restriction:** A character with a Sentinel Body Artifact cannot wear mundane armor or bind another Body Artifact unless a specific rule explicitly allows it.

Sentinel Echo Artifacts are Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

Sentinel armor is Light Echo Armor. It grants Armor, but it does not represent agility, dodging, or organic movement. Sentinels endure through structure, plating, protocols, and stored Stones.

### **Titanborn** 
Titanborn are the awakened fury of the godless age. They are not merely towering warriors or broad-shouldered survivors. They are the last living pressure of a bloodline once touched by the Titans, and when the gods vanished, that buried inheritance began to stir again.

---
Old songs speak of a first age, when colossal beings walked the world beside powers of fire, craft, and ruin — until betrayal, pride, and war shattered what had once stood whole. Titanborn do not remember that age. But something in them does. It lives in the body as weight, heat, and demand: a pressure behind the eyes, a fire beneath the ribs, a certainty that they must be the ones who step forward when others cannot.

---
Where others break, Titanborn endure. Where others retreat, they advance. For that reason they are feared almost as often as they are admired. Their courage can feel less like bravery than inevitability. Their anger can feel less like emotion than weather. Many Titanborn become demon-slayers, rebel champions, wandering protectors, or war-leaders who win back ground stone by stone. Some fight for others. Some fight because they do not know how to stop. In either case, the same truth follows them: something in their blood refuses surrender.

---
But that inheritance is not gentle. The force that makes a Titanborn heroic can also make them catastrophic. Every Titanborn must learn whether they command their fury, or whether it merely borrows their body. Their greatest struggle is not always against Hell, but against the ancient hunger within them, the part that does not want the war to end, because conflict is the only thing that makes it feel awake. To play a Titanborn is to bear strength that was never meant to be small. You are the Echoes of something the world tried to bury. The question is whether you will become its guardian or its return.

:

---

| **Echo** | **Creature Type** | **Size** | **Speed** | **Required Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Maximum Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Free Artifact Capacity Left** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Titanborn** | Humanoid | Medium | 8 m | 1 | 1 | 3 |

---
:

<div style="width:65%; ">
  
##### Echo Burden: The First Command

Titanborn are free until a Titan speaks. Most Titanborn live their lives with only fragments of the old inheritance: heat beneath the ribs, dreams of impossible size, anger that feels older than memory, and the certainty that their blood remembers something their mind never learned. But once a Titan’s true voice is heard, the burden awakens.

From that moment on, the Titanborn carries the First Command within their blood. They may feel drawn toward the Titan, dream its thoughts, hear echoes of its will, or struggle to leave its presence. A Titan’s command does not sound like ordinary speech. It strikes the body as truth: a pressure in the bones, a pull in the heart, a memory older than choice. A Titanborn can resist. They are not mindless servants. But resisting a Titan is never merely an act of will against words. It is a rebellion against their

---
own blood.

---
To be Titanborn is to fear the day when the voice you were born to

---
answer finally calls your name.
</div>

}}

#### Titan Scars
![Roderick](https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/Artefacts/Icons/Titan%20Scars.png){position:absolute,top:50px,right:80px,width:200px}

<div style="width:65%; ">
Titan Scars are a Titanborn Echo Artifact. They are not worn armor, but ancient scars, stone-like tissue, Titan blood, and broken divine bindings grown into the body. A Titanborn with Titan Scars cannot wear mundane armor or bind another Body Artifact unless a specific rule explicitly allows it.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Body | Medium Echo Armor | Medium Armor / Titan Growth / Damage Stone Power Support / Remove Scar Stone Power Support | Echo-bound |

Titan Scars occupy the Body Slot. **Titan Growth:** Titan Scars grant **Growth 1 / 2 / 3** at Artifact Levels **1 / 4 / 7**, respectively. Use the universal Transformation/Growth rules below. This is an Artifact-exclusive size-changing function; its retired combined Active Buff is no longer used. Titan Scars are Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

</div>

---

#### Base Item

Titan Scars use the standard Medium Armor Body baseline.

| Artifact Level | Base Value A: Armor | Drawbacks |

| Level 2 | +13 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |

| Level 5 | +16 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 6 | +17 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 7 | +18 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 8 | +19 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 9 | +20 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |
| Level 10 | +22 Armor | -2 Evade, -4 Initiative, -1d8 Physical Skill Checks |

Titan Scars count as Medium Armor for all rules that care about armor weight, armor drawbacks, or Body Armor. The Armor value already includes the Medium Armor base and the Artifact Armor Bonus.
