{{wide

### Dragonborn

Dragonborn are the last living Echoes of the First Dragons — a people who survived extermination by learning the one thing dragons were never meant to do: disappear. When the Sentinels carried out the divine purge, the survivors learned a cruel truth. Pride gets you found. Splendor gets you hunted. A lineage endures only by becoming small, scattered, and forgettable. So Dragonborn learned absence. They keep their bloodlines narrow, their gatherings rare, and their true names quiet. They avoid open glory, not because they are timid, but because every public triumph is a signal flare to old enemies. Their restraint is not weakness. It is inheritance turned into strategy.

---
Their oldest creed is simple: stay hidden. And yet the gods did not erase everything. At dawn and dusk, many Dragonborn still listen for what their hunters could never fully silence: the Melody of the Beginning, a deep soul-song said to bind their kind across distance, memory, and loss. Through it, they remember that dragonhood was not only power, but presence — something vast, ancient, and not yet entirely gone.

---
Some Dragonborn carry more than distant blood. In a rare few, a sleeping dragon soul lies coiled beneath the flesh, waiting for pressure, grief, or violence to awaken it. Others keep what remains of the old inheritance in another form: hidden relics, sealed treasures, and secret caches known as Hoard Threads, places where memory, wealth, and legacy were kept beyond the reach of divine greed. Many Dragonborn do not walk the world in their true visage. They wear chosen faces, pass under borrowed shapes, and move through the ruins of Tyhra as if they were lesser things. Survival taught them that being overlooked is sometimes the highest form of power.

---
To play a Dragonborn is to bear greatness taught to hide from the world. You are the remnant of something the gods tried to erase. The question is not whether the old song still lives in you — but what will happen if it wakes.

---
:
| **Echo** | **Creature Type** | **Size** | **Speed** | **Required Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Maximum Echo Artifacts at Character Creation** | **Free Artifact Capacity Left** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Dragonborn** | Humanoid | Medium | 8 m | 1 | 3 | 1-3 |

---
:
##### Echo Burden: The First Song

Dragonborn survived because they learned to disappear. Yet once each day, at dawn or dusk, the Melody of the Beginning calls to them. It is not a song heard with the ears alone. It rises through blood, bone, breath, and memory, binding the last dragon-blooded souls across distance, grief, lineage, and hidden names. For one brief moment, a Dragonborn may feel what was lost: kinship, ancestry, old wings, dead parents, forgotten cities, and the impossible warmth of belonging. Some hear only silence before the note. Some hear the voices of their line. Some feel the presence of those who came before them, not as ghosts, but as part of the same ancient soul-song.

---
This longing is dangerous. Dragonborn are taught to hide, to bury their names, to soften their presence, and to live as lesser things. But the First Song asks them to remember. It asks them to stop pretending. It asks them to answer. A Dragonborn may resist the call, but never without ache. The danger is not only that enemies may hear the echo. The danger is that, for one moment each day, the Dragonborn wants to be found.

}}

### Dragon Head
![DragonHead](https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/Artefacts/Icons/Dragon%20Head.png){position:absolute,top:50px,right:80px,width:200px}

<div style="width:65%; ">
Dragon Head is a Dragonborn Echo Artifact representing the awakened skull, jaws, senses, breath, and command-presence of the First Dragons.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Head | Draconic Head | Bite / Head Armor / Predator Sense Option / Breath Weapon / Roar / Stone Refresh | Echo-bound |

Dragon Head occupies the Head Slot. A Dragonborn with Dragon Head cannot wear another Head Artifact, helmet, mask, crown, or magical headgear. Dragon Head is Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, or unbound.

</div>

#### Bite and Head Armor Base

| Artifact Level | Base Value A: Bite Weapon Damage | Base Value B: Head Armor |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| Level 1 | 1d8 | +1 Armor |
| Level 2 | 2d8 | +1 Armor |
| Level 3 | 3d8 | +2 Armor |
| Level 4 | 4d8 | +2 Armor |
| Level 5 | 5d8 | +3 Armor |
| Level 6 | 6d8 | +3 Armor |
| Level 7 | 7d8 | +4 Armor |
| Level 8 | 8d8 | +4 Armor |
| Level 9 | 9d8 | +5 Armor |
| Level 10 | 10d8 | +5 Armor |

Head Armor is minor Armor and stacks with Body Armor and Shield Armor as normal Armor resolution.

---

#### Sense Option: Predator Sense

Dragon Head grants **Predator Sense** as an available Special Sense Slot option. Dragonborn often describe this as Scent of Blood, heat on the tongue, pressure behind the eyes, or the instinctive recognition of wounded prey. Predator Sense is separate from both Base Values and does not scale with Artifact Level. It uses the standard **20 m** range and the normal Special Sense Slot rules.

---

#### Breath Weapon Rules

Breath Weapon grants **two alternative catalogue modes** at the same Artifact Level: **Damage Breath = Melee AoE Attack** and **Ruin Breath = Melee AoE Special: Ruin**, both at the listed Power Level. Choose exactly one mode on each use. These are not a combined Power and use the same Breath Weapon use counter. Both modes are **Martial Melee Instant AoE Powers** with Dragon Head's **Cone** specialty (apex on you; aim the cone). Catalogue lengths apply. Instant AoE rules still apply: **no Primary Target**, one Attack Roll compared separately to each creature's Evade, equal payload to every creature hit.

**Damage Breath** uses the canonical **Melee AoE Attack** Power at its printed Power Level and — as an **explicit Dragon Head-only exception** to the universal no-Weapon-Damage-on-AoE rule — **adds this Artifact's own Bite Weapon Damage** to the shared damage pool. Do not add Dragon Claws damage or any other weapon's damage. Roll **one shared Damage Pool** (Bite + printed Power Dice + other eligible bonuses) and apply that same result to each successful AoE target after individual defenses. Dive for Cover and other catalogue AoE defenses apply normally.

**Ruin Breath** remains the canonical **Melee AoE Special: Ruin**. It applies the printed **Ruin(X)** only. It adds **no Bite Damage**, **no Power Damage**, and **no additional Specials**.

**Attack Attribute:** Both Breath modes may use either **Might** or **Agility**. Choose the Attribute before each Attack Roll. Never combine the two Attribute Pools.

Bite remains available separately as this Artifact's natural melee weapon attack.

---

}}

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Breath Weapon Uses

Breath Weapon can be used a number of times per Safe Haven Rest equal to half the character's Mastery Rank, rounded up.

**Breath Weapon may be used only ONCE PER COMBAT.** Damage Breath and Ruin Breath share this combat restriction and the Safe Haven Rest counter. Artifact Awakening does not grant an extra combat use of Breath Weapon.

| Mastery Rank | Breath Weapon Uses per Safe Haven Rest |
|:---:|:---:|
| MR 1 | 1 |
| MR 2 | 1 |
| MR 3 | 2 |
| MR 4 | 2 |
| MR 5 | 3 |
| MR 6 | 3 |
| MR 7 | 4 |
| MR 8 | 4 |

##### Draconic Recovery

When Dragon Head reaches Artifact Level 3, choose one Attribute for Draconic Recovery. This choice is permanent. Draconic Recovery can be used once per Safe Haven Rest and restores spent Stones only from that Attribute. Draconic Recovery II and III replace the earlier profile; they do not grant additional uses.

#### Dragon Claws
![DragonHead](https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/Artefacts/Icons/Dragon%20Claws.png){position:absolute,top:50px,right:80px,width:200px}

<div style="width:65%; ">

Dragon Claws are a Dragonborn Echo Artifact that turns both hands into natural weapons. A Dragonborn with Dragon Claws cannot wield another weapon, shield, or hand-based Artifact while using them.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Bonus | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Main Hand + Off Hand | Natural Two-Handed Weapon | Claw / Tail Damage Scaling | Echo-bound |

Dragon Claws occupy both hand Slots. Dragon Claws are Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed or replaced.

</div>

#### Claw / Tail Base

| Artifact Level | Base Value A: Claw / Tail Damage | Base Value B: Weapon Special | Base Value C: Weapon Special |
|:---:|:---:|---|---|
| Level 1 | 4d8 | — | — |
| Level 2 | 5d8 | — | — |
| Level 3 | 6d8 | — | — |
| Level 4 | 8d8 | Penetration(2) | — |
| Level 5 | 9d8 | Penetration(3) | — |
| Level 6 | 10d8 | Penetration(3) | — |

| Level 8 | 14d8 | Penetration(4) | Brutal Impact(4) |
| Level 9 | 15d8 | Penetration (5) | Brutal Impact(5) |
| Level 10 | 16d8 | Penetration (5)| Brutal Impact(5) |

Claw / Tail Damage is Weapon Damage. Penetration and Brutal Impact apply to Claw and Tail attacks granted by this Artifact. Rending Spiral is an **AoE** and adds **no Claw / Tail Weapon Damage**. Tail Sweep is a **single-target Martial Push** and uses matching Claw / Tail Weapon Damage when it hits; it purchases no extra Power Damage. Rending Spiral applies the full printed **Lacerate(X)** to each hit creature without splitting. Both retain their PL 4/10/16 catalogue profiles.

\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

### Unbound

The Wyld does not create species.

It creates responses.

The Unbound are those responses made flesh. They are not one people, one bloodline, or one culture. They are what happens when the world reacts to violation: when divine law is nailed into the land, when a sacred place is bent out of its nature, when hunger is manufactured, when Hell teaches the soil to rot, or when mortals are forced to become what they were never meant to be.

The Wyld responds.

But not every response wears the same shape. Some responses become bodies of fang, horn, claw, feather, hunger, and sacred violence. These are the **Beasts**. Some responses gather in circles, curses, roots, ash, old names, wet earth, and forbidden rites. These are the **Witches**. Some responses remain almost human, but arm themselves with discipline, poisons, elixirs, relics, scars, and the knowledge of how to kill what should not be allowed to live. These are the **Banes**. What binds all Unbound is not blood. It is opposition. They are made to resist what does not belong.

After the War of the Gods, when the Eight drove their order across the world and hammered Sentinels into the map like nails, many Unbound became hidden enemies of that sacred regime. They moved as saboteurs, curse-speakers, shrine-breakers, road-hunters, wilderness guardians, and cells of resistance born from forests, caverns, swamps, battlefields, and places where old wounds never closed. Now the gods are gone, and Hell has taken their place.

The Unbound do not mourn the old order. To them, demons are only another invading pressure, another unnatural claim laid upon a wounded world. And so they fight again: against corruption, possession, false rule, branded flesh, poisoned land, and all who would teach the world to accept its own violation. Most Unbound are not evil. But neither are they gentle. They are driven creatures of correction, instinct, rite, hunt, and unfinished purpose. In them, the self is often thinner than the response that made them. When they lose control, they do not simply become monsters.

They become what the Wyld made them to be. To play an Unbound is to be the world’s response to something that should not exist. You were not made for peace. The question is whether you can still choose what must be broken.

---
:::
##### Echo Burden: The Response Takes Over

All Unbound are responses of the Wyld. They were shaped to oppose violation, corruption, false rule, divine control, demonic possession, and all things forced upon the world against its nature. But the response that made them is not passive. It waits beneath choice, beneath discipline, beneath identity. When an Unbound is pushed too far, the response may take over. This does not mean they become evil. It means they become dangerously pure. The Beast becomes the shape that hunts and tears. The Witch becomes the curse that speaks through flesh. The Bane becomes the weapon that can no longer stop hunting.

Every Unbound fears this moment, because it is powerful, useful, and sometimes necessary. But it is never fully theirs.

---
::
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### The Beasts

Beasts are the body's response: Unbound shaped by transformation, instinct, animal memory, hunger, and sacred violence. Their power takes the form of wolf, bear, raven, stag, fox, boar, serpent, lynx, and stranger shapes remembered by the old wilds. Beneath every Beast lies **Primal Rage**, an instinctive violence they spend their lives learning to control. Most learn restraint, the difference between prey and friend, and how to remain themselves when blood, pain, and fear call the Beast forward. A Beast may choose the **Predator Crown**, embodying the hunting mind, natural weapons, and predatory senses, or the **Primal Vestment**, embodying the hunting body, supernatural recovery, and deliberately unleashed Rage. Primal Vestment is optional, but a Beast who chooses it automatically gains the **Primal Rage (2 Points)** Disadvantage. Rage Unbound represents the Beast consciously releasing this power and mechanically functions as **Extra Attack Stone Power Support**; Primal Rage represents the moment that control fails.

#### Predator Crown

Predator Crown is a Beast Unbound Echo Artifact representing the awakened hunting mind, bestial senses, fangs, beak, horns, jaws, and violent instinct of the Wyld written into the head. It may manifest as wolfish jaws, bear teeth, raven eyes, antlers, fox ears, tusks, slit pupils, blackened gums, a second voice in the throat, or a skull-shape that becomes more visible when blood is near.

Choose one Predator Shape when this Artifact is created:

**Wolf, Bear, Raven, Stag, Fox, Boar, Serpent, Lynx, or another Beast shape approved by the GM.**

The chosen shape defines the appearance and narration of the Artifact, but all Predator Crowns use the same rules unless a specific entry says otherwise.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Head | Bestial Head | Bite Damage / Head Armor / Predator Sense Option / Stone Power Support / Reaction: Damage / Active Buff: Damage | Echo-bound |

Predator Crown occupies the Head Slot. A Beast with Predator Crown cannot wear another Head Artifact, helmet, mask, crown, horn Artifact, magical headgear, or similar Head-based Artifact. Predator Crown is Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

---

#### Predator Crown Base

| Artifact Level | Base Value A: Bite Damage | Base Value B: Head Armor |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| Level 1 | 4d8 | +1 Armor |
| Level 2 | 5d8 | +1 Armor |
| Level 3 | 6d8 | +2 Armor |
| Level 4 | 7d8 | +2 Armor |
| Level 5 | 8d8 | +3 Armor |
| Level 6 | 9d8 | +3 Armor |
| Level 7 | 10d8 | +4 Armor |
| Level 8 | 11d8 | +4 Armor |
| Level 9 | 12d8 | +5 Armor |
| Level 10 | 14d8 | +5 Armor |

Bite Damage is Natural Weapon Damage. Bite Damage may represent a bite, beak strike, horn gore, tusk strike, throat snap, or similar head-based natural attack depending on the chosen Predator Shape. Head Armor is minor Armor and stacks with Body Armor and Shield Armor as normal Armor resolution.

---

#### Sense Option: Predator Sense

Predator Crown grants **Predator Sense** as an available Special Sense Slot option. Predator Sense is separate from both Base Values, does not scale with Artifact Level, and uses the standard **20 m** range. Predator Sense does not read thoughts, reveal full plans, or detect creatures with no readable blood, fear, weakness, or hunting trace.

---

#### Primal Vestment

Primal Vestment is a Beast Unbound Echo Artifact representing the awakened hunting body: violent endurance and the primal fury normally kept beneath conscious control. It may manifest as thick fur, altered muscle, visible veins, hardened tendons, scar tissue that closes while watched, patches of hide, elongated limbs, black blood, a second heartbeat, or a body that becomes visibly less human when violence begins.

Beasts who choose the Primal Vestment are sometimes called **Ragebound**. Even among other Unbound they are often regarded with suspicion. Their power is respected, but so is the danger of losing control. Many Ragebound become loners, are kept at the edge of camps and settlements, or find that others prefer not to stand too close when blood is spilled.

| Slot | Base Type                   | Artifact Functions                                          | Binding    |
| ---- | --------------------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------- | ---------- |
| Body | Bestial Vestment / No Armor | Healing Stone Power Support (Self only) / Rage Stone Power Support | Echo-bound |

Primal Vestment occupies the **Body Slot** and is Echo-bound. A Beast with Primal Vestment cannot use another Body Artifact or Body Armor.

**Primal Vestment has no Base Values.**

---

### Primal Rage *(2 Points)*

A Beast who chooses Primal Vestment automatically gains **Primal Rage** as a **2 Point Disadvantage**. These points count normally toward the character's Disadvantage Point total and Reroll Point maximum. Primal Rage cannot be removed while Primal Vestment remains part of the Beast. You spend your life suppressing something that wants to hunt, tear, and answer pain with violence. **Trigger:** When you lose your **second Health Level**, and each time you lose another Health Level thereafter, make a `Resolve k1` roll against **TN 6**. If one damage instance crosses several qualifying Health Levels, make one roll for each threshold crossed. No Raises may be declared.

On failure, you enter **Primal Rage** for `1d8 / 2 Rounds, rounded up`.

**While in Primal Rage:**

* Gain **+1d8 damage per Mastery Rank**.
* You must attack the nearest creature, friend or foe.
* At the end of each Round, repeat the `Resolve k1` roll against **TN 6**.

On success, Primal Rage ends. Primal Rage never forces you to spend Stones or activate a Stone Ability.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

#### Beast Artifact Exception

Primal Vestment is an explicit Beast Echo Artifact exception. It may contain **two Stone Power Support functions** and may provide **Extra Attack Stone Power Support from the Body Slot**, despite the normal Body Artifact restrictions. These exceptions apply only to Primal Vestment. The Artifact-facing name **Rage Unbound** represents the Beast deliberately releasing its fury. Mechanically, it is always **Stone Ability: Extra Attack**. Rage Unbound is controlled; **Primal Rage** is what happens when that control fails.

### Witches
Witches are the rite’s response. They do not simply cast spells. They bind old agreements, speak curses, call roots through stone, rot purity, sour blood, twist luck, and remember names the temples tried to bury. Witch traditions often gather around a dominant working: Root Witches bind, entangle, hold, and awaken the deep green will of the land; Ruin Witches curse, break, hex, and turn certainty into collapse; Blight Witches poison, corrode, wither, and teach flesh, metal, and faith how to decay. A Witch does not force the Wyld. A Witch gives it a shape and pays the price for being heard.

#### Witch Staff

A Witch Staff is a Witch Unbound Echo Artifact representing the old rite made into wood, bone, root, ash, iron, thorn, rot, or blackened saint-wood. Every Witch Staff is a two-handed arcane weapon and a Spell Focus from Level 1. Witch Staffs are not one tradition. When this Artifact is created, choose one Witch Tradition:

- **Root Staff** — Root Witch
- **Ruin Staff** — Ruin Witch
- **Blight Staff** — Blight Witch

The chosen tradition determines the Staff’s Tradition Special, Main Spell, and Paired Spell.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Main Hand + Off Hand | Two-Handed Arcane Staff | Weapon Damage / Spell Focus / Tradition Special / Raises Stone Power Support / Binding Circle (Ranged AoE Root Active) / Tradition Main Spell | Echo-bound |

Witch Staff occupies both hand Slots. A Witch with Witch Staff cannot use another weapon, shield, hand focus, claw Artifact, or hand-based magical item at the same time. Witch Staff is Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound. Witch Staff counts as a **Spell Focus** from Level 1. Witch Staff may be used with **Intellect**.

---

### Witch Staff Traditions

| Tradition | Staff Name | Tradition Special | Artifact-Facing Main Spell | Technical Main Spell |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Root Witch | Root Staff | Slow | Sapping Roots | **Ranged Single Target Special: Weaken** |
| Ruin Witch | Ruin Staff | Hex | Ruinous Word | **Ranged Single Target Special: Ruin** |
| Blight Witch | Blight Staff | Corrode | Blightcall | **Ranged Single Target Special: Blight** |

The Main Spell is granted by the Staff through its Level Progression. Each tradition also gains the same **Binding Circle** Ranged AoE Special: Root at Artifact Levels 2/5/8. Binding Circle and Main Spell are separate Actives, each using only their printed catalogue values and normal action rules. Neither Active adds damage or a second Stone Function. The Paired Spell is not granted by the Staff. A Witch character is expected to learn and advance the Paired Spell normally if they want to fully follow that tradition.

##### Root Staff

A Root Staff is grown from buried wood, grave roots, thorn knots, wet earth, and old circles drawn under the soil.

---

##### Ruin Staff

A Ruin Staff is made from blackened wood, cracked bone, broken shrine-metal, ash, old curses, and names that were erased from temple records.

---

##### Blight Staff

A Blight Staff is swollen with fungus, rust, sour water, corpse-bloom, mold, and green-black veins of living decay.

---
}}

### Banes 

Banes are Unbound who carry the hunt into the world. They are not Beasts who surrender to animal shape, and they are not Witches who bind themselves primarily through covenants and rites. Banes survive through preparation, endurance, old wrath, relics, tools, scars, and the refusal to die before monsters.

When a Bane Echo is created, choose one Bane Call:

- **Alchemist**
- **Greenwarden**
- **Relic Hunter**

The chosen Call defines the Bane's method, appearance, and expected Power direction. It does not create a separate species. It is the shape the hunt has taken.

---

#### Alchemist

![Alchemist](https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/NPC%27s/NPC/Daro-Unbound-Alchemist-noBG.png){position:absolute,top:250px,right:80px,width:400px}
<div style="width:45%; ">

Alchemists are Banes who survive through preparation. They brew potions, oils, salves, bitter draughts, tinctures, and hunter mixtures from roots, ash, venom, blood, bone, and demon-tainted remains. An Alchemist does not win by becoming a monster. An Alchemist studies the monster, prepares the body, coats the blade, drinks the poison first, and walks into the dark with a plan. Alchemists fit characters who want potions, oils, preparation, resistance, toxins, temporary empowerment, and controlled self-enhancement. Their power is preparation, not transformation.

#### Alchemist Coat

The Alchemist Coat is a Bane Echo Artifact made from hardened leather, stitched iron, bone plates, sealed vials, black glass, monster sinew, silver hooks, blood tubes, hidden needles, and reinforced potion harnesses. It is not a robe and not a trinket.

It is real armor.

The Alchemist Coat protects the body while carrying the volatile mixtures that allow a Bane Alchemist to survive the hunt.

</div>

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Body | Medium Armor | Armor / Damage Potion / Alchemist Stone Artifact Function / Critical Potion | Echo-bound |

Alchemist Coat occupies the Body Slot. A Bane with Alchemist Coat cannot wear another Body Artifact, magical armor, robe Artifact, natural armor Artifact, or body-based transformation Artifact at the same time. Alchemist Coat is Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

---
}}

{{wide
#### Alchemist Coat Base

| Artifact Level | Base Value A: Medium Armor |
|:---:|:---:|
| Level 1 | 12 Armor |
| Level 2 | 13 Armor |
| Level 3 | 14 Armor |
| Level 4 | 15 Armor |
| Level 5 | 16 Armor |
| Level 6 | 17 Armor |
| Level 7 | 18 Armor |
| Level 8 | 19 Armor |
| Level 9 | 20 Armor |
| Level 10 | 22 Armor |

Alchemist Coat uses the normal Medium Armor drawbacks unless another rule explicitly changes them.

---

#### Alchemist Potions

Alchemist Potions are normal **Active Buffs**. They follow all normal Active Buff rules. A character may normally maintain only one Active Buff at a time. If the Alchemist activates a new Potion while already maintaining another Active Buff, the previous Active Buff ends immediately unless another rule explicitly allows both to coexist. The Potion name is only the Artifact's flavour name. The mechanical Power is the listed existing Active Buff.

---

#### Red Potion

Red Potion floods the body with heat, rage, pressure, and violent focus. Mechanically, Red Potion uses the normal **Active Buff: Damage**.

---

#### Black Potion

Black Potion sharpens the senses until every pulse, blink, tendon, and weak point becomes visible. Mechanically, Black Potion uses the normal **Active Buff: Critical**.

---

#### Alchemist Stone

Alchemist Stone is a dedicated **Artifact Function** of the Alchemist Coat, not an Attribute Stone Ability. It improves one **Red Potion** or **Black Potion** Active Buff when that Potion is activated. At Artifact Level 2, 5, or 8, the effect is respectively **+1 / +2 / +3 effective Power Levels** and **+1 / +2 / +3 Rounds**. These values replace earlier Alchemist Stone values; they do not add together.

The normal Active Buff rules still apply. The effective Power Level cannot exceed the applicable Power Level cap, and Alchemist Stone does not allow a second Active Buff to be maintained. If another source empowers the same Buff, use only the highest applicable increase unless an explicit rule permits stacking.

}}

#### Greenwarden

<div style="width:100%; ">

Greenwardens are Banes touched by the old green wrath of the world. They are inspired by ancient forest guardians, thorn-covered wanderers, antlered figures, bark-skinned protectors, moss-grown bodies, and the idea that nature itself remembers what was done to it. A Greenwarden is not a Beast. They do not become an animal. They become something older: bark, thorn, moss, antler, sap, rot, root, and patient violence. Greenwardens fit characters who want thorns, reflected harm, bark-skin, close-range punishment, defensive retaliation, and the feeling that the world itself answers every wound. Their power is endurance and retaliation.

---

#### Green Warden Mantle

The Green Warden Mantle is a Bane Echo Artifact grown from bark, thorn, moss, old roots, green iron, beast hide, sacred soil, and the last living strength of the wild places. It is not a druidic robe and not a holy vestment. It is the armor of a protector. Green Wardens do not serve gods. They stand between the living world and the things that would harvest it.

</div>

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Body | Medium Armor | Armor / Active Buff: Thorns / Reaction: Ally Armor / Special Negation Stone Power Support | Echo-bound |

Green Warden Mantle occupies the Body Slot. A Green Warden with Green Warden Mantle cannot wear another Body Artifact, magical armor, robe Artifact, natural armor Artifact, or body-based transformation Artifact at the same time. Green Warden Mantle is Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

---

### Green Warden Passive Access

A Green Warden may choose the **Thorns Passive** during character creation. Thorns Passive is exclusive to Green Wardens unless another rule explicitly grants access. The Green Warden must still spend one Passive Slot to slot Thorns Passive. If Thorns Passive is not slotted, it provides no benefit. The **Thorns Active** is not exclusive. Any character may learn the Thorns Active if they meet its normal requirements. A Green Warden may have both the Thorns Passive and the Thorns Active.

---

### Green Warden Thorns

Green Warden Mantle grants access to the normal **Active Buff: Thorns**. Active Buff: Thorns represents thorns, roots, bark splinters, hooked vines, green iron spines, or living brambles striking back at nearby enemies. The name is the Artifact's flavour name. The mechanical Power is the existing **Active Buff: Thorns**.

---

### Green Warden Protection

Green Warden Mantle grants access to the normal **Reaction: Ally Armor**. This Reaction represents roots rising, bark hardening, thorns catching a blow, or the Warden forcing the living world between an ally and harm. The Reaction follows all normal Reaction rules. It applies only to the triggering ally hit or damage instance. It does not create a maintained state. It is not an Active Buff.

---

### Green Warden Stone

Green Warden Mantle supports the **Resolve Ability: Special Negation** Stone Power. The Mantle pre-fills higher tiers of that Stone Power through its Level Progression. The character must still pay all required lower tiers normally.

#### Hunter's Scourge

<div style="width:100%; ">

Hunter's Scourge is a Relic Hunter Echo Artifact: a chain-whip, iron lash, hooked cord, weighted scourge, relic whip, or segmented killing chain built to drag monsters into reach and destroy them. Relic Hunters are not holy knights. They are trained killers of demons, undead, fiends, cursed things, and monsters that survived too long. Where Alchemists survive the hunt by changing their own bodies, and Green Wardens protect what still lives, Relic Hunters end the hunt by pulling the monster close and burning it out of the world.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Main Hand + Off Hand | Two-Handed Chain Whip | Whip Damage / Finesse / Reach / Limited Single Target Strike / Expose Special / Maximization Stone Power Support | Echo-bound |

Hunter's Scourge occupies both hand Slots. A Relic Hunter with Hunter's Scourge cannot use another weapon, shield, hand focus, claw Artifact, or hand-based magical item at the same time. Hunter's Scourge is Echo-bound and cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

---

</div>

### Hunter's Scourge Base

Hunter's Scourge uses the normal **Whip** as its Base Weapon.

| Base Weapon | Damage | Hands | Specials |
|---|:---:|:---:|---|
| Whip | 2d8 | 1 | Finesse, Reach (+1 m) |

Hunter's Scourge turns this weapon into a two-handed Echo Artifact. It keeps **Finesse** and **Reach (+1 m)** from the Base Weapon. The ordinary Whip's **2d8 Weapon Damage** is its starting chassis; the bound Scourge adds **+1d8 Weapon Damage per Artifact Level**, including +1d8 at Level 1. These dice are already included in the total values below and must not be added a second time.

---

| Level 7 | 9d8 | +2 m |
| Level 8 | 10d8 | +3 m |
| Level 9 | 11d8 | +3 m |
| Level 10 | 12d8 | +4 m |

Scourge Damage is Weapon Damage. Hunter's Scourge keeps the **Finesse** quality.

### Level Progression

**Limited source:** Scourge Strike is a granted variant of the normal **Melee Single Target Attack**. It inherits that catalogue Power's full Attack Roll, action, delivery, and other rules. The normal Power bonus Damage Dice are doubled by the universal Limited rule; the weapon's Base Damage is not. Its use counter scales with effective Power Level and resets only on a Safe Haven Rest. It grants no free additional Attack Actions, and the Artifact's copy does not bypass duplicate-source restrictions.

**Hunter's Scourge Stone Support exception:** Although Hand Artifacts normally grant Might/Agility-oriented Stone support, Hunter's Scourge is an explicitly approved Echo Artifact exception supporting **Vitality: Maximization**. This grants no Vitality Stones or automatic access to locked Vitality Stone Abilities: the wielder still needs the Ability legally unlocked and must pay its required lower Ranks. This exception applies only to the named Hunter's Scourge support, not to Hand Artifacts generally.

**Maximization Support:** Scourge Strike is a **Limited Instant Active** and **may be maximized** by **Vitality Ability: Maximization**. Hunter's Scourge grants the normal Stone Power Support for this Ability at Artifact Levels 2 / 5 / 8. Support pre-fills exactly the named Rank's incremental Stone cost; lower Ranks remain payable. Choose the Rank and pay the remaining cost once during the first Preparation Phase of combat. The Support does not grant a second activation, extra uses, additional Limited uses, or a separate Power. Its **shared Maximization allowance** may be spent on Scourge Strike, another eligible Damage Roll, or an eligible **Healing Roll made by the wielder**, following the universal Maximization rules.

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Deathless

*Death has forgotten them. Something else has not.*

Death does not always hold in Tyhra. Corpses rise, drowned soldiers awaken, and the dead walk among the living. Some call them cursed, others monsters. The Deathless themselves rarely know why they have returned.

Their bodies are cold, pain feels distant, and life shines with an unfamiliar intensity. Yet something calls to them — a silent command from beyond the grave. **The Hollow King** remembers them, even when they have forgotten themselves.

Some resist the call for years. Others begin walking before they remember their own names.

Deathless are a **playable Echo**. They begin with the **Hollow Crown**, a required Echo-bound Artifact at Level 1, occupying the Head Slot and 1 Artifact Capacity.

| **Echo** | **Creature Type** | **Size** | **Speed** | **Required Echo Artifacts** | **Maximum Echo Artifacts** | **Free Artifact Capacity** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Deathless** | Undead | Medium | 8 m | 1 | 1 | 3 |

The Undead creature type grants no additional immunities or changes to Health, healing, rest, or recovery. Deathless choose their additional language freely.

---

### Hollow Crown

The Hollow Crown marks the place where death failed. It may appear as an iron circlet, funeral mask, bone halo, crown-shaped scar, or an unseen weight upon the skull.

| Slot | Base Type | Artifact Functions | Binding |
|---|---|---|---|
| Head | Deathless Echo | Deathless Body / Life Sense Option / Hollow Stillness / Life Absorption | Echo-bound |

Hollow Crown occupies the **Head Slot**, preventing the use of other Head Artifacts. It cannot normally be removed, replaced, sold, stolen, unequipped, or unbound.

---

### Deathless Nature

Deathless are neither inherently evil nor servants of demons. They retain their own will, but their bodies no longer respond to injury like living flesh.

They follow normal **Health, healing, rest, and recovery** rules. Stone Healing and Damage Transfer restore HP within the active Health Bar. Scarred Bars require dedicated full-Bar Restoration, Regeneration, or other explicitly permitted recovery.

Through **Life Absorption**, Deathless can drain living creatures to gain temporary **Absorbed Vitality Stones**, which fuel Vitality Stone Abilities such as Remove Scar. Life Absorption never restores HP or Scarred Bars directly.

---


#### Life Sense — Life Sight

Hollow Crown grants **Life Sense**, known among Deathless as **Life Sight**.

Living creatures appear as warmth, pulse, blood, or flickering life-force. Life Sight follows standard **Life Sense** rules with a **30 m range**. It neither scales with Artifact Level nor consumes a Base Value or Power Line.

It cannot reveal thoughts, memories, intentions, identity, or alignment. It does not detect lifeless objects, constructs, corpses, lifeless illusions, or other Deathless without an additional rule.

Life Sense may occupy the **Special Sense Slot** before a scene or combat. Normal Combat Awareness remains active.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Deathless Body

**Deathless Body** is an exclusive Hollow Crown Passive, unavailable to living characters.

The Deathless still lose Health and can become Incapacitated normally. However, their unnatural bodies suffer reduced **Health Penalties**.

At Level 1, Deathless Body shifts normal Health Penalties two steps toward Healthy.

| Health Level | Normal Penalty | Deathless Body I |
|:---:|:---:|:---:|
| Healthy | 0% | 0% |
| Bruised | 10% | 0% |
| Injured | 20% | 0% |
| Wounded | 40% | 10% |
| Broken | 50% | 20% |

#### Deathless Body Progression

| Hollow Crown Level | Effect |
|:---:|---|
| **1** | **Deathless Body I:** Shift 2 steps. Maximum Health Penalty **20%**. |
| **4** | **Deathless Body II:** Shift 3 steps. Maximum Health Penalty **10%**. |
| **7** | **Deathless Body III:** Shift 4 steps. Maximum Health Penalty **0%**. |

Deathless Body affects only normal Health Penalties. Conditions, Specials, curses, magic, exhaustion, and other penalties remain unaffected unless explicitly stated otherwise.

---

### Life Absorption

Deathless may drain vitality from living creatures to gain temporary **Absorbed Vitality Stones**.

Life Absorption cannot be used during combat. The target must be willing, unconscious, helpless, fully restrained, or otherwise unable to meaningfully resist. Dead creatures cannot be drained.

| **Hollow Crown Level** | **Ability** | **Maximum Stones per Safe Haven Rest** |
|:---:|:---|:---:|
| **2** | Life Absorption I | **2** |
| **5** | Life Absorption II | **4** |
| **8** | Life Absorption III | **8** |

This limit counts all Stones absorbed since the last Safe Haven Rest. Spending them does not restore absorption capacity.

---
::
#### Player Characters

Each absorbed point **Seals 1 eligible non-Colorless Stone** from the target and grants **1 Absorbed Vitality Stone**.

- **Eligible:** Ready and Exhausted Stones.
- **Ineligible:** Sealed, Bound, and Colorless Stones.

When no eligible Stones remain, the target becomes **Spent** for Life Absorption. Further absorption is impossible until Stones become eligible again.

**Life Absorption cannot kill a player character.**

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

#### Ordinary NPCs

Ordinary living NPCs have **2 Vitality Steps** instead of tracking Stones.

| Vitality State | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Whole** | Both Vitality Steps remain. |
| **Drained** | One step lost; severely weakened but alive. |
| **Spent** | Both steps lost; alive but cannot be drained further. |

Each absorbed Vitality Step grants **1 Absorbed Vitality Stone**.

---

#### Absorbed Vitality Stones

Absorbed Vitality Stones may pay for **Vitality Stone Abilities**, including **Remove Scar**, using their normal costs (**1 / 2 / 4 / 8**).

They are temporary and separate from the normal Stone Pool.

- Spent Stones are permanently lost rather than recovered.
- Stones that would normally become Sealed are instead lost.
- Unused Absorbed Vitality Stones disappear at the next **Safe Haven Rest**.

Life Absorption grants no permanent Stones and does not directly restore Health or Scarred Health Bars.

![Alchemist](https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/Adventures/The%20Clearing/NSC/Deathless%20Knight-NoBG.png){position:absolute,top:320px,right:80px,width:800px}


}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

---

### Hollow Stillness

Hollow Stillness represents the cold distance between the Deathless and the needs of living flesh. Fear becomes quieter. Pain becomes slower. Poison finds less warmth. Disease finds less purchase. The body is still vulnerable, but it no longer belongs completely to life.

| Hollow Crown Level | Hollow Stillness |
|:---:|---|
| Level 3 | Hollow Stillness I. Gain **Advantage** on Attribute Checks to resist Fear, Pain, Poison, Disease, Suffocation, Exhaustion, and effects that explicitly rely on a living body. |
| Level 6 | Hollow Stillness II. Keep that Advantage and add **+1d8 to the Pool** of those Attribute Checks. |
| Level 9 | Hollow Stillness III. Keep that Advantage and increase the Pool bonus to **+2d8**. |



---

### Level Progression

| **Artifact Level** | **Artifact Name** | **Type** | **Range** | **AoE** | **Duration** | **Effect** |
| :--: | :-- | :-- | :--: | :--: | :--: | :-- |
| **1** | **Deathless Body I**<span class="artifactTechnicalReference">Exclusive Passive: Deathless Body I</span> | Exclusive Passive | Self | — | Permanent | Shift normal Health Penalties down by 2 steps. Maximum normal Health Penalty: 20%. Hollow Crown separately grants Life Sense as a Special Sense Slot option. |
| **2** | **Life Absorption I**<span class="artifactTechnicalReference">Echo Function: Life Absorption I</span> | Echo Function | Touch | — | Special | Gain up to **2 Absorbed Vitality Stones per Safe Haven Rest** through Life Absorption. |
| **3** | **Hollow Stillness I**<span class="artifactTechnicalReference">Artifact Passive: Hollow Stillness I</span> | Artifact Passive | Self | — | Permanent | Gain **Advantage** on Attribute Checks to resist Fear, Pain, Poison, Disease, Suffocation, Exhaustion, and effects that explicitly rely on a living body. |
| **4** | **Deathless Body II**<span class="artifactTechnicalReference">Exclusive Passive: Deathless Body II</span> | Exclusive Passive Upgrade | Self | — | Permanent | Shift normal Health Penalties down by 3 steps. Maximum normal Health Penalty: 10%. This replaces Deathless Body I. |
| **5** | **Life Absorption II**<span class="artifactTechnicalReference">Echo Function: Life Absorption II</span> | Echo Function Upgrade | Touch | — | Special | Increase the Life Absorption limit to **4 Absorbed Vitality Stones per Safe Haven Rest**. This replaces Life Absorption I. |
| **6** | **Hollow Stillness II**<span class="artifactTechnicalReference">Artifact Passive: Hollow Stillness II</span> | Artifact Passive Upgrade | Self | — | Permanent | Keep that Advantage and add **+1d8 to the Pool** of those Attribute Checks. This replaces Hollow Stillness I. |
| **7** | **Deathless Body III**<span class="artifactTechnicalReference">Exclusive Passive: Deathless Body III</span> | Exclusive Passive Upgrade | Self | — | Permanent | Shift normal Health Penalties down by 4 steps. Maximum normal Health Penalty: 0%. This replaces Deathless Body II. |
| **8** | **Life Absorption III**<span class="artifactTechnicalReference">Echo Function: Life Absorption III</span> | Echo Function Upgrade | Touch | — | Special | Increase the Life Absorption limit to **8 Absorbed Vitality Stones per Safe Haven Rest**. This replaces Life Absorption II. |
| **9** | **Hollow Stillness III**<span class="artifactTechnicalReference">Artifact Passive: Hollow Stillness III</span> | Artifact Passive Upgrade | Self | — | Permanent | Keep that Advantage and increase the Pool bonus to **+2d8**. This replaces Hollow Stillness II. |
| **10** | **Hollow Crown Awakening**<span class="artifactTechnicalReference">Artifact Level 10: Awakening (Hollow Crown)</span> | Artifact Function | Self | — | One Combat | **Deathless Body:** at the **start of one chosen combat**, gain Temporary HP equal to your normal total maximum Health for that combat. This buffer does not heal or reopen Health Bars and expires at combat end. **Life Absorption:** independently, at the start of a combat, refill Absorbed Vitality Stones to **8** without draining another creature; the refill cannot exceed 8 and unused Stones granted by it expire at combat end. **Hollow Stillness III** remains permanently active. This awakening refill is separate from the ordinary eight-Stone Life Absorption acquisition limit. |

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### The Call

All Deathless hear the call. It is not always a voice. Sometimes it is a road seen in dreams. Sometimes it is a pressure behind the eyes. Sometimes it is the taste of old ash. Sometimes it is a name they know without remembering when they learned it. The call belongs to the **Hollow King**. Nothing more is certain. Some Deathless resist the call. Some follow it. Some fear it. Some believe it is the only thing keeping them from becoming truly empty. No Deathless begins with certainty about what waits at the end.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Social Challenges

*Social Challenges are an optional way to organize important conversations, negotiations, interrogations, diplomacy, intrigue, or public arguments. They are not a separate combat or resource system.*

**Use the normal Skill rules.** If one meaningful exchange can settle the question, resolve it with one Skill Check. A longer scene may contain several distinct tasks, but **no fixed number of phases or Lead rolls** is required.

### Shared Skill Procedure

1. **GM sets the check.** Establish the social objective and what the other person could plausibly agree to, reveal or believe. The GM assigns the relevant **Attribute**, applicable **Specialization** (such as Persuasion, Deception, Etiquette or Streetwise), and **Base TN = Task Level × 4** from the actual stakes.
2. **Player declares Paid Raises.** Choose their number within the Skill Rank allowance, adding **+4 TN each**. Free Raises at Ranks 4/6/8 (1/2/3) are automatic on success.
3. **Roll.** Use the GM-set **Attribute k MR** (or eligible Passive Success) against the Final TN.
4. **Allocate Raises on success.** Spend declared Paid and available Free Raises on fitting **Scope, Speed, Quality, Efficiency, Insight or Safety** effects with GM agreement, before finalizing the social outcome.

**Active opposition:** If a trained opponent is actively trying to deceive, expose, or counter a technique, use **Skill Opposition** (the first successful setup Skill result becomes the responding Skill Check's TN). For a direct contest of personal ability without a trained Skill method, use the existing **Opposed Attribute Contest**. Do not add a second invented "Social Defense" to either procedure.

**Social agency:** Persuasion is not mind control. Success cannot force an impossible concession or erase a person's essential loyalties. A better approach, a concrete offer, or uncovered evidence may make a previously unreasonable request feasible; that is a *change in the fictional situation*, not a free bonus. Passive success applies only when the existing calm, uncontested conditions genuinely hold.

### One Conversation - A City Gate

A captain hears the party's request for entry. The GM sets **Task Level 4, Base TN 16**. A negotiator has **Persuasion 4, Influence 4, MR 2** and chooses **zero Paid Raises**, so **Final TN 16** and the roll is **4k2**. On a result of **22**, the **one Free Raise** becomes available; the negotiator allocates **Quality 1** to a dependable assurance of future entry, if the captain can credibly offer it. A result of **15** fails the request. A substantially different offer may change the situation for a later check.

A result of **22** wins entry and the agreed assurance. A result of **15** fails the proposed agreement; Free Raises do not grant benefits on a failed check. A materially different offer can be attempted later if circumstances allow; repeating the same pitch endlessly is not automatic. Even a great roll cannot compel the captain to betray a non-negotiable oath.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Social Challenges - Worked Scenes

### A Major Negotiation - The Prince's Troops

The group wants a prince to send soldiers to an approaching war. This is too consequential for mere flattery, but **there is no required three-phase sequence**. The players may cooperate through several meaningful actions, or go straight to a serious proposal if they already have what they need. **Find the real concern.** A character with **Court Intelligence 3 (Investigation)** investigates the prince's situation. The GM assigns **Intellect** and **Base TN 12**. The player declares **one Paid Raise**, raising the TN to **16**. On success, it can be allocated to **Insight 1** for an additional useful fact: the prince fears leaving his capital defenseless. The information is a real clue, not an abstract bonus.

**Create a credible offer.** A character with **Etiquette 3** presents a formal undertaking. At **Base TN 16**, the player chooses **one Paid Raise**, for **Final TN 20**. On success, assign it to **Quality 1** for especially clear, credible terms.

**Ask for the troops.** The negotiator has **Persuasion 4**. The GM deems the request feasible and sets **Base TN 20**. The player chooses **zero Paid Raises**. On meeting **Final TN 20**, **one Free Raise** becomes available for **Quality 1**, securing an explicit, dependable commitment within the negotiated terms.

**What changed?** Earlier successes supplied information and changed the proposal. They did **not** create a numerical progress resource, change past TNs, or force a final roll. If the group begins with proof and a convincing agreement, the first two steps can be skipped. If a player makes a truly different offer later, set its difficulty **before** the new roll.

### Skill Opposition - A False Courier

A character with **Deception 3, Influence 4, MR 2** poses as a courier. The GM sets **Base TN 12**. The character chooses **one Paid Raise**, for **Final TN 16**, then rolls **4k2** and succeeds with **22**. Before the guard challenges the story, the player assigns **Quality 1** to strengthen one plausible detail of the account. The successful rolled result **22**, not the Raise, becomes the guard's opposition TN.

A suspicious guard with **Investigation 2, Wits 4, MR 2** checks the story. The established courier result **22** becomes the guard's TN. The guard chooses no Paid Raises, rolls **4k2**, and gets **19**; the cover holds. A result of **24** exposes it. The opposition proceeds only after any effects of the setup have been assigned.

### Shared Table Guidance

- **Cooperation:** Any character can contribute a fitting action with its own objective and consequence. No mandatory main spokesperson, once-per-phase support allowance, or flat +MR assistance bonus.
- **Raises:** Choose the number of **Paid Raises before rolling** within the ordinary Skill Rank cap (**Paid +4 TN; Free +0 TN**). On success, allocate the Paid and eligible Free Raises to effects before resolving the scene. High results create no additional Raises.
- **Consequences:** A failed social attempt can change trust, available options, time, or risk. **Fear Checks are not automatic**; use them only where the established Fear rules and actual psychological danger justify them.
- **Pacing:** One roll when one meaningful decision settles it; several linked actions when the participants genuinely need to uncover, prepare, bargain, and decide. No social progress track, separate preparation points, fixed phase count, or separate success thresholds.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Disadvantages

---
:

Every hero carries scars — some visible, others etched into the soul. Disadvantages are flaws, burdens, weaknesses, obligations, fears, curses, enemies, dependencies, or restrictions that make your character more complicated and more dangerous to play. They are not meant to make a character unplayable. They are meant to create pressure, hard choices, complications, and drama.

---

### Open List Rule

The Disadvantages in this chapter are **examples and templates**. They are not a closed list. Players may create custom Disadvantages with GM approval.

When creating a custom Disadvantage, define:

- what triggers it,
- how often it matters,
- what happens when it matters,
- whether the character can resist it,
- and how many Disadvantage Points it is worth.

A Disadvantage should be clear enough that both player and GM understand when it applies.

---

### Disadvantage Rules

- You may pick Disadvantages up to a maximum of **8 Disadvantage Points** total.
- Your maximum **Disadvantage Points** become your maximum **Reroll Points**.
- After each **Safe Haven Rest**, you start at your maximum Reroll Points.

If a Disadvantage calls for a control roll, use:

`Resolve k1`

No Raises may be declared on this roll.

---

### Rewarding Disadvantages

When a player actively roleplays a Disadvantage in a way that creates meaningful tension, risk, drama, sacrifice, or complication, the GM may allow that character to recover **1 Reroll Point**. This should reward real play, not empty mention.

Good examples include:

- acting on a fear even when it creates danger,
- refusing an easy solution because of an oath,
- accepting a complication because of a flaw,
- exposing a weakness at a dramatic moment,
- following an addiction, curse, obsession, or trauma when it matters,
- allowing Hunted, Unluck, or a Physical Scar to shape the scene,
- choosing a harder path because the Disadvantage genuinely matters.

A player cannot recover more Reroll Points than their maximum. The GM decides when a Disadvantage was played strongly enough to restore a Reroll Point.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Addiction *(2 Points)*

You are dependent on a physical, mental, magical, spiritual, or metaphysical substance, ritual, state, or contact to remain stable. This may be addiction, withdrawal, dependency, compulsion, or supernatural need.

**Effect:**

If you are denied the source of your Addiction:

| **Time Without** | **Penalty** |
|---|:---:|
| 1 Tyhran Day | –1d8 to all Attribute-based Skill Check Pools |
| 7 Tyhran Days | –2d8 to all Attribute-based Skill Check Pools |
| 1 Lunar Month (44 Tyhran Days) | –4d8 to all Attribute-based Skill Check Pools |
| 3 Lunar Months or more | –8d8 to all Attribute-based Skill Check Pools |

When the Addiction is offered, threatened, denied, or becomes dangerous to pursue, the GM may call for a **Resolve k1** control check. The GM states the trigger and TN **before** the roll:

- **TN 8 — Ordinary temptation or manageable withdrawal:** the source is nearby, available, or missed under otherwise tolerable conditions.
- **TN 16 — Acute compulsion or severe withdrawal:** a powerful trigger, prolonged denial, or a particularly dangerous opportunity to satisfy the need.

Use the fixed band that describes the **actual situation**, not the character's MR. This is an Attribute Check, not a Skill Check; **no Skill Raises** apply. The existing time-without penalties above remain in force on their stated schedule. On failure, the character suffers an appropriate complication, such as distraction, withdrawal, risky behavior, accepting a bad bargain, or exposing their weakness. Success means the character resists that specific trigger; it does not erase the Addiction or its ongoing penalties.

**Examples:**

- A Sentinel requires sacred oil to regulate its systems.
- A magic user needs Dreamdust to silence voices.
- A believer is dependent on divine communion and suffers agony without prayer.
- A blood-bound warrior craves battle or bloodshed.
- A cursed oracle needs visions, dreams, or forbidden contact to remain stable.

---

### Berserker’s Curse *(2 Points)*

You lose control when badly wounded, succumbing to blood-fury.

**Trigger:**  
When you lose your **second Health Level**, and each time you lose another Health Level thereafter, make a `Resolve k1` roll against **TN 6**. If one damage instance causes you to lose multiple Health Levels and crosses more than one qualifying threshold, make one test for each Health Level lost from the second onward. No Raises may be declared on this roll. On failure, you go **Berserk** for `1d8 / 2 rounds, rounded up`.

**While Berserk:**

- You gain **+1d8 damage per Mastery Rank**.
- You must attack the nearest creature, friend or foe.
- At the end of each round, repeat the `Resolve k1` roll against **TN 6** to regain control.

If you succeed, Berserk ends.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Hunted *(1–3 Points)*

Someone or something is hunting you and it will not stop. This may be a person, monster, faction, noble house, guild, cult, spirit, divine agent, demonic patron, family, government, or supernatural force. The examples below are not a closed list.

| **Points** | **Threat** | **Examples** |
|:---:|---|---|
| 1 | A limited or personal threat | A bounty hunter, rival, debt collector, betrayed lover, local guard captain |
| 2 | An organized threat with resources | A cult, noble house, mercenary guild, criminal syndicate, church order |
| 3 | A powerful or far-reaching threat | A demon lord, archwitch, archangel, ancient spirit, royal inquisition, planar hunter |

**Define:**

- who is hunting you,
- why they are hunting you,
- how much they know,
- how far their reach extends,
- whether they want capture, revenge, punishment, service, silence, or death.

**Effect:**  
The GM may introduce chases, clues, agents, confrontations, traps, rumors, or social complications connected to the hunter. Hunted should create story pressure. It should not appear randomly just to punish the player.

---
:

### Physical Scars *(1–3 Points)*

Your body bears lasting damage, weakness, mutation, instability, or limitation. Physical Scars are not limited to the examples below.

They may affect:

- sight,
- hearing,
- sleep,
- movement,
- hands,
- breathing,
- stamina,
- pain,
- balance,
- coordination,
- appearance,
- or physical reliability.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
| **Example Flaw** | **Points** | **Effect** |
|---|:---:|---|
| One-Eyed | 1 | **−1 die** to Ranged Attack Pools and sight-based Perception Pools. |
| One-Handed | 2 | You cannot dual wield or use a shield and one-handed weapon at the same time. |
| Heavy Sleeper | 1 | You do not wake from normal noise. You wake only when damaged, physically shaken, or directly disturbed. |
| Light Sleeper | 1 | You sleep poorly and may suffer complications during rests, watches, or exhaustion scenes. |
| Sleepless | 2–3 | You cannot sleep normally. Define with the GM how you recover during rests. |
| Chronic Pain | 1–2 | Under stress, the GM may require a **Resolve k1** check (**TN 8** for ordinary strain, **TN 16** for acute pain) to remain focused. State the TN before the roll; no Skill Raises. Failure causes an appropriate short-term pain complication; no unprinted permanent penalty. |
| Damaged Lungs | 1–2 | Smoke, poison gas, drowning, long chases, or exhaustion affect you more severely. |
| Trembling Hands | 1–2 | Fine manipulation, delicate tools, ranged precision, or spell focus may become harder under pressure. |
| Fragile Frame | 3 | Your Health Track has one fewer box on every level. |

The important question is not whether the flaw appears on this list.

The important question is:

> **How does this complicate play, and how often does it matter?**

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Mental Restrictions *(1–3 Points)*

You are bound by your past, beliefs, trauma, instincts, compulsions, vows, fears, or personality. Mental Restrictions are not ordinary preferences. They must be strong enough to create real pressure in play. The examples below are not a closed list.

---

#### Oaths

- No killing
- Chivalric code
- Always keep promises
- Never abandon an ally
- Never refuse a duel
- Never lie
- Never harm the helpless
- Never break hospitality
- Always repay debts

---

#### Fears

Fears should be handled like psychological limitations. A Fear must have a clear trigger and a clear consequence.

Examples:

- Claustrophobia
- Fear of fire
- Fear of deep water
- Fear of darkness
- Fear of being restrained
- Fear of undead
- Fear of demons
- Fear of divine symbols
- Fear of mirrors
- Fear of losing control
- Fear of becoming a monster
- Paranoia
- Hatred for a group

---

#### Personality Traits

- Arrogant — always needs to prove superiority
- Coward — retreats or hesitates when seriously threatened
- Vengeful — cannot forgive a serious offense
- Gullible — easily moved by sad stories, pleading, or false innocence
- In love with someone — acts irrationally when that person is in danger
- Obsessive — cannot ignore a specific goal, clue, person, or mystery
- Proud — cannot accept humiliation
- Cruel — struggles to show mercy
- Merciful — struggles to finish helpless enemies

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Mental Restriction Rule

To act **against** your Mental Restriction, make a `Resolve k1` roll. No Raises may be declared on this roll.

| **Difficulty** | **TN** | **Meaning** | **Points** |
|---|:---:|---|:---:|
| Easy | 4 | Minor resistance or mild discomfort | 1 |
| Normal | 8 | Strong internal conflict | 2 |
| Hard | 12 | Violates a core belief, fear, vow, or identity | 3 |

On success, you may act against the Restriction for the moment. On failure, the Restriction creates a complication. This may mean hesitation, refusal, panic, retreat, escalation, social conflict, tactical risk, or another consequence agreed by the GM and player. A Mental Restriction should create drama and pressure. It should not remove the character from play every time it appears.

---

### Unluck *(1–3 Points)*

You are cursed with misfortune. At the start of each session, the GM gains Misfortune Tokens based on your Unluck value.

The GM may spend a Misfortune Token to:

- worsen the outcome of one of your failed rolls,
- introduce an unlikely narrative obstacle,
- create an inconvenient complication,
- affect an ally caught in your bad luck,
- or make an already bad situation worse.

| **Points** | **Misfortune Tokens per Session** |
|:---:|:---:|
| 1 | `1d8 / 2, rounded up` |
| 2 | `1d8` |
| 3 | `2d8` |

Misfortune should complicate the story. It should not be used to invalidate player choices or turn success into failure.

---

### Vulnerability *(1-4 Points)*

You are unusually susceptible to a specific harmful Special. Choose one eligible negative value-based Special. Add your **Vulnerability value** to the incoming Special before Special Negation and before the target's Special Application Limit. This only affects the Special when it is applied. It does not trigger again when the Special ticks. It does not add extra hits, extra stacks, or a separate Special. It does not make the Special harder to remove unless a rule explicitly says so.

**Example:**  
If you have **Vulnerability: Ruin(2)** and an enemy applies **Ruin(3)** to you, you suffer **Ruin(5)** instead.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
**Eligible Specials:**

| **Special** | **Vulnerability Effect** |
|---|---|
| **Lacerate(X)** | When Lacerate is applied to you, increase its value by your Vulnerability value. |
| **Ruin(X)** | When Ruin is applied to you, increase its value by your Vulnerability value. |
| **Slow(X)** | When Slow is applied to you, increase its value by your Vulnerability value. |
| **Hex(X)** | When Hex is applied to you, increase its value by your Vulnerability value. |
| **Sundered(X)** | When Sundered is applied to you, increase its value by your Vulnerability value; the extra stacks affect subsequent eligible non-Spell hits under the normal Sundered rules. |

---

### Reroll Points — Fractures of Faith

Reroll Points represent the shattered, fragmented nature of the characters. Their memories, weaknesses, scars, and nightmares are not only burdens. They can also become a source of desperate strength.

---

#### Starting and Recovery

- Your maximum **Reroll Points** equal your total **Disadvantage Points**.
- You recover all Reroll Points after a **Safe Haven Rest**.
- You cannot have more Reroll Points than your maximum.
- The GM may allow you to recover **1 Reroll Point** when you strongly roleplay a Disadvantage in a way that creates meaningful tension, risk, sacrifice, or complication.

This reward should be given for real consequences, not for simply mentioning the flaw.

---

#### Using Reroll Points

You may spend **1 Reroll Point** to reroll all dice of one roll you make. You must keep the new result. You may also spend **1 Reroll Point** to force the GM to reroll all dice of one roll they make. The GM must keep the new result. Reroll Points may be combined with other reroll effects unless a rule says otherwise.

> *You hesitate. Voices in your head. And yet — you try again.*

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide

### Example: Disadvantages

The following examples show how a character might combine Disadvantages up to the maximum of **8 Disadvantage Points**. These are not fixed packages. They only show how Disadvantages can create pressure, story hooks, and Reroll Points.

---
:
#### Example: Marked Fugitive

| **Disadvantage** | **Points** | **Trigger / Effect** | **Narrative Hook** |
|:--|:--:|:--|:--|
| **Hunted** *(Demonic Cult)* | **3** | The GM may introduce cult agents, informants, ambushes, or social pressure connected to the hunter. | Someone wants the character captured, silenced, or returned alive. |
| **Addiction** *(Ritual Incense)* | **2** | If denied the source, the character suffers the normal Addiction penalties over time. | The character needs a nightly rite to remain stable. |
| **Mental Restriction** *(Never Trust the Faithful of the Old Gods)* | **2** | Acting against this restriction requires a `Resolve k1` roll against the appropriate TN. | Old betrayal makes trust in believers, temple servants, or keepers of the old rites difficult. |
| **Unluck** | **1** | The GM gains Misfortune Tokens according to the Unluck rules. | Trouble finds the character at the worst possible time. |

**Total:** **8 Points**

---
:
#### Example: Broken Veteran

| **Disadvantage** | **Points** | **Trigger / Effect** | **Narrative Hook** |
|:--|:--:|:--|:--|
| **Physical Scar** *(Damaged Lungs)* | **2** | Smoke, poison gas, drowning, long chases, or exhaustion may affect the character more severely. | The war left wounds that never fully healed. |
| **Hunted** *(Old War Enemy)* | **2** | The GM may introduce pursuers, bounty notices, old rivals, or dangerous recognition. | Someone from the past still wants revenge. |
| **Mental Restriction** *(Never Abandon an Ally)* | **2** | Leaving an ally behind requires a `Resolve k1` roll against the appropriate TN. | Loyalty is strength, but also a chain. |
| **Berserker’s Curse** | **2** | When the character loses their second Health Level, and each further Health Level thereafter, they risk losing control according to the Berserker’s Curse rules. | Pain turns discipline into fury. |

**Total:** **8 Points**

---
:
#### Example: Cursed Wanderer

| **Disadvantage** | **Points** | **Trigger / Effect** | **Narrative Hook** |
|:--|:--:|:--|:--|
| **Vulnerability** *(Ruin)* | **4** | Whenever **Ruin(X)** is applied to the character, increase the applied Ruin value by **4**. | Fire does not merely burn them; it awakens the curse. |
| **Mental Restriction** *(Fear of Fire)* | **2** | Acting calmly near dangerous flame requires a `Resolve k1` roll against the appropriate TN. | The character remembers burning, even when no fire is present. |
| **Physical Scar** *(Trembling Hands)* | **1** | Fine manipulation, delicate tools, ranged precision, or spell focus may become harder under pressure. | The body still shakes from an old trauma. |
| **Unluck** | **1** | The GM gains Misfortune Tokens according to the Unluck rules. | Misfortune follows like a shadow. |

**Total:** **8 Points**

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Example: Alaris Chooses Disadvantages

Alaris takes the full **8 Disadvantage Points**. Those points become her maximum Reroll Points, but every choice also creates a real complication.

| **Disadvantage** | **Points** | **How it matters** |
|:--|:--:|:--|
| **Lost Love: Liora** | **3** | Her connection to Liora can override caution or immediate priorities. |
| **Light Sleeper** | **2** | Rest becomes fragile in unsafe or unfamiliar places. |
| **Nightmares** | **1** | Sleep may return her to memories, omens, or images she cannot control. |
| **Vegetarian** | **2** | Scarcity can turn a personal conviction into a survival problem. |

<img src="https://assets.forge-vtt.com/6727fe2e3c793ad173f66d6b/destroyed-Faith%20Adventures/Rulebook/Alaris-Example-Disadvantages.png" style="display:block; width:680px; margin:10px auto 0 auto;" />

{{note
##### What this example shows
A useful Disadvantage creates decisions and story pressure. It should not exist only to generate Reroll Points.
}}

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## The Finished Character: Alaris

The final character should read as one connected plan rather than a collection of unrelated high values.

| **Final element** | **Alaris** |
|:--|:--|
| **Mastery Rank** | 2 |
| **Core role** | Mobile melee skirmisher |
| **Total Evade** | 17 from the MR baseline and her artifacts |
| **Armor** | 2 from Mastery Rank soak |
| **Health per Bar** | 16 from Vitality 4 × 4 |
| **Fear** | Starts at 0; accumulated Fear remains until Safe Haven Rest. |
| **Main weapon** | Moonlight Greatsword (Artifact Level 1): 5d8 Weapon Damage (4d8 Greatsword + 1d8 Artifact), Inherent Finesse, Precision(2) |
| **Assigned Stones** | Agility 1 and Vitality 1 |
| **Echo Imprints** | Catfall (Agility), Living Warmth (Vitality) |

**Moonlight Greatsword - Inherent Finesse:** This named Artifact retains the normal Greatsword's **4d8 starting weapon damage** and **Precision(2)**, while its own Inherent Ability: **Finesse** permits Agility for the Attack Roll (not for damage). At Artifact Level 1 its +1d8 Artifact Weapon Damage produces **5d8 total Weapon Damage**. Inherent Finesse is not an additional Power, a purchased Special, or a fourth Artifact Base Value.

### Her combat rhythm

1. Reach a useful melee position through normal movement, a purchased Movement Power if available, or an Agility Stone option. Catfall offers no combat movement bonus.
2. Choose between direct damage and applying Sundered for the group.
3. Let high Evade answer ordinary direct attacks.
4. Reserve Phasing for the most dangerous hits.
5. Spend Stones when movement, survival, or Special removal cannot wait.

{{note
##### What the complete example teaches
A strong Mastery character does not need to cover every role. Alaris has a clear offensive purpose, a clear defensive plan, and several supporting choices that express the same identity.
}}

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Stone Abilities

Stones are a player-facing measure of your awakened potential. In the fiction, they represent inner strength, not physical objects. A warrior aligned with Might unleashes raw destruction; a rogue aligned with Agility moves like a blur of shadows; a mage aligned with Intellect overloads the arcane weave. **Attributes and Stones are intentionally independent.** Attribute values determine dice Pools. Lifetime Experience determines how many permanent Stones you earn.

---
:
### The Core Chain
- Characters begin with **2 permanent Attribute Stones**. Both must be assigned to Attributes; **neither starting Stone may be Colorless**.
- Every **20 Lifetime XP** grants **1 additional permanent Stone**.
- Stones earned **after the 2 starting Stones** may be assigned to any Attribute or chosen as **Permanent Colorless Stones** during an **Upgrade Step**. Each such permanent Stone grants exactly **1 Attribute Stone or 1 Permanent Colorless Stone**. Unassigned Stones cannot be spent.
- Choosing a Permanent Colorless Stone costs no additional Stones (1:1). The choice is permanent. The **2 starting Stones are always Attribute Stones** and cannot later be converted to Colorless.
- The first Stone assigned to an Attribute creates that Attribute's **Stone Pool** and ordinarily unlocks **8 Stone Abilities**:
  - **4 General Stone Abilities** (same for all Attributes)
  - **4 Attribute Stone Abilities** (unique to that Attribute)
- A single Attribute may hold at most **Mastery Rank × 2** permanent Stones.
- There is **no separate Mastery Rank cap** on Permanent Colorless Stones; a character may choose every permanent Stone earned **after the 2 starting Stones** as Colorless.
- Permanent Colorless Stones are not assigned to an Attribute and do not count against any Attribute Stone limit. They may pay the normal cost of any **unlocked** Stone Ability unless a rule explicitly excludes Colorless Stones. **Only an Attribute Stone unlocks that Attribute's Stone Abilities**; Colorless Stones alone do not unlock them.
- Once a Stone is assigned or converted into a Permanent Colorless Stone, that choice remains unless a rule explicitly allows it to change.

> In short: **Attributes define capability. Stones define supernatural affinity. Colorless Stones trade normal recoverability for flexibility.**

---

#### Stone States

Stones exist in 4 states:
- **Ready**: available to be used.
- **Exhausted**: spent during the current combat flow. Attribute Stones can return through **Stone Recovery**. Colorless Stones are **not recoverable** unless selected by **Influence Stone Ability: Colorless Recovery**.
- **Sealed**: locked by Rituals or an Ability that explicitly Seals Stones (such as Remove Scar); cannot recover at the end of a Round and returns only after a **Safe Haven Rest**.
- **Bound**: Permanently invested (Artifacts, Familiars, Summons, etc.). Bound Stones are not part of your usable pool and do not recover. They return to your Stone Pool only when the bond is **broken** or the investment is **released**.

### Colorless Stones

Every permanent Stone earned **after the 2 starting Attribute Stones** may be chosen as a **Permanent Colorless Stone at a 1:1 ratio**, with **no MR-based cap**. The 2 starting Attribute Stones cannot be converted to Colorless later. Colorless Stones can pay the normal costs of any eligible **unlocked** Stone Ability, but do not unlock Attribute Stone Abilities themselves. Their source determines when they are gained, when they expire, and any explicit source-specific restrictions.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
**One recovery rule for all Colorless Stones:** Spent Colorless Stones become **Exhausted**. They **cannot be restored through normal Stone Recovery by default**, regardless of source. **Influence Stone Ability: Colorless Recovery** is the way to make up to **2 / 4 / 8 / 16** Colorless Stones eligible for the existing shared Stone Recovery limit (Ranks 1–4; normal activation costs **1 / 3 / 7 / 15** Stones). This Ability is activated **once per combat** at one chosen Rank; its designation lasts until combat ends. The activation Stones become **Exhausted**, not **Sealed**, and may be recovered according to their normal rules. It cannot be activated again or upgraded during the same combat. This does not grant Stones or increase the **MR Stones per Round** Recovery rate. The designation changes eligibility for recovery, not the Stone's Ready/Exhausted state.

- **Permanent Colorless Stones** persist between combats. Unrecovered Exhausted permanent Colorless Stones become Ready at **end of combat**, like other permanent Stones, unless Sealed or Bound.
- **Initiative Colorless Stones** are gained Ready through **Initiative Exchange** and last until **end of combat**, whether Ready or Exhausted. They do not count as permanent progression or affect MR and cannot be Burned, Sealed, or Bound.
- **Absorption Colorless Stones** are earned when eligible hostile damage causes a Health Bar to become **Scarred** while **Passive: Absorption** is slotted. Each such Health Bar uses one of at most **four Absorption triggers per combat**; the Passive grants **1–8 Stones per trigger** by Power Level. The earned Stones become Ready in the **next Preparation Phase**. They expire at **end of combat**; no HP-loss remainder is tracked.
- **Other granted Colorless Stones** follow the creation and expiration conditions of their source. They are not recoverable by default, but may be selected by **Colorless Recovery** while they exist; that Ability never extends their original duration. Any source-specific restriction on spending, Sealing, Binding, or Burning still applies.

All Colorless Stones share the same spending Pool. Source distinctions affect **persistence and any explicit source-specific restrictions**, not ordinary recovery eligibility.

---

#### Spending Stones

During the **Preparation Phase** at the start of each Round, first receive any Absorption Colorless Stones earned since your previous Preparation Phase. Those Stones are Ready and may be used when you choose and commit Stone Abilities for this Round. You may spend any number of **Ready Stones** available to you across your Stone Abilities, but **no Stone Ability can ever be raised beyond Rank 4**. Attribute Stones, Permanent Colorless Stones, Initiative Colorless Stones, and Absorption Colorless Stones may be used where their rules allow. When spent, an Attribute Stone or Colorless Stone becomes **Exhausted** unless a specific effect explicitly removes that Stone. Spent Colorless Stones remain unavailable through normal Stone Recovery unless designated by **Influence: Colorless Recovery**; temporary Stones still expire when their source says they expire. Once the first Turn of the Round begins, your Stone choices for that Round are locked unless a rule explicitly allows a change. Stone Abilities with a specific Turn timing or trigger are still prepared during the Preparation Phase, but their effect resolves only when that timing or trigger occurs.

**Round-Based Stone Ability Duration**

Round-based Stone Abilities are prepared and paid for during the Preparation Phase before the Round begins. Their effects become available immediately, unless the Ability specifies a later resolution timing or trigger. A Round-based Stone Ability remains available for the entire current Round, independently of the activating character's Turn or Initiative position. Any unused Round-based benefits expire at the end of that Round, before the next Preparation Phase begins. Round-based effects never carry over into the next Round. A character must prepare and pay for the Ability again during the next Preparation Phase to receive its benefits again. Abilities that explicitly provide a longer duration, a once-per-combat allowance, or a lasting result follow their specific rules instead.

All Stone Abilities have **4 Ranks**. They use one of two cost progressions.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
**Normal Stone Abilities**

| Rank | Additional Cost | Total Cost |
|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Rank 1** | 1 Stone | 1 Stone |
| **Rank 2** | 2 Stones | 3 Stones |
| **Rank 3** | 4 Stones | 7 Stones |
| **Rank 4** | 8 Stones | 15 Stones |

**Premium Stone Abilities**

| Rank | Additional Cost | Total Cost |
|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Rank 1** | 2 Stones | 2 Stones |
| **Rank 2** | 4 Stones | 6 Stones |
| **Rank 3** | 6 Stones | 12 Stones |
| **Rank 4** | 8 Stones | 20 Stones |

The following are **Premium Stone Abilities**: **Extra Attack, Parry, Crit, Damage Negation, Special Boost, Damage Reduction, Not a Target, and Phasing**. All other Stone Abilities use the Normal cost progression. **Rank 4 is the absolute maximum.** There are no higher Stone Ability Ranks. A Premium Rank 4 may exceed the normal Stone capacity of one Attribute and can therefore require **Colorless Stones, Stone Power Support, or another rule that explicitly provides usable Stones**.

#### Stone Power Support
