Stone Power Support pre-fills one named Rank of a Stone Ability. The supported Rank costs no Stones from the character, but every lower Rank required to reach it must still be activated and paid normally. Support never skips unpaid lower Ranks.

- Support for **Rank 2** requires Rank 1 to be paid normally.
- Support for **Rank 3** requires Ranks 1 and 2 to be paid normally.
- Support for **Rank 4** requires Ranks 1, 2, and 3 to be paid normally.

**Vitality: Maximization Support** follows this rule without adding charges of its own. Rank 2 / 3 / 4 Support pre-fills the corresponding **2 / 4 / 8 Stone** incremental cost. The character still pays lower required Ranks (**1 / 3 / 7 Stones** for supported Rank 2 / 3 / 4) during the **first Preparation Phase of combat**. The resulting allowance remains **2 / 3 / 4 shared Maximization uses for Damage or Healing Rolls during that combat**, not a second or recurring activation. Support can be used with a Limited Power, including the Hunter's Scourge Limited Strike, if its other requirements are met.

Stone Ability costs reset each **Preparation Phase** unless an Ability explicitly states that its cost is cumulative per combat.

---

#### Stone Recovery
- At the end of each Round, recover a total number of **Exhausted Stones equal to your Mastery Rank**.
- Choose those Stones freely from your **Attribute Stone Pools** and **Colorless Stones designated by Influence: Colorless Recovery**, irrespective of whether the Colorless Stones were Permanent, gained from Initiative Exchange, generated by Absorption, or granted by another effect.
- This is **one shared limit** of **MR recovered Stones per Round**. Colorless Recovery grants neither additional Stones nor additional recovery.
- Spent Colorless Stones **not designated** by Colorless Recovery remain Exhausted and cannot return through normal Stone Recovery. Temporary Colorless Stones disappear when their source duration ends; Exhausted permanent Colorless Stones return Ready at end of combat. Sealed / Bound Stones cannot be returned through this recovery.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

#### General Stone Abilities

| **Stone Ability** | **Rank 1** | **Rank 2** | **Rank 3** | **Rank 4** |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| **Extra Attack** | **+1 Attack Action** | **+2 Attack Actions** | **+3 Attack Actions** | **+4 Attack Actions** |
| **Extra Movement** | **+1 Movement** | **+2 Movements** | **+3 Movements** | **+4 Movements** |
| **Extra Reaction** | **+1 Reaction** | **+2 Reactions** | **+3 Reactions** | **+4 Reactions** |
| **Movement Enhancement** | **+4 m** per Movement | **+8 m** per Movement | **+12 m** per Movement | **+16 m** per Movement |

**Round duration:** Unless expressly stated otherwise, Stone Abilities are prepared and paid for in the **Preparation Phase**. Round-based effects apply throughout the upcoming Round and end at **Round End**, not at the start of the user's next Turn. Triggered effects resolve at their stated timing. Combat-long allowances and lasting results follow their own rules.

**Movement Enhancement:** Add the listed distance once to **each Movement Action this Round**, including normal Move, Dash, Disengage, eligible Movement Powers, and Extra Movements. Calculate the normal distance first (including Dash doubling), then add the bonus before applying distance reductions and rounding. This grants no extra Action or movement mode, changes no base Speed, bypasses no Root, Immovable, or terrain restriction, and does not affect Quick Load or Passive swapping. Normal Stone costs: **1 / 3 / 7 / 15**.

**Additional Attack Action Limit:** Extra Attack Actions from different sources do **not stack** unless explicitly allowed; use the highest single grant. Stone Power Support modifies the same Extra Attack activation, not a separate source. Your normal Attack Action remains available.

**Temporary Defensive Stone Values:** Stone bonuses to existing defenses add to their corresponding values under the normal rules. A Stone Ability can provide **Parry, Damage Negation, Damage Reduction, or Special Negation** even without a previously learned source. All Round-based defensive Stone bonuses expire at **Round End**. **Damage Negation** is a **single expendable pool for the current Round**, not a fresh allowance per attack. Before a Damage Roll, spend any number of its remaining points; each point removes **1 Damage Die**. You may divide the pool among multiple incoming attacks. Spend temporary Negation before the Passive's combat reserve. Unused temporary points expire at Round End.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Might Stone Abilities

| **Stone Ability** | **Rank 1** | **Rank 2** | **Rank 3** | **Rank 4** |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| **Parry** | **+2 Parry Pool** | **+4 Parry Pool** | **+6 Parry Pool** | **+8 Parry Pool** |
| **Damage** | **+2 Damage Dice** | **+4 Damage Dice** | **+8 Damage Dice** | **+16 Damage Dice** |
| **Armor** | **+4 Armor** | **+8 Armor** | **+16 Armor** | **+32 Armor** |
| **Armor Reduction** | Ignore **4 Armor** | Ignore **8 Armor** | Ignore **16 Armor** | Ignore **32 Armor** |

**Parry / Armor:** The bonus lasts for the current Round. Parry increases your existing **Martial or Spell Parry Pool** without changing its chosen delivery or making an ineligible effect parryable. **Damage.** Add the indicated dice to your **next damaging Martial Attack this Turn**, whether melee, ranged, natural weapon, or Martial AoE. This does not increase Spell damage.

**Armor Reduction:** Applies to **every damaging Attack you make this Round**, including Martial and Spell Attacks. Only your own attacks ignore the listed Armor; the target's Armor against other attackers is unchanged. This combines with **Corrode(X)** and eligible **Weapon Special: Penetration(X)**, but effective Armor cannot fall below 0. Penetration is not available as a generic Active or Active Buff.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Agility Stone Abilities

| **Stone Ability** | **Rank 1** | **Rank 2** | **Rank 3** | **Rank 4** |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| **Crit** | **1 pool** with Critical(1) | **2 pools** with Critical(1) | **3 pools** with Critical(1) | **4 pools** with Critical(1) |
| **Evade** | **+8 Evade** | **+16 Evade** | **+24 Evade** | **+32 Evade** |
| **Safe Movement** | Move up to **4 m** safely | Move up to **8 m** safely | Move up to **12 m** safely | Move up to **16 m** safely |
| **Invisibility Boost** | **+1 Invisibility Bonus** | **+2 Invisibility Bonus** | **+4 Invisibility Bonus** | **+8 Invisibility Bonus** |

**Crit:** During the current Round, designate the listed number of **Martial Attack Pools or damaging Spell Casting Pools**. Choose each **before its roll**. Each chosen pool gains **Critical(1)**: natural **7s and 8s explode**.

**Evade:** The bonus lasts until Round End.

**Safe Movement:** The listed movement does not provoke movement-triggered Reactions; other normal movement restrictions still apply. **Invisibility Boost.** Improves an **existing** Invisibility Bonus for the current Round and its related Perception TN. It does not grant Invisibility, change obscured Combat Senses, or block senses that could already detect the creature.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Vitality Stone Abilities

| **Stone Ability** | **Rank 1** | **Rank 2** | **Rank 3** | **Rank 4** |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| **Damage Negation** | **+2 Negation** | **+4 Negation** | **+8 Negation** | **+12 Negation** |
| **Temporary HP** | **20 Temporary HP** | **40 Temporary HP** | **80 Temporary HP** | **160 Temporary HP** |
| **Maximization** | **1 use** per combat | **2 uses** per combat | **3 uses** per combat | **4 uses** per combat |
| **Remove Scar** | **1 Bar**, Seal **1** Stone | **1 Bar**, Seal **2** Stones | **1 Bar**, Seal **4** Stones | **1 Bar**, Seal **8** Stones |

**Damage Negation:** **Premium** costs: **2 / 6 / 12 / 20 Stones**. Gain one expendable pool for the current Round. Each point removes **1 Damage Die** before rolling; divide it among incoming attacks as needed. Unused points expire at Round End. **Temporary HP.** Activate **once per combat**. The HP remain until depleted or combat ends, including across multiple hits. Spent Stones become Exhausted and may recover, but recovery does not permit a second activation. This differs from Reaction-granted Temporary HP, which protects one damage instance only.

**Maximization:** Activate once in the **first Preparation Phase of combat**. Pay **1 / 3 / 7 / 15 Stones** (eligible Colorless Stones allowed); the Stones become Exhausted. Gain **1 / 2 / 3 / 4 shared uses** for that combat. The Rank cannot change and unused uses expire at combat end.

Spend one use **before an eligible complete Damage or Healing Roll**: each d8 counts as **8**, without triggering any **natural-8** effect. For damage, apply **Damage Negation first** and maximize only the remaining dice, then resolve Armor and other mitigation. A complete roll includes eligible Weapon, Power, and Limited dice. Martial, Spell, Reaction, and Artifact damage qualify. **One shared AoE roll uses one charge; separate rolls each use one.** No targets, Range, AoE, or Action costs change.

For Healing, apply the current active-Bar HP cap. **Resolve: Healing** qualifies only where an active Bar exists; **ordinary HP Healing never reopens a fully Scarred Bar**, including on an Incapacitated target. **Restoration** has no Healing Roll. **Damage Transfer** heals only actual HP lost, even after Maximization of its Damage Roll.

**Remove Scar:** Each Rank restores **1 Scarred Health Bar** and **Seals** its printed Vitality Stones. Cumulative totals through Ranks 1–4 are **1 / 3 / 7 / 15 Sealed Stones** for up to **1 / 2 / 3 / 4 restored Bars**. Activation is allowed outside combat. Ranks already used since the last Safe Haven Rest cannot be repeated; when advancing, pay and resolve only unused Ranks. **Colorless Stones cannot pay.** Sealed Stones return only after a Safe Haven Rest.

**Artifact exception.** The Artifact Level-10 Awakening's once-per-Safe-Haven-Rest **cost-free supported activation** pays and Seals **0 Stones**, including prerequisite Ranks, and restores only still-unused Ranks. Ordinary **Stone Power Support** instead covers just its named Rank; lower Ranks must be paid and Sealed (e.g., Rank 4 Support leaves **7 Vitality Stones** to pay for Ranks 1–3).

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Intellect Stone Abilities

| **Stone Ability** | **Rank 1** | **Rank 2** | **Rank 3** | **Rank 4** |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| **Raises** | **2 Free Raises** | **4 Free Raises** | **6 Free Raises** | **8 Free Raises** |
| **Range & AoE Boost** | **+10 m** Range, **+2 m** Reach, **+1 m** Radius | **+20 m**, **+4 m**, **+2 m** | **+30 m**, **+6 m**, **+3 m** | **+40 m**, **+8 m**, **+4 m** |
| **Special Boost** | **+2 Special(X)** | **+4 Special(X)** | **+8 Special(X)** | **+12 Special(X)** |
| **Spell Resistance** | **+4 Spell Resistance** | **+8 Spell Resistance** | **+12 Spell Resistance** | **+16 Spell Resistance** |

**Raises:** These are **Combat Free Raises**, a shared allowance for the current Round on eligible Martial and Spell Attack/Casting Rolls made with any Attribute; they are **not the Skill-Rank Free Raises** for Skills and Rituals. **Before rolling, commit each Free Raise and declare whether it improves Damage or one existing numeric Special**, alongside all Paid Raises and their effects. No Raise may be reassigned after the roll. Committed Free Raises are consumed even on failure, add **no TN or Raise Cost**, and do not bypass a prohibition on Raises. **Paid Raises add +4 TN each** and have no Raise Cost. Unused Free Raises expire at Round End.

**Range & AoE Boost:** During the current Round, improve only delivery components an eligible Attack or Power **already possesses**: Range, Melee Reach, and/or an existing AoE radius. This grants no new delivery mode, AoE, target, or attack. The temporary bonus may exceed the Power's purchased radius cap without changing its base profile.

**Special Boost:** A **Premium Stone Ability**. Each eligible numeric **Special(X)** you successfully deliver during the Round gains the listed bonus, including Martial, Spell, Weapon, Power, Artifact, Reaction, and applicable persistent effects. Add successful Raise increases first, then Special Boost, eligible **Passive: Special Amplification** for the owner's attack, and target **Vulnerability**; then apply **Special Negation** and the per-Round **Special Application Limit**. The Passive does not amplify persistent effects. No new, binary, or nonnumeric Special is created or improved. **Spell Resistance.** Lasts until Round End and increases your **Final Spell TN** against both direct Spells and Spell AoEs that affect you.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Resolve Stone Abilities

| **Stone Ability** | **Rank 1** | **Rank 2** | **Rank 3** | **Rank 4** |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| **Damage Reduction** | **+10% DR** | **+20% DR** | **+30% DR** | **+40% DR** |
| **Healing** | **4d8 HP**, within **2 m** | **8d8 HP**, within **4 m** | **12d8 HP**, within **8 m** | **16d8 HP**, within **16 m** |
| **Active Buff Empowerment** | **+1 PL**, **+1 Round** | **+2 PL**, **+2 Rounds** | **+3 PL**, **+3 Rounds** | **+4 PL**, **+4 Rounds** |
| **Special Negation** | **+2 Special Negation** | **+4 Special Negation** | **+8 Special Negation** | **+12 Special Negation** |

**Damage Reduction / Special Negation:** Round-based defensive bonuses expire at Round End. Special Negation reduces incoming eligible hostile numeric **Special(X)**; if reduced to **0 or less**, it is not applied.

**Healing:** During Preparation, pay the normal cumulative Stone cost for **one Healing application**. Trigger it later in the current Round at a legal action boundary, targeting **yourself or one ally** within the listed Range. You may also trigger it **before incoming HP damage**; heal first, then resolve the hit's damage. Triggering uses **no additional Stones, Attack Action, or Reaction**. Roll once, then expend the prepared application. Unused Healing expires at Round End; Stones remain spent. Apply the cap of the target's **currently available active Health Bar**. If every Bar is Scarred, HP Healing has no eligible Bar and **cannot end Incapacitated**, even when an ally prepared and triggered it. Only Scarred-Bar recovery, such as an eligible **Restoration Active**, can reopen a Bar. Eligible **Maximization** may enhance a legal HP Healing Roll.

**Active Buff Empowerment:** Once per Combat, during the Preparation Phase, when activating an Active Buff, you may empower that Buff by the listed Power Levels and additional complete Rounds. The effective Power Level cannot exceed 16. Empowerment cannot be applied to an already maintained Buff. The allowance does not refresh when the Buff expires, is replaced, or is reactivated.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Influence Stone Abilities

| **Stone Ability** | **Rank 1** | **Rank 2** | **Rank 3** | **Rank 4** |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| **Not a Target** | **1 enemy** within **8 m** | Up to **2 enemies** within **16 m** | Up to **3 enemies** within **24 m** | Up to **4 enemies** within **32 m** |
| **Cleanse** | **Cleanse(2)** | **Cleanse(4)** | **Cleanse(8)** | **Cleanse(16)** |
| **Colorless Recovery** | Up to **2 Stones** | Up to **4 Stones** | Up to **8 Stones** | Up to **16 Stones** |
| **Enemy Evade Reduction** | **-1 Evade** | **-2 Evade** | **-4 Evade** | **-8 Evade** |

**Not a Target.** Choose the listed number of enemies in Range during Preparation. Each cannot target you with its **next attack this Round**, unless you are its **only valid target**. The restriction expires at Round End. **Cleanse.** Apply the listed **Cleanse(X)** to **one creature within 20 m**, including yourself. Distribute it among eligible negative Specials on that creature; one activation never affects multiple creatures.

**Colorless Recovery.** Activate **once per combat** during Preparation; pay the normal **1 / 3 / 7 / 15 Stones**, which become **Exhausted, not Sealed**. No **Passive: Absorption** is required. Choose up to the listed number of **Ready or Exhausted Colorless Stones**; unused selection capacity may be assigned to Stones gained later in this combat. Those selected Stones may participate in the existing **shared MR-Stones-per-Round Recovery** until combat ends. They are **not recovered immediately**. The Rank and selection limit cannot later increase, and this Ability cannot be activated or stacked again in the same combat. Unselected Colorless Stones remain ineligible for normal Recovery. This creates no Stones, grants no additional Recovery, and never extends a Stone's own expiration. The activation Stones may recover normally if eligible, but this does not grant another use.

**Enemy Evade Reduction.** For **your own attacks this Round**, treat each targeted enemy as having the listed Evade penalty. Its actual Evade is unchanged, so allies gain no benefit. This is **not Expose(X)** and applies no Special.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Wits Stone Abilities

| **Stone Ability** | **Rank 1** | **Rank 2** | **Rank 3** | **Rank 4** |
|:--|:--|:--|:--|:--|
| **Phasing** | **1 Charge** | **2 Charges** | **3 Charges** | **4 Charges** |
| **Initiative Boost** | **+1 x MR** Initiative | **+2 x MR** Initiative | **+4 x MR** Initiative | **+8 x MR** Initiative |
| **Reaction Denial** | Deny **1 Reaction** | Deny **2 Reactions** | Deny **3 Reactions** | Deny **4 Reactions** |
| **Movement Reduction** | Halve Movement of **1 enemy** | Halve Movement of **2 enemies** | Halve Movement of **3 enemies** | Halve Movement of **4 enemies** |

**Phasing:** Activate **once per combat**, regardless of Rank or source. Charges remain available until spent or combat ends. **Initiative Boost.** Activate **once per combat** during the Preparation Phase's Initiative Exchange sequence. Add the bonus **before Initiative is converted into Initiative Colorless Stones**. Spent Stones become Exhausted normally. **Reaction Denial.** Choose **one enemy** during Preparation. Deny its next **1 / 2 / 3 / 4 eligible Reaction declarations this Round**, regardless of Reaction type. A denied Reaction has no effect but **does not consume** the enemy's Reaction allowance. Each denied declaration uses one denial; the same Reaction cannot be re-declared against the **same trigger**.

**Movement Reduction:** Halve the distance of **every individual Movement Action this Round** for the selected enemies, including additional Movements. Round half-metres **up** (5 m to 3 m, 7 m to 4 m). This does not change base Speed, remove Movements, or apply twice to the same Movement. Resolve separate **Slow** reductions under their own rules.

::

### Last Breath

Once per Safe Haven Rest, when you would drop to Incapacitated, you may burn **1 Stone** from any Attribute. You remain conscious with **1 free box** in your Wounded Health Bar. Any remaining damage from the triggering damage instance is ignored. You cannot use Last Breath again until you complete a Safe Haven Rest.

::
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Types of Powers

Powers are divided into five main types:

| **Power Type** | **Short Description** |
|---|---|
| **Active** | Action-based Powers for attacks, healing, effects, zones, barriers, and Special delivery. |
| **Passive** | Ongoing character features that define your baseline and combat identity. |
| **Active Buff** | Temporary combat states, stances, auras, or short-term enhancements; mechanical size changes use Transformation, while Form is visual only. |
| **Reaction** | Immediate responses to specific triggers outside your turn. |
| **Movement Power** | Special movement options that replace your normal Movement. |

For full rules, templates, pricing, limits, and examples, see the dedicated **Power Catalogues**. Only a qualifying **damaging Instant Active Power** with its own printed Damage Dice may be learned as a **Limited Power**; see **Limited Power Modifier** for uses per Safe Haven Rest and doubled Power Damage Dice. Special-only Powers are never Limited.

### A Shared Combat Language

Destroyed Faith uses a shared mechanical language for combat. Many combat abilities are built from a limited set of standardized effects called **Specials**. Specials are not an additional layer of rules placed on top of your abilities. They are the building blocks from which many of those abilities are created.

Each Special has one clear mechanical function. **Ruin** deals damage. **Slow** reduces Movement. **Corrode** reduces Armor. **Hex** increases Spell Damage. **Sundered** does the same for non-Spell Damage, and so on. You do not need to memorize every Special before you play. Learn the ones your character uses. When another Special appears at the table, its effect can simply be looked up or explained once. Once you know a Special, you know what it does wherever it appears. Different characters may describe the same Special in completely different ways. A curse, a weapon technique, an artifact, or a supernatural power may look entirely different in the fiction while using the same mechanical effect.

Characters normally specialize in only a small number of Specials and combine them with the abilities of the rest of the group. This creates tactical combinations without requiring every individual Power to introduce its own separate set of rules.

**Specials are not additional systems to learn. They are the shared vocabulary from which much of combat is built.**

::
### Power Tags  

#### Tag: Spell  
Any Power that interacts with the **Veil** the boundary between matter and will gains the **(Spell)** tag. Only abilities that explicitly reference “Spells” can target, dispel or suppress these effects. *Examples:* Ruin, Root, Bless, Hex Bolt, Healing Pulse.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Combat Core

Combat is divided into **Rounds** and **Turns**. A **Round** is one full cycle in which every combatant gets one Turn. A **Turn** is one combatant’s moment to act. Initiative is rolled once at the start of combat. Initiative Order is established at the start of each Round and remains fixed for that Round. Initiative is not rolled again at the start of each Round unless a rule explicitly allows it.

---

### Dice Basics

All dice rolled in the Mastery System use **d8s**.

---
When a die rolls an **8**, it explodes:

- roll that die again,
- add the new result to the 8,
- if the new roll is also an 8, repeat.

Damage dice do **not** explode unless a rule explicitly says otherwise.

---

### Dice Pools

Most rolls use the format: `XkY`

---
This means:

- roll **X dice**,
- keep the best **Y dice**,
- add the kept dice together.

For most tests:

- **Pool = relevant Attribute**
- **Keep = Mastery Rank**

---

Example:

---
A character with Might 8 and Mastery Rank 3 rolls: `8k3`

---
They roll 8 dice and keep the best 3.

---

### Pool and Keep

Roll the final dice Pool after all reductions. Mastery Rank defines the maximum number of dice kept; it does not raise the number of dice rolled. **Effective Keep = min(Mastery Rank, final Pool).** For a legal standard roll, penalties cannot remove the last **two rolled dice** of a Pool constructed with at least two dice. Keep is **not** raised to two: a Fear Check still keeps one. An original Pool of one remains one; the floor never supplies unpurchased dice or refunds voluntarily spent, exchanged, or split dice (including **Guaranteed Eights**). **Full Parry/Counterspell** still cancels a roll as expressly written; the floor does not prevent negation or create a missing action.

Example: Resolve 2, MR 4 normally rolls **2k2**. Even if Fear would remove both dice, the character retains **2k2**. Resolve **k1 Fear Checks** retain two *rolled* dice but keep **one**.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Mastery and Keep

| **Mastery Rank** | **Keep** |
|:--:|:--:|
| I | k1 |
| II | k2 |
| III | k3 |
| IV | k4 |
| V | k5 |
| VI | k6 |
| VII | k7 |
| VIII | k8 |

---
:

### Raises

**One Raise catalog for every delivery.** Martial and Spell attacks use the same Raise values.

1. **Before rolling**, declare the number of **Paid Raises**, commit any eligible **Intellect Free Raises**, and **assign every Raise immediately** to one of the two effects below. Record the exact split (for example, two Damage Raises and one Special Raise). Each Paid Raise adds **+4 TN**; Free Raises add **+0 TN**. A Raise may modify only an effect the Power already has. **No effect can be chosen or reassigned after seeing the roll.**
2. Roll against the single raised TN. An AoE uses one roll and checks each target separately. A Power creating a placed area without an immediate target checks its **Creation TN** (Spell: 4 × used PL + 4 per Paid Raise).
3. On success against the applicable TN, apply the **predeclared** Raise effects before resolving Damage and Specials. Failure grants none of those effects. Intellect Free Raises committed to a failed use are still spent.

| Raises spent | Universal effect |
|:--:|---|
| **1** | Add **+MR d8 Damage** to the Power's existing Damage Roll. For an **AoE**, add **+ceil(MR / 2) d8** instead (round the number of bonus dice up). |
| **1** | Add **+MR** to **one existing numeric Special(X)** applied by the Power. |

Multiple Raises may be assigned to the same option. **Range, Melee Reach, AoE size, and Duration** use the Power's printed values and do not change through combat Raises. This keeps target declaration and Attack-Trigger Reactions in the normal pre-roll window.

**Split Attack:** One Power use makes up to two separate Attack or Casting Rolls using its divided Pool. **Before either roll**, declare one set of Paid/Free Raises and their exact Damage/Special allocation for the entire Power use. Add the same Paid-Raise TN increase to both rolls. Apply the **same predeclared effects** to each successful split; a failed split gains none. Each successful split deals full Damage and its full Special payload. The split rolls determine hits independently.

**Autofire:** Declare the ordered attack slots **and the single exact Paid/Free Raise allocation** before rolling. For attack number *i*, its TN is that target's applicable defense **+4 × (i − 1)**, plus **+4 per Paid Raise** for this Power use. This positional increase is **not a Paid Raise**, grants no Raise effect, and cannot be canceled with Intellect Free Raises. The **same predeclared Damage/Special effects** apply to every successful slot; stop on the first failed slot. Autofire uses one Attack/Casting Roll and one common Damage Roll, not one roll per slot.

**Intellect Free Raises:** Declare how many of the current Round's allowance are committed to this use before rolling. They use the same two options without raising TN, and are consumed as described by the Stone Ability. For Split Attack, commit them once for the Power, not separately for each split.

**Example:** At MR 3, a character faces a target with **TN 22** (for example, a Martial target with Evade 22). **Before rolling**, the character announces **one Paid Damage Raise** (+3d8 Damage on a direct attack; +2d8 on an AoE), making TN **26**. A roll of 25 fails; 28 succeeds and receives the **already declared Damage bonus**. If the character wanted **+3 to an existing numeric Special** instead, that choice had to be announced **before the roll**. Spells start from **4 × the used PL**, not caster MR.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Combat Senses

Combat Senses define how a creature can locate and read the battlefield. Every creature has **Normal Combat Awareness** through ordinary sight, hearing, touch, smell, attention, instinct, and battlefield awareness. It is always active unless a condition, environment, Disadvantage, or effect limits one or more of those channels. Special Combat Senses such as **Life Sense, Mage Sense, Tremor Sense, Sonar Sense,** and **Predator Sense** are additional perception channels. They do not replace Normal Combat Awareness. A Combat Sense defines **what information is available**, not automatic success. If uncertainty, Stealth, concealment, distance, pressure, or fine detail matters, roll **Perception** normally.

---

### Special Sense Slot

Every creature has one **Special Sense Slot**. A creature with no Special Combat Sense simply leaves this Slot empty. An Echo, Artifact, Species, Power, or story feature may grant **Sense Options**. Before the first Perception roll of a scene, choose one known Special Combat Sense to place in the Slot. In combat, choose it before Initiative is rolled. The chosen Special Sense remains active for that scene or combat unless a rule explicitly allows it to change. Normal Combat Awareness remains active at the same time. **Darkvision** is an upgrade to Normal Combat Awareness and never uses the Special Sense Slot.

Learning several Special Combat Senses gives more choices between scenes; it does not allow several Special Combat Senses to operate at once.

---

### Clear, Obscured, and Blocked

Use three states whenever perception matters:

- **Clear:** the active Sense can perceive the target normally. If nothing else creates uncertainty, no roll is required.
- **Obscured:** the Sense still functions, but Stealth, Invisibility, concealment, distance, noise, smoke, distraction, or another effect makes location uncertain. Roll **Perception** against the appropriate TN.
- **Blocked:** that Sense cannot perceive the target at all. No Perception roll can overcome a Blocked Sense; use another active Sense or another solution.

If **any active Sense** clearly locates a target, the creature may target it normally. Perceiving a creature does not ignore range, total cover, walls, line of effect, or another rule that prevents the Attack or Power from reaching it. A blind creature still has Normal Combat Awareness through its remaining ordinary senses, but sight is unavailable. A dwarf with Tremor Sense can therefore use ordinary hearing and smell while also reading grounded movement through the Special Sense.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Combat Sense Reference

| Sense | Range | Detects | Ignores / Excels Against | Blocked By |
|---|:---:|---|---|---|
| **Normal Combat Awareness** | 60 m | Visible creatures, sound, movement, ordinary smells, nearby physical threats | Broad ordinary awareness | Missing sensory channel, total cover, overwhelming interference, effects that explicitly block ordinary perception |
| **Darkvision** | 30 m | Normal visual information in darkness | Ordinary darkness | Smoke, fog, walls, total cover, magical darkness, visual Invisibility |
| **Life Sense** | 30 m | Living creatures, heartbeat, breath, warmth, bloodflow, vital force | Darkness, smoke, fog, ordinary visual Invisibility | No life, undeath, constructs, life-masking, strong barriers, effects that explicitly block Life Sense |
| **Mage Sense** | 30 m | Active magic, Artifacts, curses, wards, summons, illusions, necromancy, divine/demonic marks | Darkness, poor visibility, magical signatures hidden from ordinary sight | Mundane targets without a supernatural signature, anti-magic, aura masking, strong barriers, effects that explicitly block Mage Sense |
| **Tremor Sense** | 20 m | Steps, impact, weight, vibration, burrowing, structural tension through a shared surface | Darkness, smoke, fog, ordinary visual Invisibility | No shared surface, flight, hovering, vibration isolation, incorporeal movement, overwhelming vibration, effects that explicitly block Tremor Sense |
| **Sonar Sense** | 30 m | Echoes, bodies, openings, obstacles, rough silhouettes, spatial layout | Darkness, smoke, fog, blind fighting, ordinary visual Invisibility | Silence, sound absorption, overwhelming noise, no readable echo, soundproof barriers, effects that explicitly block Sonar Sense |
| **Predator Sense** | 20 m | Blood, wounds, fear, sweat, sickness, weakness, prey-signs, recent passage | Darkness, hunting, tracking wounded prey, ordinary visual Invisibility | No readable prey-sign, sealed targets, scent masking, strong wind/rain/chemicals, effects that explicitly block Predator Sense |

---

### Sense Notes

**Normal Combat Awareness** is the broad default and handles ordinary battlefield information. Darkness, smoke, fog, noise, distraction, and concealment normally **Obscure** it rather than automatically removing all awareness. **Darkvision** only upgrades Normal Combat Awareness through ordinary darkness. It does not reveal invisible creatures or bypass smoke, fog, walls, magical darkness, or total cover. **Life Sense** answers *“something living is there.”* **Predator Sense** answers *“something bleeds, fears, weakens, fled, or can be hunted.”* They are not interchangeable. **Mage Sense** includes what might otherwise be called Death Sense: undead force, necromancy, divine and demonic marks, curses, and supernatural remnants are all valid magical signatures.

A Special Sense never gains information outside its nature. Tremor Sense does not read color or writing; Sonar does not identify magic as magic; Mage Sense does not replace ordinary sight; Predator Sense does not map a room merely because prey is inside it.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Stealth, Hidden, and Invisibility

**Stealth** is the Skill used to hide presence, movement, traces, and position. **Hidden** means an observer has not successfully detected or located a creature. **Invisibility** Obscures specific Combat Senses, making the creature harder to locate, target, and perceive through those Senses. A creature may be Hidden without being invisible, invisible without using Stealth, or both. **Invisibility itself never breaks merely because the creature moves, attacks, casts, reacts, is detected, or takes damage.** Ordinary Stealth is a separate state and can still end for its printed reasons.

### Stealth

When a creature has a meaningful opportunity to hide, the GM sets its **Task Level and Base TN** from cover and circumstances. Declare any Skill Raises (**+4 TN per Paid Raise; Free Raises +0**), then roll **one Stealth Skill Check** against the final setup TN.

---
- **Failure:** Ordinary Hidden is not established. Witnesses who actually noticed the attempt need no roll to overcome it; distant creatures learn nothing automatically.
- **Success:** The creature is Hidden from observers who have not located it. Its **Final Skill Result becomes their Perception TN** under Skill Opposition.

> **Successful Stealth Final Result = Perception TN**

Each eligible observer rolls **Perception** against that TN when attempting detection. Reuse the same successful Stealth result while the hiding conditions remain unchanged.

**Ordinary Stealth only:** A normal Stealth hiding attempt ends when the creature clearly reveals its position, stops acting carefully, makes a direct Attack, casts a hostile Spell or uses a hostile Power, uses a visible Reaction, takes final HP Damage, or voluntarily moves more than **3 m**. A new normal Stealth attempt requires a meaningful new opportunity to hide. **Ending ordinary Stealth never ends Invisibility, makes an invisible creature visible, or automatically reveals it to Normal Combat Awareness.**

**Invisibility is separate:** No Stealth Check is required to keep Invisibility active, including after an attack. An invisible creature remains Obscured to Normal Combat Awareness. When it is **also Hidden** by a separate ordinary Stealth attempt, the relevant observer must overcome both concealment methods; compare one Perception result to the **higher of the applicable Stealth Final Result and Invisibility TN**. Do not add them. When ordinary Stealth ends, only the Invisibility TN remains.

---

### Invisibility and Combat Senses

Invisibility does not automatically defeat every Combat Sense. Each Invisibility Power specifies which Combat Senses it **Obscures**. Only those Senses are affected. **Passive: Invisibility** always Obscures **Normal Combat Awareness**, including **Darkvision**.
At higher Power Levels, the character may select additional Special Combat Senses:

- **Life Sense**
- **Mage Sense**
- **Tremor Sense**
- **Sonar Sense**
- **Predator Sense**

Passive: Invisibility grants one additional Special Combat Sense selection at Power Levels **4, 8, 12, and 15**. Each selection must be different.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
#### Covered and Uncovered Senses

**Covered Sense — Obscured**

If Invisibility covers a Combat Sense, the observer must succeed on a Perception Check against the creature's Invisibility TN to locate it.

**Uncovered Sense — Unaffected**

If Invisibility does not cover a Combat Sense, that Sense is completely unaffected by Invisibility. The observer perceives the creature **normally through that Sense, as though the creature were not invisible**. No Invisibility TN applies. No Invisibility Bonus applies. Neither Invisibility nor extra Skill spending prevents detection through that Sense; ordinary Stealth remains a separate possible obstacle.


The Sense must still be capable of perceiving the creature and must satisfy its normal Range and detection requirements. Other independent forms of concealment or ordinary Stealth may still apply.

**Blocked Sense — Unavailable**

A Sense that is genuinely Blocked by another effect cannot be used for Perception. Invisibility itself causes **Obscuration, not Blocking**. If an observer has at least one active, valid, unaffected Sense that clearly locates the creature, Invisibility provides no protection against that observer.

### Invisibility Detection TN

An invisible creature's Perception TN comes from its MR, Invisibility Bonus and current Health Level. Concealment established through Stealth uses its own advance setup check.

#### Step 1 — Base TN

Multiply the invisible creature's Mastery Rank by 4.

> **Base TN = 4 × Mastery Rank**

#### Step 2 — Invisibility Bonus

Add the creature's current Invisibility Bonus. For **Passive: Invisibility**, the Bonus equals its Power Level.

#### Step 3 — Health Penalty

For each lost Health Bar, reduce the TN by the creature's Mastery Rank.

| **Current Health** | **TN Reduction** |
|:--|:--:|
| Healthy | None |
| Bruised (−10%) | −1 × MR |
| Injured (−20%) | −2 × MR |
| Wounded (−40%) | −3 × MR |
| Broken (−50%) | −4 × MR |

These reductions use the number of lost Health Bars, not the percentage value of the normal Health Penalty. The reduction applies immediately when a Health Bar is lost. Becoming **Incapacitated** does not automatically suppress Invisibility. Passive Invisibility remains active while slotted; Active Buff: Invisibility remains active for its printed duration unless it ends under the ordinary Active Buff rules.

#### Final TN

> **Invisibility Perception TN = (4 × MR) + Invisibility Bonus − (Lost Health Bars × MR)**

This is a **specific combat detection defense**, not a mundane Task TN and not a consumable Skill bonus. The Health Penalty is applied once, even when multiple Invisibility effects are combined.
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
### Stealth While Invisible

**Establish Stealth before observation.** Against affected Senses, use the higher successful **Stealth roll result** or **Invisibility TN**, without summing them. Choose Paid Skill Raises before the Stealth setup; on success, allocate its Raise effects **before any observer rolls**. These effects enhance the stated concealment method, not the separate Invisibility Bonus. Untrained hiding uses Disadvantage and no Raises.

**Round-by-round visibility:** At the start of each Round, observer-specific locations found by Perception expire as described below. The Invisibility TN remains based on MR, Invisibility Bonus, and lost Health Bars only. Ordinary Stealth must follow its normal end conditions. Detection through a Clear, uncovered Combat Sense is unaffected by Invisibility.

---

### Targeting Through Concealment

Before directly targeting a Hidden or invisible creature with an Attack, Spell, or hostile Power, check the attacker's active Combat Senses. **If the target is Obscured, the attacker may make a free Perception Check immediately before attempting direct targeting. This Check costs no Attack Action, Movement, Reaction, or Stone.** The starting Perception Rank is granted free; this action still follows the detection limits below.

- **Clear:** If an active Sense can clearly locate the creature, it may be targeted normally. An uncovered Special Combat Sense ignores Invisibility entirely.
- **Obscured:** If Invisibility or another effect Obscures a valid Sense, make a Perception Check against the appropriate TN.
- **Blocked:** A Blocked Sense cannot locate the creature and cannot make a Perception Check.

**Successful Perception**

The observer locates the creature until the end of the current Round and may target it normally. **This is observer-specific targeting, not visibility:** it does not reveal the creature to everyone, change its Invisibility status, remove its Invisibility Bonus, or make it Clear to Normal Combat Awareness.

**Failed Perception**

The observer cannot directly target the creature during its current Turn unless another valid Sense or effect locates it. The attempted targeting action is not spent. Each observer may make **one free Perception Check per Hidden or invisible creature per Round**; a failed Check cannot be repeated against that same creature until the next Round. At the start of each new Round, previous Perception-based location results expire. A new Check is required if the creature remains Obscured. Area effects, zones, environmental effects, and abilities that do not require precise targeting remain usable normally.

### Attacking from Invisibility

An invisible creature **remains invisible when it attacks**. An Attack, Spell, hostile Power, Reaction, Movement, or other action does not automatically reveal its position, cancel Invisibility, or remove its protection for the remainder of the Round. **Normal Combat Awareness (including Darkvision) remains Obscured even after the attack.** Observers must locate the creature through their valid active Combat Senses using the rules above. A successful observer-specific Perception Check permits direct targeting until the end of that Round; **it does not make the creature visible or change its Invisibility status**. Normal cover and concealment rules still apply.

### Injury and Invisibility

Invisibility becomes progressively weaker as the creature loses Health Bars. Each lost Health Bar reduces the Invisibility TN by the creature's Mastery Rank. Movement, attacks, Reactions, and individual instances of HP Damage no longer apply separate numerical reductions to the Invisibility Bonus. Attacking does not automatically reveal the creature's location or deactivate Invisibility. Damage never switches Invisibility off by itself; the existing Lost Health Bar adjustment changes only its Perception TN.

---
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
### Surprise

If a creature initiates combat against an enemy that is unaware of it, the attacker gains **Surprise Priority**. Roll Initiative and resolve Initiative Exchange normally. During the first Round, a creature with Surprise Priority acts before creatures it surprised, regardless of Initiative Score; the Score itself does not change. After the first Round, Surprise Priority ends. If an unaware creature is drawn into an **Opposed Attribute Contest**, the initiator instead also gains **Advantage** and the unaware creature has **Disadvantage** for that initial Contest only.

---

{{note
##### Quick Examples

**Normal Stealth:** A successful Stealth roll of 21 sets Perception TN 21. **Invisibility:** MR 3 with Passive: Invisibility Level 6 has TN 12 + Bonus 6 = **18**. **Stealth setup:** Before the observer checks, an invisible creature rolls against **Task TN 12** and achieves **24**. The player resolves any post-success Skill Raise allocation before observation. The observer with affected Senses uses the higher TN **24**, not 24 + 18, and a Perception result **23** fails. Neither Skill Points nor Raises chosen after observing the defender's result may adjust that TN.

**Injured:** The same MR 3 character loses two Health Bars. Its Invisibility TN drops from 18 to **12**. A separately established Stealth TN remains subject to the ordinary Stealth rules, not post-roll spending.

**Uncovered Sense:** A creature with active Predator Sense encounters an invisible character who has not selected Predator Sense as part of Invisibility. Predator Sense perceives the character normally. **No Invisibility Perception Check is required.**
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Round Structure

Each combat follows this structure:

1. **Preparation Phase**
The Preparation Phase takes place before the Round begins. Creatures prepare Stone Abilities and choose any Active Buff activation or replacement before any Turn. Round-based benefits apply throughout the upcoming Round, regardless of Initiative Order.

   - At the start of this Phase, first receive Ready Absorption Stones earned through eligible Scarred Health Bars since the previous Preparation Phase.
   - On the first Round only, roll Initiative.
   - Before any creature moves, acts, or begins its Turn, all creatures prepare their Stone use for the Round.
   - Resolve **Initiative Boost** first. Any Stones used for Initiative Boost are committed and spent immediately.
   - Resolve **Initiative Exchange** and convert Initiative into Initiative Colorless Stones.
   - After Initiative Exchange, creatures commit and spend their remaining Ready Stones and any Initiative Colorless Stones on their other Stone Abilities for the Round.
   - Defensive and passive Stone values that apply during the Round become active immediately.
   - Stone Abilities with a Turn timing or trigger are prepared now but resolve at their specified time.
   - Activate, keep, or replace an **Active Buff** here. A new activation commits this Round's **base Attack Action**. Apply any **Active Buff Empowerment** chosen in this same Phase and initialize Buff resources once.
   - Once Preparation choices are complete, determine Initiative Order and lock it for this Round.

2. **Resolve Turns**
   - Creatures act in Initiative Order.
   - Each creature takes one Turn per Round.

3. **End the Round**
   - Resolve end-of-round effects.
   - Expire Round-based Stone Ability effects, unused Round-based pools, and Active Buffs reaching their final **Round End**.
   - Recover Exhausted Stones according to normal Stone Recovery.

4. **Start a New Round**
   - Keep all remaining Initiative Scores.
   - Begin a new Preparation Phase.
   - Do not roll Initiative again unless a rule explicitly allows it.
---

### Basic Action Kit

At the start of each Round, each character normally has:

- **1 Movement**
- **1 Attack Action**
- **1 Reaction**

Some Powers, Items, or Stones may grant additional Movements, Attack Actions, or Reactions. A base Attack Action committed to an Active Buff in Preparation is unavailable on that Round's Turn; legal additional Attack Actions remain usable.

---
::
### Turn Structure

On your Turn, resolve the following steps in order:

1. **Start of Turn**
2. **Movement and Attack Action**
3. **End of Turn**

---

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

### Start of Turn

At the start of your Turn, resolve all effects that trigger at the start of your Turn.

This includes:

- ongoing damage,
- Diminishing Stack ticks,
- start-of-turn buffs,
- start-of-turn penalties,
- and similar effects.

Resolve effects at the start of your Turn in this order:

1. Resolve **Ticks** of Diminishing Specials already present when this Turn began (for example, existing Ruin and Slow).
2. Apply **Natural Special Recovery** to eligible negative Diminishing Specials.
3. Apply normal **Diminishing Decay** of **−1** to each stored Diminishing stack. Resolve Root's separate start-of-turn reduction by the affected creature's MR.
4. Apply legal **start-of-turn benefits**, including **Passive: Self Cleanse** (additional to Natural Special Recovery), **Active Buff: Self Cleanse**, and eligible healing. **Active Buff: Temporary HP** refreshes in Preparation, not here.
5. Resolve **Persistent Zone start-of-turn applications** for zones the creature is currently inside. Newly applied Specials enter the active stacks now; their first normal start-of-turn Tick occurs at the creature's next eligible Turn start.

A Persistent Zone also applies when it appears around a creature or the creature first enters it during a Round, according to the zone's normal rules. Each **zone/creature pair makes at most one application attempt per Round**, including an application resisted by Spell Resistance. Once attempted, further entry or start-of-turn events in that Round do not cause another application from that zone. **Root** does not receive Natural Special Recovery or Diminishing Decay. Its own start-of-turn MR reduction is resolved in Step 3. Negative special Tick damage remains governed by its own mitigation rules.

Example:

`Ruin(4)` resolves its Tick, may be reduced by Natural Special Recovery, and then decays by **1**. Diminishing Specials cannot decay below 0.

---

### Movement and Attack Action

During your Turn, you may use:

- **1 Movement**
- **1 Attack Action**

You may use them in either order unless a rule says otherwise. A Movement may be normal Movement or a Movement Power.
An available Attack Action may be used to:

- make a weapon attack,
- use an Active Power,
- cast a Spell,
- use an item,
- or perform another action that costs an Attack Action.

**Active Buffs activate only in Preparation**, never through a Turn Attack Action.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
### Reactions

A Reaction is used when its specific Trigger occurs. You normally have **1 Reaction per Round**. Additional Reactions may be spent when an eligible Trigger occurs.

**Reaction Trigger Windows**

| **Trigger** | **Resolution window** |
|:--|:--|
| **Attack Trigger** | After the attack, its target or affected area, and its delivery are declared; **before** the Attack Roll or Casting Roll. |
| **Hit Trigger** | After a hit is confirmed; **before** Phasing, Specials, or damage from that hit are resolved. |
| **Damage Trigger** | After the Damage Roll is known; **before** Armor, Damage Reduction, Temporary HP, and Health are applied. |
| **Incoming Effect Trigger** | After Special Negation has reduced an incoming Special; immediately **before** the remaining Special takes effect. |
| **Ongoing Effect Trigger** | Immediately **before** an existing Special's next tick or other effect resolves. |

An **Ally** Trigger uses the same window when that event affects the ally. A Reaction with a **more specific printed Trigger** uses its printed moment; for example, **Reaction: Absorption Surge** triggers after an eligible Health Bar becomes Scarred. The Reaction's effect is resolved at that window, unless its text specifies a later resolution.

---
:
### End of Turn

At the end of your Turn, resolve effects that trigger at the end of your Turn.

---
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Initiative

Initiative represents combat tempo, reaction speed, and the ability to seize openings. Initiative is rolled once at the **start of combat**. Roll a number of d8s equal to your **Mastery Rank**. Keep all dice and 8s explode.

Your Initiative Score is:

`Initial Initiative Score = Initiative Roll + all Initiative modifiers`

The Initial Initiative Score can never be lower than **0**. The resulting Initiative Score remains in play until it is spent or another rule changes it. It is not reset or rolled again at the start of each Round unless a rule explicitly says so.

---

### Initiative Exchange

At the start of every Round, before any creature has moved, acted, or begun its Turn, resolve Initiative Exchange in this order:

1. On the first Round only, roll Initiative.
2. Any creature that has not used **Initiative Boost** this combat may activate it now.
3. Each creature may convert any amount of its Initiative into **Initiative Colorless Stones**.
4. After all conversions are complete, determine Initiative Order from the remaining Initiative Scores.

The cost of one Initiative Colorless Stone is:

`4 × your current Mastery Rank Initiative`

| **Mastery Rank** | **Initiative per Stone** |
|:---:|:---:|
| **MR2** | **8** |
| **MR3** | **12** |
| **MR4** | **16** |
| **MR5** | **20** |
| **MR6** | **24** |
| **MR7** | **28** |
| **MR8** | **32** |

You may buy more than one Stone during the same Initiative Exchange if you can pay the full cost of each Stone. Subtract the cost immediately. Initiative can never drop below **0**.

An **Initiative Colorless Stone** is gained Ready and joins your Colorless Pool for the current combat. It may pay any part of the normal Stone cost of an unlocked Stone Ability. When spent, it becomes **Exhausted**; it can return through normal Stone Recovery **only if selected by Influence Stone Ability: Colorless Recovery**. It remains part of your Colorless Pool for the rest of the combat and disappears when combat ends, whether Ready or Exhausted. Initiative Colorless Stones cannot be burned, Sealed, or Bound. They do not count against an Attribute Stone limit, do not count as permanent Stone progression, and never affect Mastery Rank.

---

### Initiative Order

After Initiative Exchange, higher Initiative acts first. **On equal Initiative Scores, higher Mastery Rank acts first; if still tied, compare Agility Pool (higher first). If all three are tied, tied creatures each roll 1d8; highest acts first, repeating only between creatures still tied until order is unique.** When multiple eligible Reactions to the same simultaneous event need an order, process their declarations and resolutions in this Initiative order (using the same tie breakers). Once the first Turn begins, Initiative Order is locked for the rest of that Round. Changes to Initiative do not reorder the current Round unless a rule explicitly says they do.

At the start of the next Round, before anyone moves or acts, resolve Initiative Exchange again and determine a new Initiative Order from the remaining Initiative Scores. Every creature still receives only one Turn per Round.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Attacks

An attack is any action that tries to hit a target through an attack roll. This includes weapon attacks and attack-based Active Powers. A successful hostile single-target Martial Power adds its matching Weapon Damage; an eligible single-target Spell uses Spell Focus Damage instead. **AoE Powers add neither source**, applying full printed Power effects separately to every hit creature. Non-attack Support Powers do not gain delivery damage. Spells follow the Magic rules, but still use the same core dice and Raise engine.

---
:
### Defense Structure

The Mastery System separates defense into three **Primary Defense Pillars** and four specialized **Premium Defense Systems**. **Health** is a separate structural durability axis, supported by **Passive: Health** (more Health Bars) or **Passive: Fortitude** (larger Health Bars). The Primary Defense Pillars are complete defensive foundations around which a character may build their combat identity.

| **Primary Defense Pillar** | **Stage** | **Function** |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **Parry** | Before the relevant roll | Uses the selected **Martial** or **Spell** Parry delivery to remove dice from an eligible incoming Attack Pool or Casting Pool. If the entire eligible Pool is reduced to 0, a Martial Attack is **Fully Parried** and a Spell is **Fully Countered**. Either result means the effect does not resolve. Counterspell is this Spell delivery of Parry, not a separate Power. |
| **Evade** | During the Attack Roll | Determines the Target Number an Attack must reach to hit the character. For a Martial AoE, the same Attack Roll is compared separately against the Evade of each valid creature in the area. |
| **Armor** | After the Damage Roll | Reduces the rolled damage result before it is applied to the character. |

Premium Defense Systems are powerful specialized additions. They normally do not replace a complete Primary Defense Pillar. Instead, they support and modify an existing defensive build.

| **Premium Defense System** | **Stage** | **Function** |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **Phasing** | After the Attack hits | Ignores the complete triggering hit, including its damage and applicable hit effects. |
| **Special Negation** | Before an eligible Special is applied | Reduces every incoming eligible hostile Special(X). If the Special is reduced to 0 or less, it is not applied. |
| **Damage Negation** | Before the Damage Roll | Removes any chosen number of incoming Damage Dice, up to the dice in the Pool and the defender's available Damage Negation. The entire Damage Pool may be removed. |
| **Damage Reduction** | After Armor | Reduces the remaining damage after Armor has been applied. |

**Absorption** is a separate Stone-generation subsystem, **not** a damage-mitigation or HP-granting defense. When eligible hostile damage Scars a Health Bar (maximum four times per combat), it can generate Temporary Colorless Stones for later Preparation Phases. Fortitude is the dedicated Passive for increasing Health Bar size. **Spell Resistance** is a separate, specialized defense against Powers with the **Spell** tag. Spell Resistance increases the Final Spell TN against the protected creature. It does not reduce Attack Dice, Damage Dice, damage, or Specials. **It is not a default defense:** a creature without explicitly granted Spell Resistance has **0**. Do not add it routinely just because the creature has a higher Mastery Rank.

**Base Evade = Mastery Rank × 4.** Apply equipment, Powers, Passives, Active Buffs, Reactions, items, and other defined Evade modifiers afterward.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Choosing Your Defense

Do not worry about using every defensive system listed above. Most characters focus on **one Primary Defense Pillar** and may later support it with a second Pillar or one of the specialized Premium Defenses.

A heavily armored character may rely primarily on **Armor**, while a fast character builds around **Evade**. A dedicated defender may choose **Parry**, while a durable character may invest in **Health** or **Fortitude** to endure more HP loss before Scarring. **Absorption** rewards surviving eligible Health Bars becoming Scarred from hostile damage with Stones; it does not make the character harder to injure by itself. Premium Defenses such as Damage Negation, Damage Reduction, Phasing, or Special Negation are usually added to strengthen one of these foundations rather than replace it.

You are not expected to track every step in the sequence below. When resolving an effect, simply skip every step for which your character has no relevant defense. A character without Parry ignores the Parry step; a character without Special Negation ignores the Special Negation step; and a character without Absorption ends the sequence after damage has been applied. Especially when building a new character, it is usually better to develop **one clear defensive identity first** instead of investing in several incomplete defenses.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

<div style="text-align: justify;">

### Complete Attack and Defense Sequence

When resolving an Attack, Spell, or another hostile effect, follow the applicable steps below. Effects that do not make an Attack Roll, deal damage, or apply Specials may skip the corresponding steps.

#### 1. Choose the Target and Effect

Choose the target and the Attack, Spell, Power, weapon, or other hostile effect being used. Confirm that the target is valid, within Range, and can legally be affected by the chosen effect. For **Autofire**, declare the complete ordered list of attacks before the shared roll; the same creature may occupy multiple attack slots.

#### 2. Determine the Target Number

Determine the defense used by the effect.

---
 - A direct normal Attack rolls against the target’s **Evade**.
 - A direct Spell uses **Final Spell TN = Spell Base TN + Target Spell Resistance**. The **Spell Base TN is 4 × the Power Level used for this casting**, not a value based on the caster's Mastery Rank.
 - A Martial AoE uses one Attack Roll and compares that same result separately against the **Evade of each valid creature** in the area.
 - A Spell AoE uses one Casting Roll and compares that same result separately against the **Final Spell TN of each valid creature** in the area.
 - **Autofire** checks each declared attack in order against that slot's Martial **Evade** or Spell **Final Spell TN**, with an extra **+4 × (slot number − 1)**; normal Paid Raises add their +4 per Raise to every slot.
---

AoE targets are checked separately. **Passive: Enemy Evade Reduction** and **Passive: Spell Resistance Piercing** modify only the owner's eligible Martial and Spell TNs, respectively; neither changes the target's listed defenses.

#### 3. Declare Raises

**Before rolling**, declare Paid Raises, commit Intellect Free Raises, and **allocate each Raise to Damage or to an existing numeric Special** according to the Universal Combat Raise Catalog. Every Paid Raise adds **+4** to each applicable target TN; Free Raises do not increase TN. The declaration is locked before the roll. For an AoE, check each target separately against the raised TN and use the halved, rounded-up Damage Raise dice. Split Attack and Autofire share one **predeclared** Raise allocation for their entire Power use. **Autofire's +4 per later attack is a separate cumulative hit threshold, not a Paid Raise or a Raise effect.**

#### 4. Build the Attack or Spell Pool

Build the appropriate dice pool from the relevant Attribute, weapon, Power, and all other legal modifiers. The acting character normally keeps dice equal to their Mastery Rank.

#### 5. Apply Parry

**Passive: Parry** has Martial or Spell delivery. Enter Parry at Turn start by spending your base Attack Action, or one legal additional Attack Action if the base was committed to a Buff. Never enter for free.

- **Martial Parry:** before an eligible direct Martial Attack targeting you is rolled, spend Parry. Each point removes **1 Attack Die** from the Attack Pool assigned to you.
- **Spell Parry:** when you are a target of, or a valid creature inside, a hostile Spell whose source is within **22 m** and whose casting you can perceive, spend Parry before the Casting Roll. Each point removes **1 Casting Die** from that Spell's Casting Pool.

If the remaining Pool contains fewer dice than its normal Keep value, roll and keep all remaining dice. If the eligible Pool is reduced to **0 dice**, a Martial Attack is **Fully Parried** and a Spell is **Fully Countered**. No Attack Roll or Casting Roll is made; the effect does not hit or successfully cast, deals no damage, applies no Specials, and resolves no hit- or success-dependent effects. **Attack-Trigger Reactions** are declared and resolved in this pre-roll window. Apply their defensive modifiers to each affected creature's relevant defense before rolling.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### 6. Make the Roll

Roll the remaining Attack or Spell Pool, resolve exploding dice, keep the appropriate number of dice, and compare the final result to the applicable Target Number. A Martial result below its defense misses. For a Spell, a result below Spell Base TN fizzles; a result that reaches Spell Base TN but not a creature's Final Spell TN is cast successfully but resisted by that creature. For an **AoE Attack**, use the same final roll for every valid creature in the area and compare it separately against that creature's defense. A miss against one creature does not affect any other creature. After the comparison, a creature that would be hit may use **Dive for Cover** at its specifically listed pre-payload window. For **Autofire**, use that one shared Attack or Casting Roll against each successive, cumulatively raised slot TN; **stop on the first miss or resistance**. Dive for Cover does not apply to Autofire.

Every successful hit receives its full eligible payload and Raise Effects. A successful hostile **single-target or Autofire** Martial hit adds matching Weapon Damage; a legal Spell hit instead adds matching Spell Focus Damage. An **AoE adds neither**: every hit creature receives the full printed Power Damage and Specials, without a Primary Target or division. A missed raised TN receives no payload even if the unraised TN was met.

#### 7. Resolve Phasing

Once a hit is confirmed, resolve eligible **Hit-Trigger Reactions**, including Phasing, before applying Specials or damage. If an eligible Phasing effect ignores the hit, no damage, Specials, or other hit-dependent effects are resolved against that target.

#### 8. Apply Special Negation to Incoming Specials

Calculate each incoming numeric Special as **printed Special(X) + successful Raise Special Increase + applicable Intellect Special Boost + eligible attacker Passive: Special Amplification + applicable target Vulnerability**. Then reduce that complete incoming value by the target's **Special Negation**, separately for each eligible hostile Special delivered by the Attack or effect. A Special reduced to **0 or less** has no effect. Resolve eligible **Incoming Effect-Trigger Reactions** against the remaining value here. In Step 9, apply the Special's own rules and the target's **4 × MR per-Round application limit** to the remaining eligible Diminishing Special; store only the points that fit this limit. Existing stacks may be reduced through Cleanse.
**Special Aura** increases only the **effective value** of an already present eligible Diminishing Special by +1 while the target is in the aura. It does not place a new point into the stored stack or use the per-Round application allowance.

#### 9. Resolve Hit Effects

Apply the remaining Specials, forced movement, control effects, conditions, and other hit effects according to their individual rules. A successful Attack, Spell, or AoE does not automatically trigger a second defensive roll. A later Attribute Check occurs only if the effect explicitly grants a break attempt, disbelief attempt, or another separately timed response.

#### 10. Build the Damage Pool

Build the eligible Damage Pool: a successful hostile **single-target Martial Power or Martial Autofire hit** adds matching Weapon Damage; an eligible **single-target Spell or Spell Autofire hit** adds Spell Focus Damage, even if only a Special/control effect was purchased. **AoE Powers add neither Weapon nor Spell Focus Damage.** Add printed Power Damage, successful Raise damage, and other eligible non-focus bonuses. Non-attacking Support/placement Powers gain no delivery damage. **Hex and Sundered bonus dice use only stacks present before the relevant hit; new stacks benefit later hits.**

#### 11. Apply Damage Negation

Before the Damage Pool is rolled, the target chooses how many of its assigned Damage Dice to remove, from **0 up to the full number of incoming Damage Dice**, limited only by its **total available Damage Negation** from legal sources. Each point spent removes **1 Damage Die**. The defender may remove the entire Damage Pool (for example, 8d8 becomes 0d8 by spending 8 points), in which case no Damage Dice are rolled. Spend temporary Damage Negation before the Passive Reserve; **choose the order among separate temporary pools**, track each independently, and apply its own expiry. 

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

For a shared **AoE or Autofire** Damage Roll, each defender declares Negation before rolling, **separately for each eligible hit** (including repeated hits against one creature); omit that many dice from the same ordered shared roll **for that hit only**, without rolling a new base pool. The resource is spent for each hit that uses it. Negation does not change the hit, Specials or other effects.

#### 12. Roll Damage

Normally roll the remaining Damage Dice; they do not explode unless stated. For a damaging **AoE or Autofire**, roll the common eligible Damage Pool **once**; reuse those recorded die results for every successful AoE target or Autofire hit. For Autofire, that common Pool includes eligible Weapon or Spell Focus Damage. Each hit applies its declared Damage Negation and other defenses separately; target-specific dice are resolved only for that hit. **After Damage Negation, before rolling**, you may spend one eligible use of **Passive: Maximization** or **Vitality Stone Ability: Maximization** to make every remaining die in the complete roll an **artificial 8**, with no natural-8 effects. Choose **one source per roll**, never both.

The slotted Passive grants **1 / 2 / 3 free uses per Combat** at PL 4-7 / 8-14 / 15-16. Vitality Maximization grants **1 / 2 / 3 / 4 shared Combat uses** if paid for in the first Preparation Phase. Track them separately. One shared AoE roll costs one use; separate rolls cost separate uses. Either source may maximize a legal **Limited Power** or eligible **Healing Roll**, but adds no attack or dice. Resolve Armor, DR and other mitigation normally. **Damage Transfer** has no separate Healing Roll.

After determining the Damage Roll result, the attacker may spend any **Mark already on the target before this hit began** to set that Damage Roll's floor. Decide the amount **after viewing Damage Dice but before Damage-Trigger Reactions**; change qualifying die results and reduce the target's stored Mark immediately. Mark newly applied by this hit becomes available for **later hits**, not this hit. Then resolve eligible **Damage-Trigger Reactions** against the resulting Damage Roll before Armor and other damage mitigation. A Reaction requiring a later event, such as Scarring, resolves at that specifically stated event.

#### 13. Apply Corrode, Armor Reduction, Weapon Penetration, and Armor

Start with the target's **current Armor** for this hit. Apply **Corrode(X)** present at Step 13 (including Corrode just applied by this hit), then subtract eligible attacker-specific **Passive: Armor Reduction**, **Might Stone Ability: Armor Reduction** (Rank 1/2/3/4 = 4/8/16/32), and any applicable **Weapon Special: Penetration(X)**. These stack additively against this attack only; Effective Armor cannot go below zero. `Effective Armor = max(0, Current Armor - Corrode - Passive Armor Reduction - Might Stone Armor Reduction - Weapon Penetration)`

**Corrode newly applied at Step 9 already reduces Armor for the current hit's Step 13**, because Specials resolve before Armor. Hex and Sundered keep their expressly separate prior-hit snapshot rule. For example, a target with Armor **20**, affected by **Corrode(3)**, is hit by a character with **Stone Armor Reduction 8**, **no Passive Armor Reduction**, and weapon **Penetration(4)**: `20 - 3 - 8 - 4 = 5 Effective Armor`. Only **5 Armor** is subtracted from this hit's rolled damage. Another character without Armor Reduction or weapon Penetration still encounters the target's remaining **17 Armor** after Corrode, assuming no other modifiers. Any excess reduction past zero provides no additional damage. Passive Armor Reduction applies to its owner's eligible damaging attacks while slotted; Stone Armor Reduction applies for its active Round; weapon Penetration applies only to eligible weapon hits.

Subtract effective Armor from the rolled damage using `Damage after Armor = Rolled Damage - Effective Armor`. Armor does not normally reduce Specials, conditions, or ongoing Special damage unless a rule explicitly states otherwise.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### 14. Apply Damage Reduction

Apply percentage-based **Damage Reduction (DR)** to damage remaining after Armor. Start with the character's **Passive DR**. Add eligible **Active Buff DR** up to that Buff's printed total-DR cap; then add eligible **Reaction DR** up to that Reaction's own printed total-DR cap. Each cap limits how much that particular bonus can increase the current non-Stone DR subtotal; applying a lower cap never removes DR already present. Both Buff and Reaction require the underlying Passive DR when their entries say so. Then add **Resolve Stone Ability: Damage Reduction** outside those printed Buff/Reaction caps. The final total DR cannot exceed **100%**.

**Final damage after DR = ceil(max(0, Damage after Armor) × (1 − total DR / 100)).** Apply the existing minimum-damage rule for natural 8s in Step 15. For example, 13 damage after Armor and 30% DR becomes **10**; with 80% total DR it becomes **3**. A separate explicitly stated 50% final-damage reduction such as Bulwark applies to the already calculated final damage when its own trigger resolves; round any fractional remaining damage **up**.

#### 15. Apply Minimum Damage from Natural 8s

If Armor and Damage Reduction reduce the damage to **0 or less**, the target still takes **1 damage for each natural 8 rolled on the original Damage Dice**. Only dice that were actually rolled count; Damage Dice removed through Damage Negation cannot generate minimum damage.

#### 16. Apply Temporary HP and Other Damage Buffers

Apply remaining damage to legal Temporary HP buffers before real HP, keeping distinct Passive, Active Buff, Stone, and Reaction sources separate. **Reaction Temporary HP protects only its triggering damage instance; any unused amount expires immediately afterward.** Temporary HP damage is not real HP loss.

#### 17. Apply Damage to Health

Apply all remaining damage to the target's current Health Bar according to the normal Health and Health Level rules.

#### 18. Resolve Damage Transfer (Vampyr)

For a successful **Melee Single Target Damage Transfer** or **Ranged Single Target Damage Transfer** attack or Spell, heal the attacker by the **real HP actually lost by the target from this attack** after all mitigation, Temporary HP, and other buffers. Martial Weapon Damage counts in the same damage instance; Spell delivery does not add Weapon Damage. Never count prevented damage, unused overkill, earlier damage, or HP loss from other sources. The self-healing is limited to missing HP in the attacker's **current active Health Bar**; it cannot restore Scarred bars or grant Temporary HP. No separate Healing Dice are rolled; zero real HP lost means zero healing. Limited and Maximization may modify the eligible Damage Dice, but transferred healing still equals actual real HP lost and has **no separate Healing Roll to maximize**. This fixed, single-target Transfer is not the Deathless Echo's Life Absorption Stone mechanic.

#### 19. Resolve Absorption

After the damage instance resolves, count **Health Bars newly Scarred by eligible hostile damage** while Passive: Absorption is slotted. **Each such bar consumes one of the Passive's four available triggers per combat**, even if one hit Scars multiple bars. Each valid trigger earns the Passive's Power-Level-based Stones (1 at Levels 1–2 through 8 at Levels 15–16). A qualifying Absorption Reaction may add Stones once for this damage instance; the maintained Absorption Active Buff may add Stones on the first valid trigger of the Round. Neither increases the four-trigger allowance. Voluntary Health Bar sacrifices, self-inflicted damage, and damage that does not Scar a bar generate no Absorption. Earned Stones arrive Ready in the **next Preparation Phase**. Track only triggers used (0–4), not HP lost or a damage remainder.

</div>

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Armor and Shields

Armor reduces incoming damage.

Your total Armor is:

`Armor Value + Shield Value + Mastery Rank + other Armor bonuses`

Subtract total Armor from incoming damage. Armor applies to attacks and damage instances unless a rule says the damage ignores Armor. Ongoing Special damage usually ignores Armor.

---

### Armor Types

| **Armor Type** | **Armor Value** | **Evade** | **Initiative** | **Skill Penalty** |
|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--|
| **Light** | +4 | +0 | — | — |
| **Medium** | +8 | −2 | −4 | −1d8 to Physical Skill checks |
| **Heavy** | +12 | −4 | −8 | −2d8 to Physical Skill checks |

---

### Shield Types

| **Shield Type** | **Armor Value** | **Evade** | **Initiative** | **Skill Penalty** |
|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--|
| **Parry Shield** | +1 | +4 | — | −1d8 to Physical Skill checks |
| **Medium Shield** | +4 | +0 | — | −2d8 to Physical Skill checks |
| **Tower Shield** | +8 | −4 | −4 | −2d8 to Physical Skill checks |

---

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Opposed Attribute Contests

Sometimes the question is not whether a creature can overcome a fixed difficulty, but **which of two creatures wins when they directly oppose each other**. An **Opposed Attribute Contest** resolves that immediate struggle. Use it for direct contests of body, reflex, intellect, will, presence, or another personal capability when a normal Attack, Power, Special, or Skill Check does not already resolve the situation.

---

### Core Rule

Each participant chooses an Attribute that fits **how they are approaching the Contest**. The GM may restrict the available Attributes when the fiction clearly allows only one approach.

Each participant:

1. Rolls a Pool equal to the chosen Attribute.
2. Keeps dice equal to their own **Mastery Rank**.
3. Applies normal Pool modifiers, Advantage, Disadvantage, Rerolls, and other effects that explicitly modify that roll.
4. Compares the Final Result directly with the other participant.

There is **no Target Number**. There are **no Raises**.

**Specialization Ranks and Paid or Free Skill Raises do not modify an Opposed Attribute Contest.**

The higher Final Result wins. On a tie, **the existing situation remains unchanged**.

> **Opposed Attribute Contest = Attribute Pool k Mastery Rank vs. Attribute Pool k Mastery Rank**

---

### Choosing the Attribute

The Attribute follows the method being used, not a fixed universal pairing.

| **Approach** | **Typical Attributes** |
|:--|:--|
| Raw strength, leverage, holding, forcing | **Might** |
| Speed, balance, reflex, slipping free | **Agility** |
| Endurance when the Contest is explicitly about lasting longer | **Vitality** |
| Logic, analysis, structured argument | **Intellect** |
| Wit, mental speed, reading and answering in the moment | **Wits** |
| Conviction, faith, ideological certainty, force of will | **Resolve** |
| Presence, rhetoric, social pressure, force of personality | **Influence** |

The two participants do **not** have to use the same Attribute. A creature might use **Might** to hold someone while the other uses **Agility** to slip free. A theologian might argue through **Intellect** while the opponent answers through **Resolve**. If the fiction permits only one meaningful approach, the GM may require the same Attribute from both participants. An arm-wrestling match, for example, is normally **Might vs. Might**.

---

### Surprise in a Contest

If a creature begins an Opposed Attribute Contest against another creature that is **unaware of it**, the initiating creature rolls with **Advantage** and the unaware creature rolls with **Disadvantage**. This applies only to the **initial Contest**. Once the target is aware of the struggle, later Contests are rolled normally unless another effect grants Advantage or Disadvantage. If the Contest also begins combat, normal **Surprise Priority** applies in addition to this rule.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Grapple

Grapple is a Basic Attack Action and an application of an Opposed Attribute Contest.

**Cost:** 1 Attack Action
**Range:** Melee Reach
