The initiating creature and the target make a physical Opposed Attribute Contest. **Might** and **Agility** are the only normal Attributes available for Grapple, depending on whether the creature is overpowering, bracing, twisting, slipping, or using leverage. If the initiator wins, the Grapple is established.

A Grapple is a **shared physical hold**:

- both creatures are considered **Grappled**,
- both creatures have **Speed 0 m**,
- neither creature can voluntarily move away from the other,
- Grapple is **not Root** and does not count as Root,
- Grapple by itself gives no Attack, Evade, Armor, Damage, or other numeric modifier.

**Grapple itself never deals damage.** The creature maintaining the Grapple may make an **Unarmed Basic Attack** without releasing it. If that creature makes a **weapon Attack**, it must release the Grapple first; the weapon Attack is a separate Attack, not part of Grapple. The creature that established the Grapple may release it freely on its Turn without spending an action. A creature trying to break free spends **1 Attack Action** and starts another physical Opposed Attribute Contest. If it wins, the Grapple ends. On a tie, the existing Grapple remains.

The Grapple also ends if the creatures are forcibly separated beyond Melee Reach, if one participant is teleported or otherwise removed from the hold, or if the creature maintaining the hold becomes unable to physically maintain it.

---

### Examples

#### Grapple from Behind

Alaris approaches a guard who is completely unaware of her and tries to lock him in place before he can react. Alaris uses **Agility**; the guard uses **Might** to resist the hold. Because the guard is unaware of her, Alaris rolls with **Advantage** and the guard rolls with **Disadvantage** for this first Contest. If Alaris wins, the Grapple is established and both creatures have Speed 0 m. Any later attempt by the guard to break free is rolled normally. If this starts combat, Alaris also gains normal **Surprise Priority**.

---

#### Arm Wrestling

Two characters settle a dispute with an arm-wrestling match. The fiction leaves no meaningful alternate approach: this is **Might vs. Might**. Both roll their Might Pool and keep their own Mastery Rank. The higher result wins. A tie means neither side gains ground and the Contest remains unresolved.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### A Debate at the Table

Two characters spend hours arguing across a tavern table about whether the old gods deserved obedience. The GM decides that the entire exchange can be resolved as one Contest because the only question is **who comes out on top in the argument**. One character builds a careful logical case with **Intellect**. The other argues from conviction and lived faith with **Resolve**. They roll directly against each other; the higher result wins the debate. The same rule can resolve a formal theological dispute, a battle of wits, or another focused one-on-one argument.

Winning a Contest does not create mind control. If the scene is about forcing a lasting concession, changing a major belief, negotiating complex terms, or resolving several social objectives, use the normal **Skill Check** rules instead; the optional **Social Challenges** guidance can help organize a longer scene.

---

### Skill Opposition

Some opposition is about **trained Skill use**, not raw capability: Stealth against Perception, Deception against Investigation, a concealed trail against Tracking, and similar cases.

1. **Create the opposition.** State the intended concealment, deception or setup. Set its own Task Level and Base TN, then declare **Paid Raises** before the setup roll. Only Paid Raises add +4 to that attempt's Final TN.
2. **If the setup fails**, its intended concealment or deception is not established. No opposing roll is required to overcome that failed setup. A watcher still must be in a position to observe the relevant evidence; failure does not grant distant creatures automatic knowledge.
3. **If the setup succeeds**, allocate the actor's declared Paid and available Free Raises **before any responder checks**; the successful setup **roll result** then becomes the TN for eligible observers or challengers. The responder follows the normal Skill procedure and chooses their own Paid Raises before rolling.
4. The successful setup roll is made **once** for the same disguise, trail, story, hidden camp, or other unchanged obstacle. Do not reroll it for each observer. Reassess only when the actual situation materially changes.

**NPC response:** An NPC without full player-style Attributes still **rolls** its situational Pool, keeping its MR, as described under **NPCs in Skill Checks and Contests** in the core rules. Do not waive the opposing roll merely because the responder is an NPC. **Example:** A false courier attempts Deception at Base TN 12 and declares no Raises. A result of 9 fails to establish a convincing cover; a present guard notices the obvious problem without an opposed roll. A result of 22 establishes the cover, and an actively suspicious guard may roll Investigation or suitable Perception against **TN 22**.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Attribute Checks Against Effects

Not every Attribute roll is an Opposed Attribute Contest. When a rule gives a creature a chance to **resist, endure, disbelieve, or break an effect**, resolve an Attribute Check against the TN named by that effect instead. There is no second creature rolling against it.

---

#### Resistance Attributes

| **What is being resisted** | **Attribute** |
|:--|:--|
| Bodily endurance, poison, disease, pain, physical collapse, or effects that test whether the body holds together | **Vitality** |
| Corruption, possession, divine or demonic pressure, soul binding, or effects directed at the soul | **Resolve** |
| Structured thought, memory, logic, mental analysis, or recognizing a constructed contradiction | **Intellect** |
| Immediate awareness, instinct, sensory doubt, mental intrusion, or noticing that perception has been manipulated | **Wits** |

**Might, Agility, and Influence are not resistance Attributes.** They are used in Opposed Attribute Contests when appropriate, but they do not resist hostile effects unless a rule explicitly says otherwise. A Power must name **Vitality**, **Resolve**, **Intellect**, or **Wits** directly when it creates a resistance Attribute Check. It never lists Body, Mind, or Spirit as a roll category.

---

#### Attribute Check Resolution

When an effect calls for an Attribute Check:

1. Roll dice equal to the named Attribute.
2. Keep dice equal to your Mastery Rank.
3. Apply normal Pool modifiers unless the effect says otherwise.
4. Compare the Final Result to the TN stated by the effect.

Skill Rank and Paid or Free Skill Raises do not apply unless the rule explicitly calls for a Skill Check. There is no Vitality Reserve expenditure, and no universal automatic-success mechanic for these checks. Rerolls and effects that explicitly modify Attribute Checks apply normally. When an effect explicitly allows an Attribute Check, use its **printed TN**. If no TN is printed:

- **Ranked source:** `TN = (8 × Source MR) − 2`.
- **Unranked hazard:** `TN = Task Level × 4` based on the actual danger (for example, Level 4 poison: **TN 16**).

The GM sets the TN **before** rolling. Never invent a Source MR or scale the TN with the defender's MR. **No check is added unless a rule already permits one**; a successful Attack, Spell, or Special still grants no automatic second save.

---

#### No Automatic Second Roll

A successful Attack, Spell, or AoE normally resolves its full listed payload. Push, Pull, Prone, Root, Disarm, and Stunned do not automatically grant an Attribute Check. Ongoing effects do not automatically grant repeated checks. An Attribute Check is used only when a rule explicitly creates a separately timed response, such as:

- spending **1 Attack Action** to inspect an illusion,
- attempting to break a mental or spiritual binding,
- resisting unwilling telepathic contact,
- or resolving a Fear Check when an effect explicitly calls for one.

---

### Which Rule Do I Use?

- **Creature directly against creature:** Opposed Attribute Contest.
- **Training against training or concealment:** Skill Opposition.
- **Creature against a hostile effect:** Attribute Check against the effect's TN.
- **Attack against a target:** normal Attack vs. Evade rules.
- **Complex multi-stage social objective:** Linked normal Skill Checks, optionally organized using the **Social Challenges** guidance (no special resolution rules).

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Advantage and Disadvantage

Some effects grant Advantage or impose Disadvantage. Advantage and Disadvantage modify how dice behave. They do not change Pool or Keep values.

---

### Advantage

When rolling with Advantage, any **1** rolled in the basic dice pool may be rerolled once. This applies only to the initial dice pool. It does not apply to explosion dice. The new result replaces the 1, even if the new result is another 1. Explosions are then resolved normally.

---

### Disadvantage

When rolling with Disadvantage, only **one eligible die** from the Pool may explode. Normally this is a natural **8**; on a designated **Critical** roll, natural **7s and 8s** qualify. All other eligible dice retain their face value without exploding.

---

### NPCs in Skill Checks and Contests

Outside combat, an NPC may still need to **roll** to observe a Hidden creature, challenge a Deception, negotiate, track, resist an effect, or win an Opposed Attribute Contest. **Do not build a full NPC character sheet:** assign an appropriate **situational d8 Pool** for that action and **keep the NPC's MR**. Record only the few noncombat Pools used in the scene; these do not change its printed Combat Attack Pools or damage values. For **competent, relevant NPC opposition**, these are **GM starting ranges**, not automatic values for every bystander:

| NPC Mastery Rank | Capable NPC Pool | Strong Specialist Pool |
|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **MR 1** | **3k1** | **4k1** |
| **MR 2** | **6k2** | **8k2** |
| **MR 3** | **10k3** | **12k3** |

The **MR 3 range is deliberately about four dice above MR 2**, as a playtest guideline. Even an MR 2 **6k2** guard is much better at the relevant check than a beginning hero rolling **4k2**; reserve the upper range for a genuinely capable specialist. A common bystander can use a smaller Pool. At MR 4+, set the Pool to the NPC's actual role and intended challenge; do **not** extend the +4 dice step mechanically without checking the resulting odds.

**Skill Opposition:** An NPC initiating a trained setup first succeeds against the task's own **Task TN**, then its final roll becomes the response TN as normal. A responding NPC rolls its chosen Pool against the successful setup result; it does not gain a fixed automatic opposition score. For NPC Skill Raises, assign a narrow **situational Skill Rank** only when that NPC is actually trained and intends to declare Raises; otherwise use no Raises. **Opposed Attribute Contests:** both creatures roll their relevant Pools with their own Keep as usual. A source-based resistance check still follows the effect's printed TN or its fallback rule.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

### Health & Fear
---

Damage does not only bring a character closer to defeat. It also reduces how much of their full potential they can still bring into a roll. Your **Vitality** determines the size of your Health Bars. Each Health Bar represents a deeper stage of injury.

---

#### Determining Health Bars

By default, everyone has five Health Bars.

Each Health Bar has:

<strong>Vitality × 4</strong> boxes by default. **Passive: Fortitude** may increase that multiplier to **×5, ×6, ×7, or ×8** at Power Levels **4, 8, 12, and 16**, respectively, while slotted. It changes the maximum size of normal Health Bars only; it does not create additional bars, heal lost HP, or reopen Scarred bars. **Passive: Health** and **Passive: Fortitude** share the Health category and cannot be slotted together. **Passive: Absorption** does not grant HP.

After all available Health Bars are depleted (including any additional Bars granted by Growth or other approved effects), the character enters the **Incapacitated** state, which is represented by one final box.

<ul style="margin-top:0; padding-left: 1.2em;">
  <li><strong>Active Health Bar:</strong> the highest Health Bar that is not Scarred.</li>
  <li>You can heal damage only within your current Active Health Bar.</li>
  <li>Once a Health Bar is completely filled, it becomes <strong>Scarred</strong>.</li>
  <li>A Scarred Health Bar cannot be healed during the same combat unless a rule explicitly allows it.</li>
  <li>The final box is always <strong>Incapacitated</strong>.</li>
</ul>

---

#### Health Penalties

Health penalties reduce the number of dice you roll.

| Health | Healthy | Bruised | Injured | Wounded | Broken | Incap. |
|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Pool** | — | −10% | −20% | −40% | −50% | no actions |

Round dice loss down, minimum 0. Applies to attacks, Attribute Checks, Skill Checks, spells, rituals, and initiative. Does not reduce damage dice unless a rule explicitly says so.

---

#### Order of Pool Reduction

When multiple rules modify a dice pool, apply them in this order:

1. Start with the relevant Attribute.
2. For Skill Checks, use the full relevant Attribute Pool regardless of Skill Rank. An untrained attempt normally has **Disadvantage**, not a smaller Pool. Specialist knowledge may restrict what task can be attempted, as described in Skills.
3. Apply all flat pool changes from range, environment, Powers, Stones, Buffs, Specials, **Fear**, or other effects. This includes **Weaken**, **Soulburn**, and a subtraction of **1 die for each point of Fear**.
4. Apply the current percentage-based **Health Penalty** to the remaining pool.
5. Round the number of dice lost to the Health Penalty down.
6. **Protected two-dice floor:** After penalties, a legal Pool built with 2+ dice keeps at least **2 dice rolled**, not 2 kept. Applies to Attribute, Attack/Casting, Skill, Ritual, Fear, NPC, Summon, and Initiative rolls. Pools built with 0–1 dice remain 0–1.
7. Keep up to MR dice, never more than rolled. The floor does not refund voluntarily split, spent or exchanged dice (**Guaranteed Eights**), add unpurchased dice, permit Incapacitated actions, or prevent **Parry/Counterspell** from canceling a roll.

Combine flat penalties, then apply the percentage Health Penalty. The **two-dice floor comes last**. Fear does not reduce Damage Dice, MR-based Initiative dice, or Keep.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Example

Someone with **Vitality 2** has the following Health Levels. Each normal Health Bar has:

<strong>Vitality × 4 = 8 boxes</strong>

---
:
[ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ]  -  Healthy [Vitality × 4] (No penalty)

--- 
:
[ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ]  - Bruised [Vitality × 4] (−10% dice pool)

---
:
[ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ]  - Injured [Vitality × 4] (−20% dice pool)

---
:
[ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ]  - Wounded [Vitality × 4] (−40% dice pool)

---
:
[ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ] [ ]  - Broken [Vitality × 4] (−50% dice pool)

---
:
[ ] - Incapacitated

---

#### Pool Reduction Example

A character has **Agility 16**, **Mastery Rank 3**, and is making a **Stealth** check using a learned Stealth Specialization. There is no Skill-based dice-pool threshold; the character is affected by **Weaken(4)** and is currently **Injured**.

1. Attribute Pool = 16  
2. Skill Checks use the full Attribute Pool = 16 (no Skill Rating threshold)  
3. Weaken(4) removes 4 dice → 12 dice remain  
4. Injured Health Penalty removes 20% of 12 → lose 2 dice  
5. Final Pool = **10k3**

If the same character were **Wounded**, the 40% Health Penalty would remove 4 dice from the post-Weaken pool of 12.

Final Pool = **8k3**

---

### Healing and Scarred-Bar Restoration

Healing has two distinct jobs: **restore HP in the current active Health Bar** or **return a lost Scarred Health Bar to full strength**. Each job has its own source and resource.

- **HP Healing:** **Resolve Stone Ability: Healing** heals Self or one ally with **4d8 / 8d8 / 12d8 / 16d8** at Ranks 1-4, within **2 / 4 / 8 / 16 m**. **Damage Transfer** heals its user by actual HP removed from an enemy. End-of-combat and rest recovery refill HP at their stated times.
- **Current Bar:** Ordinary HP Healing fills only an **already available** active Health Bar up to its maximum. A completely Scarred, **Incapacitated** creature has **no** active Bar: ordinary HP Healing (including external Resolve Stone Healing) cannot reopen Broken or end Incapacitated.
- **Restoring Scarred Bars:** **Restoration Actives**, **Passive: Regeneration** (Self), **Vitality: Remove Scar**, and specifically permitted natural, medical-aftercare or Artifact recovery return Scarred Bars **unscarred and at full current HP**. Each source follows its own stated cost and timing. Partially damaged, already available Bars retain their existing HP; damage stays on the Bar where it occurred. Medicine can accelerate eligible **rest**, not reopen a Bar instantly.
- **Recovery order:** Recover the lowest eligible Scarred Bar first, progressing toward Healthy. Recover **Broken** first when an Incapacitated character needs an available Bar. A specific recovery rule, such as a hidden Growth Bar rule, may specify a different eligible choice. Recalculate the highest available Bar and all applicable Health penalties after recovery.
- **Resource cycles:** Each Restoration Power has one finite pool per **Safe Haven Rest**; several uses draw from that same pool. **Regeneration** spends its own Self-only allowance per **Tyhran Day**. A Safe Haven Rest naturally returns **one Scarred Bar**, plus up to **one or two extra** if qualified Medicine 4/8 aftercare applies. It spends no Restoration Points.

Restoration works with whole Bars and their full HP capacity; Resolve Healing works with rolled HP and the current Bar. Consult *Actives* and *Passives* for the level tables, legal targets and action timing.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Incapacitation & Death

**Incapacitated is defeat in combat, not automatic death.** When all available Health Bars are Scarred, the creature falls **Prone** and unconscious. It cannot act, move, spend Stones, or deliberately activate Powers, Reactions or other abilities. Existing effects expire or persist according to their printed rules. No additional negative HP is tracked. There are **no Death Checks, Death Marks, success counters or death countdown**. More hits against an Incapacitated target cannot Scar an imaginary extra Bar and do not, by themselves, trigger an automatic death rule. However, the body remains vulnerable: further harm may worsen injuries, require extended recovery or specialized care before rest can help, or cause a genuinely fatal outcome.

**Death follows the fiction.** Execution, being devoured, destruction of the body, or an unresolved lethal situation can kill a helpless creature. The GM determines fatal outcomes from what actually happens in the scene, not from failed Death Saves or a fixed damage threshold. Where reasonably possible, make an avoidable lethal threat clear before it becomes irreversible. Incapacitated never grants immunity to death.

### Returning from Incapacitated

- **External Scarred-Bar Restoration:** A legal Restoration Active or other explicit eligible Scarred-Bar recovery can restore **Broken** at full HP. Once an available Bar has positive HP, the creature regains consciousness; it is still Prone until it can stand normally.
- **Ordinary HP Healing cannot do this.** Resolve Stone Healing and Damage Transfer restore HP only inside an **available** Bar. They never reopen a Scarred Bar. An Incapacitated creature cannot spend its own Stones; even an ally's Resolve Healing cannot create the missing Bar.
- **Natural recovery:** A completed eligible **Day of Rest** or **Safe Haven Rest** can restore a Scarred Bar and thereby end Incapacitated. Qualified **Medicine 4/8 aftercare** can accelerate this recovery during a Safe Haven Rest. Recovery requires the safety and care appropriate to the injury.
- **Self-only recovery:** Passive: Regeneration and Vitality: Remove Scar cannot be activated by an unconscious creature. Already active effects follow their ordinary rules.

### Stabilized

Successful emergency Medicine or an explicitly legal stabilization effect controls a **medically treatable immediate danger**, such as an uncontrolled wound. The patient becomes **Stable** but remains Incapacitated, unconscious and without available Health Bars. Stabilization restores **no HP**, opens **no Scarred Bar**, and prevents neither renewed injury nor an otherwise genuinely fatal event. It is not a required step before legal Restoration or natural recovery.

### Death and What Comes After

In **Destroyed Faith**, the death of a player character should carry weight in the story. While dice determine the outcome of combat, they should not alone decide a character's ultimate fate. Death should depend on the circumstances, the nature and intentions of the enemy, and the story unfolding at the table.

**Defeat does not necessarily mean death.** An enemy may choose to take prisoners, leave fallen opponents on the battlefield, or spare them for reasons of their own. These are entirely valid outcomes when they fit the situation and remain at the Game Master's discretion.

However, some situations leave no room for survival. A merciless enemy may finish off a fallen character, or a plunge into a bottomless abyss may offer no possibility of escape. When death is the inevitable consequence, **the character dies.**

What happens afterward, whether the character's story truly ends, and how the player continues are matters for the Game Master. Further guidance and possibilities are explored in the **Game Master's Guide / Architect's Guide**, rather than through additional death or reincarnation mechanics in the Core Rules.

*After all, in Destroyed Faith, death is not always quite what it seems.*

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Healing & Assistance

#### Emergency Medicine — Under Pressure

An adjacent, conscious helper may spend **1 Attack Action** to stabilize an **Incapacitated** creature. Use an appropriate Medicine Specialization and the normal Skill Check: commit Paid Raises before rolling and allocate any successful Raise effects afterward, using the existing action economy.

**Emergency Medicine Base Task Level = the patient's MR.**

**Base TN = 4 × patient's MR**, not the healer's MR.

| Patient MR | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 | 6 | 7 | 8 |
|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Base TN** | **4** | **8** | **12** | **16** | **20** | **24** | **28** | **32** |

Only declared **Paid Raises** add **+4 TN each**; the 1/2/3 **Free Raises** from Medicine Ranks 4/6/8 add no TN. Declare only the number of Paid Raises before rolling; allocate Paid and eligible Free Raise effects after a successful check, within the Skill Rank cap. Success controls the treatable immediate danger and makes the patient **Stable**, without restoring HP or Scarred Bars. Failure means the treatment does not stabilize the patient; there are no Death Marks or automatic death countdown. Obvious basic first aid may be attempted without training using normal **Untrained Disadvantage** and no Raises; genuinely specialist procedures require relevant training.

#### Medical Aftercare — Safe Haven Rest

When a patient completes a **Safe Haven Rest** under competent ongoing care, the assisting healer's Medicine Rank increases that patient's **natural** Scarred-Bar recovery:

| Healer's Medicine Rank | Extra Bars | Total Bars recovered at this Safe Haven Rest |
|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **0–3** | **0** | **1** |
| **4–7** | **+1** | **2** |
| **8** | **+2** | **3** |

This benefit requires safe conditions, appropriate supplies, and a conscious healer able to tend the patient through the rest. **No check is needed** for familiar aftercare under those conditions. One healer may provide this intensive benefit to **one patient** per Safe Haven Rest (including themself if conscious). Multiple healers never stack aftercare on one patient. Recover Broken first if the patient is Incapacitated; the patient awakens once a Bar is available. No Bar may be recovered twice, and the benefit does **not** apply to a mere Night Rest or Day of Rest. Treatment does not spend Stones or Restoration Points and is **not** instant in-combat Restoration.

---
:

### Falling Damage

A creature takes **1d8 Damage for every full 4 m fallen**.

| Distance | Damage |
| -------- | -----: |
| 0–3 m    |      — |
| 4–7 m    |    1d8 |
| 8–11 m   |    2d8 |
| 12–15 m  |    3d8 |
| 16–19 m  |    4d8 |

Continue this progression for greater distances.

**Falling Damage ignores Armor.**

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

### Fear
---

**Fear** is an accumulating value that starts at **0**. When an event or the GM calls for a Fear Check, each selected character rolls their **Resolve Pool, keeping one die** (`Resolve k1`). Use the same fixed result bands for ordinary, extreme and supernatural events. The GM chooses who is affected; the band is determined by the roll rather than Challenge MR or an adjustable Difficulty.

| **Resolve k1 result** | **Fear gained** |
|:--|:--:|
| **0-7** | **+2 Fear** |
| **8-15** | **+1 Fear** |
| **16 or higher** | **+0 Fear** |

**Applying Fear.** Add Fear to the current total. Each point removes **one die from Attribute-based Pools**, including Martial Attack, Spell Casting, Skill, Attribute, Ritual and later Fear Checks. Apply it **before percentage Health Penalties**, but **never remove the last two rolled dice** of a normal Pool that began with 2+ dice. Fear Checks still keep only **one die** (`Resolve k1`). The Fear spiral is intentional; further Fear can still hinder other higher Pools. Keep, Damage Dice, and MR-based Initiative Dice never change.

**Recovery.** Fear continues across scenes and combats. A **Safe Haven Rest** returns Fear to **0**. Healing, Cleanse, Night Rest and Day of Rest resolve their own listed effects, leaving the Fear total in place. **Check procedure.** A Fear Check uses Resolve k1 and the fixed table. Specializations and Skill Raises are not part of this check; existing Mental Restrictions apply according to their own rules. **GM caution:** Fear is intentionally cumulative and self-reinforcing. Trigger it sparingly, especially against low-Resolve groups: multiple checks in one scene can quickly impair the entire party. Encounter pacing guidance belongs in the Game Master's Guide.

::

#### Fear Check Examples

**Example — Sudden horror:** A character rolls `Resolve k1` and keeps a **7**. The result is below 8, so they gain **2 Fear**. **Example — Unsettling revelation:** Another character rolls `Resolve k1` and keeps **15**. The result falls within 8–15, so they gain **1 Fear**. **Example — Holding steady:** A third character keeps **16** (or more) and gains **0 Fear**. The same bands apply whether the event was ordinary or supernatural; the GM does not select an alternate Fear TN.

**Boundary results:** **7 → +2**, **8 → +1**, **15 → +1**, **16 → +0**. **Accumulation:** If a character begins with Fear 2 and rolls **8**, they gain 1 Fear for a total of 3. Resolve 8 then rolls 5 dice before any Health Penalty. A character with Resolve 2 still rolls **2k1** on Fear Checks despite any Fear penalty; those two dice do not protect other characteristics from a reduction down to their own two-dice floor. Fear remains until a **Safe Haven Rest** or an explicit removal rule.

---

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Combat Maneuvers

Combat Maneuvers are the universal combat options available to **all player characters and NPCs**. They do not need to be learned, purchased, or gained through a Power.

Every combatant knows how to:

* move,
* make a Basic Attack,
* and use Basic Reactions.

Powers expand, improve, or replace these basic options, but they are never required for a creature to participate effectively in combat. This means that even if a character has no suitable Power available, has already used their Powers, or has not purchased additional combat Powers, they still have meaningful Movement, Attack, and Reaction options.

Every character normally has:

* **1 Movement**
* **1 Attack Action**
* **1 Reaction**

per round, unless another rule grants additional uses. Additional Movements, Attack Actions, and Reactions may be gained through Stones, Powers, or other explicit effects. Basic Combat Maneuvers are **not Powers**. They do not become spent after use. If a character gains additional uses of the appropriate action type, the same Basic Combat Maneuver may be used again.

---
:
### Basic Attack

Every character can make a normal attack without using an Active Power. This is called a **Basic Attack**.

### Damage

A Basic Attack deals:

**Weapon Damage + MR × 2d8 Damage**

Apply any eligible Passive, Active Buff, Artifact, weapon, or other effects that normally modify the attack or its damage.

### Basic Attack Rules

**No Power Required:**
A Basic Attack does not require an Active Power.

**No Power Effects:**
You may not add damage, Specials, AoE, Split Attack, Autofire, control effects, or other effects belonging to an Active Power unless another rule explicitly allows it.

**Weapon Properties Apply:**
Normal properties and effects belonging to the weapon itself still apply when appropriate.

**Extra Attacks:**
If you gain additional Attack Actions through Stones, Powers, or another effect, you may use them to make additional Basic Attacks. A Basic Attack is not a Power and does not become spent after use.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Threatened Ranged Attacks

When you declare a Ranged Attack or Ranged Power using a bow, crossbow, thrown weapon, or similar ranged weapon **while you are within an enemy's Melee Reach**, the attack is **Threatened**. Make this Ranged Attack with **Disadvantage**. **Threatened Spellcasting:** Declare a legal Spell Casting Roll while within an enemy's **Melee Reach**: the Casting Roll has **Disadvantage**. This applies regardless of the Spell's effect or eligible delivery: damage, healing (including self-healing), Cleanse, Restoration, support, control, AoE, persistent zones, and any otherwise permitted melee- or touch-range Spell. The restriction applies to the **Casting Roll**, not to subsequent Damage or Healing Dice. It does not forbid casting, alter Spell TNs, or permit otherwise illegal Spell delivery.

Determine the threat when the Attack or Spell is **declared**, using the enemy's actual Melee Reach. Several threatening enemies still impose **one Disadvantage**, not additional stacking penalties. A persistent Spell effect does not make a new Casting Roll or receive a new threatened check merely because it ticks or affects a new creature. After declaration, each enemy **whose Melee Reach includes you** may use an available **legal Reaction** if its printed trigger and other requirements are satisfied. Threatened status creates no new Reaction type, waives no requirements, and does not change the normal rules for Reactions on Reactions. Normal Melee Martial Attacks do not gain this Disadvantage merely for being within reach.

---
:
### Movement

Every character can move without purchasing a Movement Power. Your normal Movement represents basic tactical positioning during your Turn.

#### Threat Zone Movement Triggers

A creature crossing the boundary of an enemy's Threat Zone triggers that enemy's eligible movement Reaction **once per crossing**, whether entering or leaving. Resolve an entry trigger after the step into the Zone, and an exit trigger immediately before the step out. The moving creature is inside the Zone when the Reaction resolves. **Safe Movement and Disengage** suppress movement-triggered Reactions for their protected movement.

#### Normal Movement

**Move Distance:**
Move up to your Speed in meters.

**Draw / Sheathe:**
You may draw or sheathe a weapon as part of your Movement.

**No Splitting:**
If you use a Movement Maneuver or Movement Power that replaces your Movement, it uses that entire Movement and cannot be split unless a rule explicitly says otherwise. Movement Powers provide additional or superior movement options such as Teleport, Flight, Leap, Safe Movement, or other specialized movement. They are not required to move normally.

---
:

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Basic Movement Options

Choose one option whenever you use a normal Movement, unless another rule says otherwise.

#### Move

Move up to your Speed.

This movement provokes movement-triggered Reactions normally.

#### Dash

Focus entirely on movement. Move up to **double your normal Speed**. This requires an **available base Attack Action** to forfeit for this Turn. If it was already spent or committed to an Active Buff during Preparation, you **cannot Dash**. Legal additional Attack Actions remain usable, but cannot pay this base-Action forfeit. This movement provokes movement-triggered Reactions normally.

#### Disengage

Withdraw carefully.

Move up to your normal Speed without provoking movement-triggered Reactions. This requires an **available base Attack Action** to forfeit for this Turn. If it was already spent or committed to an Active Buff during Preparation, you **cannot Disengage**. Legal additional Attack Actions remain usable, but cannot pay this base-Action forfeit.

#### Quick Load

Convert Movement into reloading. Instead of moving, spend your Movement to perform **Reload (1)**. If you gain additional Movements, you may use Quick Load multiple times during the same Turn, up to a maximum total Reload equal to your **Mastery Rank**. You still need one free hand and must be able to manipulate the weapon or ammunition. You cannot use Quick Load while you cannot legally spend **Movement**.

#### Stand Up

Recover from Prone by spending **1 Attack Action**. Until you stand, your normal Evade and passive defenses apply, but you cannot move, attack, cast or use Reactions. After standing, your Movement and other available actions function normally. Standing up itself does not provoke Reactions. Movement taken afterward may provoke normally.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

### Basic Attack Actions

Every character can use Basic Attack Actions without purchasing an Active Power. Using a Basic Attack Action spends **1 Attack Action**. Basic Attack Actions are not Powers and do not become spent after use.

---

#### Basic Attack

**Requirement:** A Weapon or Natural Weapon capable of making the Attack.

**Effect:** Make a normal Attack.

On hit, deal:

**Weapon Damage + MR × 2d8 Damage**

Eligible Passive, Active Buff, Artifact, weapon, and other effects apply normally. A Basic Attack cannot add damage, Specials, AoE, Split Attack, Autofire, control effects, or other effects belonging to an Active Power unless another rule explicitly allows it. If you gain additional Attack Actions, you may use them to make additional Basic Attacks.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Basic Reactions

Every character can use Basic Reactions without purchasing a Reaction Power. Using a Basic Reaction spends **1 Reaction**. Basic Reactions are not Powers and do not become spent after use. If a character gains additional Reactions through Stones, Powers, or another effect, they may use Basic Reactions again whenever their Trigger occurs. Reaction Powers provide stronger, more specialized, or more tactical responses, but they are not required to react in combat.

---

#### Guard

**Trigger:** You are hit by an Attack or would take damage.

**Effect:** Gain **+MR × 2 Armor** against the triggering Attack or damage instance. This Armor is added to your existing Armor and applies only against the triggering event.

---

#### Evade

**Trigger:** An Attack is declared against you, before its Attack Roll or Casting Roll.

**Effect:** Gain **+MR × 2 Evade** against the triggering Attack. This Evade is added to your existing Evade and applies only against the triggering Attack.

---

#### Dive for Cover

**Trigger:** You would be hit by an AoE Attack after its shared Attack Roll or Casting Roll has been compared to your defense, but before its damage or payload is applied.

**Effect:** Immediately move up to **2 × Mastery Rank meters**. If this movement takes you completely outside the AoE, you are not affected by that AoE. If you remain inside the area, you are affected normally. This movement does not provoke movement-triggered Reactions. Dive for Cover is available to any creature that would be hit by the AoE, including a creature standing at its center point. If you already spent your Reaction on **Reaction: Evade**, you need another available Reaction to also use Dive for Cover.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Escape Rule: Flee

To use **Flee**, spend **1 Movement and 1 Attack Action** on your Turn. Both must still be available when you declare Flee. Move up to **4 × your normal Speed** directly away from danger as that Flee action.

Until the start of your next Turn:

* you cannot make Attacks,
* you cannot use Reactions,
* and you cannot spend Stones.

This movement provokes movement-triggered Reactions normally.

---
:
### Pursuit

After **Flee**, resolve pursuit through **actual distance, Speed, movement modes, terrain, obstacles and legal Movement Powers**. When those facts determine escape or capture, describe the outcome directly. **Flee** retains its normal Action cost, distance and movement-triggered Reactions; further movement uses the character's existing options.

**When the outcome is uncertain**, resolve one meaningful chase situation with the existing rules:

- **Skill Opposition** when a plausible learned technique matters: Athletics through a crowded street, Acrobatics over a rooftop, Stealth to disappear, or Tracking to follow. Each participant describes an approach; the **GM assigns the fitting Attribute, applicable Specialization** (or Untrained), and the maneuver's **Task Level × 4** Base TN. Only a successful setup result becomes the responder's TN. Declare the number of **Paid Raises** before rolling; allocate their effects plus any Free Raises after success.
- **Opposed Attribute Contest** if this is solely a direct test of speed, strength, or endurance with no meaningful trained technique: both use their relevant Attribute Pools and normal Keep, and the higher result wins.

A successful escape attempt may put the fugitive out of reach, behind cover, or out of sight; a successful pursuit may close the distance, reveal a hiding place, or enable a catch. **The established positions and actual movement capabilities determine what is possible**, not a score threshold. Continue with another check only when a **new obstacle, tactic, or significant change** creates a new decision; do not repeat rolls for identical circumstances.

**Example:** A courier darts into a busy market using Acrobatics (Agility). The GM sets Task Level 3 / **TN 12** for negotiating the stalls. The courier rolls 19, creating **Opposition TN 19**. A pursuing guard rolls Athletics (Agility) and gets 17; the courier gains separation and can disappear in the crowd if no observer has a clear view. The roll does not override an obviously faster pursuer in an open, unobstructed street.

---
:
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Healing Sources at a Glance

| **Source** | **Recovery** | **Resource** |
|:--|:--|:--|
| **Resolve: Healing** | Rolled HP in an **already available** active Bar; never reopens a Scarred Bar | Prepaid Stone application, used during the current Round |
| **Damage Transfer** | HP equal to real HP lost by its target | Its successful damage instance |
| **Restoration Actives** | Fully refilled Scarred Bars on eligible recipients | Power-specific pool per Safe Haven Rest |
| **Passive: Regeneration** | Fully refilled Scarred Bars on Self | Limited pool per Tyhran Day |
| **Vitality: Remove Scar** | Fully refilled Scarred Bars on Self | Sealed Vitality Stones, Rank limits |
| **Natural / Safe Haven Recovery** | Printed active-Bar HP and Scarred-Bar recovery | Time and rest conditions |
| **Medicine 4/8 Safe Haven Aftercare** | **+1 / +2** extra naturally recovered Scarred Bars for one cared-for patient | One qualified healer, one patient, one completed Safe Haven Rest |

---



### Rest & Recovery

The Mastery System is balanced around **combat rounds** as the main resource economy. Outside of combat, recovery is **slow, deliberate, and story-driven**.

---
#### End of Combat

When combat ends, all remaining Specials end.

---

#### How Recovery Works

- **Bars and Scars:** Normal Health Bars progress through Healthy, Bruised, Injured, Wounded and Broken; a depleted Bar becomes **Scarred**. Scarred-Bar recovery makes that Bar available again at **full HP**.
- **Ordinary HP:** **Resolve Healing** rolls 4/8/12/16d8 according to Rank. **Damage Transfer** provides its dedicated damage-linked self-heal. These effects need an already available active Bar and cannot reopen a Scarred Bar. Combat-end and rest effects refill the available active Bar at the times stated below.
- **Restoration Actives:** Each Power has a finite pool refreshed once per **Safe Haven Rest**; spend **1 point per Scarred Bar restored to full HP**. Single Target Restoration restores Bars on one eligible recipient; AoE Restoration divides **one shared pool** among selected eligible recipients. The applicable delivery, action cost, Casting Roll and once-per-Round power-use rules govern each activation.
- **Regeneration:** This Self-only Passive supplies **floor(PL / 2)** Scarred-Bar points per **Tyhran Day** (first available at PL 2). The character consciously activates it at a legal action boundary, spending no Attack Action, Reaction or Casting Roll. A capable character may distribute uses throughout the day. An Incapacitated character receives recovery from an external legal source.
- **Other Scar recovery:** **Vitality: Remove Scar** and printed Natural, Ritual and Artifact recovery preserve their own costs and limits and restore the selected eligible Bar at full HP.
- **Safe Haven Rest:** The rest naturally restores **one** Scarred Bar, **plus +1 with Medicine 4–7 aftercare or +2 with Medicine 8 aftercare** for that patient. It refills the current active Bar, clears Fear and renews resources with a Safe-Haven cycle. Renewing a Restoration pool makes its points available for later deliberate use; the rest itself spends none of them.
- **Prepared Resolve Healing:** Pay the Stone cost during the Preparation Phase, then choose the legal target and trigger once later in the Round, including before incoming HP damage is applied. Its Healing Dice are rolled on triggering, optionally using **Maximization**. The pending application expires at Round End.

> *You can patch a wound, but you cannot undo a battle without time.*

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Stone Flow (Combat Economy)

- When you spend an Attribute or Colorless Stone, it becomes **Exhausted** unless a rule explicitly removes it.
- **End of each Round:** move a total of **MR eligible Stones** from Exhausted → Ready, chosen from Attribute Stones and Colorless Stones designated by **Influence: Colorless Recovery**. Unselected Colorless Stones cannot be recovered during combat.
- **End of Combat:** The current **available** Health Bar is restored to full HP **if one exists**. A fully Scarred, Incapacitated creature has no available Bar and does not awaken from this refill; Scarred Health Bars stay Scarred. All remaining Exhausted permanent Stones (Attribute or Colorless) return Ready; Initiative Colorless Stones and **all Absorption Colorless Stones (Ready, Exhausted, or pending payout)** disappear. Absorption trigger counts reset only when the next combat begins; no damage remainder is tracked. Sealed / Bound Stones are unaffected. Other granted Colorless Stones expire on their source's normal schedule.

---


### Rests

#### 1) Night Rest (1 Watch) — Anywhere
A Night Rest is **1 Watch (10 Tyhran Hours)** of downtime (sleep + light activity).

**Benefits**
- A Night Rest restores HP in your current **available** active Health Bar to full, **if one exists**. Scarred Bars retain their status; Night Rest alone restores no Scarred Bar and cannot wake an Incapacitated character.
- A Safe Haven Rest also refreshes rest-based resources and clears Fear, as detailed below.  

---

#### 2) Day of Rest (4 Watches / 40 Tyhran Hours) — Natural Recovery
Without magic, your body and spirit heal over **days**, not minutes.

- Each full **day of rest** restores **exactly 1 Scarred Health Bar**, which returns unscarred at full HP. An Incapacitated patient can benefit when safely cared for; an unresolved lethal injury or continuing danger may prevent valid rest until addressed.  
- Light activity (traveling, hunting, etc.) halves this rate (2 days for 1 Bar). A Day of Rest does not remove accumulated **Fear**.
- The same period of recovery cannot grant an additional separate Day of Rest Bar on top of a Safe Haven Rest's natural recovery benefit.  

---

#### 3) Safe Haven Rest
A Safe Haven Rest is a **Night Rest (1 Watch)** taken in a **Safe Haven** (secure + comfortable + uninterrupted).

**Benefits**
1) **Safe Haven Rest refresh:** restore resources that recharge **once per Safe Haven Rest**:
   - Any Stones **lost until Safe Haven Rest** return (e.g., Stones burned for Safe Haven Rest effects).
   - Ritual Stones that were **Sealed** become available again.
2) **Form Release:** Stones bound by **Stone-Bound Form** are released.
3) **Natural Recovery:** restore **1 Scarred Health Bar at full HP**, **plus 1** with continuous qualified **Medicine 4–7** aftercare, or **plus 2** with **Medicine 8** aftercare (maximum **3 Bars total**). The aftercare benefit is received once per patient per rest, cannot stack between healers, and fulfills natural recovery for that same rest period. Restoration Power pools refresh and become available for later deliberate use.
4) **Restores** your current active Health Bar to full (boxes only).
5) **Clears Fear:** all accumulated Fear is removed; set Fear to **0**.

**Limits**
- You can benefit from **one Safe Haven Rest per Tyhran Day (4 Watches / 40 Tyhran Hours)**.
- A Safe Haven is a **reward and a location**, not merely “we lay down somewhere”.

---
::
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Resources Recharged at a Safe Haven Rest

---

#### Rituals (Sealed Stones)
When you perform a Ritual, spent Stones are **Sealed** and cannot be recovered by MR. They return only when you complete a **Safe Haven Rest**.

---

#### Bound Stones (Do NOT return on rest)
Stones bound into **Artifacts, Familiars, or similar permanent bonds** remain bound until released or destroyed.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Progression (XP)

At the end of each session, the Game Master awards **Experience Points (XP)**. The recommended standard is **6–10 XP per session**, regardless of the characters' current Mastery Rank. **8 XP** is the midpoint used for the campaign-duration examples below.

Record the same award in two places:

- **Lifetime Experience** is the total XP your character has ever earned. It only increases and is never reduced by spending XP.
- **Banked XP** is the spendable currency used for Attributes, Skills, Powers, Artifacts, and other GM-approved progression options.

Players may save their XP and spend it later during downtime or another GM-approved Upgrade Step. Spending Banked XP never reduces Lifetime Experience. Every character begins with **2 permanent Stones** and earns **1 additional permanent Stone every 20 Lifetime XP**, independently of how XP is spent.

---

### Recommended XP Awards

**6–10 XP per session is the recommended range for Destroyed Faith; 8 XP is a useful middle value, not a mandatory fixed award.**

Character advancement should be enjoyable in itself. Spending XP, developing a build, and watching a character grow are part of the experience. Progression should feel rewarding without requiring characters to reach the highest Mastery Ranks. The GM may award more or fewer XP when appropriate, but **there is no requirement to increase XP rewards as Mastery Rank rises**.

For standard group play, award the same session XP to all player characters so their Lifetime Experience, permanent Stone progression, and Mastery Rank remain synchronized.

**Optional accelerated campaigns:** If reaching MR7 or MR8 is an intended campaign goal, the GM may increase the usual session award by **2 XP for each higher MR**, beginning at MR5 or MR6. Using 8 XP as the starting midpoint, advancement to MR8 takes approximately **188 sessions** when acceleration begins at MR5, or **213 sessions** when it begins at MR6, instead of **250 sessions** without acceleration. For ordinary long-running campaigns, keep the 6–10 XP range; MR6 is already a substantial long-term destination.

---

### Upgrade Steps

XP is spent during an **Upgrade Step**. An Upgrade Step usually happens during downtime, after a session, during a Safe Haven Rest, or whenever the GM allows character progression. There is **no limit on how many times an individual Attribute, Specialization, Power, or Artifact may be increased during a single Upgrade Step**. Characters may purchase multiple consecutive increases in the same value, provided they have sufficient Banked XP and meet all applicable requirements.

The following principles apply:

- Every purchased increase must be paid for at its normal XP cost.
- When purchasing multiple levels, the XP costs of all intermediate levels are added together.
- Attribute, Skill, Power Level, Artifact Level, and Mastery Rank restrictions remain in effect.
- Purchasing multiple levels does not bypass prerequisites or grant additional free abilities.
- The GM determines when an Upgrade Step is available.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Example: Legal Upgrade Step

A character spends saved XP during downtime and chooses:

- **Might** +1
- **Agility** +2
- **Climbing** +1 Skill Rank
- **Tracking** +2 Skill Ranks
- **Main Attack Power** +1 Level
- **Armor Artifact** +2 Levels

All these purchases are legal during the same Upgrade Step, provided the character can pay their complete costs and does not exceed the applicable limits. For example, increasing an Artifact from Level 2 to Level 4 costs **24 XP**: 8 XP for Level 3 and 16 XP for Level 4. There is no additional per-Upgrade-Step restriction.

---


### Attribute and Specialization Costs

Attributes retain the compressed **1–40** scale. Specializations use **Skill Ranks 1–8**. Buying a new Specialization at Rank 1, or raising an existing one by one Rank, costs **4 XP**. Multiple consecutive Skill Ranks may be purchased during the same Upgrade Step. The complete Rank 0→8 progression costs **32 XP**.

| **New Attribute Value** | **Attribute Cost / +1** |
|:--:|:--:|
| **1–4** | **2 XP** |
| **5–8** | **4 XP** |
| **9–12** | **6 XP** |
| **13–16** | **8 XP** |
| **17–20** | **10 XP** |
| **21–24** | **12 XP** |
| **25–28** | **14 XP** |
| **29–32** | **16 XP** |
| **33–36** | **18 XP** |
| **37–40** | **20 XP** |

Attributes increase the number of dice. Specialization Ranks broaden trained tasks and allow up to Rank predeclared total Raises; the Raises unlocked at Ranks **4, 6, and 8 are Free** (+0 TN) instead of Paid (+4 TN). 

*Example:* Blacksmithing Rank 4→6 costs **8 XP**. A new Alchemy Rank 1 costs **4 XP**. Intellect 8→10 costs **12 XP**.

---


No Attribute can normally exceed **40**. The cap limits dice-pool growth only; it does **not** determine Stone assignment. Stones follow Lifetime Experience and the **Mastery Rank × 2** per-Attribute concentration limit.

---

### Lifetime Experience & Stone Progression


Every character begins with **2 permanent Attribute Stones** and gains **1 additional permanent Stone every 20 Lifetime XP**. Spending XP never lowers Lifetime Experience or removes earned Stone progression. At an Upgrade Step, assign any unassigned permanent Stones earned after character creation to Attributes, up to **MR × 2 Stones per Attribute**. Alternatively, each newly earned Stone may be chosen as **1 Permanent Colorless Stone at a 1:1 ratio**. Permanent Colorless Stones have no separate Mastery Rank cap. They are not assigned to an Attribute and do not count against Attribute Stone limits. Only Attribute Stones unlock Attribute Stone Abilities.

The two starting Stones must remain Attribute Stones.

Stone progression and Mastery Rank progression both use **Lifetime XP**, but they are independent tracks:

- Every **20 Lifetime XP** grants **1 permanent Stone**.
- Specific Lifetime XP milestones increase **Mastery Rank**.
- Spending Banked XP changes neither progression track.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Core Lifetime XP Milestones

| **Lifetime XP** | **Permanent Stones Earned** | **Mastery Rank** |
|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **0 / Start** | **2** | **MR2** |
| **100** | **7** | **MR3** |
| **250** | **14** | **MR4** |
| **500** | **27** | **MR5** |
| **900** | **47** | **MR6** |
| **1,400** | **72** | **MR7** |
| **2,000** | **102** | **MR8** |
| **2,200** | **112** | **Stone Cap** |

Between these milestones, characters continue earning one permanent Stone every 20 Lifetime XP.

The character sheet should display the progression through **2,200 Lifetime XP**, with visible Stone ticks every 20 XP and Mastery Rank milestones at **100 / 250 / 500 / 900 / 1,400 / 2,000 XP**.

**MR 1–8 allow 2 / 4 / 6 / 8 / 10 / 12 / 14 / 16 assigned Stones per Attribute.**

At **2,200 Lifetime XP**, normal permanent Stone progression reaches its maximum of **112 earned permanent Stones**. No additional permanent Stones are earned beyond this point unless a rule explicitly extends progression.

This cap does not prevent further XP awards or the spending of Banked XP on otherwise legal upgrades.

Initiative Colorless Stones and other source-granted temporary Stones never increase Lifetime XP, never grant permanent Stone progression, and never affect Mastery Rank.

---

### How Long Do Real Campaigns Last?

The Lifetime XP milestones are designed with actual campaign play in mind.

A highly active group might meet once every week, completing approximately **52 sessions per year**. Many established groups play only twice per month, while others meet once a month or less frequently.

At the **8 XP midpoint** of the recommended 6–10 XP range, the approximate time required to reach each Mastery Rank is:

| **Mastery Rank** | **Sessions** | **Weekly Play** | **Twice Monthly** | **Monthly Play** |
|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **MR3** | 13 | 0.25 years | 0.54 years | 1.08 years |
| **MR4** | 32 | 0.62 years | 1.33 years | 2.67 years |
| **MR5** | 63 | 1.21 years | 2.63 years | 5.25 years |
| **MR6** | 113 | 2.17 years | 4.71 years | 9.42 years |
| **MR7** | 175 | 3.37 years | 7.29 years | 14.58 years |
| **MR8** | 250 | 4.81 years | 10.42 years | 20.83 years |

*Calculations assume a constant 8 XP per session, 52 weekly sessions, 24 twice-monthly sessions, or 12 monthly sessions per year, without cancellations or interruptions. Individual awards of 6–10 XP and changes in the award will change these durations.*

These numbers demonstrate an important design principle:

**MR8 is not the expected destination of an ordinary Destroyed Faith campaign.**

Most campaigns will spend the majority of their time between MR2 and MR5. Reaching **MR6** already represents a tremendous journey and is a meaningful long-term objective. Even a group meeting every single week requires more than two years to reach MR6 at the 8 XP midpoint. A group playing twice per month requires almost five years.

**MR6 is a perfectly reasonable long-term goal for a complete campaign.** MR7 and MR8 exist for groups that deliberately wish to explore mythic or divine levels of play.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
The GM remains free to accelerate advancement when the campaign calls for it. However, increasing XP rewards should be a conscious choice about the campaign's desired length and scope, rather than an automatic consequence of reaching a higher Mastery Rank.

---

## Mastery Rank Progression

Mastery Rank reflects how far a character has progressed through their lifetime journey and how much power they can safely express, sustain, and control. Characters normally begin at **Mastery Rank 2**, representing a trained adventurer or awakened Spark. For standard player progression, Lifetime Experience directly determines Mastery Rank. Mastery Rank is not purchased, is not calculated from assigned Stones, and is not reduced when XP is spent.

### Mastery Progression

| **Lifetime XP** | **Mastery Rank** | **Tier Description** |
|:--:|:--:|:--|
| **0–99** | **MR2 – Adept** | Awakened mortal; trained adventurer. |
| **100–249** | **MR3 – Expert** | Seasoned Spark; tactical combat options become reliable. |
| **250–499** | **MR4 – Master** | Hero-tier; multiple systems begin to work together. |
| **500–899** | **MR5 – Grandmaster** | Epic champion; exceptional mortal or supernatural elite. |
| **900–1,399** | **MR6 – Legend** | Demon Prince ceiling; world-threatening legendary beings. |
| **1,400–1,999** | **MR7 – Mythic** | Titans, demigods, archangels, divine remnants, and ancient horrors. |
| **2,000+** | **MR8 – God** | True gods and cosmic entities. The highest possible Mastery Rank. |

#### Divine Power and MR8

MR8 represents the highest level of Mastery and is not expected as part of normal campaign advancement. It exists for divine beings, cosmic entities, extraordinary ascensions, and campaigns that intentionally explore power beyond the conventional mortal and legendary scale. Not every MR8 being is necessarily equal. Lesser Gods, True Gods, High Gods, and Apex Gods may differ greatly in their nature, influence, and abilities. Such distinctions do not automatically create additional Mastery Ranks or permanent Stones.

Normal permanent Stone progression ends at **2,200 Lifetime XP**, with a total of **112 earned permanent Stones**. Reaching MR8 is not a requirement for completing a campaign, nor is it intended to be the ultimate objective of every player character.

*The purpose of progression is to support the story being played, not to force every story toward godhood.*

---

#### Power Advancement

A Power may increase by multiple consecutive Power Levels during the same Upgrade Step.

The character must pay the normal XP cost for **every intermediate Power Level** and must respect the maximum Power Level permitted by their Mastery Rank and any other applicable restrictions.

For example, increasing a Power from PL4 to PL6 costs **22 XP**: 10 XP for PL5 and 12 XP for PL6.

#### Artifact Advancement

An Artifact may increase by multiple consecutive Artifact Levels during the same Upgrade Step.

Each acquired Level must be paid for separately, using its normal advancement cost. The Artifact Level cap imposed by Mastery Rank, as well as all Artifact-specific requirements, remains in effect.

For example, increasing an Artifact from Level 2 to Level 4 costs **24 XP**: 8 XP for Level 3 and 16 XP for Level 4.

There is no additional per-Upgrade-Step limitation.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Power Costs

Power Level cost equals twice the new Power Level.

| **New Power Level** | **XP Cost** |
|:--:|:--:|
| **1** | **2 XP** |
| **2** | **4 XP** |
| **3** | **6 XP** |
| **4** | **8 XP** |
| **5** | **10 XP** |
| **6** | **12 XP** |
| **7** | **14 XP** |
| **8** | **16 XP** |
| **9** | **18 XP** |
| **10** | **20 XP** |
| **11** | **22 XP** |
| **12** | **24 XP** |
| **13** | **26 XP** |
| **14** | **28 XP** |
| **15** | **30 XP** |
| **16** | **32 XP** |

A Power may advance by multiple consecutive Levels during the same Upgrade Step. Pay the listed XP cost for each new Level reached and respect the normal Power Level cap and other prerequisites.

---

### Power Level Cap by Mastery Rank

| **Mastery Rank** | **Maximum Power Level** |
|:--:|:--:|
| **MR 1** | **Level 2** |
| **MR 2** | **Level 4** |
| **MR 3** | **Level 6** |
| **MR 4** | **Level 8** |
| **MR 5** | **Level 10** |
| **MR 6** | **Level 12** |
| **MR 7** | **Level 14** |
| **MR 8** | **Level 16** |

> **Maximum Power Level = Mastery Rank × 2**

**Artifact-granted Power exception:** The MR-based Power Level cap applies to Powers the character learns, buys, or independently upgrades. A bound Artifact (including an Echo Artifact) may grant a **fixed printed Power profile above MR × 2** when its own Artifact Level legally unlocks that profile. Use the granted profile at its printed PL; do not downgrade it to the wielder's regular PL cap. The Artifact Level cap by MR, equipment slots, Artifact Capacity, and the Power's other printed restrictions still apply. This exception does **not** raise the PL of a separately purchased Power or undo a specific modifier's stated PL cap.

**Character-creation exception:** an MR 1 character receives the standard starting Passive, Reaction, and Active Buff at PL 4. Other MR 1 Powers and subsequent advancement obey the PL 2 cap. This keeps early characters from rushing directly into high-tier Powers while still allowing strong specialization over time.

---

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Artifact Costs

| **Artifact Advancement** | **XP Cost** |
|:--|:--:|
| Bind and awaken a new Artifact at Level 1 | **Free** |
| Increase an Artifact to Level 2 or 3 | **8 XP per Level** |
| Increase an Artifact to Level 4, 5, or 6 | **16 XP per Level** |
| Increase an Artifact to Level 7, 8, or 9 | **32 XP per Level** |
| Increase an Artifact to Level 10 | **64 XP** |

An Artifact may advance by multiple consecutive Levels during the same Upgrade Step. Pay the XP cost of **every new Artifact Level reached**, not just the final Level. Artifact Level remains limited by the character's **Mastery Rank** as described in the Artifact rules.

---
::::::


}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Special Effects Reference  
*“Power leaves its mark — but every mark can be broken.”*  

---

This section summarizes all **Special Effects** by **how they work mechanically**. Specials do not grant an automatic Attribute Check when applied. **Specials apply only when the target is actually hit or affected.** A Martial Attack must hit; a direct Spell must reach that target's **Final Spell TN** (including Resistance and Raises). Merely reaching **Spell Base TN** creates the Spell but does **not** affect a resisting creature with Damage or Specials. Resolve each AoE/Autofire target separately; zones follow their own application rules.

---
Negative value-based Specials decay at the start of the affected creature's turn. Cleanse and specific rules may reduce or remove Specials.

---

#### Global Rules 

**Effect Types**  
Most entries fall into one of these types:

- **Diminishing (X→0):**  
The effect has a stack value **X**. Reapplying the same effect usually **adds stacks**. At the start of the target’s turn, the effect **ticks** (if it has a Tick), then **decays by 1**.

- **Timed:**  
The effect lasts for a listed duration (**1 Round**, **MR Rounds**, etc.), then ends. Timed effects **do not stack** unless an entry explicitly says otherwise. Reapplying them **refreshes duration** and keeps the **higher X**.

- **Until Broken / Until Used:**  
The effect lasts until its listed break condition is met, or until its uses are spent. These effects **do not stack** unless an entry explicitly says otherwise.

- **Instant:**  
The effect resolves immediately and does not persist unless the entry says otherwise.

---
:
#### Special Application Limit

A creature can receive a maximum of:

**4 × its Mastery Rank**

new points of the same Diminishing Special during a single Round. For a numeric Diminishing Special, determine the **new points applied** in this order: printed Special value, successful Raise Special Increase, eligible Intellect Stone Special Boost, eligible **Passive: Special Amplification** on the owner's attack-delivered Special, target Vulnerability, target Special Negation, then the target's remaining per-Round application allowance. If that allowance is already exhausted, the newly applied amount is 0. Apply the surviving amount to stored stacks. **Special Aura** modifies an existing stack's effective value only while in range; it is not a new application.

This limit counts all applications of that Special from all sources combined. Any amount beyond this limit is ignored. This is an application limit, not a maximum stack value. Special points already present from previous Rounds do not count against the limit.

---
:

#### Natural Special Recovery

At the start of its Turn, after resolving all Ticks, a creature reduces one or more negative Diminishing Specials currently affecting it by a total amount equal to its Mastery Rank. The creature chooses freely how to distribute this reduction among its negative Diminishing Specials. If a Special reaches 0, it ends. Any unused reduction is lost.

**Root is excluded from Natural Special Recovery.** Root follows its own Until Broken recovery rule instead.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Cleanse(X)

Choose one creature. Remove up to **X total points** from one or more ongoing negative Specials affecting that creature that list **Cleanse: Yes**. You may distribute the Cleanse value freely between those eligible Specials. If a Special reaches **0**, it ends. Any unused Cleanse value is lost. **Single-focus Cleanse:** Cleanse only reduces eligible negative Specials. It does not grant Damage, Armor, Evade, Speed, Healing, Restoration, Temporary HP, or Absorption Stones. A character needs separate legal Powers for any such additional effects.

---

#### Diminishing Effects

> **Diminishing (X→0):**  
> Reapplying the same effect adds stacks (**X → X + Y**) unless stated otherwise.  
> At the **start of the affected creature’s turn**, resolve the effect’s Tick (if any), then reduce **X by 1** (**X → X−1**, minimum 0).  
> If **X reaches 0**, the effect ends.

| **Effect** | **What It Does** | **Stacking** | **Cleanse** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|
| **Challenge(X)** | Challenge is bound to the creature that applied it. Attack Pools for attacks that do **not** include that creature as a target are reduced by **X** (floor 2 rolled dice on legal standard attacks). | Same source only | Yes |
| **Corrode(X)** | Your **Armor** is reduced by **X**. | Yes | Yes |
| **Disoriented(X)** | Your **Attack Dice** and all dice pools used to notice, locate, track, or identify something are reduced by **X** (floor 2 rolled dice on legal standard rolls). | Yes | Yes |
| **Expose(X)** | Your **Evade** is reduced by **X**. | Yes | Yes |
| **Hex(X)** | When hit by a **Spell**, take **+1d8 bonus damage for every 2 Hex**, rounded up, counting stacks **already present before this hit**. | Yes | Yes |
| **Lacerate(X)** | Every separate **Movement Event** that changes your position deals **X damage once**. Move, Dash, Reposition, a Movement Power, Push, Pull, and Teleport all count. Distance and whether the movement is voluntary do not change the damage. | Yes | Yes |
| **Mark(X)** | For a later hit against a marked target, after rolling Damage Dice but before Damage-Trigger Reactions, spend any amount of Mark present **before that hit** as the Damage Floor of each die; then reduce stored Mark by that amount. Mark applied by this same hit becomes usable on the next hit. | Yes | Yes |
| **Ruin(X)** | At Tick, take **X** damage. | Yes | Yes |
| **Slow(X)** | Each point reduces Speed by 1 m, minimum 0 m. At the start-of-Turn Tick, if Speed is 0 m or voluntary movement is prevented by another effect, take X damage, ignoring Armor. Otherwise, Slow deals no damage. Resolve before Natural Special Recovery and decay. | Yes | Yes |
| **Soulburn(X)** | All dice pools based on **Wits**, **Influence**, or **Resolve** are reduced by **X** (floor 2 rolled dice on legal standard rolls). | Yes | Yes |
| **Sundered(X)** | When hit by a **non-Spell attack**, take **+1d8 bonus damage for every 2 Sundered**, rounded up, counting stacks **already present before this hit**. | Yes | Yes |
| **Weaken(X)** | All dice pools based on **Might**, **Agility**, or **Intellect** are reduced by **X** (floor 2 rolled dice on legal standard rolls). | Yes | Yes |

---
::
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Timed and Fixed Control Effects

> Timed effects do **not stack** unless explicitly stated otherwise.  
> Reapplying a Timed effect refreshes its duration. If it has a numeric value, keep the **higher X**.  
> Fixed Control effects are binary and never gain an X-value.

| **Effect** | **What It Does** | **Duration** | **Stacking** | **Cleanse** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Blight(X)** | While affected, all your **Attribute-based dice pools** have **Disadvantage**. X is the remaining number of your Turns; reduce it by 1 at the end of each Turn. | X Turns | No; reapplication refreshes to the higher remaining duration | Yes |
| **Brace(X)** | Your **Speed becomes 0 m**. While Braced, your **Shield value is doubled** for Armor calculation. At the end of each of your turns, reduce **Brace by 1**. | Until X reaches 0 | No | No |
| **Prone** | You are knocked down and helpless until you stand. Your normal Evade still applies. Spend **1 Attack Action** to stand; then other actions and Reactions become available. Standing does not consume Movement. | Until you stand | No | No |

#### Until Broken / Until Used Effects

**Critical is not a general Special.** **Critical(X)** designates X qualifying Attack or Casting Pools to explode on 7–8 as defined in Core Terms, with the source determining the available combat or Round allowance. It may be granted through **Passive: Critical**, **Active Buff: Critical**, or **Agility Ability: Crit**; choose only one source per eligible roll. Artifact Stone Power Support may pre-fill named Ranks of the Crit Stone Ability, but it never grants Critical directly.

| **Effect** | **What It Does** | **Duration** | **Stacking** | **Cleanse** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **Bulwark(X)** | As a **Reaction** when hit by an attack you can perceive, reduce the attack’s **final damage by 50%** and consume **1** Bulwark. | Until used | Yes | No |
| **Immovable** | A wearer with Feet Artifact Base Value B: Immovable who declares it during Round Preparation has 0 m voluntary Movement, but cannot be forcibly displaced for that Round. This does not grant immunity to Prone or remove Evade. | 1 Round | No | No |
| **Root(X)** | Root has a minimum applied value of **2**. Root stacks from all sources into one value and records the **highest Mastery Rank among its contributing sources**. While Root is above **0**, your Speed is **0 m** and you cannot move voluntarily. At the start of your Turn, reduce Root by your **Mastery Rank**. Spend **1 Attack Action** or **1 Movement** to make a **Vitality Attribute Check** against **TN (8 × highest recorded source MR) − 2**. On success, reduce Root by **exactly 1**, regardless of the margin of success. Clear the source MR when Root ends. | Until broken | Yes | Yes |

---
::

#### Instant Effects
Instant Specials resolve as part of the successful hit that delivered them. They do not grant an automatic Attribute Check.

| **Effect** | **What It Does** | **Stacking** | **Cleanse** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|
| **Brutal Impact(X)** | Each damage die rolled counts as **at least X**. | No | No |
| **Penetration(X)** | The attack ignores **X Armor**. | No | No |
| **Precision(X)** | On hit, add **+Xd8** bonus damage. | No | No |
| **Push(X)** | Push the target **X m** immediately. | No | No |
| **Pull(X)** | Pull the target **X m** immediately. | No | No |
| **Disarm** | On hit, the target loses grip on one visible held item; the item falls to the ground. Recovering it requires **1 Movement** or **1 Attack Action** and makes the item ready to use again. | No | No |
| **Stunned** | On application, the target loses **1 available Attack Action in the current Round**. Resolve the Action loss immediately. | No | No |
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

#### Support Effects

| **Effect** | **What It Does** | **Duration** | **Stacking** |
|:--|:--|:--:|:--:|
| **Cleanse(X)** | Remove up to **X total points** from eligible negative Specials on one creature. The reduction may be distributed across one or more eligible Specials. | Instant | No |

---
:::::::

If you need the full wording for an effect, including timing, stacking, and removal details, see the **Appendix — Special Descriptions** below.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Equipment

Equipment is tracked through the **Inventory Grid**. This is not a weight system. It is a **space and load system** that shows how much gear a character can carry while still moving and reacting cleanly. Each item occupies a number of squares in the grid based on its listed size.

---

### Inventory Grid

Each character has an **Inventory Grid of 24 × 9 squares**.

The grid is divided into **three load zones**, each **8 columns wide**:

- **Zone 1:** Normal Load
- **Zone 2:** Encumbered
- **Zone 3:** Overloaded

The further right an item is placed, the more difficult the total load becomes to carry efficiently. If all carried items fit completely inside **Zone 1**, the character is under **Normal Load**. If any part of an item reaches into **Zone 2**, the character becomes **Encumbered**. If any part of an item reaches into **Zone 3**, the character becomes **Overloaded**. Only the **highest occupied zone** matters.

#### Load Effects

- **Normal Load:** no penalty
- **Encumbered:** **Movement −4 m**
- **Overloaded:** **Movement -6 m**

Load does not prevent carrying gear. It represents how difficult that gear is to carry efficiently in an active situation.

---

### Item Placement

Each item has an Inventory Size written as **width × height**.

To place an item into the Inventory Grid:

- the item must fit entirely within empty squares
- items may not overlap
- items must be placed as one continuous rectangle
- an item’s position determines whether it remains in Normal Load or pushes the character into a higher load zone

If even a single square of an item extends into a higher zone, that item counts as occupying that zone. Item sizes are listed in the item entry, equipment list, or VTT data.

---

### Item Rotation

Items may be **rotated by 90 degrees** when placed in the Inventory Grid. This changes the item from **width × height** to **height × width**. Rotation is allowed only if the rotated item still fits entirely into empty squares.

}}

---
In this example, the character’s load state depends on the furthest zone occupied by any item. If all items remain inside Zone 1, the character is under **Normal Load**. If at least one item reaches into Zone 2, the character is **Encumbered**. If at least one item reaches into Zone 3, the character is **Overloaded**.
::::::::::::::::::::::::
### Dropping Load

A character may drop carried gear to remove Encumbered or Overloaded. If a character is surprised while Encumbered or Overloaded, they must decide whether to fight while carrying the load or spend **Attack Actions** to drop it safely. If the gear is dropped **hastily**, it can be done immediately with GM approval, but the character accepts the risk that items inside are damaged, scattered, lost, or exposed. The GM should only apply this consequence when it matters.

Examples:

- glass bottles shatter
- oil leaks
- arrows spill
- rations are ruined
- tools scatter
- a lantern breaks
- a scroll case pops open
- fragile loot is damaged

---

### Summary

| Load State | Trigger | Movement | Safely Drop Load |
|---|---|:---:|:---:|
| **Normal** | Only Zone 1 occupied | Normal | Free / no issue |
| **Encumbered** | Any item in Zone 2 | −4 m | 1 Attack Action |
| **Overloaded** | Any item in Zone 3 | −6 m | 2 Attack Actions |

}}

> **Rule:** Everything in the **Special** column refers to a Special defined in the **Special Effects Reference** and **Appendix — Special Descriptions**.

:

### Weapon Properties

| Property | Description |
|:--|:--|
| **Finesse** | Attack Roll uses **Agility** (for To-Hit only, not for damage). |
| **Light** | May be wielded in the **off-hand**. |
| **Versatile** | When wielded **two-handed**, gain **+2d8 weapon damage** (you lose your shield / off-hand). |
| **Reach (+1 m / +2 m)** | Your melee reach is **2 m** by default; this increases it. Measure from the attacker’s token center for a single-Hex creature, or from the nearest edge of its occupied footprint for a multi-Hex creature using Growth. |
| **Heavy** | You get **–10 to your Initiative roll**. |
| **Balanced** | If a weapon has **Heavy + Balanced**, reduce the Heavy penalty to **–5 Initiative**. |
| **Ranged (X m)** | You may make a ranged weapon attack up to **X m**. |
| **Set** | If you **did not move** this round: gain **+1d8 weapon damage**. |
| **Thrown (X m)** | You may make a ranged attack by throwing the weapon up to **X m** (you must retrieve it afterward unless a rule says otherwise). |
| **Load** | After you fire, the weapon is **Unloaded**. To fire again, spend **1 Attack Action** to reload it. You need **one free hand** to do so. **Quick Load** may instead use Movement as described in the Movement rules. |
| **Defensive** | While wielding this weapon **two-handed**, add your **Mastery (max +6)** to your **Evade**. |
| **Spell Focus (+Xd8)** | Adds +Xd8 damage to a successful hostile single-target attack-delivered Spell **or each successful Spell Autofire hit**, even one purchasing only a Special/control effect. Never adds damage to AoE, Support, persistent zone or placement Spells. |

}}
