{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Magic

Magic is not a separate family of Powers. It is a **delivery method**. A Spell uses an existing eligible Power template and changes how that Power is delivered and resisted. The underlying Power remains the same mechanical Power.

A Spell keeps the Power's:

- Power Level
- printed Range
- Damage
- AoE
- Duration
- Specials
- action type
- base Technical Reference

Only the delivery changes. A Martial delivery uses a weapon, body, tool, physical technique, or another non-Spell method and normally resolves through an **Attack Roll** where applicable. A Spell delivery gains the **Spell** tag, uses a fixed **Casting Attribute**, and resolves through the **Casting Roll** and **Spell TN** rules below. Its casting difficulty is based on the **Power Level being used**, not on the caster's Mastery Rank. Changing delivery does **not** create a second Power for duplicate selection or the once-per-Round Power Use Limit. A Martial and Spell version with the same base Technical Reference are still the same Power for those rules.

---

### Spell Design Rule

Only a **Ranged Active** may use Spell delivery unless a specific rule explicitly allows another Power structure to become a Spell. Melee-only, Self-only, melee-reach, and close-combat Powers do not become ranged merely because they gain the Spell tag. A Ranged Active with one or more Specials may use Spell delivery normally. A character may also possess a limited number of **Special-less Spell Powers**. The number of character-owned Spell Powers that contain **no Special** cannot exceed the character's **Mastery Rank**.

This means a character may use Spell delivery for ordinary structures such as a damage-only Single Target Attack, Split Attack, **Restoration Power**, or another legal Ranged Active without a Special, but only up to that Mastery Rank limit. Spell Powers that already contain at least one Special do **not** count against the Special-less Spell limit. A source may explicitly grant a Spell without counting against this limit.

---
---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Catalogue Delivery Conversion

Ranged Active catalogue entries may be written using a **Martial baseline** even when the same Technical Reference can use Spell delivery. When a legal Ranged Active changes from Martial to Spell delivery:

- a printed **Ranged Weapon or Thrown Weapon** requirement applies only to the Martial version and is replaced by the normal Spell delivery requirements,
- **Attack Pool / Attack Roll** becomes **Casting Pool / Casting Roll**,
- **Evade** becomes the affected creature's **Final Spell TN**,
- **Weapon Damage** is omitted and replaced by eligible **Spell Focus Damage** for a hostile single-target attack **or Autofire hit**, even if only a Special or control effect is purchased,
- **AoE Powers** add neither Weapon Damage nor Spell Focus Damage, regardless of delivery,
- printed **Power Damage**, Specials, Range, AoE, Duration, target count, split structure, control, and other non-delivery effects remain unchanged,
- non-attacking Support, placement and persistent-zone effects gain no damage from delivery.

These substitutions do not create a second Power. They are the Spell delivery of the same base Technical Reference.

---

### Shared Power Effects and Delivery-Specific Effects

Unless a rule explicitly names a delivery-specific property, a legal effect may interact with Martial and Spell delivery alike.

Examples:

- **Critical** may apply to a Martial Attack or damaging Spell.
- **Special Boost** may increase Specials applied through Martial or Spell delivery.
- **Raises (Intellect Stone Ability)** grants one shared allowance of **2 / 4 / 6 / 8** at Ranks 1–4 for the current Round. Choose how many to use **and assign each to Damage or an existing numeric Special before each eligible roll**. They neither increase TN nor require Raise Cost; on success they apply, on failure they are consumed without effect.
- **Extra Attack** grants additional Attack Actions that may be used with any otherwise legal unused Active, including Spells.

Delivery-specific rules remain delivery-specific:

- **Weapon Damage**, **Damage (Might Stone Ability)**, and effects that explicitly require a weapon or Martial Attack do not become Spell effects.
- **Spell Focus** applies only to eligible single-target attack-delivered Spells and **Spell Autofire hits**; **Spell Resistance** applies to Spells as normally specified.
- A rule that explicitly refers to an **Attack Roll** does not automatically modify a Casting Roll, and vice versa, unless that rule says it works with both.

---

### Casting Attribute

When a Power gains Spell delivery, choose whether it uses:

- **Intellect**
- **Resolve**
- **Influence**

This choice is part of the Spell's design and is fixed for that Spell.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Casting Roll

**Casting while threatened:** If the caster is within any enemy's **Melee Reach at declaration**, every legal **Spell Casting Roll** is made with **Disadvantage**, whether the Spell damages, restores Scarred Health Bars, cleanses, supports, controls, creates a zone, or otherwise resolves through Casting. This includes any otherwise legal melee-/touch-range Spell and any self-targeted legal Spell. It does **not** reduce Healing Dice, change the Spell Base TN, or make prohibited deliveries legal. Multiple enemies do not stack this Disadvantage. See *Threatened Ranged Attacks* for the common timing and Reaction rules.

When casting a Spell, roll:

---

`Casting Attribute k Mastery`

---

The Casting Attribute is the Attribute chosen when the Spell was designed: **Intellect**, **Resolve**, or **Influence**. Spells do **not** roll against the target's Evade. Every Spell has a **Spell Base TN** determined by the **Power Level actually used for that casting**, not by the caster's Mastery Rank:

`Spell Base TN = Casting Power Level × 4`

| Used PL | Base TN | Used PL | Base TN |
|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| 1 | 4 | 9 | 36 |
| 2 | 8 | 10 | 40 |
| 3 | 12 | 11 | 44 |
| 4 | 16 | 12 | 48 |
| 5 | 20 | 13 | 52 |
| 6 | 24 | 14 | 56 |
| 7 | 28 | 15 | 60 |
| 8 | 32 | 16 | 64 |

**Mastery Rank still determines Keep and the maximum available Power Level.** It never raises the Base TN of a Spell used at the same PL. Normally cast at the recorded Power Level; optional use of a complete lower-level version is explained under **Voluntary Lower-Level Casting** below. *Example:* An MR 2 caster uses **TN 8** for a PL 2 Spell or **TN 16** for a PL 4 Spell. After rising to MR 3, that same PL 2 Spell still uses **TN 8**.

For a Spell that affects a creature, determine that creature's **Final Spell TN**:

`Final Spell TN = Spell Base TN + Target Spell Resistance`

Apply eligible reductions first, including **Passive: Spell Resistance Piercing** (`ceil(Passive PL / 2)`) for that caster's own Spells. Count no less than 0 remaining effective Spell Resistance; the target's actual resistance and Spell Base TN remain unchanged. A target without an explicit Spell Resistance source has **Spell Resistance 0**. *Example:* A PL 4 Spell has **Base TN 16**. Against effective Spell Resistance 4, its **Final Spell TN is 20**; one declared Paid Raise would make the target's required TN **24**. The Base TN remains 16 for determining whether the Spell fizzles.

For a direct Spell, compare the Casting Roll against the target's Final Spell TN. For a Spell with the **AoE** tag, make **one Casting Roll** for the entire area. Compare that same result separately against the Final Spell TN of each valid creature in the area. Each creature is checked independently using its own Spell Resistance. Spell AoEs do **not** use Evade, but they do use Spell Resistance. Legal defenses that explicitly work against a Spell AoE, such as **Dive for Cover**, still function normally.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Casting Success and Resistance

For a directly delivered Spell, the **Spell Base TN** determines whether the caster produces the Spell; placed effects use the separate raised **Creation TN** described below. Spell Resistance determines whether a successfully produced Spell affects a specific creature.

- If the Casting Roll is **below Spell Base TN**, the Spell fizzles. There is no additional penalty.
- If the Casting Roll reaches **Spell Base TN**, the Spell is successfully cast.
- A creature is affected only if that same result also reaches its **Final Spell TN**.
- If the roll reaches Spell Base TN but not a creature's Final Spell TN, that creature **resists** the Spell. The Spell does not fizzle, but **the resisting creature receives no Damage or Specials from that application**.
- For an AoE Spell, resistance by one creature does not affect any other creature.

**Placed and persistent Spell exception:** When a Spell creates an effect without directly affecting a creature, it must reach **Creation TN = Spell Base TN + 4 x declared Paid Combat Raises**. Merely reaching the unraised Spell Base TN is insufficient when Raises were declared. The placed effect is not created if Creation TN is missed; later target applications use the stored result against each target's Spell Base TN + effective Spell Resistance + the **same declared Paid Raises x 4**.


### Voluntary Lower-Level Casting

A Spell normally uses the **Power Level the character has purchased or the source explicitly grants**. At declaration, a caster may voluntarily choose a **lower Power Level** if the complete lower-level entry is available and legal for that Power and source. Choose the used PL **before** the Casting Roll, target resolution and Raise declaration; the Base TN is **4 × that used PL**. Downcasting is optional, costs no extra XP and does not give another use of the Power.

Use the **entire** lower-PL Power profile for that casting: Damage, Specials, Range, Area, Duration, number of targets and any other PL-dependent effects or limits. Never combine a lower casting TN with higher-level effects, guess a missing profile, or scale printed numbers by approximation. If the complete lower-level profile cannot be consulted, use the recorded PL. One declared PL applies to the **whole Power use**, including every target, AoE application, Split Attack roll, Autofire slot and later application of a persistent zone. Downcasting does not change the Casting Attribute, Attribute Pool, MR Keep, Technical Reference, or once-per-Round Power Use Limit.

**At-table recommendation:** Record the PL you actually intend to use most often. You do **not** need to maintain separate sheets for all weaker versions; consult a lower profile only when you deliberately choose to cast it. A stored Power in a Minor Magic Item is fixed at the PL recorded **when that item was created** and cannot be downcast at activation.

---

### Willing Spell Targets

A creature that is aware of a Spell and willingly accepts it may choose to treat its **Spell Resistance as 0** against that Spell. The choice is made before the Casting Roll. Spell Base TN is never reduced this way. This allows willing healing, support, and beneficial Spells to function without making Spell Resistance a penalty to their intended recipient.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Persistent Spell Zones

When Spell delivery creates a **Persistent Diminishing Zone**, choose the casting PL and make one Casting Roll when the zone is created (including threatened Disadvantage if applicable at that declaration). Record the **chosen PL and final result**. Later zone applications use that same PL and stored result; they do not cause new Casting Rolls or new threatened Disadvantage.

- Declare any Paid Combat Raises before rolling; **Creation TN = (4 x chosen PL) + (4 x declared Paid Raises)**. Record the number of Paid Raises with the zone.
- If the Casting result is below **Creation TN**, the zone is **not created**, even if the result reaches the unraised Spell Base TN. There is no additional penalty.
- If the result reaches **Creation TN**, the zone exists for its normal Duration. **Record the Damage/Special Raise allocation declared before casting**; it remains fixed for all later applications of this zone.
- When a creature would be affected, compare the stored result against **current target Final Spell TN + (4 x the zone's recorded Paid Raises)**. Target SR can change; the declared Raises cannot.
- A creature whose Final Spell TN is not reached resists that application; the zone remains.
- The zone's normal once-per-Round application limit still applies.

---

### Spell Conversion Summary

A Spell is an eligible Power using magical delivery. The Spell tag changes **how the Power is delivered and resisted**. It does not replace the Power's underlying effect structure or create a second copy of the Power. A Spell may therefore be damage-only, healing-only, utility-oriented, or Special-based if the underlying Ranged Active is legal. **Casting Base TN = used PL × 4**, while **MR sets Keep and the Power Level cap**. Special-less Spells are limited to a number equal to the character's Mastery Rank; Spells that already contain Specials do not count against that limit.

---

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Powers

Powers are the mechanical building blocks of character abilities. This chapter does not explain every Special again. Specials are defined in their own rules chapter. Instead, this chapter explains the main Power types and points to the dedicated Power catalogues.

A Power belongs to one of the following categories:

- **Actives**
- **Passives**
- **Active Buffs**
- **Reactions**
- **Movement Powers**

Each category has its own design rules, limits, timing, and pricing logic.

### Power Use Limit

A specific Power can be used only **once per round**. After you use a Power, that Power is considered **spent** until the start of the next round. The Power's identity is its base **Technical Reference**, not its character-facing name, source, or current PL. Changing between Martial and Spell delivery does not create a second Power identity; delivery method is not a way to bypass duplicate rules or the once-per-Round Power Use Limit.

This applies to all Power Types:

- Actives
- Active Buffs
- Reactions
- Movement Powers
- other named Power effects

Passive Powers are not “used” each round unless they contain a specific activated or triggered effect. If you gain extra Attack Actions, extra Reactions, or extra Movement, you may use them only with Powers that have not already been used this round. Additional Attack Actions from multiple sources do **not** stack unless a rule explicitly says otherwise; use the highest single grant of additional Attack Actions. You cannot use the same Active Power multiple times in the same round. You cannot use the same Reaction multiple times in the same round unless that Reaction explicitly allows repeated uses. Each repeated use still costs **1 Reaction** and does not grant additional Reactions. You cannot use the same Active Buff multiple times in the same round. To attack multiple times with Powers in the same round, you need multiple different Active Powers. To react multiple times in the same round, you need multiple different valid Reactions and enough Reaction uses. To move with multiple Movement Powers in the same round, you need multiple different Movement Powers and enough Movement uses.
Basic Attacks are not Powers and may be repeated if you have enough Attack Actions.

### Power Categories

| **Power Type** | **Purpose** | **Primary Use** | **Catalogue** |
|---|---|---|---|
| **Actives** | Direct actions, attacks, effects, zones, barriers, and Special delivery. | Your main action-based tools. | <a href="https://homebrewery.naturalcrit.com/share/Xlht6xfyn5br" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Actives</a> |
| **Passives** | Always-on character identity and reliable baseline benefits. | Permanent or pre-selected character traits. | <a href="https://homebrewery.naturalcrit.com/share/m74ur1W6_7MQ" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Passives</a> |
| **Active Buffs** | Temporary combat states that enhance the user or allies for a limited time. | Stances, short-term boosts, protection, escalation. | <a href="https://homebrewery.naturalcrit.com/share/-LDVoC64w7wX" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Active Buffs</a> |
| **Reactions** | Immediate answers to a specific trigger. | One-hit defense, interception, emergency protection, counters. | <a href="https://homebrewery.naturalcrit.com/share/mnNMj2Jj--vp" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Reactions</a> |
| **Movement Powers** | Alternative movement modes. | Dash, leap, teleport, flight, safe movement, burrow, phase passage. | <a href="http://homebrewery.naturalcrit.com/share/pyfkMUFdD_C7" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Movement</a> |

---

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Mechanical Powers and Character Expression

> **You pay for what your character can do, not for what your character looks like.**

Powers in this system are written as mechanical templates. A Power's listed name describes what the Power does mechanically. It does not define what the Power looks like, sounds like, feels like, or means for the character. Players are encouraged to rename, describe, and flavor their Powers to match their character. The same mechanical Power may look completely different for different characters.

Examples:

- A **Ranged Single Target Attack** may be an arrow, a thrown knife, a lightning bolt, a shard of glass, a beam of moonlight, a dragon spark, or a poisonous thorn.
- A **Melee Push / Pull** may be a shield bash, a hammer blow, a gravity pulse, a burst of wind, or a spectral beast strike.
- A **Mental Attack** may be a psychic lance, a whispered curse, a memory spike, a pressure wave of fear, or a silent scream.
- A **Mind Illusion** may be a nightmare image, a false wall, a phantom voice, a distorted reflection, a veil of insects, or a hallucinated enemy.
- A **Heal** may be divine light, blood magic, herbal restoration, ancestral warmth, repairing nanogrowth, or stolen life returned.

The mechanics stay the same. The fiction belongs to the player.

---

### Player-Named Powers

When a player chooses a Power, they may give it a character-facing name. The technical name remains the rules reference. The character-facing name is used in play, on the character sheet, and in narration.

Example:

Technical Power: **Mental Attack** Character Power Name: **Psychic Lance**

Technical Power: **Ranged Single Target Special: Ruin** Character Power Name: **Blackfire Arrow**

Technical Power: **Mind Illusion** Character Power Name: **The Wall That Was Never There**

Technical Power: **Melee Push / Pull** Character Power Name: **Titanbreaker Blow**

A character-facing Power name does not change the Power's mechanics. Power identity follows the technical name across every source, including purchases, Echo Artifacts, other Artifacts, Trees, and granted abilities. Two character-facing names with the same Technical Reference are the same Power for duplicate selection and the once-per-round Power Use Limit. Changing between Martial and Spell delivery does not create a new Power identity. If a player describes a Power in a way that sounds stronger than its rules text, use the rules text.

If a player describes a Power in a way that sounds weaker, stranger, or more personal, the description is allowed as long as the mechanics remain clear.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Actives

Actives are action-based Powers. They are used to attack, apply Specials, create areas, shape the battlefield, create barriers, or produce another immediate effect. Actives are the main place where **Special delivery** happens. Most combat Powers that directly affect enemies, spaces, or battlefield objects are Actives.

Actives may include:

- damage,
- healing,
- Specials,
- AoE,
- persistent zones,
- barriers,
- control effects,
- forced movement,
- utility effects,
- or other paid mechanical effects.


See: <a href="https://homebrewery.naturalcrit.com/share/Xlht6xfyn5br" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Actives</a>

#### Autofire — Stepped Attack Chain

**Autofire is not AoE.** Before rolling, declare up to the Power's printed **Attack Capacity** as an ordered series. A creature may appear more than once (A → B → A or A → A → A). Every slot must be within printed Range, and each slot after the first must be within **4 m of the preceding target**. The first slot costs no additional TN. Make **one** Martial Attack Roll or eligible Spell Casting Roll. A Spell Autofire uses the **same chosen casting PL and Spell Base TN** for every slot. Resolve the declared slots in order. For slot *i* (starting at 1):

**TN(i) = target's applicable Evade or Final Spell TN + 4 × (i − 1) + 4 × declared Paid Raises.**

The cumulative +4 steps are **hit thresholds, not Paid Raises**; they grant no additional Damage or Special effect and are not removed by Intellect Free Raises. **The first unsuccessful slot ends the chain**; successful earlier slots remain hits. A Spell below Spell Base TN fizzles as normal. Reaction: Evade works against Martial Autofire; legal Spell defenses apply to Spell Autofire. **Dive for Cover is not available.**

Every successful slot is a **separate hit**, even against the same creature. Roll matching **Weapon Damage (Martial)** or **Spell Focus Damage (Spell)** together with the Power's eligible Damage Dice **once**; reuse the same die results for all successful hits. Resolve Armor, Damage Reduction, Damage Negation, Temporary HP, Special Negation and other legal defenses **per hit**; do not combine repeated hits before mitigation. Apply the normal Raise effects **declared before the shared roll** to every successful hit, according to the normal rules.

*Example:* Three attacks against Evade 10 require **10 / 14 / 18** before ordinary Paid Raises. An Attack Roll of **16** hits the first two; the third misses. All damage dice are rolled just once. The Power's existing PP costs, range, Attack Capacity and PL damage progression remain unchanged.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Passives

Passives are reliable, always-on character identity. They do not require an action to activate. Every current Passive belongs to exactly one Passive Category. Combined Passives are not available in this release. A Passive Category is a mechanical classification, not flavour text. The authoritative **28-Passive catalogue** includes defenses such as Armor, Evade, Health/Fortitude, Temporary HP, Parry and Damage Negation; offensive choices such as Damage, Armor Reduction, Critical, Maximization, Enemy Evade Reduction, Spell Resistance Piercing and Special Amplification; plus Self Cleanse, Regeneration, Speed and other support Powers. **Passive: Regeneration**, not a generic "Healing" Passive, handles its printed Scarred-Bar recovery.

Offensive / Defensive / Utility & Mobility selection groups are for display only; **mechanical Passive Categories** still determine slot compatibility. Passives may not hide Active effects inside always-on features. They may not grant free attacks, extra actions, Movement Powers, Reactions, Spell effects, full Persistent Zones, or casual hard control.
See: <a href="https://homebrewery.naturalcrit.com/share/m74ur1W6_7MQ" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Passives</a>

---

### Active Buffs

Active Buffs are temporary combat enhancements that are activated during the **Preparation Phase**, before the Combat Round begins. **Activation Cost:** Activating an Active Buff consumes the character's **base Attack Action** for the upcoming Round. This Action is committed during Preparation and cannot be used during that character's Turn, whether for a Basic Attack, an Active Power, or another action requiring it. Additional Attack Actions granted by Stones or other legal sources remain available. The same base Attack Action cannot be spent or forfeited a second time to pay for another effect.

**Activation and Duration:** The Active Buff becomes effective at the beginning of the upcoming Round, before any creature takes its Turn. It normally lasts **Mastery Rank complete Rounds**. The activation Round counts as the first Round. At **Round End** of the final Round, the Buff expires. Its duration is independent of the activating character's Initiative or Turn. **Maintenance and Replacement:** A character may normally maintain only one Active Buff and may activate or replace an Active Buff only during a Preparation Phase. Replacing an existing Buff ends the previous Buff. Active Buffs cannot stack with themselves.

**Round-Based Resources:** Resources granted by an Active Buff for each Round become available during the activation Preparation Phase and refresh once during each subsequent Preparation Phase while the Buff remains active. Unused Round-based resources expire at Round End. Other effects with explicitly defined triggers, such as Start of Turn or End of Turn, retain those triggers unless the specific Buff states otherwise. Active Buffs cannot be activated or replaced during a creature's Turn or through an ordinary Attack Action. Explicit exceptions must be stated in their own rules.

---

#### Active Buff Limits

Apply the **Preparation Phase activation and replacement rules above**: at most one activation or replacement each Round, one maintained Buff, and no self-stacking. Only an explicit written exception may override a limit.

---

Active Buffs may be:

- single-focus defensive buffs,
- single-focus offensive buffs,
- single-effect aura buffs,
- or dedicated premium subsystem buffs.

Combined Active Buffs are not selectable in this beta release. Active Buffs do not apply Specials by default. Active Buffs in this catalogue do not apply or increase numeric Specials. **Passive: Special Amplification**, **Intellect Stone Ability: Special Boost**, and the **Special Increase Raise Effect** are separate legal sources; none is a Combined Active Buff.

See: <a href="https://homebrewery.naturalcrit.com/share/-LDVoC64w7wX" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Active Buffs</a>

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Reactions

Reactions are immediate answers to a specific trigger. A Reaction is not a second turn. A Reaction is not a hidden Attack Action. A Reaction is not a way to add free offensive pressure outside your turn. A Reaction costs your **Reaction for the round**. You may normally use only **one Reaction per round**.

Reactions answer narrow moments such as:

- you are attacked,
- you are hit,
- you would take damage,
- an ally nearby is attacked,
- you fail a defensive moment,
- or a premium defensive subsystem is triggered.

If a Reaction grants Armor, Evade, Temporary HP, Damage Reduction, or Phasing, that benefit applies only to the triggering event unless the entry explicitly says otherwise. Reactions do not apply or increase Specials by default.

See: <a href="https://homebrewery.naturalcrit.com/share/mnNMj2Jj--vp" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Reactions</a>

---

### Movement Powers

Movement Powers replace your normal Movement for the round.

They are used to change:

- how you move,
- where you can move,
- or what kind of path you may take.

A Movement Power is not an Attack Action. A Movement Power is not an Active Buff. A Movement Power is not a Reaction. A Movement Power is not a Special-delivery system. When you use a Movement Power, the listed distance is your **total Movement for that Movement Power**. It is not added on top of your normal Movement unless the entry explicitly says otherwise.

Movement Powers may include movement types such as:

- Ground Dash,
- Safe Movement,
- Teleport,
- Teleport with Ally,
- Flight,
- Leap,
- Wall Walk,
- Burrow,
- Phase Passage,
- or Trample.

See: <a href="http://homebrewery.naturalcrit.com/share/pyfkMUFdD_C7" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Movement</a>

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Form and Transformation

**Form** describes how a character looks: human, wolf, bear, a clawed monster, or another suitable appearance. Every Form uses **one character sheet** with the same purchased Attributes, Skills, Powers, Stone Abilities, attacks, Damage, Armor, Health, senses, Movement, Reach, Range, and occupied Hexes. A character may describe or change their Form freely, **including during combat**, without an Action, Stone cost, use limit, or activation time. Features such as keen scent, special vision, claws, flight, or a powerful bite use the corresponding abilities already purchased on the character sheet.

**Transformation** changes a creature's **actual size and occupied Hex footprint**. Its mechanical consequences are specified by the applicable Growth rule. **Polymorph and True Polymorph are not separate Powers**: appearance changes use Form, and changes to occupied Hexes use Transformation.

- **Uses:** A character may activate a size-changing Transformation **Mastery Rank times per Safe Haven Rest**. Each transformation into a larger footprint uses one use; selecting a different visual Form does not.
- **Activation:** A Transformation takes only a few moments and costs no Stones by itself. It has no fixed duration.
- **In combat:** Choose the Transformation before combat. The occupied footprint stays fixed until combat ends. Visual Form may still change freely.
- **Return:** Outside combat, returning to original size is free. Becoming **Incapacitated** immediately returns the character to original size while all injuries and Incapacitated remain in effect.
- **Size direction:** Transformation expands the original footprint; the character may return to their original footprint outside combat. The original footprint is the minimum size.
- **Space:** The entire larger footprint must fit in free, traversable Hexes. Occupied or blocked Hexes prevent Growth; creatures and obstacles keep their positions.

Growth grants its explicitly defined, purchased mechanical benefits. The character retains all other purchased abilities; its appearance determines none of their values.

#### Growth Geometry and Combat Effects

**Growth G** adds **G complete Hex rings** around the character's original single-Hex space. A creature at Growth G occupies `1 + 3 × G × (G + 1)` Hexes. Each ring adds accessible surrounding space and increases how many adjacent creatures may engage the transformed character.

| **Growth** | **Occupied Hexes** | **Adjacent Hexes** | **Total Base Melee Reach** | **Additional 0-Penalty Health Bars** | **Push/Pull Reduction** | **Physical Ranged and Spell Range** |
|:--:|--:|--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| **0** | 1 | 6 | 2 m | 0 | 0 | Normal |
| **1** | 7 | 12 | 4 m | 1 | MR × 1 m | Normal |
| **2** | 19 | 18 | 6 m | 2 | MR × 2 m | ×2 |
| **3** | 37 | 24 | 8 m | 3 | MR × 3 m | ×2 |

**Melee Reach:** Add **2 m per Growth** to the normal 2 m base. Measure reach and ranged distances for a multi-Hex creature from the nearest edge of its occupied Hex footprint to the target, using the normal distance rules; weapon Reach and eligible bonuses add normally. **Physical Ranged** means bow, crossbow, thrown weapon and comparable physical ranged attacks. At **Growth 2 or higher**, their Range and Spell Range are each **doubled once**, never again at later Growth steps. Apply any additional flat range bonuses under their normal rules.

**Push/Pull:** Subtract **Growth × the transformed character's Mastery Rank in meters** from each incoming Push or Pull distance, to a minimum of **0 m**. Other effects of the hit resolve normally. **Space and targeting:** Every occupied Hex must remain traversable through movement and fit when transforming. Growth leaves nearby creatures and obstacles in place. An AoE affecting several of a creature's occupied Hexes still resolves **once against that creature**. Growth changes the footprint, Reach, Range and Health Bars specified here; other combat values are those purchased on the character sheet.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Growth Health Bars

A size-changing **Transformation with Growth G** provides **G additional 0-penalty Health Bars** before the character's regular Healthy Bar. Each Growth Bar has **Vitality × 4 HP** and is damaged **before normal Health Bars**. All Growth Bars must become Scarred before damage progresses into the normal bars. Growth Bars do not increase the size of normal bars and grant no inherent Armor or Temporary HP.

**Each Growth Bar keeps its own damage and Scarred state across transformations.** Returning to original size merely hides the Growth Bars; it does not heal, restore, or reset them. Transforming to Growth again makes the corresponding bars available in precisely their previous state. A Scarred Growth Bar remains Scarred and is skipped as usual until restored by the existing recovery rules. **A hidden Scarred Growth Bar remains eligible for Safe Haven Rest, Natural Recovery, Remove Scar or Restoration**; the player may choose it whenever that recovery permits choosing an eligible Scarred Health Bar. Reopening a hidden Growth Bar does not change the character's current size. Partially damaged Growth Bars likewise retain that damage. Changing size leaves normal Health Bars unchanged.

---

### Defensive Effects

Armor, Evade, Health Bars, Temporary HP, Damage Reduction, Phasing, Anti-Displacement and the different forms of recovery belong to distinct defensive or restorative mechanics.

Use the relevant Power or Stone Ability for the listed effect:

| **Effect** | **Usually Found In** |
|---|---|
| Armor | Passives, Active Buffs, Reactions |
| Evade | Passives, Active Buffs, Reactions |
| Health Bars | Passives |
| Temporary HP | Passives, Active Buffs, Reactions |
| Ordinary HP Healing | Resolve: Healing (Stone Ability); Damage Transfer |
| Scarred-Bar Restoration | Restoration Actives and Artifact-granted Restoration |
| Self Regeneration | Passive: Regeneration |
| Damage Reduction | Dedicated Passive, Active Buff, and Reaction entries |
| Phasing | Dedicated Passive, Active Buff, and Reaction entries |
| Anti-Displacement | Passives, Active Buffs, Reactions, or specific Tree exceptions |

These effects are explained in the relevant catalogue entries, not in this chapter. Movement Powers provoke movement-triggered Reactions normally unless the entry explicitly says otherwise. Only Movement Powers that say they prevent such Reactions do so. See: <a href="http://homebrewery.naturalcrit.com/share/pyfkMUFdD_C7" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Movement</a>

---

### Closed Premium Subsystems

Some mechanics are closed premium subsystems. They may only appear in their dedicated Power entries.

The most important closed premium subsystems are:

- **Damage Reduction**
- **Phasing**

Damage Reduction and Phasing may not be casually added as secondary riders. They may not be bundled into unrelated Powers under a different name. They may only appear where the dedicated catalogue entry explicitly allows them.

---

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

## Echo Imprints

*The soul remembers what the person has forgotten.*

Echo Imprints are small, instinctive supernatural manifestations of memories that outlast their owners. Some are traces of earlier lives; others survive as impressions of an Echo's ancient people, ancestral customs, and forgotten history. An Imprint might steady an object, touch the rhythm of life, create a faint illusion, or guide a familiar movement. These are **not Powers, Spells, combat techniques, Skill Ranks, or Rituals**.

Each Imprint has a clear minor effect and a short **GM - Echo Memory** prompt. When a character uses an Imprint, the GM should add one brief sensory impression from an earlier life or their Echo's inherited past: another face, body, city, scent, language, relationship, or feeling. Prefer a sentence rather than a scene. A player may suggest details. Repeated motifs can slowly establish the shape of a remembered life; no new person or location is required on every use.

The memory is atmospheric, not a source of guaranteed facts, lost training, secret history, or mechanical bonuses. Imprints do not decide the truth of reincarnation or reveal another creature's soul. Those deeper mysteries belong to the setting and the GM.

---

### Choosing Echo Imprints

There are **four Imprints for each of the seven Attributes**: Might, Agility, Vitality, Intellect, Resolve, Influence, and Wits (28 total). A character may choose **up to their Mastery Rank in Echo Imprints total**, across any eligible Attributes. Each selected Imprint requires its linked Attribute to be **4 or higher**. A character at MR 2 can therefore select two Imprints; both may belong to the same Attribute. New selections are made during character creation or advancement, not changed freely between scenes.

**No Reroll Points, Stones, XP, or usage charges are paid to select or activate an Imprint.** The MR selection limit remains in force. Changing an existing selection outside normal advancement requires GM approval, not a refund mechanism.

---

### Automatic Progression

Each selected Imprint improves with its linked Attribute; it is not separately ranked or purchased.

| Current Attribute | Imprint Tier |
|:---:|:---:|
| 1-3 | Unavailable |
| 4-7 | 4 |
| 8-11 | 8 |
| 12-15 | 12 |
| 16-19 | 16 |
| 20-40 | 20 |

Use the highest unlocked tier. Intellect 11 uses Tier 8 of Mage Hand; Vitality 20 uses Tier 20 of Living Warmth. No Tier above 20 exists, even when an Attribute reaches 40.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

### Use and Boundaries

The minor supernatural manifestation itself succeeds without a roll in ordinary circumstances. It cannot automatically accomplish an additional **risky, contested, complex, or professionally trained task**. For such an objective, use the normal Skill Check, Task TN, declared Raises, and Skill Opposition rules. An Imprint never grants extra dice, free Raises, Skill Ranks, automatic discovery, or a TN discount.

**Imprints are for exploration and roleplay, not tactical Powers.** They cannot inflict damage, heal HP or Scarred Health Bars, stabilize Incapacitated characters, grant Armor/Evade, bypass Fear, produce extra actions, replace Movement Powers, create valuable items, or substitute for Ritual analysis and wards. During combat they may color narration or harmless interaction, but cannot confer a combat advantage or replace an Active, Passive, Active Buff, Reaction, Stone Ability, or maneuver.

An Imprint may show **where to look**, what a faint impression feels like, or a possible approach. It does not establish that the character succeeds at the underlying Skill. When an Imprint intersects with another rule, apply the narrower Imprint limit and resolve the actual task normally.

---

### GM Guidance: One Action, One Memory

Every use should feel like a trace of another existence, even if it is only a quick phrase. Use the individual **GM - Echo Memory** example as a starting point, not a fixed biography. Let memory motifs recur: the same red alley, a remembered laugh, a different hand, or the scent of a lost harbor. Do not interrupt fast play with a mandatory monologue. **Example - Catfall:** A character rolls gently from a low ledge. The GM says, *"For a heartbeat the cobbles become red dust. You remember landing here before - in another city, in another body."* The movement is controlled; a genuinely dangerous fall still uses the normal rules.

**Example - Hollow Echo:** A character touches a stone wall and hears an uneven hollow note. The GM says, *"Something behind this section sounds different. It might be worth searching."* The Imprint has supplied a clue, **not a discovered secret door**. Investigation or an appropriate task is still needed to locate a seam, find a mechanism, or open anything. **Example - Living Warmth:** A character warms a friend's hands. For an instant they remember cradling a sleeping child in a winter they never lived. No HP or Health Bar changes.

---

### Scope of the Catalogue

Each entry gives the **effect**, five Attribute **tiers**, and a short **GM - Echo Memory**. Higher tiers increase only the effect's subtlety, range, number of trivial objects, or duration. They never turn an Imprint into a Power, professional Skill, or Ritual. The listed effects and limits are rules; the memory is an illustrative prompt.

---


}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide


#### Might - Anchor Touch
*By touching an unattended object, steady it briefly: a rattling shutter, loose door or unstable stack. The effect holds ordinary objects in place for seconds, giving someone time to secure them.*

| Attribute | Effect |
|:--:|---|
| **4** | A light object remains steady for a single breath. |
| **8** | A loose door, shutter, or small stack remains stable for a moment. |
| **12** | A larger everyday object stops shifting for a few seconds. |
| **16** | A sizable but ordinary unstable structure becomes briefly still, allowing someone to secure it by normal means. |
| **20** | Several connected, uncontested parts of a local obstacle can be steadied for a few seconds; lasting repairs still require appropriate work. |

*Cold iron presses into your palms. In another lifetime, a storm rattled a strange front door while a child wept behind you.*

---

#### Might - Burden's Mercy
*Make a load you can already lift feel lighter while carrying it at an unhurried pace. Normal lifting limits and combat encumbrance still apply.*

| Attribute | Effect |
|:--:|---|
| **4** | A small heavy bag or toolkit feels lighter for about a minute. |
| **8** | An ordinary pack feels lighter for several minutes. |
| **12** | A chest or load you can already lift becomes more comfortable to carry over a short distance. |
| **16** | A bulky but portable load remains easier to carry along a longer safe path. |
| **20** | An exceptionally heavy yet still carryable everyday load becomes bearable for part of a short journey. |

*The weight of a house beam rests on your shoulder. When you look up, the street belongs to a long-vanished harbor town.*

|:--:|---|
| **4** | Leave a fingerprint or tiny symbol in stone, wood, or soft metal. |
| **8** | Emboss one clear palm-sized mark or a few rough characters. |
| **12** | Leave a short line of simple, coarsely pressed characters. |
| **16** | Form a distinct, palm-sized relief with a recognizable motif. |
| **20** | Create a small, simple commemorative relief without tools, but never precise craftsmanship or structural alterations. |

*Your fingers remember a farewell sign pressed into a gray city wall. You no longer know its language, only the grief.*

| **16** | Distinguish several broad resonance differences within the same accessible surface. |
| **20** | Feel a clearer local pattern of pressure and hollow space; it can suggest where to search, but precise discovery remains a Skill task. |

*The vibration becomes another hammer striking another stone. For a heartbeat you stand in a mine abandoned centuries ago.*

| **12** | Slow the aging of a small basket of ordinary food for one day. |
| **16** | Keep a small bag of simple perishables fresh for a few additional days. |
| **20** | Preserve a modest box of everyday perishable food for up to a week, without purifying or enhancing it. |

*The fruit keeps its scent. You remember baskets aboard a fishing boat and the dread of a winter arriving too soon.*

}}

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Rituals

Rituals are deliberate supernatural workings outside ordinary combat: interpretation, binding, cleansing, distant sight, memory, sealing, and spiritual transformation. They are **normal Skill Checks with a prescribed supernatural effect and a Sealed-Stone cost**, not another dice engine.

### Rituals Use the Skill Check Engine

**Every character knows all sixteen Core Rituals from creation onward.** To perform one, describe the method—song, forged sigil, herbal arrangement, prayer, dance, diagram or remembered gesture—provide the printed targets and materials, spend **one Tyhran Hour** and Seal the required Stones. Choose the **Attribute that genuinely powers the approach** and roll **Relevant Attribute k Mastery Rank**. The Ritual's Task Level follows the supernatural task or source, independently of the caster. Rituals are available to every character; the method defines how each character expresses the same magic.

A genuinely applied **learned Specialization** contributes **1/2/3 Free Raises at Rank 4/6/8**. A character using another approach performs the same Ritual with Paid Raises. This **does not impose mundane untrained Skill Disadvantage** on a Ritual. Inventing a superficial explanation for an unrelated high-Rank Skill does not earn Free Raises.

**Rituals use the Skill dice and TN engine with one important exception: Ritual tiers and all Raise effects are committed BEFORE rolling. Ordinary Skill effects are allocated AFTER success.**

- Start at **Ritual Task Level 3 (Base TN 12)** for each of the sixteen Rituals. If the target or task has a **genuinely higher** relevant Task Level, use it instead. **Base Ritual TN = max(3, relevant Task Level) x 4**, never from the caster's own MR.
- Select a named **effect tier 0-4**: the tier costs that many Raises and includes lower tiers on success.
- You may declare appropriate additional **Scope, Speed, Quality, Efficiency, Insight, or Safety** Raises. **All Rituals have a maximum of 8 total declared Raises**, whether or not a relevant Skill is learned; named plus generic, Paid plus Free.
- Allocate any eligible Free Raises **before rolling**. **Only Paid Raises add +4 TN**; one final TN decides the whole declared result. Missing it means failure, even if the roll meets the unraised base TN.
- Pay and **Seal** the Ritual's Stones **before** the attempt. They are a price for supernatural work, not Skill Points.

Printed Ritual limits override generic Raise effects. An ordinary Raise cannot create a new Power, raise a named Ritual tier above 4, grant magical healing, or restore a Scarred Health Bar.

### Rituals Are Not Skill Replacements

Rituals do not replace normal Skill Checks. If a task can reasonably be solved with a mundane Skill Check, use the Skill Check instead.

Rituals should not replace:

- hiding a camp,
- finding a safe road,
- following tracks,
- treating normal post-combat injuries,
- navigating wilderness,
- making a social promise,
- or discovering ordinary information through investigation.

Rituals are used when the effect is supernatural, long-lasting, hidden, spiritually significant, or impossible through normal action. Scarred Health Bars are not restored by Rituals unless a specific rule outside the Ritual system says so.

---

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Approaches, Not Skill Prerequisites

Skill Fields are **examples of how** a Ritual may be performed, never eligibility lists. A player may draw upon **any** suitable Specialization and use any Attribute justified by the described method:

| Field | A possible Ritual approach |
|---|---|
| **Physical** | Measured strength, breath, rhythm, or the placement of heavy stones |
| **Finesse** | Precise knots, hand gestures, engraving, or delicate components |
| **Survival** | Herbs, animal signs, roots, constellations, or the cycles of a place |
| **Science & Craft** | Forged seals, alchemical vessels, instruments, or geometric designs |
| **Occult** | Prayers, spirit names, sacred words, wards, or rites |
| **Social** | Song, communal witness, vows, storytelling, or guided remembrance |
| **Investigation** | Reconstructing the history and traces of an object or location |

These are **inspiration**, not a mandatory catalogue. The GM asks: *What exactly do you do? Why would this method work? Which Attribute expresses it?* A fitting learned Skill can supply Free Raises; the same Ritual works without that Skill.

---

### Choosing an Approach for a Ritual

1. Choose **any of the sixteen universally available Rituals**. Describe **what the character physically or spiritually does** and why it suits their background. The entry's **Example Approaches** are illustrations, never restrictions.
2. Choose the **Attribute** that best represents that working. A Blacksmith may forge a binding mark with Might; a singer may use Influence; a priest may pray with Resolve; a herbalist may use Vitality; a scholar may build an Intellect diagram. The GM approves a credible match.
3. If a **learned Specialization actually contributes**, name it and its Rank for Free Raises. No relevant Skill? The Ritual is still allowed with **no Free Raises** and no extra untrained penalty.
4. Set **Task Level 3 (TN 12) as the minimum**, or use the higher genuinely relevant source/task level; select a named effect tier and optional generic Raise effects (up to **8 total**), then mark Paid and Free Raises **before** the roll.
5. Pay and Seal Stones, finish the Ritual's required time, and resolve **one final Attribute k MR check**, unless Passive Success applies.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Ritual Task Level: The Task, Not the Caster

**Minimum Base Ritual TN = 12 (Task Level 3) for every listed Ritual.** If the actual target/source or the intrinsic task is harder, use its higher Task Level. **Base Ritual TN = 4 x max(3, relevant Task Level).** A Ritual involving the same seal, spirit, artifact, or place has the same difficulty regardless of the caster's MR. Do not charge a higher TN just for choosing a different credible Attribute or Skill approach.

| Ritual target or obstacle | Task Level to use |
|---|---|
| Ordinary site or unranked phenomenon | **Task Level 3 (TN 12)** by default; increase only for a concretely more difficult working |
| Existing Spell, Power, or Spell-based effect | The relevant **Power Level** |
| Artifact or levelled relic being examined, opened, or overcome | The relevant **Artifact Level** |
| Creature, hostile will, or creation with a known **Source MR** | **2 x Source MR** (the same tier as the maximum Power Level of that MR) |
| Multiple genuinely relevant sources | The highest **relevant** Task Level; do not add unrelated values |

The GM may set a **higher intrinsic Task Level** if the Ritual itself is more difficult than the source being affected (for example, deciphering a complex buried truth). Use the highest **genuinely relevant** Task Level, with a floor of 3. Do not inflate a TN merely because the caster is powerful, and do not make an ordinary site scale with the party. A named ritual effect tier changes the number of **declared Raises**, not this baseline difficulty.

**Examples:** Read faint unopposed Resonance at the minimum Task Level 3 = **TN 12**; identify a PL 6 magical working at Task Level 6 = **TN 24**; examine an Artifact Level 10 at Task Level 10 = **TN 40**; challenge a creature at MR 4 at Task Level 8 = **TN 32**. The relevant facts and the ritual's limits determine what can actually be learned or changed.

### Ritual Raises: One Final Target Number

A Ritual effect tier (0-4) determines the **named results** available from its printed table; tier 3 requires **three declared Raises** and normally provides tiers 0-3 on success. **Explicit alternative modes**, such as Seal Passage Raise 4 opening, replace incompatible lower-tier effects rather than stacking them. Further Raises, if eligible, use the same universal Skill catalogue. **Free Skill Raises count toward the named tier or generic benefits**, but not toward the TN increase.

**Final Ritual TN = (Task Level x 4) + (declared Paid Raises x 4).**

Agree on optional Scope, Speed, Quality, Efficiency, Insight, or Safety improvements **before** rolling. The total across named tier plus generic improvements, **Paid and Free together**, cannot exceed **8 for any Ritual**. A relevant learned Skill contributes Free Raises at Rank 4/6/8 but **does not limit access or the number of Paid Ritual Raises**. Printed Ritual requirements and Limits always override generic benefits; generic Raises cannot produce effects reserved for a higher named tier.

| Roll result | Resolution |
|---|---|
| **Below Final Ritual TN** | The entire declared attempt fails. No named tier, no generic Raise benefits; paid Stones remain Sealed. |
| **Meets Final Ritual TN** | The ritual takes effect, including its selected named tier (and compatible lower tiers; explicit alternative modes are exceptions) plus every legally declared generic improvement. |

A very high result never grants undeclared Raises, effects, or Stones back.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Ritual Cost

Rituals continue to pay their costs with **Sealed Stones** before the check. The original Stone-cost scale and **3-Stone ceiling** stay unchanged. **Stone cost counts all declared effects (Paid + Free)**; only the final TN distinguishes them.

| Total declared Raises (named tier + generic, Paid + Free) | Stone Cost |
|:--:|:--:|
| **0-1** | **1 Stone** |
| **2-3** | **2 Stones** |
| **4-8** | **3 Stones** |

Use any available Stone; its color need not match the Skill Field. The stones paid to attempt the ritual become **Sealed**, cannot recover through normal Round recovery, and return after a **Safe Haven Rest**, unless the ritual has a more restrictive printed rule (for example, Word of Recall). **Sealed Stones remain spent on failure.** A group may contribute Stones with GM approval, but performs **one lead-caster check** with at most **8 declared Raises**. Only the lead caster's applicable Specialization grants Free Raises.

### Additional Raises Do Not Rewrite a Ritual

The printed **named tiers 0-4** establish the ritual's distinctive supernatural effects. Other declared Raises may improve only appropriate secondary features using the normal Skill catalogue: **Scope**, **Speed**, **Quality**, **Efficiency**, **Insight**, or **Safety**. For example, **Speed 1** can halve a normal 1-Tyhran-Hour ritual to **1 Forearm**. **Insight 1** can reveal one further supportable clue *within the named tier already reached*. Scope cannot circumvent a ritual's fixed targets, range restrictions, or named area upgrades. **Efficiency never reduces Sealed-Stone costs**; Safety cannot cancel explicit ritual dangers. No optional Raise supplies a spell, an entirely new magical effect, a higher printed tier, or a bypass of Ritual Limits.

---

### Hidden Truths, Resonance, and Artifacts

Rituals do not automatically reveal everything. Some Rituals, especially **Read Resonance**, read the traces left behind when power presses into the world. This trace is called **Resonance**. Resonance is not the same as generic magic.

Resonance may be left behind by:

- Ritual workings,
- Artifacts and relics,
- Wards and Seals,
- demonic influence,
- divine residue,
- necrotic or death-bound power,
- curses and spiritual bindings,
- possession pressure,
- planar or Veil disturbances,
- and other supernatural scene effects.

Reading Resonance does not reveal exact Powers, Stone choices, Stone colors, character builds, Power names, or mechanical values. It shows that something unnatural, awakened, bound, cursed, divine, demonic, ritualized, or spiritually significant has affected the target or area. Powerful items, Artifacts, divine relics, demonic marks, intelligent objects, ancient wards, and spiritually significant places may hide, distort, or selectively reveal information.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
An Artifact may only show what it wants to show. A demonic mark may present a false history. A divine relic may conceal its deeper purpose until the bearer proves worthy. A ward may reveal its outer structure while hiding the name of its creator. A place may hold Resonance without revealing who caused it. The GM may divide information into layers.

| Layer | What it may reveal |
|---|---|
| **Surface** | Whether Resonance is present, broad type, visible function, obvious category |
| **Pattern** | Strength, age, state, emotional pressure, origin style, repeated structure |
| **Structure** | Anchor, binding logic, hidden restriction, dormant power, ward logic, seal logic |
| **Truth** | True source, real purpose, creator, taint, pact, command phrase, awakening condition |
| **Secret** | Dangerous or protected knowledge the target actively resists revealing |

The **selected named Ritual tier** determines which layers of information a successful Ritual reaches. A tier-0 success may reveal only that readable Resonance exists; higher tiers can reveal source, pattern, direction, or a masked truth. Extra Insight Raises do not unlock higher named tiers. Some secrets still require a later scene, key, bond, connection, or more capable effect. A failed Ritual does not establish that no Resonance exists. It simply supplies no reliable ritual reading.

---

### Ritual Time

**All sixteen listed Rituals take 1 Tyhran Hour** of preparation and execution (2 Forearms, about 59 Earth minutes). They are **never combat actions**. An eligible **Speed 1 Raise**, declared before rolling, may halve this time to **1 Forearm**; it adds +4 TN if Paid or +0 if Free. Rituals cannot normally be accelerated below 1 Forearm, and further Speed Raises on a standard Ritual have no effect. An interrupted Ritual normally fails and its paid Stones remain Sealed. Special timing or activation restrictions still apply after a Ritual is established (for example, Word of Recall).

---

### Ritual Failure

If the Ritual Skill Check fails, the Ritual does not produce its intended effect. The Stones paid for the Ritual are still Sealed. The GM may apply a fitting consequence, especially if the Ritual involved dangerous Resonance, hostile entities, corrupted places, ancient relics, demonic marks, the dead, dreams, or the Veil.

Possible consequences include:

- the caster faces a Fear Check (Resolve k1; fixed bands: below 8 → +2 Fear, 8–15 → +1 Fear, 16+ → +0 Fear),
- the caster experiences an unsettling, unreliable impression **without receiving a successful Ritual tier**,
- something notices the caster,
- the target becomes harder to affect again,
- a ward reacts,
- a spirit, demon, relic, or place answers incorrectly or dangerously,
- the caster receives only a symbolic impression instead of useful clarity.

Failure should create pressure, not shut down the story.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide
### Ritual Summary — One Roll, Any Credible Method

1. Choose **any of the 16 Core Rituals** (all are available to every character for free from the start). Describe **how you perform it**; there are **no required Skill Fields or specific Specializations**.
2. Choose a fitting **Attribute** for that method. If a learned Skill truly contributes, use its **1/2/3 Free Raises** at Rank 4/6/8; otherwise every Raise is Paid. No untrained Disadvantage just for lacking a ritual Skill.
3. Start with **Task Level 3 (Base TN 12)**, then use a higher Task Level when the actual work or source requires it, never based on the caster's MR. **Base TN = 4 x max(3, relevant Task Level)**.
4. Declare a named effect tier **0-4** and suitable generic Raise effects, **maximum 8 Raises total**. Count Free and Paid Raises before rolling.
5. **Final TN = Base TN + 4 x Paid Raises**. Pay and **Seal** Stones based on **all** Raises: 1 Stone for 0-1, 2 for 2-3, 3 for 4-8.
6. Perform the Ritual for **1 Tyhran Hour** (or 1 Forearm with an eligible Speed Raise), then roll **relevant Attribute k MR**, unless a legal Passive Success applies. Success grants exactly the declared effects; failure grants none and Stones remain Sealed.

### Passive Ritual Success

A learned Skill does **not automatically complete a supernatural Ritual** merely because the character knows routine professional tasks. The normal **Passive Skill Value = 2 x relevant Attribute** may resolve a familiar, safe, uncontested, non-urgent Ritual **only if it reaches the entire final TN**, including Paid Raises. Supernatural opposition, danger, unstable sites, or meaningful immediate risk require a roll. **The Stones are still Sealed**, with or without a roll. Passive success never creates undeclared effects.

### Example: Three Ways to Cleanse a Shrine

A shrine has a fixed **Task Level 4 (Base TN 16)**. One character traces an alchemical circle using **Alchemy 4 + Intellect**; a second chants through **Performance 4 + Influence**; a third lays out a pattern of stones using **Survival + Wits**, without a relevant learned Specialization. **Each can attempt the same Purify Ground Ritual.** They must describe their method; the Task Level does not change because the method or caster changes.

All declare **named tier 2** (two Raises, 2 Sealed Stones). Alchemy 4 and Performance 4 each provide **one Free Raise and one Paid Raise**, so their final TN is **20**. The third character has **two Paid Raises**, so the final TN is **24**. If a character meets their final TN, tiers 0-2 occur. A high roll adds no undeclared effect.

### Example: No Learned Ritual Skill

Another caster with no related Specialization reads **PL 6** Resonance at **Base TN 24**, declaring **named tier 2**. Both Raises are Paid: **Final TN 32**, **2 Sealed Stones**. The Ritual is permitted; the character's lack of training only means there are no Free Raises. With a genuinely applicable **Warding Traditions 4**, one Raise would be Free and the final TN would be **28** instead.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide
### Read Resonance (Ritual)

*You quiet your senses and listen for the pressure left behind when power touched the world.*

---

**Time:** 1 Tyhran Hour  
**Base TN:** **12 minimum** (higher for harder sources or tasks)  
**Effect Duration:** Concentration, up to 1 Forearm
**Requirement:** The caster must focus on an area, object, threshold, corpse, mark, relic, symbol, wound, seal, or visible phenomenon.  
**Example Approaches:** Occult (listen to old names), Science & Craft (tune instruments), Investigation (reconstruct traces), Physical (measured tapping).

---

#### Named Ritual Effect Tiers (0-4)

| Raise Level | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Raise 0** | You learn whether readable Resonance is present within **10 meters**. If Resonance is present, you learn its broad type: **Ritual**, **Artifact**, **Ward**, **Seal**, **Demonic**, **Divine**, **Necrotic**, **Cursed**, **Spiritual**, **Planar**, or **Unknown**. |
| **Raise 1** | You learn the Resonance's rough strength and state: faint, strong, active, dormant, fresh, old, fading, unstable, concealed, or corrupted. You can tell whether a Ritual was worked here, an Artifact was active here, a Ward or Seal is present, or a demonic or divine influence touched the area. |
| **Raise 2** | You read the Resonance pattern. You learn what kind of thing the Resonance clings to: a place, object, body, threshold, mark, wound, altar, doorway, weapon, blood, name, or spiritual anchor. You may also learn whether the Resonance was created by protection, binding, hunger, command, sacrifice, death, memory, travel, concealment, or corruption. |
| **Raise 3** | You may trace one Resonance to its nearest source, anchor, exit point, or strongest remaining direction within **100 meters**, if such a trace still exists. This can reveal that a powerful Artifact was carried away, a Ritual was worked from a specific point, a Seal is anchored elsewhere, or a demonic influence entered through a specific mark or threshold. |
| **Raise 4** | You pierce common masking, false traces, and concealment. You learn the true dominant Resonance beneath the surface layer, unless it is protected by a stronger story-level ward, Artifact will, divine concealment, demonic deception, or specific GM protection. |

---

#### Scope & Limits
Read Resonance detects **supernatural traces** and identifies their broad character or origin. Investigation, Tracking and Perception handle mundane evidence; **Learn Artifact** examines an Artifact's function and binding. The achieved tier determines the depth of the reading. Failure gives no reliable conclusion.

> **Danger:** Strong Resonance can answer back; ancient Seals, demonic marks, divine ruins and intelligent Artifacts may notice a reading.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide
### Locate Object (Ritual)

*You close your eyes and trace a pattern in the air. A pull forms within your mind, guiding you toward what you seek.*

---

**Time:** 1 Tyhran Hour  
**Base TN:** **12 minimum** (higher for harder sources or tasks)  
**Effect Duration:** Concentration, up to 1 Forearm
**Requirement:** You must have seen or touched the object, or know its precise form.  
**Example Approaches:** Survival (dowsing and landmarks), Science & Craft (compass or sympathetic tool), Investigation (study connections), Social (recall a promise).

---

#### Named Ritual Effect Tiers (0-4)

| Raise Level | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Raise 0** | You sense the direction of one specific object you describe within **60 meters**, provided no major ward, consecrated barrier, sealed vault, or Resonance-blocking obstruction blocks the link. |
| **Raise 1** | The range expands to **300 meters**. You can distinguish between multiple similar items and sense which one best matches your mental image. |
| **Raise 2** | You receive short sensory flashes from the object's immediate environment, such as smell, texture, temperature, pressure, or nearby sound. |
| **Raise 3** | The Ritual can reach through thin barriers, shallow ground, or up to **2 meters of stone**. Range extends to **1 kilometer**. |
| **Raise 4** | You glimpse one brief memory tied to the object, such as who last touched it, where it last rested, or what strong emotion clings to it. Range extends to **5 kilometers**, unless a stronger ward blocks the link. |

#### Scope & Limits
Locate Object tracks a **specified object or defined object type**, including one carried by a creature. Tracking and Investigation address ordinary evidence; **Learn Artifact** reveals an Artifact's purpose.

> **Danger:** Powerful, cursed or intelligent objects may notice your search.

---

### Clairvoyance (Ritual)

*You open a window through distance, letting your mind wander where your body cannot.*

---

**Time:** 1 Tyhran Hour  
**Base TN:** **12 minimum** (higher for harder sources or tasks)  
**Effect Duration:** Concentration, up to 1 Forearm
**Requirement:** You must name a place you know or a creature familiar to you.  
**Example Approaches:** Occult (meditation), Science & Craft (mirrors and lenses), Investigation (mental reconstruction), Social (rhythmic chanting).

---

#### Named Ritual Effect Tiers (0-4)

| Raise Level | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Raise 0** | You project your senses to a known location or familiar creature within **1 kilometer**. You can see from that point as though present, though sight is hazy and colorless. |
| **Raise 1** | The vision becomes clearer and gains sound. You can faintly hear conversations, movement, and nearby environmental noise. |
| **Raise 2** | You can shift the viewpoint up to **20 meters** around the initial location, or follow the creature slowly at half walking speed. |
| **Raise 3** | The image becomes vivid and detailed. You may focus on small objects, writings, symbols, faces, or gestures clearly. |
| **Raise 4** | You may scry across any distance on the same plane. With a strong sympathetic connection and GM approval, the Ritual may glimpse across a planar boundary. After ending the Ritual, your body remains senseless for a brief moment. |
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Scope & Limits
Clairvoyance observes a known place or familiar creature from a distance. Wards, divine concealment, demonic deception and protected minds apply normally; the caster remains an observer rather than an actor at the remote location.

> **Danger:** Distant or planar sight may attract the attention of what is watched.

### Threshold Alarm (Ritual)

*You mark a threshold, camp edge, door, window, or boundary so that the place remembers being crossed.*

---

**Time:** 1 Tyhran Hour  
**Base TN:** **12 minimum** (higher for harder sources or tasks)  
**Effect Duration:** Until the next Safe Haven Rest or one Tyhran Day, whichever comes first
**Requirement:** You must mark or touch the threshold, boundary, or protected entry point.  
**Example Approaches:** Survival (knotted boundary line), Finesse (precise markings), Science & Craft (chimes and wire), Occult (warding words).

---

#### Named Ritual Effect Tiers (0-4)

| Raise Level | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Raise 0** | Ward one doorway, window, tunnel mouth, room boundary, or camp edge. When a creature crosses it, one named wardkeeper within **1 kilometer** receives a clear alarm. This does not block, harm, slow, or reveal the creature. |
| **Raise 1** | You may name up to **Mastery Rank** creatures who do not trigger the alarm. |
| **Raise 2** | The alarm can cover a small house, campsite, chamber, or connected room cluster instead of one entry point. The wardkeeper senses the direction of the breach. |
| **Raise 3** | The alarm conveys the approximate number and size of creatures crossing the threshold. Mundane stealth does not prevent the alarm. |
| **Raise 4** | The alarm may wake all named allies inside the warded area. It cannot be silenced or bypassed without overcoming the established warding TN or using a specific countermeasure. |

#### Scope & Limits
Threshold Alarm **warns of a crossing**; the named tiers determine its reach and the detail conveyed. Intruders remain free to enter, and defensive protection requires a separate effect.

---

### Seal Passage (Ritual)

*You press power into a door, gate, hatch, tunnel, archway, or ancient seam, telling the world that this way is closed.*

---

**Time:** 1 Tyhran Hour  
**Base TN:** **12 minimum** (higher for harder sources or tasks)  
**Effect Duration:** Until broken, opened, dismissed, or overcome
**Requirement:** You must touch the passage, seal, door, gate, hatch, or boundary being affected.  
**Example Approaches:** Science & Craft (forge a seal), Physical (stonework gestures), Occult (inscribe a rune), Social (bind a declared promise).

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Named Ritual Effect Tiers (0-4)

| Raise Level | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Raise 0** | You seal one accessible mundane passage, such as a door, gate, hatch, window, tunnel mouth, or stone seam. The passage closes and locks without needing a key. If you are attempting to open an existing sealed or forgotten passage, you instead confirm whether such a passage or seal truly exists. |
| **Raise 1** | The sealed passage cannot be opened by mundane keys, latches, or ordinary handling. It must be forced, ritually opened, dismissed by the caster, or overcome with an appropriate check against the established warding TN. |
| **Raise 2** | The sealed passage becomes twice as physically durable. If the object has Health, Structure, or a similar value, double that value for the purpose of breaking through. |
| **Raise 3** | The sealed passage becomes as hard as solid granite for the purpose of forcing, breaking, burning, cutting, or smashing it. |
| **Raise 4 - Alternative Opening Mode** | You may use this Ritual to open an existing sealed, hidden, or forgotten passage, but only if such a passage truly exists. This mode costs **3 Stones** and **does not also apply any Raise 0-3 sealing effects**. It cannot create a new tunnel, doorway, road, or exit where none exists. |

#### Scope & Limits
Seal Passage applies a supernatural seal to an **existing passage** or opens such a seal at Raise 4. Declare **sealing or opening before rolling**; the Raise-4 opening mode replaces the lower sealing tiers. Ordinary locks, construction and excavation use their relevant Skills.

### Purify Ground (Ritual)

*You cleanse a place where something wrong has soaked into the world.*

---

**Time:** 1 Tyhran Hour  
**Base TN:** **12 minimum** (higher for harder sources or tasks)  
**Effect Duration:** Instant, with lasting consequences as described by the Raise result
**Requirement:** You must remain within the area being purified for the full Ritual.  
**Example Approaches:** Survival (herbal smoke), Science & Craft (salt patterns), Occult (prayer), Social (purifying song).

---

#### Named Ritual Effect Tiers (0-4)

| Raise Level | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Raise 0** | Purify a small area, such as a room, grave, altar, blood circle, ritual mark, corpse pile, shrine, or battlefield focus, from surface-level necrotic, demonic, infernal, or corrupt ritual residue. |
| **Raise 1** | Before the residue fades, you learn its broad type and emotional pressure, such as hunger, fear, command, grief, sacrifice, binding, or desecration. |
| **Raise 2** | You suppress one minor ongoing scene effect caused by the residue, such as whispers, corpse-stirring, nightmare pressure, ritual sickness, or spiritual contamination. |
| **Raise 3** | You purify a larger connected site, such as a crypt chamber, ruined shrine, small battlefield, ritual cellar, desecrated house, or corrupted grove. |
| **Raise 4** | You may break one active residue-anchor, lingering desecration, corpse-binding, or demonic trace if the declared Task Level adequately accounts for the relevant source. The GM may require a specific focus, name, remains, or symbol. |

#### Scope & Limits
Purify Ground cleanses **spiritual and ritual residue** from an existing site at the declared tier. Physical evidence, structural damage, Health and Scarred Bars remain as they were; Safe Haven and Healing follow their own rules.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Learn Artifact (Ritual)

*You study a relic until its surface stops being an object and starts becoming a history.*

---

**Time:** 1 Tyhran Hour  
**Base TN:** **12 minimum** (higher for harder sources or tasks)  
**Effect Duration:** Instant
**Requirement:** You must hold, touch, examine, or remain close to the Artifact or relic for the full Ritual.  
**Example Approaches:** Science & Craft (inspect workmanship), Investigation (reconstruct provenance), Occult (speak its names), Social (ritual testimony).

---

#### Named Ritual Effect Tiers (0-4)

| Raise Level | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Raise 0** | You learn the Artifact's visible category, basic function, occupied Slot, binding status, and whether it is mundane, Resonant, Echo-bound, cursed, awakened, dormant, or unknown. |
| **Raise 1** | You learn its origin style, creator tradition, previous owner trace, emotional residue, or broad purpose. |
| **Raise 2** | You learn one hidden restriction, cost, dormant function, binding rule, warning sign, or danger connected to using or bonding with it. |
| **Raise 3** | You may learn an awakening condition, command phrase, pact mark, curse logic, hidden limitation, or why the Artifact rejects or accepts a bearer. |
| **Raise 4** | You reach one deeper truth: true creator, true purpose, concealed taint, secret command, hidden price, or a protected memory the Artifact is willing or forced to reveal. |

#### Scope & Limits
Learn Artifact reveals **functions, binding rules, costs or hidden history** in proportion to its achieved tier. Intelligent, divine or demonic Artifacts can keep secrets behind appropriately high source difficulty. Read Resonance identifies their supernatural character; this Ritual investigates the Artifact itself.

### Forgotten Memory (Ritual)

*You touch a place or object and listen for the feelings it could not let go.*

---

**Time:** 1 Tyhran Hour  
**Base TN:** **12 minimum** (higher for harder sources or tasks)  
**Effect Duration:** Concentration, up to 1 Forearm
**Requirement:** You must touch the object, location, wall, floor, weapon, relic, or remains being read.  
**Example Approaches:** Investigation (reconstruct a scene), Occult (meditation), Social (lamentation), Survival (read a place through living signs).

---

#### Named Ritual Effect Tiers (0-4)

| Raise Level | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Raise 0** | You sense the strongest emotional residue tied to the place or object, such as fear, grief, hunger, relief, rage, devotion, betrayal, or hope. |
| **Raise 1** | You receive one sensory fragment connected to that residue: sound, smell, touch, color, pressure, taste, or a single image. |
| **Raise 2** | You witness a brief sequence of events around the memory. The sequence is emotional and symbolic, but it shows what kind of event created the residue. |
| **Raise 3** | You identify one important person, symbol, object, phrase, direction, or relationship connected to the memory. |
| **Raise 4** | You step into the memory for a brief moment and experience it from within. If the memory is violent, demonic, death-bound, or horrific, the GM may call for a **Fear Check (Resolve k1; fixed Fear result bands)**. |
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Scope & Limits
Forgotten Memory reads **subjective emotional residue in a place or object**. Its impressions may be incomplete or symbolic; Investigation establishes factual conclusions. Personal former-life memories belong to **Echo Imprints**.

---

### Word of Recall (Ritual)

*You etch a hidden mark of return upon a sanctuary, a promise to the world that you will one day come home.*

---

**Time:** 1 Tyhran Hour  
**Base TN:** **12 minimum** (higher for harder sources or tasks)  
**Effect Duration:** Permanent until used, dismissed, broken, or removed
**Requirement:** The place must be meaningful to you and prepared through prayer, blood, sacrifice, craft, memory, or witness.  
**Example Approaches:** Survival (route knots), Science & Craft (engrave a homeward mark), Occult (true-name rite), Social (ceremony of belonging).

---

#### Special Cost Rule

The Stones paid for Word of Recall remain Sealed while the mark exists. They return only after the mark is used, dismissed, broken, or removed, followed by a Safe Haven Rest. Activating the mark is not a combat action. Unless a specific rule says otherwise, activating the recall requires at least **1 Forearm** of focus outside combat.

---

#### Named Ritual Effect Tiers (0-4)

| Raise Level | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Raise 0** | You bind a spiritual mark to the location. At a later time, you may return to that exact point. The mark fades when used. |
| **Raise 1** | You may bring one willing ally who stands within **2 meters** of you when the recall is activated. |
| **Raise 2** | The recall becomes gentler. Willing allies arrive standing, stable, and with fragile gear intact unless the destination itself is dangerous. |
| **Raise 3** | The recall can pass through minor wards, interference, or sanctum pressure, unless a stronger effect specifically blocks planar or spatial travel. |
| **Raise 4** | The mark becomes a lasting bond. It may be used once every 7 Tyhran Days without fading, but the Stones remain Sealed while the bond exists. |

#### Scope & Limits
Word of Recall anchors a return to a **previously visited, meaningful location**. The destination retains its actual hazards. The return uses the listed out-of-combat activation, subject to binding seals, travel prohibitions and exceptional warding.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}

{{wide
### Dreamwalk (Ritual)

*You step through the mirror of sleep, drifting into another's dreams to seek truth, warning, or solace.*

---

**Time:** 1 Tyhran Hour  
**Base TN:** **12 minimum** (higher for harder sources or tasks)  
**Effect Duration:** Up to 1 Forearm inside the dream
**Requirement:** The target must be asleep and willing, or their soul must be unguarded with GM approval.  
**Example Approaches:** Occult (trance), Social (lullaby), Science & Craft (dream mandala), Survival (sleep herbs).

---

#### Named Ritual Effect Tiers (0-4)

| Raise Level | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Raise 0** | You enter the target's dreamscape, perceiving symbolic visions of their fears, memories, desires, or current emotional state. You may speak within the dream as a guiding voice. |
