| **Raise 1** | You may interact with elements of the dream, reshape minor symbols, calm a nightmare scene, or create a safe image for conversation. |
| **Raise 2** | You can communicate directly with the dreamer's subconscious. A simple message, warning, apology, or promise may persist after waking. |
| **Raise 3** | You glimpse buried memories or truths the target hides even from themselves. These are emotional and symbolic, not guaranteed factual records. |
| **Raise 4** | You risk full immersion, walking the dream as if real. The dreamer may see you clearly. If the dream is hostile, demonic, cursed, or traumatic, the GM may call for a **Fear Check (Resolve k1; fixed Fear result bands)**. |

#### Scope & Limits
Dreamwalk allows an encounter **within a dream**, respecting the sleeper's agency and defenses. Protected secrets require an adequate target-based Task Level, the appropriate named tier and GM approval. Social persuasion and lasting psychological recovery follow their own rules.

---

### Last Light (Ritual)

*You kindle a flame for the fallen, guiding what remains beyond the veil so it may rest.*

---

**Time:** 1 Tyhran Hour  
**Base TN:** **12 minimum** (higher for harder sources or tasks)  
**Effect Duration:** Instant, with lasting consequences as described by the Raise result
**Requirement:** Performed within sight of the body, grave, ashes, relic, or death-place of the deceased.  
**Example Approaches:** Social (farewell and witness), Occult (liturgy), Science & Craft (memorial craft), Survival (funerary herbs).

---

#### Named Ritual Effect Tiers (0-4)

| Raise Level | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Raise 0** | You perform a rite that severs the soul's lingering ties, helping it pass peacefully and preventing ordinary undeath or spiritual corruption from taking hold. |
| **Raise 1** | The immediate burial area is purified from minor necrotic residue, corpse-stirring, grave whispers, or restless spiritual pressure. |
| **Raise 2** | You may carry one short message from the living to the dead, such as farewell, forgiveness, warning, or love. |
| **Raise 3** | If the spirit is willing or able, it may manifest briefly, sharing one phrase, image, emotion, or final impression before passing beyond reach. |
| **Raise 4** | The grave, body, or death-place becomes hallowed against future desecration or necromantic use unless overcome by a stronger effect. |
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Scope & Limits
Last Light guides a deceased soul's **natural passage** and protects ordinary funerary rites from spiritual contamination or undeath. It is a funerary working; resurrection, combat against undead and Safe Haven recovery use their existing systems.

---

### Augury — Signs of Tomorrow (Ritual)

*You ask the world which shadow a decision may cast.*

**Time:** 1 Tyhran Hour  
**Base TN:** **12 minimum** (higher for harder sources or tasks)  
**Effect Duration:** Instant
**Requirement:** State **one specific course of action** you genuinely intend to attempt within the next Tyhran Day; arrange a symbolic medium, such as ashes, cards, water, metal shavings, bones, or written omens.  
**Example Approaches:** Occult (prayer), Survival (natural signs), Science & Craft (star chart), Social (oracular storytelling).

#### Named Ritual Effect Tiers (0-4)

| Raise Level | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Raise 0** | You receive a symbolic omen: **favorable**, **dangerous**, **mixed**, or **uncertain**, based on the course's plausible near-term consequences. |
| **Raise 1** | The omen suggests the broad **nature of the main opportunity or danger**, such as betrayal, violence, spiritual pressure, loss, or assistance. |
| **Raise 2** | One image, sound, or phrase hints at a **condition or action that could alter** the likely outcome. |
| **Raise 3** | One important **person, place, object, or choice** connected to the possible outcome appears symbolically. |
| **Raise 4** | You glimpse one **plausible short future scene** and a significant condition under which it could occur. It is not an inevitable timeline. |

#### Scope & Limits
Augury offers a **plausible omen based on current circumstances**, not a fixed future. A changed situation can change the omen. Repeating an unchanged question adds no new information; ambiguous questions receive ambiguous signs.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Circle of Quiet (Ritual)

*You draw a boundary around a little peace, asking the world's restless whispers to soften.*

---

**Time:** 1 Tyhran Hour  
**Base TN:** **12 minimum** (higher for harder sources or tasks)  
**Effect Duration:** Until the next Safe Haven Rest or 1 Tyhran Day, whichever comes first
**Requirement:** Remain within the intended space and mark its boundary throughout the working.  
**Example Approaches:** Occult (quiet litany), Social (shared lullaby), Science & Craft (harmonic chimes), Survival (woven boughs).

---

#### Named Ritual Effect Tiers (0-4)

| Raise Level | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Raise 0** | A **2-meter area** grows quiet in a **spiritual**, not acoustically silent, sense. Faint ambient whispers and harmless psychic unease from old residue soften. |
| **Raise 1** | The circle may cover **one modest chamber** rather than a 2-meter area. Normal speech and ordinary environmental sounds remain audible. |
| **Raise 2** | Willing sleepers inside experience relief from **minor, non-hostile residual dream imagery**. Genuine nightmares caused by active curses, Powers, or hostile spirits remain effective. |
| **Raise 3** | Minor recurring **ambient echoes** from an old event (distant knocks, flickering spiritual afterimages, meaningless murmurs) quiet down while the circle lasts. The source itself is not purified. |
| **Raise 4** | The circle may cover **a small connected group of rooms or campsite**, sharing its peace with willing occupants. It still cannot overpower an active hostile working. |

#### Scope & Limits
Circle of Quiet softens **ambient spiritual noise** so a place feels calmer. Normal sound, hostile Powers, fear, healing and rest use their existing rules. **Threshold Alarm** detects crossings; **Purify Ground** removes residue.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Binding Oath (Ritual)

*You give a spoken promise a witness that words alone cannot provide.*

---

**Time:** 1 Tyhran Hour  
**Base TN:** **12 minimum** (higher for harder sources or tasks)  
**Effect Duration:** As stated by the chosen tier, no longer than 1 Lunar Month
**Requirement:** Two **informed, willing** oathmakers agree to **clear, specific terms** and take part in the full ceremony. They may create a shared witness token.  
**Example Approaches:** Social (public witness or song), Occult (names and vow), Science & Craft (engraved token), Investigation (precise wording).

---

#### Named Ritual Effect Tiers (0-4)

| Raise Level | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Raise 0** | A faint shared sign manifests for **1 Tyhran Day**, bearing witness that both parties freely spoke **these agreed terms**. It does not prove that either intended to comply. |
| **Raise 1** | For up to **7 Tyhran Days**, either party may touch the sign to recall the **precise terms** they voluntarily accepted. The Ritual does not alter memories or beliefs. |
| **Raise 2** | The sign can show whether **one clearly measurable condition** has been **fulfilled, broken, or remains unresolved**, if the facts are accessible to the working. Ambiguous duties remain unresolved. |
| **Raise 3** | When that objective condition is fulfilled or broken, both willing signers feel **one brief, non-damaging spiritual echo**, even if separated on the same plane. It does not reveal location or intention. |
| **Raise 4** | The shared witness token preserves the agreed words and the observable condition's status for **up to 1 Lunar Month**, visible to anyone the signers allow to examine it. No private thoughts are recorded. |

#### Scope & Limits
Binding Oath records a **mutually voluntary, clearly worded promise** as a lasting supernatural witness. It observes declared conditions without compelling conduct or punishing breaches. Unclear terms yield an uncertain result; **Oath Ember** is the temporary personal sign.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Voice of the Wild (Ritual)

*For a short while you listen to the meaning beneath an animal's cries, and let it hear your own.*

---

**Time:** 1 Tyhran Hour  
**Base TN:** **12 minimum** (higher for harder sources or tasks)  
**Effect Duration:** Concentration, up to 1 Forearm after completion
**Requirement:** A **living animal** must be within **10 meters** when communication begins. The caster prepares natural tokens, gestures, sounds, or another suitable working.  
**Example Approaches:** Survival (Animal Handling or field signs), Social (song), Science & Craft (sympathetic instrument), Occult (spirit-tongue rite).

---

#### Named Ritual Effect Tiers (0-4)

| Raise Level | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Raise 0** | Sense one animal's **simple present emotion or need**: hunger, fear, comfort, distress, attachment, or territorial tension. |
| **Raise 1** | Understand **simple impressions and intentions** the animal is already expressing: shelter, food, danger, young, familiar companion, or a remembered trail. |
| **Raise 2** | Convey **one basic peaceful intention or request** through feelings and images. The animal understands the request but may refuse or ignore it. |
| **Raise 3** | Exchange one **recent, firsthand sensory impression**, such as a familiar path, strange smell, passing herd, or nearby landmark the animal noticed. It is not a map or objective report. |
| **Raise 4** | Hold a short exchange of related impressions with **a few willing nearby animals**, allowing a composite sense of what **they personally witnessed**. Each remains independent. |

#### Scope & Limits
Voice of the Wild conveys **an animal's simple feelings and perceptions**, subject to the animal's own awareness and willingness. Training, tracking, taming and commands remain Animal Handling or other relevant tasks. Supernatural beasts may present their own resistance.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Whispers of Lost Tongues (Ritual)

*An ancient alphabet stirs with the voice of someone who once knew how to speak it.*

---

**Time:** 1 Tyhran Hour  
**Base TN:** **12 minimum** (higher for harder sources or tasks)  
**Effect Duration:** Concentration, up to 1 Forearm after completion
**Requirement:** A **real sample** of unfamiliar writing or speech must be available during preparation. The working focuses on **one language or script**, not all unknown languages.  
**Example Approaches:** Investigation (study letterforms), Science & Craft (reconstruct a text), Social (chant the cadence), Occult (listen to old names).

---

#### Named Ritual Effect Tiers (0-4)

| Raise Level | Effect |
|---|---|
| **Raise 0** | Sense the **broad purpose or tone** of one short passage: warning, prayer, dedication, instruction, lament, record, or threat. |
| **Raise 1** | Grasp the **general meaning** of one short paragraph or spoken statement, without guaranteeing exact words, names, or numbers. |
| **Raise 2** | Understand a **brief ordinary notice or instruction** well enough to act on its plain meaning. Cultural idioms remain uncertain. |
| **Raise 3** | Recover **significant names, dates, numbers, or one crucial nuance** from a brief passage, provided they are not deliberately hidden or mystically sealed. |
| **Raise 4** | Comprehend the **broad faithful meaning** of up to **one short page** or one speaker's ongoing simple speech during the Ritual's effect duration. This does not teach fluency. |

#### Scope & Limits
Whispers of Lost Tongues reads **the general meaning of a present language sample** at the achieved tier. Codes, ciphers, guarded command phrases and disputed translations may require research, source difficulty or Investigation; speaking and writing fluency remain separate.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Summons

Summons are creatures created through **Bound Stones** and improved with **Summon Tokens**. Every Summon uses the same mechanical foundation. There are no separate Familiar, Companion, or Host classes. A Summon may be expressed as a beast, spirit, demon, construct, undead, elemental, shadow, echo, weapon-shard, swarm, plant-creature, memory, or any other fitting form. The mechanics define what the Summon can do. The player defines what the Summon is.

---

### Creating a Summon Bond

To create a Summon Bond, bind at least **1 Stone**. A Bound Stone is removed from the character's usable Stone Pool and does not regenerate while the bond persists. Every Bound Stone grants **8 Summon Tokens**, including the first Stone.

**Formula:**  
**Summon Tokens = Bound Stones x 8**

| **Bound Stones** | **Summon Tokens** |
|:--:|:--:|
| 1 | 8 Tokens |
| 2 | 16 Tokens |
| 3 | 24 Tokens |
| 4 | 32 Tokens |
| 5 | 40 Tokens |
| 6 | 48 Tokens |
| 7 | 56 Tokens |
| 8 | 64 Tokens |
| 9 | 72 Tokens |
| 10 | 80 Tokens |

Bonus Summon Tokens granted by a Passive, Artifact, Active Buff, or other explicit rule are added after this calculation. Bonus Tokens do not count as additional Bound Stones and remain subject to every spending restriction written by their source.

---

### Universal Base Summon

| **Stat** | **Starting Value** |
|---|:--:|
| HP | 10 |
| Armor | 0 |
| Evade | 4 |
| Attack | 2d8 |
| Damage | 1d8 |
| Movement | 8 m in one chosen Movement Mode |
| Attacks | 1 Summon Attack |
| Specials | None |
| Shared Senses | None |
| Bodies | 1 Summon Body |

The Base Summon is intentionally simple. Tokens determine whether it becomes a durable guardian, an elite attacker, a scout, a group of weak bodies, or another concept.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### One Movement Mode

When the Summon Bond is created, choose exactly **one Movement Mode** that fits its Expression:

- Walking
- Flying
- Swimming
- Climbing

All Bodies belonging to that Summon Bond use the chosen Movement Mode. The selected Movement Mode begins at **8 m** and may be increased to a maximum of **16 m**. The same Movement Mode may be improved more than once. A Summon Bond may never buy or possess a second permanent Movement Mode through Summon Tokens. Teleport, Burrow, Phase Passage, and similar premium movement are not normal Movement Modes. They require a canonical Movement Power or another explicit rule. A purchased Movement Power temporarily replaces normal Movement when used. It does not grant a second permanent Movement Mode.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Summon Token Costs

Summon Tokens may buy improvements to the Bond, to an individual Body, or to the Bond's selected **Summon Skill Dice**. These are not player Specialization resources.

| **Upgrade** | **Scope** | **Starting Value** | **Cost** | **Gain** |
|---|---|:--:|:--:|---|
| HP | Body | 10 HP | 1 Token | +20 HP |
| Armor | Body | 0 Armor | 2 Tokens | +4 Armor |
| Evade | Body | 4 Evade | 2 Tokens | +4 Evade |
| Attack | Bond | 2d8 Attack | 2 Tokens | +2d8 Attack Dice |
| Damage | Bond | 1d8 Damage | 2 Tokens | +1d8 Damage |
| Movement | Bond | 8 m | 1 Token | +2 m in the chosen Movement Mode |
| Additional Body | Body | 1 Body | 2 Tokens | +1 Summon Body |
| Shared Sense | Body | None | 2 Tokens | +1 Shared Sense Group |
| Summon Skill Dice | Bond | None | 1 Token | +2 Skill Dice to distribute |
| Extra Attack | Bond | 1 Attack | 8 Tokens | +1 Summon Attack |
| Special Access | Bond | None | 4 Tokens | Unlock 1 eligible Special at Special(1) |
| Special Value | Bond | Special(1) | 2 Tokens | Increase the chosen Special by +1 |
| Canonical Power | Body | None | Special | Pay 1 Token per 10 PP, rounded up |

---

### Upgrade Scope

**Bond Upgrades** apply once to the entire Summon Bond. All Bodies use the Bond's Attack, Damage, Movement, available Summon Attacks, selected Special, and purchased Summon Skill Dice. **Body Upgrades** are assigned to one specific Summon Body. HP, Armor, Evade, Shared Senses, and purchased Powers do not automatically transfer to another Body.

An Additional Body begins with the Universal Base Summon's Body values:

- 10 HP
- 0 Armor
- 4 Evade

It then uses the Bond's Attack, Damage, Movement, Summon Attacks, Special, and purchased Summon Skill Dice.

---

### Upgrade Limits

| **Upgrade** | **Limit** |
|---|---|
| Movement | Maximum 16 m; only the one chosen Movement Mode. |
| Extra Attack | Maximum 3 Summon Attacks per Bond per Round. |
| Special Access | Maximum 1 Special per Bond. |
| Special Value | Maximum Special(4). |
| Summon Skills | Two to four selected Skills, based on Bound Stones. |
| Canonical Powers | Maximum Power Level is set by the owner's Mastery Rank. |

HP, Armor, Evade, Attack, and Damage have no separate Summon maximum. Their practical limit is the available Token investment.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Multiple Bodies and Summon Attacks

Additional Bodies do not grant additional Summon Attacks by themselves. All Bodies share one Summon Activation and the number of Summon Attacks purchased for the Bond. Each available Summon Attack may be made by any active Body. Several attacks may be made by the same Body or divided between different Bodies.

#### Summon Attack Rolls

Whenever a normal Summon Attack or a purchased Power requires an Attack Roll or Casting Roll, use:

`Summon Attack Roll = Bond Attack Dice k owner's Mastery Rank`

The Keep value can never exceed the number of Bond Attack Dice actually rolled. All Bodies in the Bond use the same Bond Attack Dice and the owner's Mastery Rank for these rolls. A purchased **Spell Power** uses **Base TN = 4 × its chosen, legal Power Level**; the owner's Mastery Rank only sets Keep and the relevant Power Level cap, not the Spell's Base TN. A normal Summon Attack deals the Bond's Damage and may apply the Bond's eligible Special, subject to the once-per-Round Special limit below.

If the Bond has a Special, that Special may apply only **once per Round**, regardless of how many Bodies or Summon Attacks the Bond has. Multiple Bodies do not receive repeated attempts at the same non-combat task.

> **One task allows one Summon Skill Check per Bond.**

---

### Summon Activation

Summons act on the owner's Initiative. At the start of each Round, the owner chooses whether the Summon Bond acts immediately before or immediately after them.

During the Summon Activation:

- every active Body may move once,
- the Bond may use its available Summon Attacks,
- and purchased Powers may be used through their normal action type.

Summons cannot delay, ready, or select a separate Initiative count. Summons cannot use Stone Abilities or Artifacts, cannot create another Summon Bond, and cannot bind Stones of their own.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Summon Skills

A Summon does not receive Attributes or the complete character Skill system. Instead, the Bond selects a limited number of **Summon task skills from the closed list below**. The player-character Specialization list remains open and freely named.

| **Bound Stones** | **Selected Summon Skills** |
|:--:|:--:|
| 1 | 2 Skills |
| 2 | 3 Skills |
| 3 or more | 4 Skills |

Bonus Summon Tokens do not increase the number of selected Skills. The **only permitted independent Summon Skill selections** are these ten simple observation, movement, hiding, and fieldcraft tasks:

- Perception (universal; the owner receives Perception Rank 2 for free)
- Tracking, Navigation, Weather Sense
- Stealth, Concealment
- Athletics, Acrobatics
- Search (basic physical searching), Survival (outdoor fieldcraft)

This is a **closed Summon list**, not a mandatory player Skill list. Summons cannot independently purchase Social persuasion, academic lore, Medicine, Blacksmithing, Alchemy, engineering, or other professional tasks. For each selected Summon task except Perception, the owner must possess a **learned, fitting Specialization**.

---

### Buying and Distributing Skill Dice

**Match meaning, not spelling:** A player's freely named **Woodland Stalking 3** can qualify a Summon for **Stealth** in woodland, if the agreed Specialization scope covers that task. It does **not** automatically qualify the Bond for urban Stealth, Tracking, or every other Summon task. **Tracking** or **Stealth** named directly qualifies when appropriate. The GM confirms the correspondence **when the Bond's Summon Skills are selected**, recording any terrain or method restriction. The mapping does not grant the Summon additional Skills, player Skill Ranks, Free Raises, or broader professional competence.

Each **Summon Skill Dice** purchase costs **1 Token** and grants **2 Skill Dice**. These dice may be divided freely among the Bond's selected Summon Skills, including one die at a time. For any selected Summon task except Perception, the number of assigned Skill Dice cannot exceed **twice the Rank of one matching, learned owner Specialization** (as agreed and scoped above). Do not add multiple Specialization Ranks together. A Summon cannot select a task without a matching learned Specialization. For the universal **Summon Perception** task, the cap remains **twice the owner's Mastery Rank**, independent of the owner's free Perception Rank.

These caps limit **Summon Token purchases only**. Player characters never add these dice to their Attribute Pools.

#### Example

The owner has:

- Mastery Rank 2 (Summon Perception Dice cap 4)
- Stealth Rank 2 (Summon Stealth Dice cap 4)
- Tracking Rank 1 (Summon Tracking Dice cap 2)

The Bond has bought 4 Skill Dice. It may distribute them as:

- Perception 2d8
- Stealth 1d8
- Tracking 1d8

It could instead assign all 4 dice to Perception or Stealth, but it could not assign 4 dice to Tracking because the owner's Tracking Rank 1 allows a maximum of 2 assigned dice.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Summon Skill Checks

When a Summon independently performs a selected Skill task, roll its assigned Skill Dice and keep dice equal to the owner's Mastery Rank.

> **Summon Skill Check = assigned Skill Dice k owner's Mastery Rank**

The number of kept dice can never exceed the number of dice rolled. Summon Skill Dice are a **separate Summon Bond purchase**, not consumable player Skill Points. They are the complete dice pool used for the Summon's independent check. Summons do not gain character Skill Ranks or declare **Paid or Free Skill Raises**, and cannot perform complex professional tasks merely by rolling well. All Bodies share the same selected Skills and Skill Dice. Body count never grants repeated rolls for one task.

---

### Shared Senses

A Shared Sense is assigned to one Body.

| **Shared Sense Group** | **Cost** |
|---|:--:|
| Sight | 2 Tokens |
| Hearing | 2 Tokens |
| Taste / Smell | 2 Tokens |
| Touch / Pressure | 2 Tokens |

When the owner actively perceives through a Body's Shared Sense, the owner makes an ordinary universal Perception check with **their own relevant Attribute and Mastery Rank**, from the Body's position and through that Sense Group. The owner uses their **Perception Rank** (starting at 2) for any legally declared Skill Raises; Perception adds no dice to the Attribute Pool. When the Summon observes independently, it uses the Bond's purchased Summon Perception Dice instead. Switching between the owner's senses and a Summon's Shared Senses costs **1 Movement** during combat. Outside combat, switching senses requires no action. Shared Senses grant sensory access only. They do not grant automatic success, knowledge, analysis, or additional Skill checks.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Purchasing Canonical Powers

Summons do not use separate miniature abilities such as Pounce, Guardian, Scout Training, or other Summon-only substitutes. If a Summon needs a special combat ability, it buys a complete existing Power from the canonical Power catalogues.

> **Power Token Cost = the chosen Power Level's PP value divided by 10, rounded up.**

Use the Power's own written PP calculation. The Power keeps its normal Type, requirements, range, duration, trigger, effect, limitations, and action economy.

#### Standard Power Cost Reference

| **Power Type** | **Summon Token Cost** |
|---|:---:|
| Active | 3 Tokens per Power Level |
| Passive | 2 Tokens per Power Level |
| Reaction | 2 Tokens per Power Level |
| Active Buff | 3 Tokens per Power Level + 1 Token |
| Movement Power | Written PP value divided by 10, rounded up |

#### Power Level Cap

| **Mastery Rank** | **Maximum Power Level** |
|:--:|:--:|
| **MR 1** | **Level 2** |
| **MR 2** | **Level 4** |
| **MR 3** | **Level 6** |
| **MR 4** | **Level 8** |
| **MR 5** | **Level 10** |
| **MR 6** | **Level 12** |
| **MR 7** | **Level 14** |
| **MR 8** | **Level 16** |

A purchased Power is assigned to one specific Body. The Body must meet every requirement of the Power. A natural attack may satisfy an **Unarmed** requirement, but it is not automatically a Melee Weapon, Ranged Weapon, Spell Focus, shield, or other required item. A Summon has no normal Attributes. If a Power requires an Attack Roll or Casting Roll, roll the Bond's Attack Dice and keep dice equal to the owner's Mastery Rank. The Keep value can never exceed the number of Bond Attack Dice actually rolled. A Power that requires an Attribute, resource, item, or subsystem the Summon does not possess cannot be purchased or used unless an explicit rule provides the missing requirement.

---

### Power Action Economy

- **Active:** uses one available Summon Attack.
- **Active Buff:** uses one available Summon Attack to activate. A Summon Bond may maintain only one Active Buff of its own at a time.
- **Passive:** affects only the Body to which it is assigned.
- **Reaction:** a Summon Bond may use no more than one Reaction per Round, regardless of Bodies or purchased Reactions.
- **Movement Power:** replaces the using Body's normal Movement for that Turn and does not add a second permanent Movement Mode.

A purchased Power cannot grant Stones, Artifacts, another Summon Bond, or additional actions beyond the normal limits of the Summon subsystem.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
### Summon Specials

Special Access unlocks one eligible numeric **Special(X)** at **Special(1)** for the Bond's normal Summon Attacks. Special Value increases that Special by +1, to a maximum of **Special(4)**. The Special applies only when a Summon Attack hits and may apply only once per Round for the entire Bond. A canonical Power applies only the Special written in that Power and follows the Power's normal rules. It does not grant permanent Special Access to the Bond's normal attacks.

::
### Bond Ritual and Destroyed Bodies

A Bond Ritual takes **1 Tyhran Hour**.

During a Bond Ritual, the owner may:

- create or release a Summon Bond,
- add or remove Bound Stones,
- change the Bond's Expression,
- change its one selected Movement Mode,
- redistribute Summon Tokens,
- change selected Summon Skills and Skill Dice,
- assign Body Upgrades and Powers,
- or restore destroyed Summon Bodies.

A Body reduced to 0 HP becomes **Dormant** and leaves play. Its upgrades and assigned Powers remain part of the Bond but cannot be used while it is Dormant. Dormant Bodies return at full HP after a Bond Ritual or Safe Haven Rest. Bound Stones remain Bound when a Body is destroyed. They do not become inert for a random number of days. When the Bond is released through a Bond Ritual, its Bound Stones return to the owner's usable Stone Pool.

---

### Summon Active Buffs

The dedicated Summon Active Buffs are:

- **Active Buff: Summon Damage Aura**
- **Active Buff: Summon Armor Aura**

They use their complete Power Level profiles, bonuses, radii, restrictions, and timing from the <a href="https://homebrewery.naturalcrit.com/share/-LDVoC64w7wX" target="_blank" rel="noopener noreferrer">Active Buffs catalogue</a>.

Do not use the progression or radii of **Active Buff: Damage** or **Active Buff: Armor** for these Powers. Both affect only the caster's own Summons and use the owner's maintained Active Buff slot. Each Summon must meet all radius and eligibility requirements in the catalogue.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Summon Examples

The following examples are not separate Summon classes. Every example uses the same Universal Base Summon and the same Token list.

### Scout Owl - 1 Bound Stone

**Concept:** A simple flying Familiar used for reconnaissance.

**Available:** 8 Summon Tokens **Selected Movement Mode:** Flying **Selected Skills:** Perception, Stealth

| **Purchase** | **Cost** |
|---|:--:|
| Shared Sight | 2 Tokens |
| Shared Hearing | 2 Tokens |
| 4 Summon Skill Dice | 2 Tokens |
| +4 m Movement | 2 Tokens |
| **Total** | **8 Tokens** |

Distribute the four Skill Dice as **Perception 2d8** and **Stealth 2d8**, assuming the owner has **MR 1+** and **Stealth Rank 1+**.

**Finished Owl:**

- 10 HP
- Armor 0
- Evade 4
- 12 m Flying Movement
- Shared Sight and Hearing
- Perception 2kMR
- Stealth 2kMR
- 1 weak Summon Attack at 2d8 Attack and 1d8 Damage

The Owl is a complete scout with one Stone. It can observe independently or act as a remote sensory position for its owner.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Great War Bear - 4 Bound Stones

**Concept:** A durable tank and heavy melee attacker.

**Available:** 32 Summon Tokens **Selected Movement Mode:** Walking

| **Purchase** | **Cost** |
|---|:--:|
| +120 HP | 6 Tokens |
| +12 Armor | 6 Tokens |
| +4d8 Attack | 4 Tokens |
| +4d8 Damage | 8 Tokens |
| Special Access: Challenge(1) | 4 Tokens |
| Special Value +2 | 4 Tokens |
| **Total** | **32 Tokens** |

**Finished Bear:**

- 130 HP
- Armor 12
- Evade 4
- Attack 6d8
- Damage 5d8
- 8 m Walking Movement
- 1 Summon Attack
- Challenge(3), applicable once per Round

The Bear is difficult to remove, dangerous in melee, and can pressure one enemy through a normal existing Special rather than a separate Summon-only tank rule.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Skeleton Warband - 4 Bound Stones

**Concept:** Many individually weak melee fighters controlled through one Bond.

**Available:** 32 Summon Tokens **Selected Movement Mode:** Walking

| **Purchase** | **Cost** |
|---|:--:|
| 6 Additional Bodies | 12 Tokens |
| 2 Extra Attacks | 16 Tokens |
| +2d8 Attack | 2 Tokens |
| +1d8 Damage | 2 Tokens |
| **Total** | **32 Tokens** |

**Finished Warband:**

- 7 Skeleton Bodies
- 10 HP, Armor 0, and Evade 4 per Body
- 8 m Walking Movement
- Bond Attack 4d8
- Bond Damage 2d8
- 3 Summon Attacks per Round

The seven Skeletons occupy separate spaces and may move independently during the shared activation. The Bond still makes no more than three attacks per Round. The remaining Bodies provide presence, positioning, and additional targets without multiplying the action economy.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Shadow Panther - 2 Bound Stones

**Concept:** A fast, elusive predator inspired by an elite magical panther companion.

**Available:** 16 Summon Tokens **Selected Movement Mode:** Walking **Selected Skills:** Perception, Stealth, Tracking

| **Purchase** | **Cost** |
|---|:--:|
| +40 HP | 2 Tokens |
| +4 Evade | 2 Tokens |
| +2d8 Attack | 2 Tokens |
| +1d8 Damage | 2 Tokens |
| +4 m Movement | 2 Tokens |
| Passive: Damage, Power Level 2 | 4 Tokens |
| 4 Summon Skill Dice | 2 Tokens |
| **Total** | **16 Tokens** |

Assign the four Skill Dice to **Stealth 4d8**, assuming the owner has **Stealth Rank 2 or higher**.

**Finished Panther:**

- 50 HP
- Armor 0
- Evade 8
- Attack 4d8
- 2d8 base Damage, plus 1d8 from Passive: Damage (3d8 total for eligible damage rolls)
- 12 m Walking Movement
- Stealth 4kMR
- Passive: Damage at Power Level 2

Passive: Damage (PL 2) adds **+1d8 Damage** to the Panther’s attacks without a movement condition. Its Bond Damage remains 2d8, so its normal total is **3d8**, provided both sources are eligible for that attack. The Passive is a complete current catalogue entry, not a custom Summon-only effect.

---

### What These Examples Demonstrate

The same Summon system can create:

- a non-combat reconnaissance Familiar,
- one heavy frontline creature,
- many weak Bodies with a controlled shared action economy,
- or a mobile elite companion with a real Power.

No example requires a separate Chassis, class, or private subsystem.

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Minor Magic Items

Minor Magic Items allow characters to store their own eligible Active Powers in temporary magical objects. A Minor Magic Item may take any appropriate form, such as a potion, grenade, rune, prepared weapon, mechanical trap, or enchanted charm. The chosen form does not change the stored Power.

### Creating Minor Magic Items

During a Safe Haven Rest, a character may create, replace, or dismiss Minor Magic Items. A character may maintain a number of Minor Magic Items equal to their **Mastery Rank**. Each Minor Magic Item stores one use of a single eligible Active Power and counts against this limit until it is used, triggered, destroyed, or dismissed. An **eligible Active Power** is an Active Power the creator currently has access to when the item is created. This includes the character's own Active Powers and Active Powers granted by an equipped Item or Artifact.

A Power belonging only to another creature cannot be copied. Only Active Powers may be stored. Active Buffs, Passives, Reactions, Movement Powers, Rituals, Stone Abilities, Artifact Functions, and other Power types are not eligible. If the stored Power is granted by an Item or Artifact, use the values currently granted by that Item or Artifact, but never values above the **Artifact Level 4–6 / Power Level 10** band. If the source currently grants lower values, use those lower values. Values from Artifact Level 7 or higher, Power Level 16, or Ultimate progression cannot be stored in a Minor Magic Item.

The stored Active Power must have an **Instant** duration. A Power with a persistent or duration-based effect cannot be stored, including a persistent zone, barrier, construct, terrain effect, image, aura, or similar ongoing effect. Minor Magic Items cannot store or reproduce **Restoration of Scarred Health Bars**, Health Level Recovery, Stone Refresh, Safe Haven Rest resources or uses, or any other resource recovery tied to a Safe Haven Rest. Restoration instead follows its dedicated Safe Haven recovery resource. If an otherwise eligible Active Power includes both an allowed immediate effect and one of these excluded effects, the excluded effect is not stored.

Creating a Minor Magic Item requires no Stones, currency, or special materials. Any object or weapon included in its description must be present, but provides no mechanical benefits. An empty place within this limit can only be filled during a Safe Haven Rest. Existing Minor Magic Items may be retained and continue to count against the creator’s limit, even if they are carried by another character.

### Stored Values

The Minor Magic Item records the Power at the moment of its creation, including:

* Its **chosen and fixed Power Level** at item creation (a documented lower profile may be chosen then, but not on activation).
* Its purchased Effects and Specials for that stored PL.
* The creator’s relevant Attack Pool or Spell Casting Pool.
* The creator's relevant Keep value.
* Its Damage, Area, Targets, and other Power values. (Restoration uses dedicated Safe Haven recovery resources and is outside Minor Magic Item storage.)

Later changes to the character or Power do not alter an existing Minor Magic Item. The recorded Keep value is the Keep value the creator had when the item was created. It remains unchanged even if the creator's Mastery Rank or other values later change. The item stores and resolves only the pure Active Power. Temporary bonuses, Active Buffs, auras, Artifact Effects, Weapon Damage Dice, Weapon Specials, and other modifiers that are not part of the stored Power are ignored both when the item is created and when it resolves.
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
If the stored Power requires an Attack Roll or Spell Casting Roll, roll normally using the recorded applicable Pool and Keep value. A stored Spell uses **Spell Base TN = 4 × its recorded Power Level**, plus any effective target Spell Resistance and applicable Paid Raises; it never uses the activator's MR to determine Spell Base TN. A Minor Magic Item does not hit or cast automatically.

---
An offensive Minor Magic Item deals only the Damage of the stored Power. It never adds the Damage Dice or Specials of a weapon used as its physical form. Specials purchased as part of the Power still apply. A Single Target Power remains Single Target. It does not become an Area Power merely because the Minor Magic Item is described as a grenade or explosive device. For an Item- or Artifact-granted Power, all recorded values are subject to the **Artifact Level 4–6 / Power Level 10 cap** described above.

---

### Using Minor Magic Items

A Minor Magic Item may be activated directly or prepared as a Trap. Activating or arming it requires the same type of Action as the stored Power. When prepared as a Trap, the character determines its location, orientation, affected area, and one simple trigger. When that trigger occurs, the stored Power resolves from the Trap’s location using its recorded values. If several prepared Traps share a trigger and that trigger occurs, all of them trigger. Resolve each Trap separately using its own recorded values. There is no once-per-Round limit on the number of Traps that can trigger at the same time.

Minor Magic Items are an explicit exception to the normal Power Use Limit. Each item is one independent stored use. Using or triggering one item does not mark the creator's or activator's own copy of the Power as used, and it does not spend or prevent the use of another item with the same Technical Reference. Any number of items with the same or different Technical References may therefore be used in the same Round if their Actions or triggers allow it.

After the Power has resolved, the Minor Magic Item loses its magic and no longer counts against the creator’s limit.

> ##### Example: Prepared Crossbow
>
> A character stores their Single Target Attack in a loaded crossbow and places it as part of an ambush. When a creature enters the chosen area, the stored Attack Pool is rolled with the Keep value recorded when the Trap was created. On a hit, the Trap deals the Damage and applies the Specials of the stored Single Target Attack. The crossbow’s Weapon Damage Dice and Weapon Specials are not added.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Artifacts

> *“Power never comes freely. Every Artifact leaves its mark upon the one who carries it.”*

Artifacts are supernatural objects, inherited manifestations, living relics, awakened weapons, magical armor, cursed tokens, or supernatural features permanently bound to a character. An Artifact is more than ordinary equipment. It has its own identity, occupies one or more **Equipment Slots**, and develops new functions as its Artifact Level increases.

---

#### Binding Artifacts

A character can normally be bound to up to **4 Artifacts** at the same time.

To bind an Artifact, the character must have:

1. sufficient **Artifact Capacity**, and
2. the required free **Equipment Slot or Slots**.

Artifacts may occupy the following Slots:

* Main Hand
* Off Hand
* Body
* Head
* Feet
* Amulet
* Ring

If an Artifact occupies a Slot, that Slot cannot be used by another Artifact or by normal equipment. A two-handed Artifact occupies both **Main Hand** and **Off Hand**, but still counts as only one Artifact.

---
### Binding and Awakening an Artifact

A newly acquired ordinary Artifact is initially dormant until the character chooses to bind it. **Binding requires no Ritual, Skill Check, Stone expenditure, or XP.** If the character has sufficient **Artifact Capacity** and the required free **Equipment Slot or Slots**, they may bind an acquired Artifact and awaken it at **Artifact Level 1**. Binding does not bypass ordinary requirements for holding, wearing, or changing equipment during combat. If the capacity or slot requirements are not met, the Artifact remains dormant and grants no Artifact functions.

**Artifact Level 1 is free.** No XP is required for the first awakening. A bound Artifact counts against Artifact Capacity and occupies its required Equipment Slots as normal. Later Artifact Levels are purchased through XP progression. **Echo Artifacts granted at character creation begin already bound at Artifact Level 1**, subject to their normal capacity and slot rules; they never require a separate binding procedure.

**Artifact-granted Powers use their ordinary catalogue rules.** An Artifact-granted Active, Active Buff, Reaction, or Movement Power follows the matching Power Catalogue entry and its explicitly printed modifiers; it is not a separate Power type. In particular, a successful single-target Martial attack adds the matching **Weapon Damage** even if its printed Special-focused Power profile adds **0d8 Power Damage**. The 0d8 describes the Power bonus, not the weapon. AoE and non-attacking Powers retain their normal exclusions.

**Fixed Artifact-granted Powers:** Once an Artifact's own Level unlocks a printed Power profile, the bearer uses the printed PL even if it exceeds the ordinary **MR x 2 Power Level cap**. For example, a legal MR 3 wielder may use a PL 10 Power granted at Artifact Level 4, although their directly purchased Powers remain capped at PL 6. The wielder must still obey the Artifact Level cap, slot/Capacity requirements, activation limits, and the printed Power's target rules. Artifact-granted Spells use their actual printed PL for casting TN (**4 x printed PL**).

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

#### Echo Artifacts

Some Echoes grant predefined **Echo Artifacts**.

Echo Artifacts follow the normal Artifact rules:

* they count against Artifact Capacity,
* they occupy real Equipment Slots,
* and they advance through normal Artifact Levels.

Unlike ordinary Artifacts, Echo Artifacts are part of the character’s origin and cannot normally be removed, sold, stolen, replaced, or unbound.

---

#### Artifact Progression

Artifacts have **10 Artifact Levels**. As an Artifact grows, it may improve its Base Profile, unlock new Powers, or strengthen existing functions. At Artifact Level 10, the Artifact gains **Artifact Awakening**. It uses its existing Powers, Stone Abilities and resource functions; no individual Level-10 Ultimate Power is required. The normal MR 6+ Level cap and 64 XP advancement cost apply. Existing printed Base Values continue to use their own progression; the Awakening rule creates no new Base Value. Players do not freely purchase arbitrary effects for an Artifact. Each Artifact has a fixed mechanical structure that represents its identity, history, and supernatural nature.

### Artifact Awakening (Artifact Level 10)

**All Artifact Level 10 benefits recharge once per Safe Haven Rest.**

At Artifact Level 10, the wielder may awaken the highest unlocked version of each distinct ability granted by that Artifact:

- **Active Powers, Active Buffs, Reactions, and Movement Powers:** Each may be used once without spending its normal Action or Reaction use. An awakened **Reaction costs 0 Reactions from the Round allowance**, effectively leaving one additional Reaction available that Round. It still requires its regular Trigger. All awakened Powers use their highest granted Power Level (up to PL 16), with their normal targets, Range, per-Round Power-use limits, other costs, and maintained-state rules. Only one action-free Artifact Awakening Power may be used per Round.
- **Stone Power Support:** Each supported Stone Ability may be activated once at the highest Rank supported by that Artifact without paying Stones for that Rank or any prerequisite lower Ranks. **For Remove Scar, this also waives Vitality Stone Sealing for the included Ranks.** The ability must be unlocked, and its normal timing, effects, and use restrictions apply.
- **Stone Pools, Stone Batteries, and similar stored Stone resources:** Each reservoir may be refilled once to its currently listed capacity at the start of a combat without obtaining or spending Stones from another source. The refill cannot exceed capacity. Stones supplied by this one-combat refill that remain unused at the end of combat expire; previously stored Stones retain their normal source rules.
- **Other activated Artifact Functions:** Where a function has a usable activation, its highest granted profile may be used once without a normally required Action, but it gains no additional output or uses beyond its printed limits.
- **Permanent Passives and Base Values:** Continue to function normally. They require no activation; Awakening does not invent an additional Passive Power or a new Base Value.

An Artifact Level-10 row refers to these rules rather than granting a separate custom Ultimate. Only explicit Level-10 exceptions printed in that Artifact's row add another effect.

---

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Artifact Level Cap by Mastery Rank

Artifact progression is limited by the wielder's Mastery Rank.

| **Mastery Rank** | **Maximum Artifact Level** |
|:--:|:--:|
| **MR 1** | **Level 1** |
| **MR 2** | **Level 2** |
| **MR 3** | **Level 4** |
| **MR 4** | **Level 6** |
| **MR 5** | **Level 8** |
| **MR 6+** | **Level 10** |

A character may never increase an Artifact beyond the maximum Artifact Level allowed by their current Mastery Rank, even if they have enough XP. After the free awakening at Artifact Level 1, apply the **Artifact Costs** progression for each **new Artifact Level reached**: **Levels 2–3 cost 8 XP each, Levels 4–6 cost 16 XP each, Levels 7–9 cost 32 XP each, and Level 10 costs 64 XP**. Multiple consecutive Artifact Levels may be purchased during one Upgrade Step if all intermediate costs are paid and the Mastery Rank cap is respected.

#### Complete Artifact Rules

The complete rules for:

* Artifact Capacity,
* Equipment Slots,
* Base Profiles and Base Values,
* Artifact Levels,
* Artifact Functions,
* Artifact Powers,
* Stone Functions,
* Amulet, rings, feet, weapons and armor Artifacts,
* Sense Options,
* Echo Artifacts,
* and example Artifacts

can be found in the separate **Artifacts Catalogue**:

**Artifact sources:** This Core Rulebook defines the general Artifact rules and the Echo Artifact progressions printed here. The separate **Artifacts Catalogue** provides additional Artifact profiles and construction guidance. Use a catalogue edition that matches your current rules. Every newly bound Artifact begins at **Level 1 for free** and advances through the printed Artifact Costs.

[Open the complete Artifacts Catalogue](https://homebrewery.naturalcrit.com/share/TigfuySgic8x)

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
<div style="max-width: 980px; margin: 0 auto; background: rgba(255,255,255,0.07); border: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.16); padding: 10px 12px; border-radius: 10px; font-size:1.1em; line-height:1.1;">

#### **Player Cheat Sheet: Core Flow**

<div style="display:flex; gap:14px; align-items:flex-start;">
<div style="flex: 1;">

#### **1) Core Roll**

**Attribute k MR:** Roll Attribute d8, keep MR, sum the kept dice. Natural **8s explode** (designated Critical: **7–8**). **Guaranteed Eight:** trade 8 Pool dice for one exploding 8, counted as one result. Example: Might 8, MR 3 = `8k3`.

#### **2) Skill Checks**

**Four steps:** **(1) GM** sets task, relevant Attribute, applicable Specialization (or Untrained) and **Base TN = Task Level × 4**. **(2) Player** declares only the number of Paid Raises (+4 TN each, within Rank allowance); Free Raises at Ranks 4/6/8 (1/2/3) need no advance selection. **(3) Roll** Attribute k MR against Final TN (or eligible Passive Skill Value = 2 × Attribute). **(4) On success**, allocate declared Paid + available Free Raises to suitable effects confirmed by the GM. Untrained attempts use Disadvantage and no Raises; familiar safe work may succeed routinely for the basic outcome.

**Echo Imprints:** Immediately after starting Attributes, choose up to MR free Imprints tied to Attributes **4+**; they express small magic and Echo Memories.

#### **3) Raises**

**Combat:** Before every Attack or Casting Roll, assign all Paid and eligible Intellect Free Raises to Damage or an existing numeric Special. **Paid +4 TN; Free +0.** On a hit, each Damage Raise grants **+MR d8** (AoE: **+ceil(MR/2) d8**) or a Raise adds **+MR** to its declared Special. Split Attacks, Autofire and Zones retain the allocation declared for that use. Spells use **Base TN = cast PL × 4**; direct Spells add effective Spell Resistance and Paid Raises to the target's TN. Placed Spells/Zones require **Creation TN = Base TN + 4 × Paid Raises**.

**Rituals:** Every character knows all **16 Core Rituals**. Choose a fitting approach and Attribute; spend **1 Tyhran Hour** and Seal Stones. **Base TN = 4 × max(3, Task Level)**; **8 Raises maximum** across named tier 0–4 and valid generic effects. Applicable learned Skills grant **1/2/3 Free Raises** at Ranks 4/6/8. Seal **1 Stone for 0–1 Raises; 2 for 2–3; 3 for 4–8**, including Free Raises. Roll the final TN (**Base +4 per Paid Raise**); success gives the declared effects, while failure leaves Stones Sealed.

</div>
<div style="flex: 1;">

#### **4) Round & Turn**

Each creature receives **one Turn**, normally **1 Movement, 1 Attack Action and 1 Reaction** per Round. Movement and uncommitted Attack Actions can be used in either order. Activating an Active Buff during Preparation commits the base Attack Action. Reactions follow their printed trigger.

#### **5) Power Use Limit**

A named Power may be used **once per Round**, identified by its **base Technical Reference** across sources, PLs and delivery styles. Extra actions permit different legal Powers or Basic Attacks, not a repeat use of the same Power. Reactions with explicit repeat permission follow their text. Competing additional Attack Action grants use the highest single grant unless expressly stackable.

#### **6) Initiative**

At combat start, roll **MR d8, keep all**; 8s explode. Add Initiative modifiers. The resulting score persists across Rounds unless spent or changed by a rule.

#### **7) Initiative Exchange**

At Round start, before actions: apply first-round Initiative rolls, resolve any once-per-combat Initiative Boost, optionally convert Initiative to **Initiative Colorless Stones at 4 × MR score per Stone**, then lock turn order from remaining scores.

| MR | Initiative cost / Stone |
|:--:|:--:|
| 2 | 8 |
| 3 | 12 |
| 4 | 16 |
| 5 | 20 |
| 6 | 24 |
| 7 | 28 |
| 8 | 32 |

Initiative stays at or above 0. Initiative Colorless Stones pay ordinary unlocked Stone costs and become Exhausted when used; recovery during combat requires **Influence: Colorless Recovery**. They disappear when combat ends and cannot be Sealed, Bound or burned.

</div>
</div>
</div>
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
<div style="max-width: 980px; margin: 0 auto; background: rgba(255,255,255,0.07); border: 1px solid rgba(255,255,255,0.16); padding: 10px 12px; border-radius: 10px; font-size:1.0em; line-height:1.12;">

#### **Player Cheat Sheet: Defense & Resources**

<div style="display:flex; gap:14px; align-items:flex-start;">
<div style="flex: 1;">

#### **8) Attacks**

1. Choose the attack and legal targets; **declare and allocate all Paid/Free Raises before rolling**.
2. Build the Pool and resolve **one raised TN per target**: Martial = effective Evade + 4 × Paid Raises; Spell = 4 × cast PL + effective SR + 4 × Paid Raises. AoE uses one roll against each target's TN.
3. On each successful hit, apply the declared effects and eligible Special modifiers, roll Damage, then apply Armor and Damage Reduction.

**Special value:** printed + predeclared Raises + Stone Boost + eligible Passive Amplification + Vulnerability − Negation; then apply the Round cap. Spell Resistance Piercing applies to the caster's Spells. A direct Spell result below its Base TN fizzles; otherwise each target compares its own Final TN. Placement uses the separate **Creation TN**.

#### **9) Damage**

Damage dice explode only when their own rule allows it. Apply **Armor**, then combined **Damage Reduction (max. 100%)**.

`Final Damage = ceil(max(0, Damage − Effective Armor) × (1 − total DR/100))`

If this yields zero, the natural-8 minimum still deals **1 damage per natural 8 on the original Damage Dice**. Ongoing Special damage follows its own Armor rule.

#### **10) Defense**

**Base Evade = MR × 4**, modified by gear and effects. Direct creature-vs-creature struggles use **Opposed Attribute Contests**: both roll their relevant Attribute k MR, higher wins, ties preserve the current situation. The initiating side has Advantage and an unaware opponent has Disadvantage for the initial contest. **Attribute Checks** occur only when an effect calls for them: Vitality (body), Resolve (soul), Intellect (analysis), Wits (intrusion/senses). Their TN is printed or derived from Source MR/unranked danger as specified in Core.

</div>
<div style="flex: 1;">

#### **11) Health & Fear**

**Health:** Five normal Bars, each with **Vitality × 4 boxes** (Fortitude can raise that to ×5/×6/×7/×8 at PL 4/8/12/16). A fully marked Bar becomes **Scarred**. HP Healing refills an available Bar; **Restoration** reopens a Scarred Bar at full HP, including Broken to awaken an Incapacitated creature. Emergency Medicine: adjacent helper, **1 Attack Action**, **Base TN = patient MR × 4**, success stabilizes; medical aftercare during Safe Haven Rest restores **2 Bars total with Medicine 4–7**, **3 with Medicine 8**, instead of the normal 1. Incapacitated characters cannot act or spend Stones; death follows the fiction, with reincarnation reserved for the GM Guide.

**Fear:** Roll **Resolve k1** when called for: **below 8: +2 Fear; 8–15: +1; 16+: +0**. Each Fear removes one Attribute-Pool die before Health penalties; standard affected rolls retain **at least 2 dice** if the original Pool began at 2+. Fear is cleared by Safe Haven Rest.

#### **12) Active Buffs**

Activate or replace one Active Buff during **Preparation**, committing that Round's base Attack Action. It lasts **MR full Rounds**, including the activation Round. Existing Buffs can be maintained without additional cost. Eligible Temporary HP/Damage Negation pools refresh during Preparation while the Buff remains active.

#### **13) Stones**

Start with **2 Attribute Stones**. Earn **1 permanent Stone per 20 Lifetime XP**; later Stones go to an Attribute (max **MR × 2 per Attribute**) or become Permanent Colorless at 1:1. Stone Abilities have **4 Ranks**: normal cumulative costs **1/3/7/15**; Premium **2/6/12/20**.

**Ready** = available; **Exhausted** = spent until recovery; **Sealed** = returns after Safe Haven Rest; **Bound** = invested in a bond. At Round End, recover **MR eligible Exhausted Stones total**: Attribute Stones, plus Colorless Stones selected by **Influence: Colorless Recovery**. That Ability is prepared once per combat, and uses one shared MR recovery limit. At combat end, exhausted permanent Stones return and temporary Initiative Stones vanish. Sealed and Bound Stones follow their separate recovery rules.

#### **14) Reroll Points**

Maximum = Disadvantage Points. Refresh after **Safe Haven Rest**. Spend 1 to reroll your entire result or require the GM to reroll one roll; the new result stands.

#### **15) Safe Haven Rest**

Restore **Reroll Points, Sealed Stones, eligible spent Stones and the available Health Bar**; release Stone-Bound Form investments, return **1 Scarred Bar** (Medicine aftercare: **2 or 3 total**), and clear **Fear**.

</div>
</div>
</div>
}}

### Source-Based TN Reference: Keep the Scales Separate

**Ordinary Skills:** Base TN = **4 x Task Level**. **Rituals:** Base TN = **4 x max(3, relevant Task Level)**, minimum **12**. A specific spell or power being examined uses the relevant **Power Level** as its Task Level; a levelled Artifact uses **Artifact Level**. A ritual targeting a creature or creator with **Source MR** uses **2 x Source MR** as Task Level. Never use the performing character's MR to make the same task harder.

**Hostile Attribute Checks against effects:** Use any printed TN first. If none is printed and the hostile effect has a real **Source MR**, the default remains **(8 x Source MR) - 2**. If there is **no meaningful Source MR** (e.g., natural poison or a collapsing floor), use **4 x the hazard's Task Level**, fixed by the GM before rolling. An Attribute Check is available only if another rule calls for it; none of these TN formulas adds an automatic second save. This is **not** the Ritual formula.

| Relevant Source MR | Ritual Task Level (2 x MR) | Ritual Base TN | Hostile Attribute Check default TN |
|:--:|:--:|:--:|:--:|
| 1 | 2 | **12 (minimum)** | 6 |
| 2 | 4 | 16 | 14 |
| 3 | 6 | 24 | 22 |
| 4 | 8 | 32 | 30 |
| 5 | 10 | 40 | 38 |
| 6 | 12 | 48 | 46 |
| 7 | 14 | 56 | 54 |
| 8 | 16 | 64 | 62 |

**Unranked ordinary Skills:** Simple 4, Easy 8, Moderate 12, Demanding 16, Difficult 20, Very Difficult 24, Exceptional 28, Extraordinary 32+. **Unranked Rituals always start at Moderate 12** and rise only for a concretely harder task. **Ordinary Skills:** total Raises capped by Skill Rank. **Rituals:** total Raises capped at 8, with no Skill prerequisite. Applicable Skills may supply Free Raises at Ranks 4/6/8 (1/2/3); **only Paid Raises add +4 each**. **Spell Casting:** base TN 4 x Power Level plus applicable Spell Resistance.

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Appendix — Special Descriptions

This appendix contains the full wording for Special Effects. The table in the main rules section is the quick reference. The entries below are the detailed rules text.

---

#### Blight(X)

**Type:** Timed (X Turns)

---
**Stacking:** No. Reapplying Blight refreshes its remaining duration to the higher of the current remaining duration and the newly applied X.

---
**Cleanse:** Yes

---
**Effect:** Blight represents disease, corruption, or pestilence. While affected by Blight, roll **every Attribute-based dice pool with Disadvantage**. Blight changes neither Dice Pool nor Keep, and it does not reduce Healing Dice, Cleanse, Armor, Speed, or damage by itself. **Duration:** X is the number of the affected creature's Turns remaining. Blight takes effect immediately when applied. At the **end of each affected creature's Turn**, reduce its remaining duration by **1**. At 0, Blight ends. Reapplication does not add durations; keep the higher remaining value. Blight does **not** use Natural Special Recovery, because it is Timed rather than Diminishing. Cleanse may reduce its remaining duration as an eligible negative Special.

**Disadvantage reminder:** Under the Core Disadvantage rule, one eligible die may explode (normally an 8, or a 7–8 on a designated Critical roll). Blight uses that same Disadvantage rule.

---

#### Challenge(X)

**Type:** Diminishing (X→0)

---
**Stacking:** Same source only

---
**Cleanse:** Yes

---
**Effect:** Challenge represents pressure, provocation, pursuit, protective positioning, or a threat that is difficult to ignore. When Challenge is applied, record the creature that applied it as the **challenger**. Whenever you build an **Attack Pool** for an attack that does **not** include the challenger as a target, remove **X dice** from that pool. If the attack includes the challenger as one of its targets, Challenge does not reduce that Attack Pool. Challenge never forces you to attack, move toward, or remain near the challenger.

Challenge reduces Attack Pools only; it does not change Attributes, Keep, Damage, Evade or non-attack checks. Apply it before percentage Health Penalties. The **two-dice minimum** for legal standard Attack Rolls still applies; a separately fully Parried attack remains canceled. A creature can have only one challenger at a time. Reapplying Challenge from the same challenger adds stacks normally. Challenge from a different source does not add to the existing value; it replaces the current Challenge only if the newly applied value is higher.

At the start of your turn, Challenge decays by **1**. If Challenge reaches **0**, it ends.

---

#### Corrode(X)

**Type:** Diminishing (X→0)

---
**Stacking:** Yes

---
**Cleanse:** Yes

---
**Effect:** Corrode breaks, melts, cracks, weakens, or bypasses defensive material. Your **Armor** is reduced by **X**, including for the hit that just applied Corrode, since the hit's Special resolves before Armor. At the start of your turn, Corrode decays by **1**. If Corrode reaches **0**, it ends.

---

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Disoriented(X)

**Type:** Diminishing (X→0)

---
**Stacking:** Yes

---
**Cleanse:** Yes

---
**Effect:** Disoriented represents confusion, blindness, sensory disruption, unstable footing, shock, smoke, noise, or broken focus. Your **Attack Dice** are reduced by **X**, and so are dice pools used to notice, locate, track, or identify something. The normal **protected two-dice floor** applies after other penalties. At the start of your turn, Disoriented decays by **1**. If Disoriented reaches **0**, it ends.

---

#### Expose(X)

**Type:** Diminishing (X→0)

---
**Stacking:** Yes

---
**Cleanse:** Yes

---
**Effect:** Expose makes a target easier to hit. Your **Evade** is reduced by **X**. At the start of your turn, Expose decays by **1**. If Expose reaches **0**, it ends.

---

#### Hex(X)

**Type:** Diminishing (X→0)

---
**Stacking:** Yes

---
**Cleanse:** Yes

---
**Effect:** Hex makes a target vulnerable to Spell damage. When you are hit by a **Spell**, take **+1d8 bonus damage for every 2 Hex already present before that hit**, rounded up. Hex applied by the hit contributes starting with the next eligible hit. At the start of your turn, Hex decays by **1**. If Hex reaches **0**, it ends.

---

#### Lacerate(X)

**Type:** Diminishing (X→0)

---
**Stacking:** Yes

---
**Cleanse:** Yes

---
**Effect:** Each separate Movement Event that changes your position deals **X damage once**. This includes ordinary Movement, Dash, Reposition, forced Push/Pull, and Teleport. One continuous path is one Movement Event regardless of distance or the number of Hexes crossed. Two distinct movements are two events. Resolve Lacerate damage when the movement event occurs. At the start of your turn, Lacerate decays by **1**. If Lacerate reaches **0**, it ends.

---

}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
#### Mark(X)

**Type:** Diminishing (X→0)

---
**Stacking:** Yes

---
**Cleanse:** Yes

---
**Effect:** Mark represents aim, pursuit, target lock, exposed movement, and precise battlefield focus. Mark is tracked as one global counter on the target. A Mark application becomes available **after its delivering hit finishes**. On a **later hit**, after its Damage Dice are rolled and before a Damage-Trigger Reaction is resolved, the attacking creature may spend any amount of **Mark** that was present on the target before that later hit. The amount of **Mark** spent becomes the **Damage Floor** for that damage roll. Each damage die that rolled lower than the spent Mark value is treated as if it had rolled that value. Damage dice that rolled equal to or higher than the spent Mark value remain unchanged. After the damage roll is adjusted, reduce **Mark** by the amount spent. If Mark is reduced to **0**, it is removed. At the start of your turn, Mark decays by **1**.

---
**Limits:** Mark affects damage dice only.

---
**Example:** A target has **Mark(12)**. An attacker hits the target and rolls damage: `1, 1, 1, 2, 5, 7, 8`

---
The attacker spends **5 Mark**. All damage dice lower than 5 become 5. The adjusted damage roll becomes: `5, 5, 5, 5, 5, 7, 8`

---
The target's Mark is reduced from **Mark(12)** to **Mark(7)**.

---

#### Root(X)

**Type:** Until Broken

---
**Stacking:** Yes

---
**Cleanse:** Yes

---
**Effect:** Root is a movement-lock Special. Root can only be applied with a minimum value of **Root(2)**. **Root(1)** cannot be applied as a valid Root effect. While Root is above **0**, your **Speed is 0 m** and you cannot move voluntarily. Root does not prevent attacking, casting, using Reactions, or being moved by forced movement. You cannot use Movement Powers to leave your current space while Root remains above 0. At the start of your Turn, reduce Root by your **Mastery Rank**. If Root reaches **0**, it ends.

Root does **not** receive Natural Special Recovery and does **not** use normal Diminishing Decay. All active Root contributions form **one shared Root(X) stack**. Record the **highest Mastery Rank of any source that contributed to this currently active stack**. This recorded MR determines the single break TN, including after natural reduction, until Root reaches 0; then clear both Root and the recorded source MR. For example, MR2 applies Root(3) and MR4 adds Root(5): Root(8), with break TN **30**. You may also spend **1 Attack Action** or **1 Movement** to make a **Vitality Attribute Check** against:

`TN = (8 × highest recorded Root source Mastery Rank) − 2`

On a success, reduce Root by **exactly 1**. The amount does not increase with the result above TN. If Root reaches **0**, it ends.

---

#### Ruin(X)

**Type:** Diminishing (X→0)

---
**Stacking:** Yes

---
**Cleanse:** Yes

---
**Effect:** Ruin deals ongoing damage. At Tick, take **X damage**. Ruin damage ignores Armor unless a rule says otherwise. At the start of your turn, after the Tick, Ruin decays by **1**. If Ruin reaches **0**, it ends.

---

---
}}
\page
{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide

#### Slow(X)

**Type:** Diminishing (X→0)

---
**Stacking:** Yes

---
**Cleanse:** Yes

---
**Effect:** Each point of **Slow(X)** reduces your **Speed** by **1 m**, to a minimum of **0 m**. At the **start of your Turn**, before Natural Special Recovery and decay, if your Speed is **0 m** or another effect prevents you from moving voluntarily (such as **Root or Brace**), take **X damage**, ignoring Armor like Ruin. If you are still able to move voluntarily, Slow deals no damage. Choosing not to move or having already spent your available Movement does not count as being unable to move.

Slow deals no immediate damage when applied. Damage occurs only at the regular start-of-Turn Tick. After the Tick, apply Natural Special Recovery and reduce Slow by **1**. At 0, Slow ends. **Example:** A creature with **10 m Speed** and Slow(5) has **5 m Speed** and takes **0 damage**. With Slow(10), its Speed becomes **0 m** and it takes **10 damage** at Tick. If the creature has Slow(5) and is also affected by Root, it cannot move voluntarily and therefore takes **5 damage** at Tick, even though Slow alone would leave it with 5 m Speed. The values in this example assume no recovery between applications.

---

#### Soulburn(X)

**Type:** Diminishing (X→0)

---
**Stacking:** Yes

---
**Cleanse:** Yes

---
**Effect:** Soulburn erodes instinct, presence, will, and spiritual stability. Whenever you build a dice pool based on **Wits**, **Influence**, or **Resolve**, remove **X dice** from that pool. Soulburn reduces dice pools, not the Attributes themselves. It does not reduce **Keep**, Damage Pools, derived values, resource maximums, Health Bars, or other fixed values created from those Attributes. Apply Soulburn before percentage Health Penalties; legal standard rolls retain the **two-dice penalty floor**. At the start of your turn, Soulburn decays by **1**. If Soulburn reaches **0**, it ends.

---

#### Sundered(X)

**Type:** Diminishing (X→0)

---
**Stacking:** Yes

---
**Cleanse:** Yes

---
**Effect:** Sundered makes a target vulnerable to non-Spell attacks. When you are hit by a **non-Spell attack**, take **+1d8 bonus damage for every 2 Sundered already present before that hit**, rounded up. Sundered applied by the hit contributes starting with the next eligible hit. At the start of your turn, Sundered decays by **1**. If Sundered reaches **0**, it ends.

---

#### Weaken(X)

**Type:** Diminishing (X→0)

---
**Stacking:** Yes

---
**Cleanse:** Yes

---
**Effect:** Weaken suppresses physical force, coordination, and structured thought. Whenever you build a dice pool based on **Might**, **Agility**, or **Intellect**, remove **X dice** from that pool. Weaken reduces dice pools, not the Attributes themselves. It does not reduce **Keep**, Damage Pools, derived values, resource maximums, Health Bars, or other fixed values created from those Attributes. Apply Weaken before percentage Health Penalties; legal standard rolls retain the **two-dice penalty floor**. At the start of your turn, Weaken decays by **1**. If Weaken reaches **0**, it ends.

---

---
}}

### Timed and Fixed Control Special Descriptions

#### Brace(X)

**Type:** Timed

---
**Stacking:** No

---
**Cleanse:** No

---
**Effect:** Brace locks you into a defensive stance. Your **Speed becomes 0 m**. While Braced, your **Shield value is doubled** for Armor calculation. At the end of each of your turns, reduce **Brace by 1**. If Brace reaches **0**, it ends.

---

#### Prone

**Type:** Until Ended

---
**Duration:** Until you stand

---
**Stacking:** No

---
**Cleanse:** No

---
**Effect:** You are knocked down and helpless. While Prone you cannot make attacks, cast Powers, move or use Reactions; your existing Evade and passive defenses still apply. You must stand before doing anything else. Standing spends **1 Attack Action** but leaves your Movement available. Prone has no numeric value.

---

### Until Used Special Descriptions

#### Bulwark(X)

**Type:** Until Used

---
**Stacking:** Yes

---
**Cleanse:** No

---
**Effect:** Bulwark stores defensive impact protection. As a **Reaction** when you are hit by an attack you can perceive, reduce the attack’s **final damage by 50%** and consume **1 Bulwark**. If Bulwark reaches **0**, it ends.

---

#### Immovable

**Type:** Until Duration Ends

---
**Stacking:** No

---
**Cleanse:** No

---
**Effect:** Only Feet Artifact Base Value B can grant this option. Declare it during the Round's Preparation Phase while allocating Stone Abilities (no separate Action or Stone cost). Until the end of this Round, voluntary Movement is 0 m and forced displacement (Push, Pull, knockback and equivalent effects) fails. Prone and other non-displacement conditions are not automatically prevented; normal Evade and other defenses still apply. Declare it again in each later Round you want to use it.

---

### Instant Special Descriptions

#### Stunned

**Type:** Instant

---
**Stacking:** No

---
**Cleanse:** No

---
**Effect:** When Stunned is applied, remove **1 unspent Attack Action** from the target's available Attack Actions **in the current Round**. Resolve the loss immediately; the effect leaves no ongoing Stunned state. If the target has no available Attack Action remaining this Round, there is no Action to remove.

---

#### Brutal Impact(X)

**Type:** Instant

---
**Stacking:** No

---
**Cleanse:** No

---
**Effect:** Brutal Impact creates a minimum damage result for one damage roll. Each damage die rolled counts as at least **X**. A die that rolls lower than **X** is treated as if it had rolled **X**. A die that rolls equal to or higher than **X** remains unchanged.

---

#### Disarm

**Type:** Instant

---
**Stacking:** No

---
**Cleanse:** No

---
**Effect:** Disarm forces the target to lose grip on one held item. If an attack with Disarm hits, the target loses grip on one visible held item. The item falls to the ground. The attacker chooses the affected item. Disarm can affect a weapon, shield, focus, wand, staff, tool, or other object currently held in the target's hand or grip. Disarm cannot affect armor, worn Artifacts, rings, amulets, natural weapons, claws, bites, body parts, or items that are not currently held.

Recovering a disarmed item requires **1 Movement** or **1 Attack Action**. Recovering the item also makes it ready to use again. If an item is physically attached, magically bound, Echo-bound, enormous, or otherwise impossible to remove, the GM may declare that Disarm has no effect on that item. Any other effects of the attack still apply normally.

---

#### Penetration(X)

**Type:** Instant

---
**Stacking:** No

---
**Cleanse:** No

---
**Effect:** Penetration bypasses Armor for one hit. The attack ignores **X Armor**. Penetration does not reduce the target's Armor value. Penetration applies only to the attack that has Penetration.

---

#### Precision(X)

**Type:** Instant

---
**Stacking:** No

---
**Cleanse:** No

---
**Effect:** Precision adds damage to a successful hit. On hit, add **+Xd8** bonus damage.

---

#### Push(X)

**Type:** Instant

---
**Stacking:** No

---
**Cleanse:** No

---
**Effect:** Push moves the target away immediately by up to **X m**. The creature applying Push chooses the legal destination and path, moving the target away from the source along legal Hexes. Push is a forced Movement Event. If position changes, Lacerate triggers once.

---

#### Pull(X)

**Type:** Instant

---
**Stacking:** No

---
**Cleanse:** No

---
**Effect:** Pull moves the target closer immediately by up to **X m**. The creature applying Pull chooses the legal destination and path, moving the target toward the source along legal Hexes. Pull is a forced Movement Event. If position changes, Lacerate triggers once.

---

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
### Support Special Descriptions

#### Cleanse(X)

**Type:** Instant Support

---
**Stacking:** No

---
**Cleanse:** No

---
**Effect:** Choose one creature. Remove up to **X total points** from one or more ongoing negative Specials affecting that creature that list **Cleanse: Yes**. You may distribute the Cleanse value freely between those eligible Specials. If a Special is reduced to **0**, it ends. A single Cleanse(X) can affect only **one creature**. If the creature has less than **X total eligible Special value**, any unused Cleanse points are lost. Cleanse does not affect a Special that lists **Cleanse: No**, and it does not remove a Power, Spell, Barrier, Wall, Summon, or other effect unless that effect explicitly states that Cleanse can remove it.

##### Example

A creature is affected by **Ruin(3)**, **Slow(2)**, and **Hex(4)**.

Using **Cleanse(6)** could:

- remove **Ruin(3)** and **Slow(2)**, then reduce **Hex(4)** to **Hex(3)**,
- remove **Hex(4)** and reduce **Ruin(3)** to **Ruin(1)**,
- or distribute the 6 points in any other way among those eligible Specials.

The Cleanse value cannot be transferred to another creature.

}}

{{pageNumber,auto}}
{{wide
## Limited Power Modifier

Only an **Instant Active Power that deals damage through its own printed Power Damage Dice** may be learned as a **Limited Power**. Choose this variant when learning that damaging Power; it replaces the standard version and cannot be switched on and off between uses. Its **Power Level, XP cost, PP budget, delivery, Range, area, target capacity, and normal action cost** do not change. Eligible deliveries include damaging Melee Actives, damaging Ranged Actives, damaging Spells, and **instant damaging Attack AoEs**. Damage Transfer Actives qualify because they contain purchased Power Damage Dice; their self-healing remains dependent on actual HP lost and is never doubled independently.

A Power is **not eligible** if it has no own purchased printed Damage Dice, even when its delivery uses a weapon or applies a numeric Special(X). Specifically, **Special-only Actives** (including Slow, Ruin, Corrode, and Blight), Healing, Restoration, Cleanse, utility and purely defensive effects cannot be Limited. Active Buffs, Passives, Reactions, Movement Powers, Auras, Persistent Zones, maintained states, lasting fields, Summons, Barriers, Rituals, Stone Abilities, and Artifact-only functions are also excluded from this generic construction option. A directly granted Artifact version of an otherwise eligible catalogue Damage Active follows the expressly written artifact exception, if any.

**Limited output:** Each use doubles **only the eligible Instant Active's own printed Power Damage Dice once** when resolving the damage. For example, a Power that purchases 6d8 damage deals 12d8 Power Damage Dice with Limited. **Never double numeric Special(X), Special duration, Special Application, Weapon Damage, external bonuses, Healing Dice, Restoration, Armor, Evade, Range, Radius, target count, number of Actions, or other effects.** Limited never adds Special application or repeats later ticks or hits. Existing caps, legal targets and defenses remain in force.

| **Power Level** | **Limited Uses / Safe Haven Rest** |
|:--:|:--:|
| 1-2 | 1 |
| 3-4 | 2 |
| 5-6 | 3 |
| 7-8 | 4 |
| 9-10 | 5 |
| 11-12 | 6 |
| 13-14 | 7 |
| 15-16 | 8 |

**Spend one use when declaring the damaging Instant Active**, before its Attack Roll or Casting Roll; a failed roll still spends the use. An Instant damaging AoE spends **one** use for the whole declared area attack, not one per target, and follows the normal AoE damage rules. Mark each use on that named Power's Safe Haven Rest counter. Once its uses are spent, the Limited Power cannot be used until the next **Safe Haven Rest** (recharging once per Safe Haven Rest). It does not become a normal, repeatable Power. The once-per-Round named Power use limit and normal Attack Action costs remain unchanged.

**Interaction with Vitality: Maximization:** A Limited damaging Instant Active may consume one prepared Maximization use on its combined Damage Roll. First double **only its printed Power Damage Dice**, build the complete legal Damage Pool, and then maximize eligible remaining Damage Dice. The Limited modifier never doubles weapon dice or any healing component. This spends one Limited use and one Maximization use; it never produces a second activation or an additional Damage Roll. **Pricing:** Limited is an explicit construction choice, not a generic PP discount. The printed Power Damage Dice are doubled on use; the Power's PP budget, XP cost and Power Level stay unchanged. No Special(X) magnitude or Special cost is altered.
}}
